listed separately, «порознь по статьям по делом» ("separately by entries according to their duties"). So the nominal composition of the Oryol
350 and 100 may have existed not in one but in several copies: central, regimental and local town ones. The document sought should now also
be looked for under the formulas «противень списка» ("counterpart copy of the list"), «по головам» ("by golovy"), «списки розданы» ("lists
distributed") and «порознь по статьям» ("separately by entries"), in later extracts on service and oklady, and not only under the title
«Орловская десятня» ("Oryol desyatnya"). The lists themselves are not yet available, so the surnames of the men among the 350 or the 100 are
still unknown.

Two official inventories of the Razryad Prikaz have also been checked. In inventory 9, «Столбцы Московского стола» ("Scrolls of the Moscow
Desk"), file 463 is indeed described as a complex of 7080/1572 on the gathering of servicemen to guard the border along the Oka. No other
sixteenth-century Oryol numerical or transfer file was found in all 445 pages of the inventory. The old publication gives fols. 21–85 for the
regimental roster; the modern inventory, fols. 22–41. This is a change in the foliation of one complex, not two different lists.

Inventory 4, «Десятни» ("Desyatni"), has also been examined in full. It begins in 1577, but its earliest file is the Kolomna desyatnya, not the
Oryol one. The first explicitly named Oryol part of an enrolment desyatnya appears only in 1628: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 270, fols.
141–151**. So the absence of an "Oryol desyatnya of 1566" is now not just an unsuccessful web search but a verified limit of what survives in
the two main Razryad inventories. This does not mean there was no recruitment; it means that its document must be sought under another title,
in another chancellery complex or through later extracts from a lost book.

Official indexes: [inventory 4](http://rgada.info/poisk/index2.php?str=210-opis_4),
[inventory 9](http://rgada.info/opisi/210-opis_9.pdf).

A third obvious Razryad reserve has also been checked — **inventory 17, «Столбцы Дополнительного отдела (“Безгласные”)»** ("Scrolls of the
Supplementary Section ('Voiceless')"). All 81 images and all 236 files have been examined. The name «безгласные» ("voiceless") means scrolls
that have lost their original titles, not secret lists of people. There are exactly two early files here: precedence (mestnichestvo) cases of
1576 and of no earlier than 1583. File 3 already begins with documents of 1613; no separate pasted-in Oryol list of the sixteenth century was
found in the rest of the inventory. File 2 names Roman Olferyev and Pyotr Golovin, but they are parties to a dispute over service honour, not
Oryol settlers.

**Inventory 13, part 1, «Столбцы Приказного стола»** ("Scrolls of the Prikazny Desk") at first looked promising, because its extreme date is
1581. However, the official Rosarkhiv guide lists only isolated materials before the Time of Troubles: a prescription for the armourer
(oruzhnichy) Bogdan Yakovlevich Velsky of 1581, the Toropets statutory charter (ustavnaya gramota) of 1591 and then a record of 1609 on the
admission of servicemen to the Postelnoye porch. There is no Oryol recruitment or allotment of pomestya (ispomeshchenie) among the early
materials. The first spreads of the inventory itself likewise begin mainly with files of 1614–1620s. So downloading all 608 files holds out no
promise of a hidden transfer list of 1566–1571: it would be earlier than the inventory's lower date and would have been noted among the
exceptional early documents.

Official sources: [inventory 17](http://rgada.info/poisk/index2.php?str=210-opis_17),
[guide to the Prikazny Desk](https://guides.rusarchives.ru/terms/9/2482/stolbcy-prikaznogo-stola).
The verified negative result, with a full "Names and Meaning" section, has been entered in the card
`PUB-RGADA-F210-EARLY-OREL-CATALOG-AUDIT`.

### Why the Very Oryol Book We Need May Not Have Survived

The official Rosarkhiv guide gives not merely another place to search but an explanation of the main gap. **The archive of the Pomestny Prikaz
(Service Land Chancellery) was destroyed by the fire of 1626.** This was the office that kept account of pomestye lands and assigned and
actually allotted land pay to servicemen. Consequently, the instruction, the allotment book (otdelnaya kniga) or the distribution book of the
first Oryol allotment of pomestya must have arisen precisely in its records or have reached it from the local chancellery.

The present **РГАДА, f. 1209, Archive of Former Votchina Affairs** is not a continuous sixteenth-century archive. The guide says explicitly that
it includes surviving books, documents of local institutions transferred after the fire, materials of other chancelleries and papers later
returned from other archival complexes. So the absence of a whole "Oryol allotment book of 1566" is now historically explicable. This is not
proof that it existed and perished, but it is a strong limit of survival.

The practical conclusion changes: what must be sought is **secondary citation** of the lost document — sotnye (hundred-extract) and ownership
extracts, transfer books (otkaznye knigi), lawsuits over old pomestya, the formulas «по старым писцовым книгам» ("according to the old cadastral
books"), «по отдельной книге» ("according to the allotment book") and «служил прежде с такого-то города» ("formerly served from such-and-such a
town"). The later name «стол Диких поль» ("Desk of the Wild Fields") must not be carried back to 1566: this desk for the mass southern land
distributions was created only in 1682/83.

Source: [official section "Pomestny Prikaz"](https://guides.rusarchives.ru/terms/9/2444/pomestnyy).
Detailed card: `PUB-RGADA-POMESTNY-1626-FIRE-OREL-SEARCH`.

In addition, the old printed finding aid of the land archive itself has been checked — the name index to the first volume of «Описание документов
и бумаг МАМЮ» (Description of the Documents and Papers of the МАМЮ, the Moscow Archive of the Ministry of Justice) (1869), covering more than 2,700
cadastral, census, inspection, tax and boundary books. On facsimile pp. 305–306 the alphabet runs continuously from Ananyinsky and the Andreyevs
through the Androsovs, Anikiyevs, Annenkovs and Antipins to the Apraksins. The forms **Anfilogov / Anpilogov / Onfilogov / Ampilogov** are not in
this section. This is not a census of every landholder: the index is selective and treats scribes and surveyors in particular detail. But the
general route through the ready-made МАМЮ alphabet is closed; the surname line should be sought in the text of a specific book, a personal extract
or a land lawsuit. Photocopies of both control pages have been saved in the card.

### Fond 141: there is no separate block for 1577

The search layer of the scholarly description of fond 141 used to leave a loophole: a file
might not have the word «Орёл» (Oryol) in its title. Now the [official gallery of the
old handwritten inventory](http://rgada.info/poisk/index2.php?str=141-opis_1) has been checked,
291 images in all. The original zoomable scans show an unbroken
chronological transition: images **0019–0020** contain separate
entries for 1559, and image **0021** begins the entries for 1589 and then
for 1592–1593. No spread is missing between them.

This rules out not only an «Орёл» heading but any separate storage
unit for 1560–1588 in the old inventory of f. 141, op. 1. So there is no longer any need to order
"the files of fond 141 for 1576–1578": there is no such chronological
block in the inventory. A single document of 1577 later
filed into a case of another date, or regrouped when the fond was reworked, cannot be ruled out,
but it can only be looked for through an exact reference in the scholarly description, not by a blanket
order. This makes the miscellaneous scroll files (stolbtsy) of the Razryad Prikaz and the late
land extracts a higher priority than fond 141.

### An exact fallback archive: five land cases of Anpilogovs

A specialist study by A. L. Zakharov on the service population of the Oryol region
confirmed that late lawsuits of the descendants of the first settlers sometimes contain
historical digressions into the 17th century and even the end of the 16th. The author worked not only
with РГАДА fonds 210 and 1209, but also with **f. 1325, Disputed Cases of the General
Survey**, and in the State Archive of Oryol Oblast above all with
**f. 6, Oryol Chamber of the Civil Court**, and **f. 68, Oryol Assembly of Noble
Deputies**. The dissertation abstract gives no list of the first Oryol intake,
but it does name a proven pocket where old information survived at second hand.

A check of the [official ГАОО catalogue](https://catalog.gaorel.ru/) immediately produced
five exact cases for the target surname:

- **f. 6, op. 1, d. 527**, 5 May 1783 – 30 June 1784: disputed land in
  Kromy district between the odnodvortsy Anpilogovs;
- **d. 1541**, 10 June 1791 – 11 May 1806: Anpilogovs against S. F.
  Dolgov, on a report of the Kromy lower court (nizhnyaya rasprava);
- **d. 1901**, 5 June 1794 – 13 August 1809: the landowner V. D. Kamynin
  against the odnodvortsy Trunovs and Anpilogovs;
- **d. 2568**, 20 September 1820 – 9 October 1837: an internal land
  dispute among Anpilogovs of Oryol district;
- **d. 2964**, 30 June 1836 – 8 August 1842: a dispute between Anpilogovs of the village
  of Dolgolaptevka and Anpilogovs of the village of Pokrovskoye, Oryol district.

What matters most here is not the late dates but the structure of the disputes. Two village branches
of Anpilogovs asserted their rights against each other, and in another case Anpilogovs
appeared together with the **Trunovs** — a rare surname from the early neighbourhood
circle of Bazdyrevo. Inside there may be chains of divisions of jointly held land,
ownership extracts and references to old cadastral books. But a catalogue
heading is not yet the content of the leaves: the personal names of Anpilogovs, a common
ancestor and the earlier town are still unknown. So no profiles or map route
are created until copies have been ordered.

All five headings and dates have now been independently checked against the published scans
of the [inventory of f. 6, op. 1](https://catalog.gaorel.ru/object/7198276). The former archival
numbers can also be read there: **d. 1541 — arch. No. 2436, d. 1901 — arch.
No. 2907, d. 2568 — arch. No. 171**. They need to be repeated in the archive request.
The "Number of leaves" column is empty against all five cases. The number **13** by
d. 527 is the case index in the old reworked inventory, not 13 leaves.

The reading priority has been refined: first d. 1901 (former arch. No. 2907), because
the combination of Anpilogovs with the Trunovs gives the narrowest bridge to the neighbourhood circle
of 1594/95; then d. 2964 and d. 2568 (former arch. No. 171). The cards with all
the dates, the limit of the evidence and a detailed "Names and meaning" section:
`PUB-GAOO-F6-ANPILOGOV-LAND-DISPUTES-1783-1842` and
`PUB-ZAKHAROV-2009-OREL-SERVICE-SETTLEMENT-ARCHIVAL-LEADS`.

A new exact search on the opposing party changed the order of the request. The catalogue
shows one more case with the same initials: **f. 6, op. 1, d. 268,
13 January – 3 February 1781**, «об отказе имения в Карачевском уезде
помещику Камынину В. Д.» ("on the conveyance of an estate in Karachev district
to the landowner V. D. Kamynin"). It is followed by **d. 457, 17 May 1783 – 25 October
1784**, on the conveyance of an estate in the same Karachev district, this time to the landowners
the Kamynins. Direct catalogue cards: [d. 268](https://catalog.gaorel.ru/object/1875135554),
[d. 457](https://catalog.gaorel.ru/object/1875154994) and
[d. 1901](https://catalog.gaorel.ru/object/1875670534).

This does not prove that V. D. Kamynin is the same person in all the headings, or
that Anpilogovs were disputing precisely the land from the 1781 conveyance. But there is now
a verifiable pair of documents: first, in d. 1901 one has to identify the disputed holding (dacha) and
read the title deeds presented, then compare it with the Karachev estate of
d. 268. This check matters especially because the early family complex
below the Minov forest already lay on the Oryol–Karachev border and was surveyed in the
joint book of 1584/85. Ordering d. 1901 alone without d. 268 is now
a poor bargain: one might get a long lawsuit but lose the document on how the
opposing party acquired the land.

Separately, the search by all forms of the surname in the official ГАОО catalogue was repeated.
The form Anpilogov yields seven cases, including all five land cases named above;
Anfilogov, Onpilogov and Onfilogov — none; Ampilogov — four later
cases on military conscription and an ownership record; the short forms Anpilov and
Ampilov yield only late items. No hidden local case on ancient
nobility was found. So the second main complex is still in
РГИА: f. 1343, op. 16, d. 2345.

### A breakthrough in the same book: ancient branches of Anfilogovs themselves

A full review of РГИА, f. 1343, op. 51, d. 593 changed the search priority.
Kirill Ivanovich's 1846 entry in Part II turned out not to be the only one:
there are **at least ten entries of Anfilogovs/Anpilogovs in Part VI, ancient
nobility**. The earliest were accepted by a single protocol of **20 January 1798**:

- No. 109, p. 107: **Ivan, Efrem and Yakov Vasilyevich Anfilogov**;
- No. 110, p. 115: **Mikhail Vasilyevich**, his wife Natalya Astafyevna, children
  Fedot, Foma, Roman and Avdotya, and then the wives and children of Fedot and Foma.

The second line preserves three generations. Fedot's children are named as Matvey, Konstantin
and Avdotya; Foma's as Fyodor and Fedosya. This is the literal family composition in the book,
not a reconstruction from matching names.

On the same spread, No. 112 — **Alexander and Ilya
Markovich**, and No. 113 — **Fedot Vasilyevich** with his wife, his son Tikhon and
grandchildren, were also read. Further entries read: titular councillor **Alexander Markovich** with his wife and
six children (1824, No. 188); **Vasily Fyodorovich Anpilogov** (1830, No. 205);
six **Alexandrovichi** (1833, No. 235); the chancery clerk **Yakov Fedotovich**
(1835, No. 264); ensign **Alexey Ivanovich Anpilogov** (1839, No. 320) and
**Ivan Yakovlevich** (1849, No. 410). An image of each line and a detailed
"Names and meaning" section are preserved in the card
`PUB-RGIA-1343-51-593-ANFILOGOVY-ANCIENT-NOBILITY`.

The main archival key is **the decree of the Governing Senate of 18 October 1849,
No. 7806**. One number is attached to Mikhail Vasilyevich and his sons,
Fedot Vasilyevich and family, Yakov Fedotovich and Ivan Yakovlevich. This
proves a common proof proceeding, but removes the over-simple
chain Mikhail → Fedot → Yakov → Ivan: the entries contain different Fedots.
The kinship has to be confirmed word for word by the generational pedigree (pokolennaya rospis).

This is more important than the detour through the Bazdyrevs. The first thing to look for is the proceeding under
decree No. 7806 and the materials of the protocol of the Kursk Assembly of the Nobility of 20
January 1798. РГИА, f. 1343, op. 16, d. 2345 begins only in 1826,
so the early proof of 1798 may have been kept in a separate file.
That is where the documents should be on the basis of which the branch was recognised as
ancient: service extracts, ownership deeds or an earlier pedigree.

The limit of the evidence remains strict. **Part VI does not mean
an automatic pedigree back to the 16th century** and does not connect the applicants with
Oryol Anfilogovs of 1594/95. It proves something else: the proof proceeding sought
did exist, and its people, dates and
decree number are now known. Nothing is added to the map yet: the book does not name the earlier town or route.

The printed source of this formula has now been identified exactly. The article
«Анфилоговы» in the *New Encyclopaedic Dictionary* (St Petersburg, 1911, vol. 3,
col. 108; RSL rsl01004103475, frame 69) says: «Анфилоговы (в старину
также Анпилоговы и Онпилоговы), русский дворянский род, ведущий начало
от Василия Дмитриевича А., “записанного по городу Орлу московского стола
в десятках”».

> *Translation:* "Anfilogovs (in olden times also Anpilogovs and Onpilogovs), a Russian noble family descending from Vasily Dmitrievich A., 'recorded under the town of Oryol of the Moscow Desk in the desyatni'."

The RSL scan confirms the text, but the article gives neither the year
nor the call number of the desyatnya. The coincidence of the rare first name and patronymic, the surname variants and
the town with the original Oryol entry of 1620/21 makes P0034 a very
strong candidate for that very ancestor, but the final identity
must be confirmed by a service-record (razryad) extract from the Heraldry Office file.

The place of this article in the chain of sources has now also been established. The old
Brockhaus–Efron *Encyclopaedic Dictionary* had no separate article
«Анфилоговы»: alphabetically "Anfal" is followed by "Anfelt", and
the list of new articles explicitly assigns "Anfilogovy" to the material first
added in the НЭС. The 1911 article is printed without a signature or reference. At the same time
it does not repeat Savyolov of 1906: he has neither Vasily
Dmitrievich nor the formula about the desyatni of the Moscow Desk. So the text reached
the editors by a separate route. An archival or
heraldic note is the most plausible, but its specific file can only be named
after the РГИА leaves have been read.

The apparent contradiction in the name of the desk has also been resolved. The original entry
of Vasily of 1620/21 now has the call number **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 14, d. 502**, and
inventory 14 is called «Столбцы Севского стола» ("Scrolls of the Sevsk Desk"). However, S. K. Bogoyavlensky
showed back in 1894 that the late archival division of part of the
Razryad documents was arbitrary. Most important is his account of the real structure of the prikaz:
in **1634 Oryol was directly part of the 3rd povytye (section) of the Moscow Desk**, which
was headed by Pyatoy Spiridonov. The Sevsk Desk took shape considerably later, out of
the Grain Desk. Consequently, the list of 1620/21 was created when Oryol
record-keeping really did belong to the Moscow Desk, and it ended up in the inventory of
the Sevsk Desk only during later territorial systematisation. The formula
of 1911 now matches P0034 not only in first name, surname, town and type of
document, but also in its historical departmental affiliation. The only alternative
is another lost Oryol desyatnya with a full namesake; it
cannot be ruled out without the Heraldry Office file.

An earlier but **not identical** printed compilation has also been found: L. M. Savyolov,
*Родословные записи* [Genealogical Notes], issue 1 (Moscow, 1906), p. 66. The original ГПИБ scan
shows that Savyolov's article does not trace the family from Vasily Dmitrievich. It
lists eight Oryol landholders of 1594 — Vasily, Timofey and Karp
Mikulovich, Belyay Vasilyevich, Ivan Pershin, Ivan Nechaev, Sofon
Timofeevich and Ermak Pakhomovich — and then states: **Vasily Semyonovich in
1628 was enrolled with a novice's oklad under Kursk**, and **the Stary Oskol syn
boyarsky Ivan Anpilogov served among the lancers (kopeyshchiki) in 1695**.

A repeat check of the full open scan corrected one significant letter:
for Vasily, Savyolov printed not the general abbreviation «А.», but precisely **«О.»** — from
the variant **Onpilogov** given in the article's heading. This fully matches
the primary Kursk form «Васка Семенов сын Онпилогов» (Vaska Semyonov syn Onpilogov) and shows that
Savyolov kept the different initial vowels of his sources rather than mechanically
normalising everyone to Anfilogov.

This is an important divergence in the chain of printed sources. The 1911 formula about
Vasily Dmitrievich was not a simple abridgement of Savyolov's 1906 article and
probably goes back to other material — most likely the Heraldry Office file
or an extract from the Kursk Assembly of the Nobility. At the same time Savyolov gives
a verifiable geographical arc **Oryol 1594 → Kursk 1628 → Stary Oskol
1695**. It shows the early presence of the surname group in three southern
service centres, but proves neither the kinship of the two later men with a specific
Oryol branch nor the direction of a move. So separate events in Kursk and Stary Oskol
have been added to the map without a connecting route.
The full card and two Records:
`PUB-SAVELOV-1906-V1-P66-ANFILOGOVY`,
`PUB-SAVELOV-1906-P66-KURSK-1628-VASILII` and
`PUB-SAVELOV-1906-P66-STARY-OSKOL-1695-IVAN`.

The check of the heraldic detour is now more precise. In the open name
index of **РГАДА, f. 286, op. 2**, the alphabetical position itself on
p. 8 has been examined: after «Анучины, род» (Anuchin family) comes «Анфилатов Ксенофонт» (Anfilatov Ksenofont), then immediately
«Анц (Анцов) Лаур» (Ants (Antsov) Laur). So the absence of Anfilogovs/Anpilogovs/Onpilogovs
here is not a query glitch or an OCR omission but a reliable negative result
for the published index of inventory 2. Anfilogy
Semevsky, found in the same index, belongs to the surname Semevsky and has nothing to do with the family.
Inventory 2 should not be gone through again; what remains are the uncovered inventory 1
of fond 286, the reference books of f. 388 and above all the РГИА proof file.

The question is rightly posed: by 1594 there are already many of them in Oryol district, while
the town has stood for only twenty-eight years. Oryol itself was indeed settled
by transferred servicemen ("svedentsy"), voluntary newcomers and
the local population. But this does not yet prove that Anfilogovs in particular arrived
with a ready-made surname: their name could have formed in Oryol district itself from
the name of a common ancestor, Anpilog. Below, the general way the
Oryol service core was formed and what is known so far about the family itself are shown separately.

## How the Oryol service core arose

### Smirnov, 1910: names, mechanism and the limit of the evidence

The full text of the main study has been found and checked against page images:
P. P. Smirnov, «Орловский уезд в конце XVI века по писцовой книге
1594–5 гг.» [Oryol District at the End of the 16th Century According to the Cadastral Book of 1594–5] (Kyiv, 1910), **pp. 58–60**. Official card and digital
copy: [Presidential Library](https://www.prlib.ru/item/355286).

Smirnov begins with an honest statement of the problem: «Население переселялось и было
переселяемо. Откуда?» ("The population migrated and was
resettled. From where?") He had no list of the first manning of Oryol:
the book of 1594/95 does not refer to earlier cadastral or construction books and
is very sparing with explanations. So the author gathers indicators of origin from
family bynames and recurring clan names.

| person in the book of 1594/95 | where recorded | what it points to | primary page |
|---|---|---|---:|
| Overkey Ivanov syn **Putivltsov** | Taichukov stan, Startsovo selishche | Putivl, the Seversk towns | 1063 |
| Grisha Obakumov syn **Starodubtsov** | Nepolotsk stan, zaimishche on the Gustovar | Starodub, the Seversk towns | 879 |
| the widow Anna, wife of Fyodor **Serpukhovitinov**, son Sergey; Lev Ekhimov syn Serpukhovitinov-Teryaev | Nepolotsk stan, Rosokhovtsy | Serpukhov | 870–871 |
| Ivan Istomin syn **Kolugin** | Kamensk stan, the new clearing (pochinok) Fyodorov on the Mekhovitsa | Kaluga | 884 |
| Karp Ermolin syn Kolugin; Erokha Ivanov syn **Kalugin** | Taichukov stan, Ryabtsovo | Kaluga | 1061–1062 |
| Maksim Ivanov syn **Rezantsov** | Korchakov stan, the hamlet of Oblomnoye Boloto on the Orel at Ponikov spring | the Ryazan land; the surname also existed in Tula | 942 |

The full structured register with caveats:
`data/genealogy/indexes/orel-origin-indicators-1594.json`. Smirnov's own main caveat
is fundamental: from a byname alone one cannot establish who
came directly from the named place, who kept the memory of a distant
ancestor, who came of his own accord and who was transferred. So these are **name-based indicators
of the core's origin**, but not yet personal migration documents.

The strongest new thread is **Livny**. Smirnov reports not a single similar
surname but a «значительное количество» ("considerable number") of names of deti boyarskie that coincide in
Oryol district and in the early Livny priprava (checking) books. His reference leads to
G. M. Pyasetsky, «Исторические очерки города Ливен и его уезда» [Historical Sketches of the Town of Livny and Its District], «Труды
Орловской учёной архивной комиссии» [Proceedings of the Oryol Learned Archival Commission], 1893, issues 3–5. This calls for a separate
line-by-line comparison: a match in 1615 does not by itself say in
which direction the family moved, but the mention of **earlier priprava
books** may lead to a pre-Oryol generation.

The full Pyasetsky volume has been found and checked. On pp. 73–76 he does indeed give
an alphabetical list of the surnames of the first Livny landholders from the Priprava and
census books of the 17th century. The result cannot be reduced to "similar
surnames were found":

- **there are no Anfilogovs, Anpilogovs, Onfilogovs or Ampilogovs**;
- of the immediate Bazdyrevo cluster, **the Trunovs, Nemtsovs,
  Krivolapovs and Bazdyrevs** were not found;
- on the other hand, a broad common circle is confirmed: **the Voloboevs, Grevtsovs, Kalugins,
  Karlovs, Krivtsovs, Mezentsevs, Ozerovs, Rozantsevs, Starodubtsevs and Faustovs**.

This is an important distinction. Smirnov is right: Livny and Oryol belonged to one
mobile service world. But the most characteristic family-and-neighbour group of
Anfilogovs does not yet recur in the published Livny alphabet. So
**a Livny → Oryol route for the family is not confirmed at present**. There remains a loophole
that the alphabet of surnames cannot check: an ancestor named Anpilog could have been
recorded under a different surname. That needs M. A. Matsuk's line-by-line publication of the book
of 1615/16 and the early grounds for the estates, not just the index.

Source: G. M. Pyasetsky, «Исторические очерки города Ливен и его уезда»,
«Труды Орловской учёной архивной комиссии», 1893, issues 3–5, pp. 73–76,
[full scan from the I. A. Bunin Library](http://www.buninlib.orel.ru/ekoll/UAK-1893-(III-IV-V).pdf).

A stricter check of parts 1–2 of M. A. Matsuk's scholarly line-by-line publication
«Город Ливны и Ливенский уезд в 1615/16 году» [The Town of Livny and Livny District in 1615/16] has also been carried out. In the searchable
text, the full forms of the surname, the truncated roots `пилог`, `нфилог`,
`нпилог`, `мпилог`, the personal name Anpilog, the Onanyins and the rare neighbourhood circle were checked.
**There are no family hits.** But from the broad circle, the following are recorded directly in 1615/16:

| Livny serviceman | No. | page of the publication |
|---|---:|---:|
| Kharya Fyodorov syn Mezentsov | 177 | 128 |
| Vasily Zakharyev syn Mezentsov | 267 | 131 |
| Ivan Pavlov syn Kalugin | 410 | 135 |
| Afanasy Fyodorov syn Voloboev | 635 | 141 |
| Sofon Efimov syn Ozarov | 648 | 141 |

This refines the chronology: not all the surnames from Pyasetsky's long alphabet
were already in the book of 1615/16 — some reached him from later censuses
of the 17th century. The direct Livny cross-section confirms Kalugin, two Mezentsovs,
Voloboev and Ozarov, but not Anfilogovs or the Bazdyrevo cluster. Digital
copies: [part 1](https://books.google.com/books?id=sFxpAAAAMAAJ) and
[part 2](https://books.google.com/books?id=vR1pAAAAMAAJ).

Smirnov also reveals the mechanism by which the garrison turned into an odnodvortsy core.
The primary act of 1577 refines his retelling: Oryol was due 500 mounted
Cossacks, but only 80 had been «прибрано» ("recruited"), 78 of them watchmen; **420 is
a shortfall, not an intake that took place**. Fit mounted watchmen had their
estates increased from 20 to 50 chetverti and were assigned a cash salary, and the shortfall
was covered by two internal measures: unfit or horseless watchmen were replaced
with «добрыми и конными» ("good and mounted") men from the rank-and-file Cossacks, and on top of the existing Cossack
complement, deti boyarskie with small estates were sent. This is not evidence of the transfer of
420 men from a single outside town. The book of 1594/95 already knows
stanitsa and watch deti boyarskie. Smirnov considers some of them Cossacks
enrolled as deti boyarskie and cites an exact parallel:
in Epifan in 1586, **300 Cossacks** were enrolled this way with oklads of 40 and 30
chetverti. So some of the future Oryol odnodvortsy surnames may have
appeared not as ready-made old noble families but through the service enrolment (verstanie) of
the Cossacks of the first garrison.

This hypothesis has now been tested against the family's land itself, not just against a general
analogy. Converting ploughland and wild field to a single field, Belyay Vasilyevich had
60 chetverti, Mikula's three sons jointly — 57, Sofon Timofeevich — 40,
Ivan Pershin — 33, Ermak Pakhomovich — 26, Osip Vasilyevich — 24; Ivan
Nechaevich was given 15 chetverti of not yet cultivated wild field in 1595.
The figures 57 and 60 at first look like a trace of the 50-chetvert watchman norm.
A control on the nearest neighbours, however, removes this as proof: in the same
Bazdyrevo the holdings of Marya Trunova, Gavrila Nemtsov, Lev Krivolapov,
Nikifor Bazdyrev and the three sons of Ivan Krivolapov amounted to 56, 58, 50,
50 and 56 chetverti. That is the usual local size of a share (zhereb). Besides, the actual
area in the cadastral book and the pomestye oklad are different measures. So
Cossack enrolment remains a possible mechanism for the family's formation, but
these figures do not allow one to call any specific Anfilogov a former Cossack.

The official РГАДА reference guide adds an important but internally contradictory
limitation to this picture. On p. 17 the compilers M. Yu. Zenchenko and
G. A. Ivanova write that until the beginning of the 17th century no scribes or inspectors (dozorshchiki) were sent to Oryol, Novosil and
Chern districts: at the end of the 1570s these new
towns were still unfinished service projects. Yet on the very next page
they name the surviving description of Oryol district by Dementy Yakovlev and
Leonty Sofronov **of 1594/95**. So the first sentence cannot be taken
literally as proof that there were no scribes at all before 1600.

The reliable, narrower conclusion is different: the catalogue knows of no complete general Oryol
cadastral book corresponding to the service muster of 1577. This is to be expected,
since the verdict required not a general cadastre of the district but an in-person muster of men,
horses and oklads, with separate lists returned to the Razryad. The land and service
composition of Oryol will have to be reconstructed from razryad, watch and pomestye
documents of different campaigns.

The same guide describes the roster of 1576 precisely. The Razryad Prikaz
checked how many mounted Cossacks had been **ordered to be recruited** and how many there actually
were: Oryol required 500, had 80, and was short by 420, that is 84%.
Consequently, 420 is not the number of people resettled but the size of an unfilled vacancy.
This confirms a staged model: the Oryol core did not arrive as one finished
party, but was still being manned ten years after the foundation.
Source: M. Yu. Zenchenko, G. A. Ivanova, «Писцовые книги юго-западного
порубежья» [Cadastral Books of the South-Western Borderland] (Moscow, 2013), pp. 17–18,
[official РГАДА PDF](http://rgada.info/sprav/sprav/zenchenko_ivanova_2013.pdf).

A full check of the catalogue removes a possible misreading. The «сохранившееся описание 1577/78 года» ("surviving description of 1577/78")
mentioned in the preface refers to Yaroslavl and
serves as a comparative example, not as the designation of an Oryol book. For Oryol
the first surviving land book is dated only **1594/95**:
the description and measurement of Dementy Yakovlev and the clerk (podyachy) Leonty Sofronov, **РГАДА,
f. 1209, op. 1, bk. 329, fols. 1a–63a, 1–937v.** Moreover, what has come down to us is a 17th-century
copy under the authenticating signature (skrepa) of the dyak Venedikt Makhov, not the field original.

The books of Ivan Kutuzov and Ivan Korovin of 1624–1626, bks. 330–331, describe
some of the vacant lands as recorded «по приправочным книгам» ("according to the priprava books"). The catalogue gives no
grounds for turning this formula into a separate Oryol inventory of 1577/78;
the nearest documented priprava basis is the book of 1594/95. So
what has to be looked for is not an imaginary complete cadastre of 1577 but the Razryad copy
of the name muster, memoranda (pamyati) of the Pomestny Prikaz, separate extracts on land grants and their
copies in later land lawsuits.

On 28 August 2026 a full name check of all 790 pages
of the official PDF was also carried out. Roman Dmitrievich Buturlin, Grigory Fyodorov
Andreev-Pavlov, Andrey Myasnoy and Bogdan Ksenofontov do not lead in the catalogue to
a separate Oryol book or to whoever carried out the muster; the
formulas 7085/1577 and the oklads of 20, 30 and 50 chetverti also gave no result. This is a negative
result only for the contents of the catalogue, not proof that an implicitly
described scroll of the Razryad Prikaz has perished. The catalogue's reference to АМГ, vol. I,
pp. 35–37 concerns a document of the **Razryad** Prikaz, not of the Streletsky Prikaz;
the earlier classification has been corrected.

The official digital copy of the Watch Book (Storozhevaya kniga) itself has also been found: **РГАДА, f. 210,
op. 6, d. 1**, 378 images,
[РГАДА viewer](http://rgada.info/kueh/index2.php?str=210_6_1). A check of the
foliation confirmed the target sections: fols. 121–202, 213–222 and 231–243 are
in the book and available for direct reading. The control spreads of the first block
show above all a detailed list of watch posts and shifts, not a sequence of
personal names. The next two blocks run as coherent text of orders; at their beginning
and end no separate appendix with the names of rank-and-file men is visible. So the formula
«роспись Орельским сторожам» ("roster for the Oryol watchmen") cannot yet be understood as a proven personal
register of 78 men. The digital book has provided a primary corpus for a full check,
but the name register itself has still not been found.

### The Karachev breakthrough: the joint book of 1584/85

The Livny search gave a useful negative result, but a stronger
lead turned up on the western border of Korchakov stan. The «Дозорная книга Карачевского уезда 1614 года» [Inspection Book of Karachev District, 1614] has been checked in full —
РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, bk. 167; scholarly publication in «Русский дипломатарий» [Russian Diplomatarium], issue 10 (2004).
It collects the sworn statements (skazki) and old ownership documents of the Karachev deti
boyarskie and refers many times to **the books of description and measurement of Yury Pushechnikov
and Mikhail Shishelov of the year 7093**.

Calendar precision is needed here. The Muscovite year 7093 covers 1584/85.
One extract is dated directly: **25 November 7093**, that is, 25 November
1584. In it Pushechnikov and Shishelov are called «карачевскими и орловскими
писцами» ("Karachev and Oryol scribes"). Consequently, about ten years before the known book of 1594/95
there was a joint Oryol–Karachev cadastral cycle.

Both scribes have now been identified more fully. **Yury Fyodorov syn Pushechnikov** in
the list of select dvoryane (vybornye) of 1577 stands in the Aleksin section with an oklad of 500
chetverti and the note «с государем» ("with the sovereign"). **Mikhail Ivanovich Shishelov** in 1578
described Galich with its district; the surviving sotnaya (extract) is authenticated with his seal. This
matters not as a genealogical clue but as an explanation of the mechanism: experienced
sovereign's scribes were sent from one region to another to survey and
secure service estates. So their joint book is a document of the state organisation of the new borderland,
but not a ready-made list of
settlers. Sources: [the select list of 1577](https://vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/21-40/26.htm),
[the Galich sotnaya of 1578](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Sotn_Galic_1578/text.php).

The main entry is on fols. 79v–81. Afanasy Vasilyev syn Karpov
presented a charter for two parts of one land complex on the Sinitsyn
spring: **40 chetverti in Korchakov stan of Oryol district below the Minov
forest** and **20 chetverti in Gorodskoy stan of Karachev district** on the same
spring. Both parts were measured according to the book of Pushechnikov and Shishelov of 7093.
Karpov himself was granted the land only in 1609, so he cannot be made an ancestor
or former neighbour of Anfilogovs. But the topography is fundamental: the family's
Bazdyrevo and Belaya in 1594/95 also lay **below the Minov forest**. So
right at the Anfilogov nest, estate complexes really did cross the border of
Oryol and Karachev districts and were recorded in a single cadastral cycle.

There is also a second micro-geographical contact. Istoma Bovykin declared
the Karachev estate of Pashkovo on the Shemyatitsa with lands «к Радимлю и вверх по
Радимлю» ("towards the Radiml and up the Radiml"). In the Oryol book of 1594/95 Ivan Nechaev syn Anpilogov had
a zaimishche on the Radomlya. This is not the same owner and not kinship, but one more
piece of evidence of a single border landscape.

The full text of 1614 has been checked for the variants Анфилог/Анпилог/Онфилог/
Амфилог, for the Onanyins and for the surnames of the Bazdyrevo neighbourhood cluster. The family is
not there. So the conclusion is strict: **Karachev has not yet been proven to be the town of origin of
Anfilogovs, but the Karachev direction has become the main one**, because it
leads to a specific book of the previous generation and to the exact locality
of the family nest. A complete separate copy of the book of 1584/85 has not yet been located in the accessible
catalogues, but one more surviving fragment
of its documentary progeny has been found. In **РГАДА, f. 281, op. 11, general No. 5939,
private No. 1** there is a 16-leaf extract of the Pomestny Prikaz from the books of Yury
Pushechnikov and Mikhail Shishelov. It was issued to the Voskresenskaya Tikhonova
Karachev hermitage (pustyn) and describes its holdings, the small village of Grebennikovo, hamlets,
clearings, waste lands, fisheries on the Navlya and wild-hive (bortnye) lands in Gorodskoy stan.
The extract has no ending. The family's micro-geography by the Minov forest is not in the catalogue
heading, but the find changes the strategy: fragments of the book must be sought not
only in f. 1209 but also in monastic, church and family collections, by
owners of specific lands. The first document to order is now known
exactly — f. 281, op. 11, No. 5939/1.

The official handwritten inventory of the fond has been checked directly: the entry is
on [image 0413](http://rgada.info/opisi/281-opis_11/0413.jpg) in the section
«Карачевского уезда» (Karachev district) and gives No. 5939/1, **the year 7093/1585** and a volume of **16 leaves**.
This makes it possible to remove an apparent contradiction of dates. The old inventory dates
the extract by the cadastral book of 1584/85 on which it is based. The modern
detailed description dates the making of the surviving copy itself to
**1673–1682**, names the dyak Ivan Ragozinin and defines the document as
an original chancery extract. In other words, what we have is not an autograph of the scribes
of 1584/85 but a late official copy from a lost book.

The neighbouring Karachev entries of the inventory have also been examined. Immediately next to it
there is no second 16th-century extract from the same cycle: No. 5940 belongs to inspection and
boundary material of 1616, and the following items are later. This does not close
the search in other ownership sections of the fond, but it shows that No. 5939/1 is
so far the only precisely located remnant of the book in the Karachev group.

Primary text and archival reference: [DrevLit](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVII/1600-1620/Dozor_kniga_karacev_uezd_1614/text.php),
[archaeographic preface](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVII/1600-1620/Dozor_kniga_karacev_uezd_1614/pred.phtml).
The next target is now precisely formulated: the book or additional extracts
of Pushechnikov and Shishelov of 7093 for Korchakov stan, the Minov forest,
the Sychov and Sinitsyn springs, the Otsna and the Radomlya.

### Pushechnikov's old book was still held in the Pomestny Prikaz in 1717

In the published «Список старых писцовых книг Поместного приказа 1717 г.» [List of the Old Cadastral Books of the Pomestny Prikaz, 1717],
under the heading «Карачев» (Karachev), a separate line has been found: «Книги старые писцовые
Юрья Пушечникова 203 году за их писцовыми руками» ("The old cadastral books of Yury Pushechnikov of the year 203, signed by the scribes' own hands") (ОДБ МАМЮ, vol. 5, Moscow,
1888, p. 367). The high-resolution scan confirms that the printed reading is indeed
**203**, not an OCR error.

K. V. Baranov, in the modern catalogue of cadastral books, interpreted this line as
**the year 7103 (1594/95)** and entered it as a separate Karachev book of Yury
Pushechnikov. However, this cannot yet be considered a definitively established
date. The Inspection Book of 1614 repeatedly cites the books of Pushechnikov and
Shishelov **of 7093 (1584/85)**, including the extract of 25 November 7093,
and in none of the places checked does it name the year 7103. So two
versions are possible: Pushechnikov really did carry out a second Karachev cycle ten
years later, and the 1888 edition misprinted «203» instead of «103»; or
the inventory line refers to the book of 7093, but its date is more badly corrupted
than the cataloguer supposed.

Regardless of how the figure is resolved, an important archival fact has been established: at least
one old Karachev cadastral book of Yury Pushechnikov was still
in the chancery repository in **1717**, that is, it survived the fire of the Pomestny
Prikaz of 1626. Now its fate must be traced between the inventory of 1717 and
the modern inventory of fond 1209. Until then a separate book of 1594/95,
and Anfilogovs contained in it, cannot be considered proven.

The current digital check has confirmed this gap. In the РГАДА search layer,
updated on 25 August 2026, both the recognised PDFs
and the metadata of all inventories were checked separately. In fond 1209 no file was found for the variants
**Пушечников / Пушечникова / Пушешникова**, **Шишелов / Шишелова**, nor
for the dates **7093**, **7103**, **1584** and **1594** in combination with Karachev
books. A search for the scribes' surnames across all fonds yields only later
namesakes and reveals no other copy of the early book. The modern
list of Karachev cadastral materials of fond 1209 begins with the inspection
book of 1614. Consequently, the complete volume cannot yet be identified with
any modern storage unit: its archival chain is documented
up to 1717, after which it breaks off. The practical path now is not a search for
"the whole Pushechnikov book" in the current inventory of 1209 but collecting ownership extracts
from monastic, church and family fonds.

An independent historiographical check supports precisely the date 1584/85.
A. P. Pavlov calls the Karachev complex **the cadastral priprava
book of Yu. Pushechnikov of 1584/85**: later scribes kept it before them
as the previous description and checked new holdings against it. So the numerous
references of the 1614 inspection are not random retellings but a trace of systematic
use of the old book. This raises the value of every surviving
extract: it may literally repeat the owners and land grants of the
generation preceding the Oryol book of 1594/95.

As of August 2026 no digital copy of No. 5939/1 has been found in open access.
On 30 July 2026 the Karachev district researcher Evgeny
Prudnikov publicly looked for existing scans and proposed jointly ordering
all 16 leaves from РГАДА; in the discussion found, the answer comes down to ordering photography of
the paper original, not to an already existing digital copy. This is a fresh and
verifiable channel for obtaining the primary text, but until images appear
no supposed names from this file can be entered into the database.

At the same time a more substantial surviving intermediary has been found: **the cadastral book
of Karachev district of 1626–1627 by V. P. Suvorov-Naumov and V. Repyev —
РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, pt. 2, d. 9181, 579 leaves**. This is a 17th-century copy,
that is, the now accessible complete Karachev volume of the next cadastral cycle.
It is especially important because the general description of the 1620s was checked against
the old priprava books, and so it may name former owners
from Pushechnikov's book even where no separate ownership extract has
survived. A targeted web search for the surname variants and family toponyms in
the accessible indexes gave no result; this is not a negative conclusion about the file,
since the 579 handwritten leaves have not been published in full text. Viewing priority
within d. 9181: Roslavl and Khotimsk stans, then Gorodskoy stan
by the Oryol border, checking for the Minov forest, the Otsna, the Radomlya and the formulas
«по книгам Юрья Пушечникова» ("according to the books of Yury Pushechnikov").

An address for the first targeted viewing has now been found in the official archival
alphabet. All 68 images of **alphabet 120 to the Karachev
cadastral, census and conveyance books** (РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 85) have been checked in full. The sections
for А and О contain no surnames Anpilogov, Anfilogov, Onfilogov or Onpilogov. This is
stronger than the negative result of the web search, but still does not rule out a personal
name or surname inside an unindexed formula about a former owner.

The alphabet does, however, point to: **«Озеровы. Кни. 1 писц.; № 181»** ("Ozerovs. Bk. 1 cadastral; No. 181"). The opening table of
the same alphabet matches the first cadastral book to the general number **9181**.
But the heading of the alphabet literally says: **«Фамилии владельцев с показанием
№ книги и дела»** ("Surnames of owners with the No. of book and file"). So No. 181 is not a leaf of the manuscript but the internal number
of an ownership entry. The earlier request for fol. 181 was mistaken and has been cancelled.

The paired historical inventory 47 discloses this entry on internal p. 25:
**«Иваново поместье Иванова сына Озерова, пуст[ошь] на колодце Речечку»** ("Ivan's pomestye of Ivanov syn Ozerov, a waste plot on the Rechechek spring").
The last toponym is a provisional reading. The formula names Ivan Ivanovich
Ozerov: «Иваново поместье» — Ivan's pomestye, «Иванова сына Озерова» —
of Ivanovich Ozerov. The inventory does not give the leaf of the book itself, so the exact request
now reads: establish the leaves of **internal entry No. 181** in d. 9181 and
make a copy of the whole entry, with its beginning and continuation, from the microfilm.

There is an independent continuation. The inspection of 1614 reports that on 24 September
Vasily Ivanovich Yurasov was conveyed twenty chetverti of land in Gorodskoy stan —
**«Дмитреевское поместье Иванова сына Озерова»** ("Dmitry's pomestye of Ivanov syn Ozerov"). This formula
names Dmitry Ivanovich Ozerov. Ivan and Dmitry were probably sons of
one Ivan Ozerov, although there is no direct word "brothers" yet.

A comparison with Oryol has weakened the hypothesis of a personal move. In the book of 1594/95,
on p. 959, Afanasy, Shiryay, Sevastyan, Ivan, Sava and Timofey are named —
**Gavrila's children Strelkov-Ozerov** from Nizhnie Mokrishchi of Korchakov stan.
The Karachev Ivan and Dmitry had a father Ivan; the Oryol group of brothers had
Gavrila. These are two different named patronymic groups. The coincidence of the surname
on both sides of the border remains evidence of a common service milieu, but not
proof of a resettlement of Anpilogovs.

A control find shows why exact numbering matters: **the Grevtsovs**
are assigned in the alphabet to «книге 1 отказной, № 295» ("conveyance book 1, No. 295"), that is, to the later
file **9185**, not to cadastral book 9181. The Bazdyrevs, Krivolapovs and Trunovs
have not been found in the alphabet of file 9181. No separate public profile or
point on the family map is created for the Ozerovs: this is an unrelated control surname, and
a personal link with the researched group has not been proven.

The modern inventory of the fond has been checked separately to rule out an error in the general
numbering. On **p. 52, line 109** it states directly: No. 9181, dates 1626–1627,
scribes V. P. Suvorov-Naumov and V. Repyev, volume 1–579 leaves and the note
**«МИКР.»** (microfilmed). Thus the summary caption of 1686–1693 in the search
results of the historical inventories is not the dating of file 9181. The main
practical consequence of the note: the book has already been microfilmed. First the archive
must find the leaves of internal entry No. 181, after which a
copy of it can be ordered from the microfilm rather than new photography of the whole volume.

The authorised FamilySearch catalogue has been checked for the Russian and Latin
spellings of Karachev and for the title of the book: no catalogue record or DGS number
was found. An exact web search also yielded no open image or
transcription of entry No. 181. This does not rule out a private copy, but at present
the confirmed
access channel is the РГАДА microfilm.

[Official gallery of alphabet 120](http://rgada.info/dela/1209/index2.php?str=120),
[the alphabet leaf with the Ozerovs](http://rgada.info/dela/1209/1/1209-opis_120i/0041.jpg),
[paired inventory 47](http://rgada.info/dela/1209/index2.php?str=47),
[entry No. 181 in the inventory](http://rgada.info/dela/1209/1/1209-opis_47i/0028.jpg),
[modern inventory of f. 1209, op. 1, pt. 2](http://rgada.info/opisi/1209-opis_1-2.pdf).

[K. V. Baranov's modern catalogue](http://rgada.info/materiali/baranov_catalog.pdf),
[digital copy of ОДБ МАМЮ, vol. 5](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:%D0%9E%D0%BF%D0%B8%D1%81%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%BA%D1%83%D0%BC%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_%D0%B8_%D0%B1%D1%83%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%B3,_%D1%85%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%8F%D1%89%D0%B8%D1%85%D1%81%D1%8F_%D0%B2_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%BC_%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%85%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B5_%D0%9C%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B8%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%80%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B2%D0%B0_%D1%8E%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%86%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%9A%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B3%D0%B0_05_1888-1-.pdf).

### The Kursk branch of 1628: not the source of Oryol but a separate early generation

The open database correctly led to the file **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 270**, but
the town attribution cannot be taken from the name of a random folder. The Oryol
selection on Yandex Disk contains only fols. 141–151; the Kursk entry of Vasily
Semyonovich is on **fol. 185** and is not in this folder. All 21 frames
of the Oryol segment have been checked, so none of them is used as
photographic evidence for Vasily's line.

On the other hand, a photocopy of p. 48 of A. N. Zertsalov's 1895 edition has now been saved to the project.
It literally prints: **«марта въ 15 день … Васка Семеновъ
сынъ Онпилоговъ»** ("on the 15th day of March … Vaska Semyonov syn Onpilogov"). The full modern publication of manuscript fol. 185 adds:
the father died, there are no brothers, and Vasily holds 40 chetverti of his father's pomestye. This
proves Kursk landholding by the previous generation before March 1628,
but not a transfer from Oryol. Moreover, the documented direction of mass movement at the end of
the 16th century was the opposite: Kursk was settled with transferees from
Oryol and Mtsensk. So the Kursk cluster must be investigated as a possible
offshoot of Oryol or as an independent line, not designated as the
source of the Oryol landholders of 1594/95.

The working fork is now more precise: Vasily's deceased father is a third Semyon
of the family circle, distinct from Semyon Osipovich and Semyon
Yakovlevich, who were alive in 1630. The Semyon Anpilogov of the 1619 customs entry fits in time,
but his fatherhood is not proven. Fyodor Anpilogov, indexed in Kursk within
the broad range 1605–1618, remains an even earlier candidate until
the exact call number of his file and the manuscript leaf are found.

### How neighbouring Yelets was settled: three channels instead of one resettlement

For Oryol no single list of the first garrison of 1566 has yet been found. But
an almost laboratory-detailed complex survives on the settlement of the next southern
fortress — Yelets in 1592–1594. This is not a source for the origin of
Anfilogovs but a documentary model of how the state actually
assembled a new service town.

A report (otpiska) of the Yelets voivodes of 19 August 1592 says that orders had been given
to settle 200 deti boyarskie. They recruited **non-serving sons, brothers
and nephews** of serving fathers, brothers and uncles. From various towns
164 men were enrolled; the assessors (okladchiki) chosen from the towns divided them into three grades:
200 chetverti and 6 roubles, 150 chetverti and 5 roubles, 100 chetverti and 4 roubles.
The enrolment books and surety records were sent to Moscow. A charter of 31 August
ordered land to be measured out for the new settlers along the Sosna and beyond the Sosna, to let them plough
and build homesteads, and not to send them on watch or town-building duty until they were settled.

The second channel was Cossack recruitment. A memorandum of 5 September 1592 names
four men from the Chern Cossack settlement (sloboda) who enrolled as Yelets Cossacks,
and a similar intake from Krapivna. They received money, grain, arquebuses (pishchali), powder and
lead; when their former commanders held back the recruits, their wives and property,
Moscow ordered the men to be sent to Yelets with their families and belongings.

The third channel passes directly through Oryol. The Yelets strelets **Grigory Ivanov
syn Andreev** had earlier lived with his father under the Oryol syn boyarsky Alexey
Pantyukhin. Having enrolled as a strelets, he built the Yelets town and stockade (ostrog) and
set up a homestead there. The Pantyukhins held back his wife and property; a charter of
12 September 1593 ordered the Oryol voivode Lavr Kandaurov to return everything.
This is the first route found here that is not supposed but literally recorded:
**Oryol → Yelets**, although it concerns an unrelated man, not the family.

An earlier dispute of September 1592 shows that the move was already regulated
by family composition. The full edition allows the name to be refined: the Yelets strelets
**Trenka Zakharov syn Logachev** enrolled in the new town from his brother — the Oryol
Cossack **Osip**. Different leaves have the forms Lagachev and Golovachev, but
the editorial notes consider the reading «Логачев» (Logachev) correct; expanding
Trenka into Terenty without a direct source is not permissible. The Oryol Cossack head Kazarin
Lodyzhensky put Trenka in prison, took his property and forced him to promise to
live in Oryol. Moscow demanded his release, but the Oryol side cited
a rule of the Streletsky Prikaz: of three brothers one was to be released, of four —
two, and of two none. This is direct evidence of family
Cossack homesteads in Oryol by 1592 and of the state's concern that
a group of brothers should not vanish from the old garrison entirely. The document does not name the earlier town
of the Logachevs.

This is not the only link. The newly recruited Yelets strelets **Gerasim Yakovlev
syn Bykov** had earlier served «на одной службе» ("in one service") with the Oryol Cossack Fyodor
Vavilov syn Savenkov. After Bykov enrolled in Yelets, Savenkov kept
his property and his share of jointly sown grain. A repeat petition on
fol. 229 specifies that for two years they «жили на половине» ("lived on halves"). The place of their former joint
service is not named, so this is a personal Oryol–Yelets link, but not necessarily
proof that Bykov formerly lived in Oryol. The earlier reading «Исаков» (Isakov) has been
corrected on the basis of the full publication of fol. 95 and the confirming leaf 229.

The most detailed report shows movement in both directions. In Yelets
280 resident Cossacks were recruited; many fled to Dankov, Epifan, Chern, Novosil,
Krapivna, Mtsensk, Tula and **Oryol** and there enrolled again as Cossacks and streltsy.
At the same time the Livny voivode was ordered to select **60 of the best, well-off and
family-burdened Cossacks** and transfer them to Yelets. They came without their families, and the Yelets
head drew up a list by name of the parents, brothers,
sons, nephews and sons-in-law left behind in Livny. Aslam Golenishchev's brother Mikhail, Andros
Lapygin's stepfather, and the brother Ivan of yet another transferred Cossack had already enrolled in
Yelets as deti boyarskie. One kinship network could be in
two towns and two service ranks at the same time — this is especially important for understanding
the future odnodvortsy surnames.

Here the earlier conclusion had to be corrected. Khotyaintsov does indeed write that he
drew up a list of those **«которые с Ельца збежали и где хто живет»** ("who fled from Yelets and where each lives"), and sent
it together with the charter. The full edition of 2001 shows that the appendix is not
lost: on fols. 127–128, immediately after the families of the Livny transferees, comes the heading
**«Имена новоприборным козаком, которыя со государевой службы збежали»** ("Names of the newly recruited Cossacks who fled from the sovereign's service") and
a list by name. It preserves groups in Chern, Novosil, Krapivna,
Epifan, Tula district and Dankov, as well as people who vanished without trace or were held back
by private owners.

But the list still did not yield the hoped-for name from Oryol. The general part of fol. 125 says
outright that some fugitives «писалися в казаки и в стрельцы ... на Орле» ("enrolled as Cossacks and streltsy ... at Oryol"),
yet no surviving line of the list ties a specific person
to Oryol. So the list exists, but its Oryol part either perished in
the damaged places of the unglued scroll or was not entered in the surviving
list. There is no longer any need to order fols. 125–127 as a supposedly unpublished appendix;
what should be sought is a duplicate (protiven), a copy or a separate Oryol report on
the reception of these people.

Neighbouring charters of the same file show how productive private
disputes over families and property are. They literally reconstruct four channels
of recruitment:

- **Yushko Danilov syn Malyavin** came from a Mtsensk service family. After
  his father's death the family moved to Novosil district, and when Yelets was set up Yushko
  enrolled as a syn boyarsky, built the town and set up a homestead: Mtsensk →
  Novosil district → Yelets;
- **Panka Dmitriev syn Putyatin** left the household of Vasily Antonov in
  Zaostrovsky stan of Tula district and enrolled as a Yelets Cossack;
- **Afonka Smykovsky** had earlier lived on the pomestye of the Bolkhov syn boyarsky
  Stepan Bulgakov, and then enrolled among the Yelets deti boyarskie;
- dependants of the Kashira syn boyarsky Epishka Reptyukhov left and enrolled
  at Yelets as Cossacks, and **Ivashka Vasilyev syn Dolzhnikov** as a strelets.

This is no longer a general model but four named trajectories from the Mtsensk, Novosil,
Tula, Bolkhov and Kashira surroundings. They confirm that the new
garrison was assembled individually and from different social strata. That is exactly why
a similar trace of an Oryol Anfilogov is more likely to be found in a petition about
a family, homestead and property left behind than in a single list of "settlers".

The public Yandex Disk link given in a later discussion of this
report was checked on 29 August 2026 via the open API: it now leads
to a database on 18th-century Sloboda Ukraine, not to images of the file. However,
another open source has been found — the full edition **«Российская крепость на южных
рубежах. Документы о строительстве Ельца, заселении города и окрестностей в
1592–1594 годах»** [A Russian Fortress on the Southern Frontiers. Documents on the Building of Yelets and the Settlement of the Town and Its Environs in 1592–1594] (Yelets, 2001), prepared by V. N. Glazyev,
A. V. Novoseltsev and N. A. Tropin. It is hosted by the Scientific Library of Yelets State University (ЕГУ)
and reproduces the whole unglued scroll of 248 leaves, not a selection of individual
documents.

A full-text search has now been carried out not only across the **118 separate publications**
of G. N. Anpilogov's corpus and the separate Yelets book of 1593/94, but also across the full
text of the 248-leaf file. The variants Анфилог/Анпилог/Онфилог/Ампилог/
Амфилог/Онпилог and the Bazdyrevo neighbourhood circle were checked. There are no bearers of the surname group;
«Анпилогов» occurs only as the surname of the earlier publisher, G. N. Anpilogov.
In Osip Kaverin's strelets hundred, on fol. 19v, a Maksim Nemtsov is found, but
he cannot be turned into a relative or fellow countryman of Gavrila Fyodorov syn Nemtsov of
Bazdyrevo: only the surname matches.

There is an important asymmetry in what has survived. The official description of РГАДА fond 141
specifically names complexes on the building and settlement of Voronezh and
Yelets in 1585–1593, but names no similar complex on the founding of
Oryol in 1566. So the next search should be divided into three groups:

1. the original list of Oryol deti boyarskie and siege homesteads;
2. surety records and separate review (razbor) materials from the enrolment;
3. lists by name of the Oryol Cossacks and of the deti boyarskie with small estates,
   which the boyar verdict of 1577 ordered to be drawn up and delivered to
   the Razryad office (izba); the verdict itself has survived, but the resulting lists have not yet been
   found.

Primary publications: [report on the 164 deti boyarskie](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/25.htm),
[charter on their land and settlement](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/24.htm),
[memorandum on the Cossacks of Chern and Krapivna](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/22.htm),
[charter on the Oryol man Grigory Andreev](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/30.htm),
[petition of Gerasim Bykov](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/33.htm),
[list of Livny transferees and fugitive Cossacks](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/41-60/41.htm),
[Yushka Malyavin's Mtsensk — Novosil — Yelets chain](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/41-60/42.htm),
[inquiry on the Tula Cossack Panka Putyatin](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/41-60/44.htm),
[petition on the recruitment of dependants from Kashira district](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/41-60/50.htm),
[the Bolkhov–Yelets case of Afonka Smykovsky](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/41-60/54.htm),
[the separate Yelets book of 1593/94](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Otvod_zalov_elec_1593/text.htm),
[ЕГУ catalogue and full edition](https://lib.elsu.ru/kraevedenie.html),
[description of the archaeographic completeness of the publication](https://www.reenactor.ru/ARH/PDF/Timoshina_00.pdf).
Archival address of the main charters: РГАДА, f. 141, op. 1, 1593, d. 1,
fols. 13–15, 36–37, 55–55v, 61–64, 90–95, 125–128, 179, 229;
of the separate book — f. 1209, bk. 26,
fols. 2–24.

### Four lines in the original Oryol list of 1627/28

The full publication of the document **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 14, d. 22** makes it possible
to see not only the four family lines but also their place within the Oryol
service corporation. The Oryol section begins on fol. 118; Anfilogovs
are distributed across different service grades and pomestye oklads:

- **Vaska Dmitreev syn Onpilogov**, fol. 123 — gorodovoy (town-list) syn boyarsky, 200 chetverti;
- **Ivashko Vasilyev syn Onfilogov**, fol. 125 — gorodovoy, 150 chetverti;
- **Oleshka Vasilyev syn Onfilogov**, fol. 128 — novik of 7130/1621–1622,
  200 chetverti;
- **Marka Karpov syn Онфилохов (Onfilokhov)**, fol. 133 — novik of the same year 7130,
  100 chetverti.

**Names and meaning.** Vaska, Ivashko, Oleshka and Marka are the usual chancery forms
of the names Vasily, Ivan, Alexey and Mark, not designations of children. The formulas
«Дмитреев сын», «Васильев сын» and «Карпов сын» ("son of Dmitry", "son of Vasily", "son of Karp") preserve the fathers' names. The form
**Онфилохов** (Onfilokhov) is especially valuable: in it the scribe rendered the same family group through
the stem *Онфилох-*, close to the church name Amfilokhy. This is not a different
surname and not a different family: Mark Karpovich in the muster roll of 1626 is already
twice called Onfilogov and Anfilogov.

The service categories do not allow all four to be treated as one age
group. Vasily Dmitrievich and Ivan Vasilyevich already stand among the gorodovye,
whereas Alexey Vasilyevich and Mark Karpovich are retrospectively called noviki
of 1621/22. "Novik" here means a serviceman enrolled with his own oklad for the first time,
not a recent settler in Oryol. The pomestye oklad is also
not equal to the land actually ploughed: it is a calculated norm of provision for
service.

For Mikula's line, Mark Karpovich is important. In Bazdyrevo around 1625 the brothers
Martyn and Kuzma, "Karpovy deti Anfilogova" (Karp's children, Anfilogov), are named separately, and in the book of 1594/95
Karp Mikulin syn Anfilogov sat in the same place. The chronology, the rare patronymic,
the surname and the place add up to a strong model **Mikula → Karp → Mark,
Martyn and Kuzma**. But neither the list of 1627/28 nor the short register of 1625
says literally that the father of the three Karpovichi was precisely Karp Mikulich. So
the chain remains a probable reconstruction, not a published kinship
formula.

The source confirms the stability of several Oryol branches, but does not
name their earlier towns. No new route has been added to the map. The full
text of the Oryol section is published in the scholarly almanac «Российская
генеалогия» [Russian Genealogy], issue 13, pp. 318–322; [open transcription of the section](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5703/135781/p4873195.htm#pp4873195),
[name index of the edition](https://oldbasman.ru/f/almanax-13_boyare_spiski_imena.pdf).

A later comparative check confirms the stability of precisely the family's Oryol
registration. The list of 1629 (РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12 pt. 1, d. 27)
brings together deti boyarskie of various towns assigned to service in Mtsensk,
Novosil and Chern. A modern name index distributes 2,080 entries
between Oryol, Mtsensk, Novosil, Karachev, Bolkhov, Chern and
an unidentified group. All four family lines — **Vaska, Ivashka,
Mark and Oleshka Anpilogov** — stand in the Oryol group. So even when
Anpilogovs were sent to serve in other fortresses, the records continued to count them as
Oryol servicemen. This does not settle the question of the generation before 1566,
but it weakens the assumption that the known family group was a late
branch of one of the neighbouring towns.

The archival inventory warns that the file is «без начала» ("without its beginning"), and the published
index does not give the leaves of the four lines. So for now they are gathered in the lead
`LEAD-210-12-27-OREL-1629`; separate Records and final profiles should
be made after reading the photocopies of the file that researchers have already obtained but not yet published.
Source of the index: [the list of 1629](https://dompredkov.ru/dvoryane-i-deti-boyarskie-raznyh-gorodov-1629g/).

### Two books of 1628: four elders and the new Afonka

First, a separate subsection was found in the РГАДА inventory: **«Книга 7136 года о
верстании новиков денежным жалованием по городу Орлу»** [Book of the year 7136 on the enrolment of noviki with cash salary for the town of Oryol] — f. 214, op. 1,
pt. 1, d. 16, fols. 138v–140. Its full text was published by A. N. Zertsalov in
1895. The digital copy in the
[Presidential Library](https://www.prlib.ru/item/1291783) has been read in full;
the Oryol section occupies pp. 69–70.

Here it is especially important not to confuse two meanings. A **novik** is not necessarily
a new resident or settler. He is a young serviceman who was enrolled
for the first time with his own cash oklad. **Okladchiki** (assessors) are experienced local
servicemen who took part in assessing the noviki.

In Oryol four different okladchiki are named:

- Gavrilo Olferyev syn Andreyanov;
- Kuzma Ivanov syn Penshin;
- Andrey Grigoryev syn Kharechkov;
- Parfen Mikhaylov syn Gostev.

Kharechkov is repeated in the printed text. There follow 56 line mentions of
noviki, but two people are repeated — Vaska Sergeev syn Voloboev and Mikitka
Semenov syn Davydov. So the minimum composition is **54 different noviki**.
Among them the variants Anpilogov/Anfilogov/Onpilogov/
Onfilogov/Ampilogov/Amfilogov have been fully checked: **the surname is absent**.

The family itself is indisputably older than this campaign. The primary leaves of the muster roll
of 1626 already name four different Oryol servicemen:

- Vasily Dmitrievich Onpilogov;
- Ivan Vasilyevich Anfilogov;
- Alexey Vasilyevich Onfilogov;
- Mark Karpovich Anfilogov.

But then a fundamentally important difference emerged: there was **a second
book, not identical with Zertsalov's edition**. This is the review (razbornaya) book of the servicemen
of seven towns of 1628/29 — РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6d, d. 1. A modern
open [index by "Dom predkov"](https://dompredkov.ru/noviki-orla-1628-goda/)
publishes 123 Oryol lines and names as the first family line
**«Анпилогов Афонька»** (Anpilogov Afonka). The old handwritten alphabet of the Razryad Archive
independently gives for the same file **«Онфилоговъ, л. 109»** (Onfilogov, fol. 109).

A photocopy of the primary leaf has been obtained and located. A repeat extended
viewing corrected the earlier incomplete transcription: the lower entry of fol. 109 reads
**«Офонко Осипов сын Онфилогов»** (Ofonko Osipov syn Onfilogov). The same full name is given independently by an open
line-by-line extract of the Oryol section. The heading places him among noviki not previously
enrolled; in 7137 Ofonka was enrolled in **the third grade**, at
**100 chetverti and 5 roubles**. A fold and a later line prevent only the reading of
the individual formula about the pomestye actually held by him or his father.

A probable later trace of Afonka has already been found. An academic publication
of a conveyance record of 1640 literally names **the Oryol man Afonka Anpilogov**
and reproduces the form in which he «означил себя» ("signed himself"): «Аеонька Фнеилогов». The rare
diminutive name, the surname and the town match; twelve years separate the entries.
The full formula now closes the biographical chain. **Ofonko Osipov syn
Onfilogov** of 1628/29 and **Ofonasey Osipov syn Anfilogov** of 1641/42 are
one person: the name, patronymic, rare surname and Oryol coincide. The Oryol man Afonka
Anpilogov who wrote the conveyance book in 1640 is almost certainly
an intermediate mention of the same biography. The former profiles P0077 and P0081
have been merged into P0077.

For the early Oryol core the patronymic matters most. Ofonko was **Osip's son**.
The most likely father is Osip Vasilyevich Anfilogov, landholder of Bazdyrevo
in 1594/95: the town, the rare surname, the name and a suitable generation match.
However, the entry does not say "son of Osip Vasilyevich of Bazdyrevo", so this is
a strong working link, not a definitively proven kinship.

Zertsalov adds the history of the first document. It is a paper of the former Razryad
Prikaz, wrongly assigned in 1835 to the Siberian Prikaz. The Oryol
noviki were enrolled by the group of the boyar Prince Dmitry Mikhaylovich Pozharsky and the dyak
Andrey Podlesov. A note to the publication indicates the leaves used,
85v, 138v, 139 and 140; the modern inventory puts the beginning of the Oryol
subsection on fol. 138.

The primary Record `RGADA-210-6D-1-L109`, the document R0062 and the single
profile P0077 have been updated; an extended fragment of the full name line has been kept. Only the event of the first enrolment
in the already known Oryol is added to the map. No route
is built: the document does not name an earlier town, and the individual pomestye
formula has not yet been fully read. The full name-by-name transcription of the 54 people from
the separate book remains in the card `PUB-ZERTSALOV-1895-OREL-NOVIKI-1628`.

A complete check of the same edition produced an important refinement for the neighbouring Kursk
branch. On p. 48, still within the Kursk section and before the heading «Ряжской» (Ryazhsk),
it is printed in full: **«марта в 15 день … Васка Семенов сын Онпилогов»** ("on the 15th day of March … Vaska Semyonov syn Onpilogov").
A note assigns the Kursk material to fols. 78v–82 of the same archival book.
This is the primary basis for Savyolov's short phrase about Vasily Semyonovich, enrolled
under Kursk in 1628, and the natural predecessor of the Kursk line of 1630
with an oklad of 100 chetverti. The profile already existed, so not a duplicate but
a new Record `PUB-ZERTSALOV-1895-P48-KURSK-VASILIY-SEMYONOV` was created and the source
attached to Vasily. The link with the Vaska Anpilogov without a patronymic of 1642,
who had earlier lived on the pomestye of his uncle Gavrila, remains probable but unproven.
This line adds no direct route to the origin of the Oryol core:
"novik" means the start of independent service, and the earlier town of the father, Semyon,
is not named.

Finally, the Anfilogov family nest lay in one of the youngest
areas. By Smirnov's calculations, the Nepolotsk and Kamensk stans, shielded by the fortress, were occupied more densely first,
then the outer Korchakov, Taichukov and
Nougorsk stans. In Korchakov stan about **90.8%** of the recorded arable area
was still wild field. This agrees well with the picture of Bazdyrevo and
Belaya, but does not answer where the fathers of Anfilogovs came from.

### The main discovery: the northern path across the Oka and the Tula towns

K. B. Kudlanov and R. O. Golubkov, in a 2023 article, brought together the earlier observations of
D. K. Zelenin and V. M. Nedelin and reconstructed the directions of settlement of
Oryol district after the town was founded in 1566. The main flow came **from the
north, from the Oka basin and through the towns of the future Tula abatis line (zasechnaya cherta)**.

| direction | towns and groups named | strength of the conclusion |
|---|---|---|
| main, northern | Serpukhov, Kaluga, Belyov, Novosil, Krapivna | a published reconstruction of the general Oryol flow |
| north-eastern | Pronsk and the Ryazan land | a noticeable but smaller branch |
| neighbouring, Upper Oka | Bolkhov, Mtsensk, Karachev, the lands of the future Livny district | the local population and servicemen of the nearest old towns |
| western and southern | people from Putivl, Starodub, Lithuania; individual Don atamans | small admixtures, not the basis of the core |

Source: K. B. Kudlanov, R. O. Golubkov, «Сравнительный анализ траекторий
однодворческого заселения Путивльского и Орловского уездов» [A Comparative Analysis of the Trajectories of Odnodvortsy Settlement of Putivl and Oryol Districts], 2023, pp. 94–97,
[PDF of the article](https://vestnik-brgu.ru/wp-content/numbers/2023-02/v2023-02.pdf),
DOI 10.22281/2413-9912-2023-07-02-91-101. This is **a map of the origin of the whole
Oryol service stratum**, not a ready-made pedigree of Anfilogovs. Moreover,
the authors cite some of the specific towns from earlier local-history
reconstructions, not from a single surviving transfer list.

An earlier basis for this scheme has been found in the 1913 book by the ethnographer D. K. Zelenin,
§ 63, p. 158. He formulates the mechanism thus: in the Oryol population
former deti boyarskie and other servicemen predominated, **«подвинутые
вперёд, в степь, с прежних сторожевых линий»** ("moved forward, into the steppe, from the former watch lines"). From the cadastral book he sees in
old Oryol district people from Livny, a Bolkhov sotnik, Mtsensk
sotniki, Kalugins, Serpukhovitinovs, Ryazantsovs, Putivltsevs and
Starodubtsevs; separately he notes Polish, Tatar and Don elements.
Zelenin cautiously considered the Mtsensk sotniki temporary residents. This is an analysis of
the names and surnames of the book of 1594/1595, not a separate name register of
the transfer, but it independently confirms the composite origin of the core.
Digital copy: [НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000199_000009_003803593/).

The full text of V. M. Nedelin required the strength of this conclusion to be corrected. The author
does indeed describe the rapid settlement of the new town by "svedentsy" —
people whom the state transferred for permanent residence and service.
But the famous list of towns of origin is structured in a more complex way. A full-text
check of [the main chapter on the founding](https://www.rulit.me/books/orel-iznachalnyj-read-297827-15.html)
showed the exact sequence of the argument. First the author writes: **«Можно
предполагать»** ("It may be supposed") that in spring 1566 the gathering of servicemen and posokha (levy) men
and «переселение сведенцев» ("the resettlement of svedentsy") began. Then **Belyov, Mtsensk, Bolkhov and Novosil** are named
as suppliers of servicemen and workers for the rebuilding of Oryol in
**1635–1636**. And only after that comes the conclusion: **«По-видимому, эти же
города, а также соседний Карачев»** ("Apparently, these same towns, and also neighbouring Karachev") took part in the building of 1566.
There is no separate reference for this paragraph in the notes to chapter IV. So the later
[affirmative sentence on page 52](https://www.rulit.me/books/orel-iznachalnyj-read-297827-52.html)
is a condensed repetition of the author's analogy, not a pointer to yet another
list of svedentsy of the first Oryol.

The whole neighbouring block of notes 11–14 has been checked separately to rule out
a hidden archival basis. The call number **РГАДА, f. 181, bk. 99, pt. 1, fol. 435** in
note 12 refers only to the next statement — the appointment in April
1567 of the first Oryol voivodes, Vasily Rostovsky and Vladimir
Bezobrazov. Note 11 confirms the chronicle date of the founding, 13 —
the strength of the Oryol detachment in 1572, 14 — campaign information of the 1570s–1580.
None of them contains a list of svedentsy or names their earlier
towns. So f. 181, bk. 99 cannot be used as proof of a
Belyov/Mtsensk/Bolkhov/Novosil/Karachev → Oryol route.

Names and meaning are especially important here. Vladimir Mikhaylovich Nedelin is a historian
who formulated an influential version, not a witness of the resettlement. "Svedentsy"
is a real service category, but the word itself gives neither the surnames nor
the earlier towns of the people. The four towns are reliable for the events of 1635–1636;
Karachev and the transfer of all five to 1566 are a reasoned analogy.
So the card `PUB-NEDELIN-2001-OREL-FIRST-SETTLEMENT-HYPOTHESIS`
