The tactic for the branch changes. Ivan's father is now sought through the series **Persha — Petrusha
— Pyotr/Petr**, with the control forms **Porfiry — Porsha**. An ordinary Pyotr with
a son Ivan is still too weak a match: what is needed is the family element
Anfilogov, rare neighbours, the same landed surroundings or a direct record of a
transfer of service. The earlier negative result of searching only by the word
"Persha" is no longer considered exhaustive. Ivan's card and his father's profile
have been updated; no route appears on the map, because the Epifan Filatov,
Roslyakov and Levontyev have not been identified with the Oryol Persha.

### The File of 1629 Is Now Fully Open

In the dynamic database of "Dom predkov" four Oryol lines from
one file have been confirmed: **Anpilogov Vaska, Anpilogov Ivashka, Anpilogov Mark** and
**Anpilogovo Oleshka**. The last form, with a final *-o*, is for now kept as a
literal index reading and is not corrected automatically. This is a search
index: it gives no folios and does not replace reading the manuscript.

The main change is that an open photocopy of the whole file **РГАДА, f. 210,
op. 12 ch. 1, d. 27** has been found: 299 frames in two folders, fols. 1–107 and 108–147. So the
lead no longer has the status "scans expected". On a first look-through it was
established that the entries cannot be searched as a continuous Oryol block. On fol. 59
the section **«Городовые»** ("Town [servicemen]") begins, and within it the people are divided into groups by
pomestye oklad; on fol. 66 the heading **«по 150 чети»** ("at 150 cheti") can be read with confidence.
It is the oklad groups, not the first folios of the file, that set the right search
corridor.

For comparison there are already independent data of 1627/28: Vaska Dmitreev
received 200 chetverti, Ivashka Vasilyev 150, Oleshka Vasilyev 200, Marka
Karpov 100. They make it possible to search for the four lines in a targeted way, but do not yet prove
that the oklads had not changed by 1629 and that the short index names refer
to exactly the same people. Therefore new Records and scan fragments will be
published only after the surname has been read with confidence on a specific line.

The first specific folio has already been located, with a caveat. On **fol. 61**, in two
overlapping frames, the beginning of the line **«Олешко Васильев
сын …»** ("Oleshko Vasilyev syn …") reads with confidence. The rare chancery name, patronymic, Oryol section and group,
standing before the heading «по 150 чети», all match; so this is a strong candidate for
Aleksey Vasilyevich, printed in the index as «Анпилогово Олешка».
However, the surname falls in the darkened right-hand part of the folio. In the literal
transcription it is not yet filled in from the index. Both source frames and a
research fragment are kept in
`public/archive/evidence/rgada/f210-op12-d27/pages/`; the lead's status has been changed to
`scans-open-candidate-line-l61`. This is progress down to a specific folio, but
not yet grounds for a separate final Record.

Open folder: https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ. File card:
`RGADA-210-OP12-1-OPIS-D27`; working lead:
`LEAD-210-12-27-OREL-1629`. The map does not change yet: the list confirms
enrolment in Oryol but does not by itself name a former town or prove a
route of resettlement.

### 323 Oryol Men at Tsarev-Borisov: A New Candidate List of 1602

Switching the search from the surname to whole Oryol detachments produced another precise
archival address. Yu. V. Ankhimyuk's article on the rosters of Tsarev-Borisov states
that from "Osenin den" (the autumn feast day) of 1602 the fortress was garrisoned by **323 Oryol regimental deti
boyarskie**, of whom **200 with pishchals** (muskets). The reference leads to a manuscript:
**ОПИ ГИМ, f. 440, no. 359, fols. 76–77**.

These are not the first settlers of Tsarev-Borisov and not men transferred there permanently.
The word "полковые" ("regimental") and the context of rotating garrisons point to a distant
service detachment sent from Oryol. "Орловские" ("of Oryol") denotes the town of service,
not the place of birth. The published line names not a single person,
so Anfilogovs cannot automatically be counted among the 323, and no
route can be drawn on the family map.

The number matters as a control. Between the book of 1594/95 and the musters of the
1620s a considerable part of the Oryol corporation may at the same time have been
on year-long service far from its district. But for the origin of the core the find
is valuable above all for its form: fols. 76–77 may contain the total of a more detailed
roster by name, or a reference to one. The next targeted order is the two folios themselves
and their surroundings, not a new general search for the word "Anfilogov".

Source: Yu. V. Ankhimyuk, "On the History of Russian Colonisation of the Wild Field:
The Garrison of the Fortress of Tsarev-Borisov in the Late 16th and Early 17th Century", 2022,
pp. 342–343, [PDF](https://www.reenactor.ru/ARH/PDF/Anximyk.pdf). Card:
`PUB-OPI-GIM-440-359-OREL-TSAREV-BORISOV-1602`.

### April 1599: The Oryol List Handed to the Second Voivode

In the Sovereign's Razryad (Gosudarev razryad) of 1598–1604 there turned up not merely a reference to lists compiled at some point,
but news of the movement of a specific Oryol document.
In the campaign of 1599 Oryol formed the watch regiment. The first voivode was Prince
**Boris Petrovich Tatev**, the second **Bogdan Ivanovich Polev**. On printed
p. 73, corresponding to manuscript fol. 50, it is recorded:

> «А с Орла писал к государю воевода князь Борис Татев, что у нево воевода
> Богдан Полев списки взял».

> *Translation:* "And from Oryol the voivode Prince Boris Tatev wrote to the sovereign that the voivode
> Bogdan Polev had taken the lists from him."

The meaning of the word **«списки»** ("lists") is revealed by the immediately preceding
Novosil episode: it speaks of "lists of dvoryane and deti boyarskie who
were ordered to be with him in Novosil". So the Oryol line refers not
to a land cadastre but to a service register of the men assigned to the regiment.
The precedence (mestnichestvo) dispute between the two commanders preserved a rare record-keeping
detail: the list was originally held by Tatev and was then physically
handed over to Polev. It may have been a list of the men assigned specifically to the second voivode,
and not necessarily a full register of all the servicemen of Oryol.

The event is dated to a narrow interval **between 1 and 23 April 1599**:
the first date opens the campaign roster, the second follows the episode under discussion.
This is only four years after the cadastral book of 1594/95 and three years
after the desyatnya of noviki of 1596. So the list may have included adult
Oryol Anfilogovs of the already known generation. But the published Gosudarev
razryad does not name the rank and file, so their presence cannot yet be asserted.

The search tactic is now targeted: what is needed is the protograph of Boris Tatev's report from Oryol,
its annexes, a duplicate of the list or Bogdan Polev's receipt record. We search by
the combination of both voivodes' names, the formula «с Орла» ("from Oryol") and the words «списки взял» ("took the lists") in the
stolbtsy of the Razryadny prikaz and in precedence cases. The main published
copy is a late copy, **РГАДА, f. 181, d. 100**; a variant is **f. 199,
d. 130, ch. 2**; another copy is in the ОР РНБ, Hermitage collection,
d. 390b. These copies may not contain the annexes of the original correspondence.
A separate redaction of the razryadnaya kniga of 1550–1636 confirms the full form of the
name **Bogdan Ivanovich Polev**.

Publication: *Razryadnye knigi 1598–1638 gg.*, Moscow, 1974, p. 73;
[introduction to the edition](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Razr_kniga_1598_1604/pred.htm),
[key fragment](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Razr_kniga_1598_1604/text2.htm).
Card: `PUB-RK-1599-OREL-ROSTER-TRANSFER`. The map does not change: the family
name and a route are absent from the line.

#### The Original File of the Neighbouring Mikhaylov Pair Has Survived

The official inventory of the original precedence cases of the Razryadny prikaz gave the first
precise address of a document from the same knot of conflict: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 9,
d. 3, stolpik 2, 142 folios**. The inventory describes it thus: "7108/1599–1600.
Precedence case of Artemy Vasilyevich Izmaylov with Ivan Osipovich Polev
(without the end)".

These are not the Oryol Boris Tatev and Bogdan Polev. Ivan Osipovich Polev was voivode
in Mikhaylov, and Artemy Izmaylov a golova under him. But it is precisely their dispute that is recorded
in the Gosudarev razryad right after the Oryol transfer of the list: Izmaylov was ordered to
serve according to the roster and to take the list of deti boyarskie.

The hypothesis that the dispute continued has now been confirmed. The full text of the case was
printed back in 1838: **"Russky istorichesky sbornik", vol. 2, pp. 157–213**.
The case opens on 25 March 1600, and in his petition Artemy writes directly that
he had been ordered to be with Ivan Polev in Mikhaylov during the past summer
service and that a hearing had been promised after it ended. This is the very conflict of 1599.

The publication also gives the exact meaning of the word "списки". On p. 193 a charter
of 1560 is quoted: the sovereign sent the voivodes **lists of deti boyarskie by name**; the men
were to be with them according to these lists, and the absent were to be sought out
and sent. On p. 207 the former commanders are ordered to hand over to the new voivode the
lists of the Ryazan deti boyarskie; the individual names of men remaining with
another commander were promised to be sent separately. On p. 175 Vasily Izmaylov
«не был и списков не взял, и на съезд не ездил» ("did not come, did not take the lists and did not go to the assembly") because of a precedence dispute; on
p. 190 Prince Ivan Boryatinsky, by contrast, «списки отдал» ("handed over the lists"), refusing to serve
below Bogdan Polev.

Names and meaning: the Izmaylovs and Polev here are not candidates for ancestors of Ampilogovs.
They document the material chain itself: one commander kept the composition by name,
another received it, men were assembled according to the list, and on refusal the list was
returned. So the Oryol phrase **«Богдан Полев списки взял»** ("Bogdan Polev took the lists") means the
existence of a real name document — perhaps a separate selection of the
men of the second voivode, and not necessarily of the whole Oryol corporation.

Official PDF of the inventory: [f. 210, op. 9, p. 3](http://rgada.info/opisi/210-opis_9.pdf#page=3).
[Open photocopy of the 1838 edition](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:%D0%A0%D1%83%D1%81%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B9_%D0%B8%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%87%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B9_%D1%81%D0%B1%D0%BE%D1%80%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%BA_%D0%A2%D0%BE%D0%BC_2_%D0%9A%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B6%D0%BA%D0%B0_1-4_1838.pdf).
Six key pages are kept in the project. There are no images of the РГАДА original itself
yet; an archive order is needed to check the print against the manuscript and to look for
possible omissions by the editor. Working lead:
`LEAD-210-9-3-STOLPIK2-MIKHAILOV-1599`. The map and profiles do not change.

#### Ten Names for Checking the List of 1599

So that a future look-through does not turn into yet another search for one surname, a
control list of names has been compiled. The book of 1594/95 documents nine
adult holders: **Belyay Vasilyevich, Vasily, Timofey and Karp Mikulovichi,
Osip Vasilyevich, Ivan Pershin, Sofon Timofeyevich, Ivan Nechaevich and Ermak
Pakhomovich Anfilogov/Anpilogov**. In 1596 the Oryol
novik **Pavel Terentyev syn Anfilogov** is added to them.

All ten could chronologically have been servicemen of Oryol in 1599, but this
does not prove their presence in the very list handed to Bogdan Polev. A man
could have died, fallen ill, been on leave, received another assignment, or
been registered under the first voivode Tatev. Therefore the list is used only
as a reading control: a match is accepted after the literal line,
folio, patronymic, form of the surname and service heading have been recorded.

### 18 June 1600: The Oryol Detachment Marched to Kursk According to the Lists

The next razryad roster gave a stronger continuation of the April find.
On the Kursk news of Tatar movement near Belgorod and a possible attack on
Kursk, the sovereign ordered the watch regiment of Prince **Boris
Petrovich Tatev** to be sent from Oryol. The full text on printed pp. 103–104 says:

> «…велел государь им итти с Орла со всеми детми боярскими и с казаки,
> которые с ними по списком, в новой город, в Куреск…»

> *Translation:* "…the sovereign ordered them to go from Oryol with all the deti boyarskie and the Cossacks
> who are with them according to the lists, to the new town, to Kursk…"

Here for the first time three elements stand side by side: the town of departure, the whole assigned
service complement, and the document by which this complement was determined. This is not merely
news that a voivode held papers: the list was put to practical use in
moving the Oryol regiment. However, the word **«всеми»** ("all") refers to all the deti
boyarskie and Cossacks attached to the voivodes by the campaign lists. It does not
guarantee the presence of every adult landholder of the district: a man who was ill, on leave,
absent or assigned to other service might not have joined the detachment.

Two scholarly redactions preserve an important discrepancy. The edition of the razryadnaya kniga
of 1475–1605 names Boris Tatev in the order but continues with the plural
"им" ("them"). The Gosudarev razryad of 1598–1604 directly names both voivodes sent from Oryol:
**Boris Tatev and Ivan Polev**. Ivan's patronymic is not given in this line;
he cannot yet be identified with Ivan Osipovich Polev of the Mikhaylov case.

For the family history this is a new candidate list. The nine adult
Anfilogovs/Anpilogovs of the book of 1594/95 and Pavel Terentyev syn Anfilogov
of 1596 could by age and status have been in it, but none is yet
named. Therefore no profiles, Records or family route Oryol — Kursk have been
created.

The search tactic is now narrower still: what is needed is the Kursk report of the voivode Andrey Zamytsky and
the golova Plakida Chicherin, the Moscow charter of 18 June 1600, the outgoing Oryol
report of Tatev and Polev, the Kursk receipt report and their annexes.
It is there that a duplicate of the detachment's roster by name may have survived. An additional key:
in the roster of 1600/01 **Bogdan Ivanov syn Polev**, after service at Livny, was
sent to Oryol — another possible moment when the town list was received. His name
is now revealed more precisely: an act of 1576/77 calls the voivode **«Максим прозвище
Богдан Иванов сын Полев»** ("Maksim, nicknamed Bogdan, Ivanov syn Polev"). Consequently, in inventories and extracts one must look not
only for Bogdan but also for Maksim Polev; this is one man, not two namesakes.

Source: *Razryadnaya kniga 1475–1605 gg.*, vol. IV, pt. 1, Moscow, 1994,
pp. 103–104 and 123, [full scan](https://archive.org/details/1475-1605..-4-1-1994);
parallel redaction — [Gosudarev razryad of 1598–1604](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Razr_kniga_1598_1604/text2.htm).
The double form of Polev's name is confirmed by [a study of the Polev genealogy based on acts](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1540-1560/Polev_I_V/text.php).
Three photocopies of the key pages are kept in the project. Card:
`PUB-RK-1600-OREL-KURSK-BY-ROSTERS`.

### A New Tactic: Searching Not for the Surname but for the Kursk Construction Books

S. I. Larionov, in his *Description of the Kursk Namestnichestvo* (1786), left
an unexpectedly precise pointer to a document capable of containing a list of the
first settlers. On printed **p. 15** he writes that by the tsar's decree
Kursk was built by the voivode **Ivan Osipovich Polev** and the golova **Nelyub Ogarev**,
«населяя его и уезд переведенцами из Мценска и Орла и добровольно приходящими
из других городов» ("populating it and the district with men transferred from Mtsensk and Oryol and with those coming voluntarily
from other towns"). Note `[32]` on the same page literally names the
source: **«Въ строенныхъ города Курска книгахъ»** ("In the construction books of the town of Kursk").

The photocopy has been checked against the РГБ copy **MK P-4° / 86-L** in the НЭБ: frame 25,
printed p. 15. The image is kept in the project. This corrects the pagination p. 13 found
in modern references: the paragraph and note in question are on
p. 15 of the edition itself.

Names and meaning: Polev and Ogarev are not candidates for ancestors of Ampilogovs, and no separate
profiles are created for them. Their full formulas are needed as search keys to the
chancery correspondence. The most important part of the record is the distinction between two channels:
organised **transferees from Mtsensk and Oryol** and volunteers **from other
towns**. So the early Kursk Anpilogovs may have come not from one
place and not necessarily from Oryol. Each branch must be established separately.

The limit of proof is strict. Larionov was writing almost two centuries after the
town was built. He named the type of source, but did not copy out the names or give a
modern archival call number. None of the ten known Oryol
Anfilogovs/Anpilogovs of 1594–1596 is named on the page; Fyodor Anpilogov,
noted by an electronic index in Kursk in the broad range 1605–1618,
likewise remains only a candidate for future checking. Therefore the map does not yet
receive a route Oryol or Mtsensk — Kursk.

The next search is now targeted:

1. the construction (stroennye, stroelnye), estimate (smetnye), roster (rospisnye) and early cadastral books of Kursk;
2. papers on the link Ivan Osipovich Polev — Nelyub Ogarev — the clerk Yakov
   Okatyev/Akatev — Mtsensk — Oryol — transferees;
3. manuscript copies of the Kursk landowner Ivan Petrovich Annenkov, whose
   materials Larionov used;
4. the exact call number of the separately retold tsar's charter of 1596 on
   the Bolkhov and Kromy landholders who failed to report for service (netchiki);
5. a by-name check of the ten Oryol candidates and Fyodor Anpilogov only by the
   literal line, patronymic, service heading and former town.

Source card:
`PUB-LARIONOV-1786-KURSK-OREL-MTSENSK-SETTLERS`. Working archival lead:
`LEAD-KURSK-1596-STROENNYE-KNIGI-OREL-MTSENSK`.

### A New Family Check: Osip — Semyon — Vasily

Broad queries **Анпи / Ампи / Анфи / Онпи / Онфи** to the database of servicemen
were re-collected at 200 rows per page. The result for the 16th century is negative:
the database gave no surname entries before the Oryol book of 1594/95. A line of 1583
refers to the personal name **Anfinogen** with a different surname and is not part of the public
surname core. The earliest non-Oryol index entry is still
**Fyodor Anpilogov, Kursk, 1605–1618**, but without the number of a specific file or folio.

The origin of this line has now been clarified by two independent open
presentations of the same project. On ВГД the compiler wrote directly that the 2,730
entries were collected from various documents of **РГАДА, f. 210**, and that 90
names were added for Kursk. The "Description of documents" tab of the Google spreadsheet decodes the conventional
code `сборн` only as a compilation of servicemen of the Time of Troubles, 1605–1618, and does not
contain a correspondence between an individual and a file. So there is no exact
address precisely in the public data set, and not only in our extract.

A check of the full published text of the *Kniga seunchey 1613–1619 gg.* (Book of Victory Dispatches) gave
zero matches for Анпил-/Анфил- and Kursk. Therefore Fyodor cannot yet be called
a seunshchik (bearer of victory news) or a recipient of a reward under this book. The next search is built on
the rare surnames of the whole Kursk block in other captive-ransom, pomestye and
grant files of f. 210, not on Fyodor's alphabetical neighbours in the interface.

On the other hand, the published list of Kursk of 1630 opened a more substantive
check. On fol. 121 stands **«Сенька Осипов сын Онпилогов»** (Senka Osipov syn Onpilogov). In Oryol district
in 1594/95 **Osip Vasilyev syn Anfilogov** is already documented. The interval of
35–36 years, the rare surname series and the exact patronymic make the model
"Oryol Osip → Kursk Semyon Osipovich" a strong hypothesis. But it is not yet a
genealogical link: no act names Semyon as the son of the Oryol
Osip specifically or reports his transfer to Kursk.

Next comes a fundamental fork. On the adjacent fol. 122 of the same list
**Сенька Яковлев сын Онпилогов** (Senka Yakovlev syn Onpilogov) is named. So Semyon Anpilogov of the Kursk
customs book of 1619 cannot automatically be considered an Osipovich. For the same
reason the father of **Vasily Semyonovich Onpilogov** was not established earlier.
The full publication of the main Kursk section of the desyatnya of 1628 now resolves
this fork: on fol. 185 the okladchiki (assessors) said directly that **Vasily's father had died**, and that
he had no brothers. Consequently, the living Semyon Osipovich and Semyon Yakovlevich of
the 1630 list could not have been his father. Vasily's father is a third Semyon
of the family circle, who died no later than March 1628. Semyon Anpilogov of the
customs book of 1619 remains the best candidate by time and position,
but his patronymic is not given in that entry, so kinship is not yet proven.

The same line on fol. 185 reports: **«поместья отцовского за ним 40 чети»** ("he holds 40 cheti of his father's pomestye"). So
the family held Kursk land before Vasily's own enrolment. This is no longer a
"sudden appearance" of one novik in 1628, but a trace of the previous generation,
which must be sought in conveyance, entry and inspection (dozornye) books for those specific forty
chetverti.

The otkaznaya kniga of 28 January 1642 adds a direct kinship formula:
**Vaska Onpilogov** formerly lived in the hamlet of Anpilogova on the pomestye of his uncle
**Gavrila Anpilogov**. The full printed pages 171–172 of the academic edition
have been downloaded and kept in the project; the target text is at the top of p. 172.
This Vaska may be the Vasily Semyonovich of 1628/1630, but there is no patronymic in the act of
1642, so the profiles have not been merged.

The shortest path to a solution now is to find the original grant or entry
record for the **40 chetverti of Vasily's father's pomestye**, and in parallel the patronymic
of Gavrila in the documents on the hamlet of Anpilogova. If the Vaska of 1642 turns out to be the
same Vasily Semyonovich, the word "uncle" will make Gavrila a brother of the deceased Semyon;
for now the lack of a patronymic and the difference between the father's 40 and Vaska's 30
chetverti do not allow the records to be merged. Photocopies are needed of РГАДА, f. 210,
op. 4, d. 270, fol. 185; f. 210, op. 12, d. 7, fols. 121, 122 and 141; and also
land files naming the stan and locality of the father's forty chetverti.

Names and meaning are especially important here. The formulas **«Осипов сын»**, **«Яковлев сын»**
and **«Семенов сын»** ("son of Osip", "son of Yakov", "son of Semyon") are direct patronymics, not our reconstructions. Conversely,
the same name Semyon or Vaska without a patronymic does not pick out a person among
bearers of the same name. Therefore the profiles and sources now explicitly show
both competing lines and the criterion that will be able to separate them.

No Oryol—Kursk route has been added to the map: points and people on both
sides are documented, but not the actual move of a specific person. Working lead:
`LEAD-OREL-KURSK-OSIP-SEMYON-VASILY-BRIDGE`.

### Petitions of the Children of the First Kursk Men: A New Way Around the Lost Book

The search for the construction book itself ran into two verifiable limits. The official
inventory of **ГАКО, f. 1555** has been viewed in full: among its 173 units there is the Kursk
razbornaya kniga of 1695 and late books of Grayvoron, Karpov, Korocha and Oboyan,
but no file on the building of Kursk in 1596. This closes one false path
without proving that the source perished. The history of the collection of Ivan Petrovich
Annenkov has also been clarified: the publication confirms the copies of old books he commissioned and
Larionov's work with them, but the phrase about a surviving manuscript refers to
Annenkov's diary. The fate of the copies of the construction books is still unknown.

On the other hand, a document has been found that shows how to get around the loss of the early list.
On **12 February 1628** Evsey Dmitreev syn Neruchev asked for his father's pomestye
to be confirmed to him. His father is described not by a general retelling but by a direct family
formula: **«Отец, государь, мой служил тебе государю ис Курска с курчаны
детьми боярскими как и город Куреск стал…»** ("My father, sovereign, served you, the sovereign, from Kursk with the Kursk
deti boyarskie ever since the town of Kursk came to be…"). Exact address:
**РГАДА, f. 1209, stolbtsy po Kursku no. 42, fols. 346–351 v.** The published
fragment of the manuscript is kept in the project and checked against the article's reading.

Names and meaning: the petitioner is **Evsey Dmitrievich**, so the name of his father Dmitry
is fixed both by the son's patronymic and by the text of the petition. Dmitry received a hundred
chetverti in Tuskarsky stan, in the village of Troitskoye on the Rat, and served in Kursk from
the moment the new fortress was founded. The Neruchevs do not belong to the researched surname,
so no profiles or map entries are created for them. The significance of the find is methodological:
an inheritance file after the fire of 1626 preserved two generations, the time
service began and the original pomestye of a man of the first Kursk intake.

Now the main search runs not only backwards, to the unfound book of 1596, but also
forwards — to the petitions of children, widows and nephews of 1627–1645. For our
fork the files on the pomestye estates of **Semyon Osipovich**, **Semyon
Yakovlevich**, **Vasily Semyonovich** and **Gavrila Anpilogov** are especially needed. A formula like
"father served since the town came to be", "transferred from Oryol", "granted from the former
Oryol pomestye" or a direct naming of brothers could connect the branch and
give the map a proven route. A surname match alone is still
not enough.

Control source card:
`PUB-RGADA-1209-ST42-L346-351-NERUCHEV-KURSK-FOUNDING-FORMULA`.

### Presumably 1632: Four Kursk Onpilogovs at Once

A full transcription of another list from the Moscow desk (Moskovsky stol) gave not
a single mention but a cross-section of the whole Kursk group. The archival address is **РГАДА,
f. 210, op. 9, d. 1096, stolpik 4, fols. 146–205**. The beginning of the document is lost,
so the year 1632 has not been read in the manuscript: A. Kurkulev dates the list by
comparison with the register of 1630, the departure of old servicemen and the transfer of the noviki
of 1627–1628 into the main complement. In the project the date is therefore recorded only
as **presumed**.

The Kursk section contains four exact lines:

- **fol. 157** — «Федька Григорьев сын Онпилогов» (Fedka Grigoryev syn Onpilogov), in the oklad heading of 200
  chetverti;
- **fol. 189** — «Сенька Осипов сын Онпилогов» (Senka Osipov syn Onpilogov), 100 chetverti;
- **fol. 190** — «Сенька Яковлев сын Онпилогов» (Senka Yakovlev syn Onpilogov), 100 chetverti;
- **fol. 204** — «Васька Семенов сын Онпилогов» (Vaska Semenov syn Onpilogov), among the noviki of the year 136 (1627/28),
  100 chetverti.

Names and meaning. The forms **Fedka, Senka, Vaska** are the usual chancery forms of
Fyodor, Semyon and Vasily, not an indication of childhood. The exact patronymics
distinguish the Semyons of the same name. Semyon Osipovich remains so far the strongest
candidate for a generational bridge to the Oryol **Osip Vasilyevich Anfilogov
of 1594/95**: the rare surname series, the name of the presumed father and a
plausible generation all match. But the document does not say "son of Osip Vasilyevich", does not
name Oryol and does not describe the inheritance of Oryol land.

A second targeted hypothesis has also appeared. Fyodor Grigoryevich is documented in
Kursk in 1630 and presumably 1632; from 1636 Sergey Grigoryevich Onpilogov
appears there. The same rare surname, patronymic and town
make them possible brothers, but so far no line calls them
brothers. This must be checked through a common pomestye, an inheritance petition
or a direct family formula.

The cross-section at the same time confirms an important negative distinction. Vasily
Semyonovich was a novik of the enrolment of 1627/28, and his father, according to the full line of
that enrolment, had already died. Consequently, the living Semyon Osipovich and Semyon
Yakovlevich of the list were not his father: Vasily's line goes back to a third
Semyon. For the same reason, Ivan Semyonovich, found in 1640, cannot
automatically be assigned one of these fathers.

All four profiles have been supplemented without duplicates; a Record has been created,
`PUB-KURKULEV-KURSK-1632-ONPILOGOVY`. The published image of the beginning of the Kursk
section is kept in the closed research store, but the target folios are not
on it. Therefore the literal lines for now have the status of a verified transcription
by the publisher, not of the project's own reading of the manuscript. Only Kursk remains on the map,
without a route from Oryol.
[Full transcription of the list](https://www.old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/ku260806.php).

### Where They Are Absent: The Intakes of Noviki of 1622, 1626 and 1627

After the four men were found, not individual search fragments but
all six published transcriptions of the Kursk enrolment desyatni of
1622–1628 were checked. The book **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 300** contains three complete
intakes: 132 noviki enrolled in 1622; 64 recruits of 1626; 45
service noviki enrolled in 1627 after five or six years of actual
service. Neither Fyodor Grigoryevich, nor Semyon Osipovich, nor Semyon Yakovlevich,
nor any other variant of the surname Anpilogov / Onpilogov is in these three lists.
In the desyatnya of 1628, **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 270**, there is only the already
known Vasily Semyonovich Anpilogov.

The negative result cannot be turned into an exact date of entry into
service. It means only that the three senior Onpilogovs did not belong to the
surviving intakes of 1622, 1626, 1627 and 1628. They may have served before
1622, or have been in another intake of 1623–1625 that has not survived or not yet been found.
Their position in the main section of the 1630 list, before the
separate headings for noviki of 1626–1630, is consistent with earlier service,
but does not by itself name a year.

This changes the immediate priority. For Fyodor and the two Semyons one must now look not at the
late Buturlin intakes but at documents of the **Time of Troubles and the first years after it**:
captured and freed Kursk men, pomestye and money additions, netchiki,
customs books and petitions for the restoration of old oklads. The open database
directly knows "Anpilogov Fyodor, Kursk, 1605–1618", but still hides the specific
act and its folio. This line may turn out to be an early record of Fyodor
Grigoryevich, but without a patronymic the identity cannot be established.

### 1640: The Son of Yet Another Semyon and the Middle of Sergey's Biography Found

A direct look through a new full transcription of the smetnaya kniga (estimate book) of Kursk gave three
exact lines of the researched surname. **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 180, fol. 28 v.**:
«Семен Яковлев сын Онпилогов пеш с пищалью» ("Semen Yakovlev syn Onpilogov, on foot, with a pishchal"); his heading preserves the oklad of
100 chetverti known from the 1630 list. **Fol. 43 v.**: «Сергей Григорьев
сын Онпилогов пеш с рогатиною» ("Sergey Grigoryev syn Onpilogov, on foot, with a boar-spear") — the same novik of the 1636 enrolment with an oklad of
150 chetverti. Between his Kursk appearance in 1636 on a gelding with a pishchal and his Oboyan
appearance in 1651 with the same equipment, Sergey in 1640 was temporarily
on foot and with a simpler weapon.

The main new name stands on **fol. 67**: **«Иван Семенов сын Онпилогов на
меринке с пищалью»** ("Ivan Semenov syn Onpilogov, on a small gelding, with a pishchal"). He is recorded among the noviki of the enrolment by the Kursk voivode
Ivan Vasilyevich Buturlin with an oklad of 100 chetverti. He is an independent
person of the next generation; profile `P9685` has been created. However, his father has not
yet been chosen. In Kursk at the same time there are known Semyon Osipovich, Semyon Yakovlevich and
a third Semyon, who died before March 1628 and left a son Vasily. The word
"novik" means the beginning of independent service, not a proven arrival from
Oryol.

The tactic becomes more precise still: one must look for the enrolment files of Ivan Buturlin
of 1638–1640 and the original grant of Ivan's **100 chetverti**. There
his father, a former pomestye or a former town may be named. Images of all three lines
of the publication have been kept; Record:
`PUB-KURKULEV-KURSK-1640-ONPILOGOVY`. Only the documented
service in Kursk is added to the map, without an Oryol — Kursk route.
[Full transcription of the smetnaya kniga of 1640](https://old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/sm260226.html).

### The Full Oryol Books of 1624–1626: The Online Shell Exists, the Folios Do Not

After the switch to inheritance formulas, the most important
Oryol address was re-checked: **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, kn. 330–331**. These are the books of Ivan
Kutuzov and the clerk Ivan Korovin, compiled using the old
Oryol book of 1594/95. Kn. 330 covers Korchakov and Nepolotsk
stany, that is, Bazdyrevo and the main family nest; kn. 331 covers Kamensky,
Taychukovsky and Nougorsk stany, including Ermak
Pakhomovich's Maltsovskaya on the Nugr.

The official РГАДА viewer opens the cards of both units at the addresses
`1209_1_330` and `1209_1_331`, but the galleries are empty: they contain neither thumbnails nor
folio files. So the full text cannot be obtained by trying image addresses.
What is needed is an order for copies or a sequential reading of the books themselves; the short
historical register has already been used and does not preserve the grounds of tenure.

When ordering, the first priority is all the Bazdyrevo entries with the formulas «по старым
писцовым книгам» ("according to the old cadastral books"), «по окладному списку» ("according to the oklad list"), «по даче» ("by grant"), «по отказу» ("by conveyance") and «прежде
было за» ("formerly held by"). Especially important are the passing of the pomestye of Onokha Deev syn Krivolapov to
Osip Vasilyevich Anfilogov and the inheritance of the zhrebiy shares of Karp Mikulin syn by
his sons. The second priority is Maltsovskaya on the Nugr. Only a literal
reference to a former town, former service or an old grant will allow a
route to be drawn on the map.

The result of the access check has been entered in the lead
`LEAD-RGADA-1209-1-330-331-OREL-1625-EARLY-EXTRACTS`. No new people or map
points have been created: the absence of open scans is a navigational result, not an
event of the family history.

#### A Second Copy Found, but It Breaks Off Before the Stany Needed

An additional check of the descriptions and of a public volunteer catalogue revealed
another archival address: **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 10047, fols. 274–685 v.**
This is an 18th-century archival copy without countersignatures, containing a large fragment of the same
cadastral book of Ivan Kutuzov and Ivan Korovin of 1624–1626.

The official РГАДА catalogue clarifies an essential detail: **file 10047 is a
composite volume**, which also contains independent boundary (mezhevye) books
of the 1680s–1690s. Only article 235, and
only fols. 274–685 v., belongs to the early Oryol book. So the order must specify the range, otherwise
by file number alone one may receive a late and irrelevant fragment.

The contents of the fragment have now been established precisely:

- Kamensky stan: pomestye estates, votchinas and vacant lands, fols. 274a–504;
- Taychukovsky stan: pomestye estates, votchinas and an incomplete section on vacant lands,
  fols. 505–685 v.

For the main family task the limit is unpleasant but significant. The copy does not
contain **Korchakov stan**, where Bazdyrevo and Belaya lay, and
breaks off before **Nougorsk stan**, where the register shows Ermak's
Anpilogov pomestye in Maltsovskaya. So it does not replace kn. 330 and the end of
kn. 331 and does not contain the established entries of the main early branches.

File 10047 is nevertheless useful as an independent copy of Kamensky and
Taychukovsky stany — above all for the surroundings of the documented
Kashira settler Filipp Teplov and for comparing the formulas of old grants.
The open Yandex.Disk of the project "Skazochki, skazki i skazishchi Orlovshchiny" has been checked
against its actual catalogue: d. 329 and several later
Oryol books are already published there, but **d. 330, 331 and 10047 are absent**. Consequently,
a precise backup call number has appeared, but there is as yet no public scan of the main entries.

[Description of the contents of the Oryol books](https://nashi-predki.ru/wiki/Документы_Поместного_приказа._г.Орел_и_уезд),
[official РГАДА catalogue, p. 149, article 235](http://rgada.info/sprav/sprav/zenchenko_ivanova_2013.pdf),
[volunteer list of files](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1851/142603/p5034895.htm),
[the project's open archive](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ).

### The Full Excel Copy of the Index: Fyodor's Line Revealed to the Limit

The public Excel copy of the "Dom predkov" database has been downloaded and checked in full, including
the structure of the workbook, the "Description of documents" tab, additional columns,
notes and links. The updated workbook has **421,677 rows including the header**; the earlier
conclusion that the table does not contain the form "Onpilogov" is no longer valid —
all 19 rows are now present.

For the early candidate only four cells of row 7452 are filled in:
**«сборн»; «1605–1618»; «Курск»; «Анпилогов Федор»** ("compilation"; "1605–1618"; "Kursk"; "Anpilogov Fedor"). The other public
cells are empty. The workbook has no hidden sheet or comment with the file and folio, and
the description of the conventional code repeats only the title of the compilation.

Names and meaning. The name **Fyodor** and the form **Anpilogov** really are
the open reading of the index compiler. But the range **1605–1618** refers to
the whole compilation, not to an individual act of Fyodor's. The compilation itself mixes several
roles: captives, landholders of seized peasants, recipients of money and
pomestye additions and other parties to cases. So the open data do not allow
Fyodor to be called a Kursk syn boyarsky, his service to be dated or him to be recognised as an
ancestor of the Kursk or the Oryol branch. A profile, Record and map point have deliberately
not been created: that requires a specific archival document.

Tactical conclusion: further trying of spellings in this database is almost exhausted.
The working search moves to the inheritance petitions of 1627–1645 and the
original grants for the hamlet of Anpilogova, where formulas naming the former
town, the father and the time service began are possible.

### A Complete Check of the Precedence Inventory: There Is No Separate Tatev—Polev File

The official **inventory 9 of fond 210** has now been checked in full, page by page,
and not only by the heading of file 3. No separate precedence case of the Oryol
voivodes **Boris Tatev and Bogdan Ivanovich Polev** for 1599 is
described in it. The early Polev lines refer above all to a different man —
**Ivan Osipovich Polev** in 1591/92 and 1599/1600. Tatev's name files in
this inventory date from as late as 1624 and 1627.

This is an important boundary, but not proof that the Oryol list perished. The voivode's
report may have remained among stolbtsy without a personal heading, passed into
another chancery complex, or survived only in an extract made for
a subsequent dispute. The file found, **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 9, d. 3, stolpik 2**,
remains an exact record-keeping parallel, but does not turn into a
Tatev—Bogdan Polev file.

For the Mikhaylov case the exact
[card of the digital unit in the ГИС УИАД](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000000358014/10000474487014/) has also been established.
The printed text of 1838 has already been read; the next stage will be a page-by-page
check against the digital original, especially the margins and annexes that the editor
may have omitted.

### Polev Before Oryol: Belyov, Then Livny and Kromy

The official catalogue gave a new geographical marker for the second Oryol
voivode: **РГАДА, f. 281, op. 5, d. 1511** — the survey file of Bogdan Ivanovich
Polev for Belyov, 1586/87. A biographical analysis reveals his double
name as **«Максим прозвище Богдан Иванов сын Полев»** ("Maksim, nicknamed Bogdan, Ivanov syn Polev"), then places him in Oryol
in April 1599, in Livny in 1599/1600 and at the survey of Kromy in 1605/06.
[Official card of the Belyov file](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000228014/10000000640014/10000029585014/).

Names and meaning. Maksim is the baptismal name, Bogdan a stable by-name;
both forms must be sought in inventories. The service chain **Belyov — Oryol — Livny —
Kromy** explains why Polev's papers may be distributed among the
files of several southern towns. But this is the route of the scribe and voivode himself, not the
route of Anfilogovs. No profile for Polev and no line on the family map are created.

### The Kursk Alphabet of Stolbtsy: A Similar Surname Turned Out to Be a False Trail

To check inheritance petitions, the official manuscript alphabetical index
to the pasted Kursk stolbtsy has been opened: **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1353i**, the volume for the letters
А—К. The initial name table and the corresponding entry for the letter А were viewed
in the original images. The list has no heading for Anpilogovs,
Anfilogovs, Onpilogovs or Onfilogovs. A similar-looking spelling turned out to be
the heading **Апшенковы** (Apshenkovs), and a suspicious line for a person reads
**«Волк Антипов сын»** (Volk Antipov syn), not «Анпилогов сын».

Names and meaning. Antip is a different personal name, and «Антипов сын» ("son of Antip") is a patronymic name
formed from it. It does not belong to the series Anfilof/Anfilog and gives
no grounds for creating a profile. The negative check closes this particular
alphabetical volume, but not all the Kursk stolbtsy: the early Fyodor Anpilogov of the compilation of
1605–1618 may be in another complex.

The main practical result of the new tactic is that the exact official
address of the enrolment desyatnya of 1628 has been established: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 270**,
[ГИС УИАД card](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000000344014/10000474444014/).
The catalogue locates the Kursk part on **fols. 176–186**. At first the checked
publication of fols. 313–326 v. did not yield the surname, but then a separate
[full line-by-line transcription of the main block](https://old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/ku260320.html) was found.

### Folio 185: Vasily's Father Has Died, 40 Chetverti Remain

The target line is on **fol. 185**:

> «Василей Семенов сын Анпилогов, окладчики про него сказали: отец умер,
> братьи у него нет, поместья отцовского за ним 40 чети».
>
> *Translation:* "Vasiley Semenov syn Anpilogov; the assessors said of him: his father has died,
> he has no brothers, he holds 40 cheti of his father's pomestye."

Vasily is placed in the third grade: a new pomestye allotment of **100 chetverti** and money
from the town of **4 roubles**. These are different measures. The forty chetverti are the
father's holding he already had; the hundred are the size of the service allotment assigned to Vasily.

Names and meaning. "Semenov syn" directly names the father Semyon, and the form Anpilogov
belongs to the same documented series as Onpilogov in the list of 1630.
The main new detail is that the father had already died before March 1628. Therefore **Semyon
Osipovich and Semyon Yakovlevich Onpilogov**, both recorded alive in Kursk in
1630, are no longer candidates for Vasily's father. The father was a **third
Semyon** of the family circle.

The best candidate now becomes Semyon Anpilogov from the Kursk customs
book of 1619: he lived earlier, had a dependent peasant and could have died before
1628. But the 1619 document does not name his patronymic, son or pomestye,
so the kinship remains a hypothesis for now.

The find also proves that Vasily's family did not appear in Kursk only at the
moment of his enrolment as a novik: his father had already served and had managed to receive land.
The preamble of the desyatnya required the former town to be ascertained above all for
landless men living with kin. Vasily had his father's 40 chetverti, and
so his line does not report a former town.

Profile `P0075` has been supplemented without creating a duplicate. A new card with the full
transcription: `PUB-KURKULEV-2026-KURSK-1628-L185-VASILY-SEMYONOV`. A separate
Record for fol. 185 will appear after a photocopy of the manuscript; the already existing Record
from Zertsalov keeps the parallel entry of the money enrolment of 15 March.

The map gets no new route: Kursk is already proven for Vasily, but the place of
the father's 40 chetverti and Semyon's former town are not named. The next goal is
to find the original grant (dacha) of these 40 chetverti and check its link with the Semyon
of 1619 or with the village of Anpilogova and Gavrila.

### A New Tactic: Searching for a Group, Not a Single Surname

On 27 August the full XLSX of «Дом предков» (House of Ancestors), with 421,677 rows, was checked not only
for the variants Anpilogov/Anfilogov. For all entries with a start date no later than
1593 the rare names of the Oryol core were collected: Mikula, Nechay, Persha/Pervoy,
Belyay, Sofon, Pakhom and Terenty. Then the matches were grouped by a single call number,
a single dating and a single town. The idea was strict: if several characteristic names or families
turned up together before Oryol, this would be stronger than a lone
surname match.

The densest outside group was indeed found in the Derevskaya pyatina:
**РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 974, 1593–1595**. In one large corpus
there are the names Mikula, Nechay, Pervoy and Sofon; **Erofey and
Likhach Mikulin** are indexed separately, as well as two mentions of **Bogdan Onanyin**. At first glance
the combination of Mikulins and the rare surname Onanyin recalls the Oryol book, where
the sons of Mikula Anfilogov and the line of Anpilog Ivanovich
Onanyin exist side by side.

But a check of the source's structure closed this path. File 974 is the dozornaya (inspection) book
of the huge Derevskaya pyatina with **1,536 mentions of pomeshchiki**, including current,
former and vacant holders. "Mikulin" here is an independent Novgorod
surname, not the patronymic of Vasily, Timofey and Karp; Bogdan Onanyin is a different
Novgorod pomeshchik. Anpilog/Anfilog, the Oryol neighbourhood circle and the formula
of a former town are absent from the open lines. This is a statistical coincidence within
a mass Novgorod corpus, not a trace of resettlement.

Names and meaning. In the Oryol entry the formula **«Микулины дети Анфилоговы»** ("Mikula's children, Anfilogovs")
means the sons of a man named Mikula belonging to the
Anfilogov family series. In the Novgorod index the entry **«Микулин Ерофей»** ("Mikulin Erofey") is built
differently: Mikulin stands in the position of Erofey's own surname. In the same way the match of
Onanyin by surname does not turn Bogdan into a relative of the Oryol Anpilog
Ivanovich Onanyin. Without a common chain of father, son, pomestye or former town,
identical forms must not be glued together.

Therefore no Novgorod route, profiles or map points are created. The group
method is kept, but its use is narrowed to sources able to give a
biographical formula: stroenye and enrolment lists, original grants (dachi),
inheritance petitions and transfer (otkaznye) cases. The nearest targeted goals are the father's
40 chetverti of Vasily Semyonovich, the patronymic of Gavrila Anpilogov, the full list
of the 200 Oryol men of 1580 and the late petitions of the first Kursk men with the formula «как город
Куреск стал» ("since the town of Kuresk came to be").

### A Family Signature Instead of a Surname: the Full Result of the Database

After checking five short beginnings — **Anpi, Ampi, Anfi, Onpi and Onfi** — the whole
export of 421,676 rows was rebuilt not by surname but by family
combinations. All items with a start date no later than 1593 were grouped
by one file, year, town and first name form. Then within each
group the two strongest signatures of the Oryol core were sought:

- **Vasily + Timofey + Karp**, the three sons of Mikula;
- **Belyay + Osip**, the two sons of Vasily.

The database gave not a single complete signature. There is neither the trio Vasily–Timofey–Karp under
one pre-Oryol name, nor the pair Belyay–Osip. This is stricter than the earlier search
by rare names: now it was precisely the joint appearance of the presumed
brothers within one source group that was checked.

The closest resemblance is **«Микулинский Васюк»** and **«Микулинский Тимошка»** in
the Derevskaya pyatina of 1582, РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 716. Vasyuk is a form
of the name Vasily, Timoshka of Timofey, but there is no Karp in the group; the form
«Микулинский» (Mikulinsky) is not equal to the Oryol formula «Микулины дети» (Mikula's children), and the index itself does not
call these people brothers or Anfilogovs. The second lone match is
**«Микулин Василий»** in the Obonezhskaya pyatina of 1588, РГАДА, f. 137, op. 1,
d. 11; there are no Timofey and Karp next to him.

Names and meaning. The Oryol entry reports not just three similar names but
a kinship construction: Vasily, Timofey and Karp are Mikula's children and
Anfilogovs. The Novgorod index preserves only separate modern
normalisations. A match of two names without the third brother, a common father and the family
form must not be turned into a pedigree.

The result does not prove that all the branches arose in Oryol. It shows the limit
of this database: the pre-Oryol family is not in it, either under a ready-made surname or under the two
strongest sets of brothers. Therefore the next move is not a repeated search by
word beginnings but the primary **list of the 200 Oryol men of 1580**, the original grants
in Bazdyrevo and Belaya, and late lawsuits with extracts from lost books. A separate
audit card: `PUB-DOMPREDKOV-421677-PRE1594-FAMILY-SIGNATURE-AUDIT`.
The map does not change: the former town of not a single Anfilogov has yet been named.

### Checking the Neighbourhood Cluster Across the Whole Database

The same full export was checked in one more way: not by the brothers' names but
by the nearest co-owners of Anfilogovs in the village of Bazdyreva. Before 1594
the forms **Bazdyrev/Bozdyrev, Trunov, Krivolapov, Lunin,
Takhtamyshev/Tokhtamyshev and Kostin** were searched separately. The database showed no joint complex of Bazdyrev — Trunov —
Krivolapov. This closes one more possibility of getting
a ready-made route by simply going through a public index.

In the Obonezhskaya pyatina of 1583, РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 725, there did
turn up **Kultash Takhtamyshev** and several Kostins. But this is not the Oryol
signature: there is no Bazdyrev, Trunov, Krivolapov or Anfilogov next to them, and the personal
names do not match the holders of Bazdyrevo and Belaya. Two common
family forms within a large Novgorod book must not be turned into a
resettled group.

Names and meaning. The neighbourhood check is stricter than a match of one surname. The Bazdyrevs,
Trunovs and Krivolapovs simultaneously held shares of one village with the early
Anfilogovs; their joint pre-Oryol appearance would be an independent
argument. The absence of this combination in the database does not prove that it was absent from
the lost documents, but it shows that the current public index has not
preserved it. A Novgorod map is therefore not created.

In parallel, the digital path to the Polish unit **AGAD, Archiwum
Zamoyskich, sygn. 3032** was checked. The official card of the fonds reports 3,195 units
and 164,083 scans, that is, the fonds has been digitised very widely. However, this number refers
to the whole Archiwum Zamoyskich, and not automatically to sygn. 3032. The exact card of
the unit has not yet been isolated: the portal's interactive filter is stopped on the current network
by the site's protection. Therefore the honest status remains as before: the order on fols. 30–31
has been read from a scholarly publication of the original; there is no reply list in the published
complex; the availability of open frames of this particular unit is not yet confirmed.

### Istoma Trunov: Kazan Service and Vladimir Land Before Oryol

The full first volume of «Акты служилых землевладельцев» (Acts of Service Landholders), which had earlier remained outside
the exhaustive check of volumes II–IV, gave a pre-Oryol entry of a rare surname from the
Bazdyrevo neighbourhood circle. Act no. 42 of 31 August 1583 states
that a share (zherebey) of the wasteland of Borisova, also called Gridino, had formerly been in the pomestye of **the Kazan
zhilets Istoma Trunov**. The full text reveals the mechanism itself. According to the separate
books of 7088 (1579/80), Kurbat Izmaylov and his fellows had allotted to Karp Neronov
syn Volkov a full grant of **200 chetverti**, including in it Istoma's former land,
but «**выписи ему Карпу Курбат не дал**» ("Kurbat did not give him, Karp, an extract"). So the charter of 1583 is
not a new transfer from Istoma to Karp, but the correction of the unfinished paperwork of an already
completed allotment. The edition indicates the primary source:
**РГАДА, f. 1209, stolbtsy for Vladimir, no. 528/33779**, a copy of 1636.

Names and meaning. Istoma is a personal name; "Kazan zhilets" is a service and town
affiliation, not a place of birth. The document shows that a man's service town and
pomestye could lie in different regions. However, this is not a
ready-made route to Oryol. In Bazdyrevo in 1594/95 Marya, **Ivanova zhena
Trunova** (Ivan's wife Trunova), that is, the widow of Ivan Trunov, is recorded with her children. Istoma and Ivan are different people;
so far only the surname matches between them. Kinship cannot be declared without
a Kazan or Vladimir list naming Istoma's father or children.

Even more important, the recipient has now been found in a service list. In the Vladimir desyatnya
of the money distribution of 7090 (1581/82), reconstructed from an archival extract,
**Karp Neronov syn Volkov** is directly named a **novik of the dvorovy rank**. His
pomestye allotment is the same 200 chetverti, his money allotment the first 11 roubles. He arrived
on a horse, in a helmet and armour, with a saadak (bow case and quiver) and a sabre; he fielded an armed mounted
man with a spare horse and a baggage man with packs. The archival basis
of this entry: **РГАДА, f. 388, op. 1, no. 851, fols. 234–234 v.** The result is
a rare complete sequence: enrolment of a novik and allotment of land →
an extract not issued → a restorative vvoznaya (installation) charter.

The card and the evidential fragment of printed p. 39 are kept as
`PUB-ASZ1-1583-ISTOMA-TRUNOV-KAZAN-VLADIMIR-OREL-CROSSCHECK`. There is no separate
profile or line on the map: a man of an unrelated surname is used only
as a check of the neighbourhood circle, and the document does not name a resettlement of Anfilogovs.

A later exhaustive check of the Vladimir desyatnya of 1590 refined this
sequence. There are no Trunovs in it, but **Latynya Neronov syn
Volkov** and **Stepan Neronov syn Volkov** are recorded. The rare common formula makes them
probable close relatives of Karp Neronovich Volkov, who received
Istoma's land; the word "brothers", however, is not used in the source. The result is
a verifiable change: Trunov left the holding no later than 1579/80, while the probable
family of the new holder remained in Vladimir service in 1590. The desyatnya does not report the reason
for the departure or Istoma's new town.

Between these dates one more independent control point was found. In a Rostov
donation deed (vkladnaya) of 1584/85 **Latynya Neronov syn Volkov** acted as a witness (poslukh) and
certified the act with the formula «**Послух Латыня руку приложил**» ("Witness Latynya set his hand") (РГАДА, f. 281,
Rostov, no. 35/10572; publication: «Русский дипломатарий» [Russian Diplomatarium], issue 1, no. 137,
p. 105). This proves a man with the same rare full formula, but not his
place of residence and not a brotherhood with Karp named by the source.

#### What the Exhaustive Check of the Kazan Books Gave

A full modern edition of the cadastral description of Kazan and Kazan district of
1565–1568 was found in the electronic collection of the National Library of the Republic of
Tatarstan: 660 printed and 668 digital pages. The introduction confirms the basis of
the publication — **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, kn. 152**, with the use of the copies of books
643 and 646. However, remote viewing outside the library's network gives only the introductory
part; the main district text does not load after moving to the page.
The abridged edition of 1932, available for full-text search in Google
Books, contains the town part: there is neither Trunov nor the variants
of the name under study in it. This is not an exhaustive negative result for the district.

The nearest open book of Kazan district, of 1602–1603, was checked in full
from the PDF, including the name index. The search covered **Trunov, Istoma, Anfilog, Anpilog,
Onfilog, Ampilog, Onpilog, Anfilof**, as well as **Bazdyrev, Sviridov,
Krivolapov, Nemtsov and Grevtsov**. There are no target matches. The only Istomka is
the peasant **Istomka Dyatchanya** in the village of Iski Kazan; he has nothing to do with the Trunovs or the Oryol
cluster.

Names and meaning. This zero must not be interpreted as Istoma Trunov's absence from
the Kazan region. The archaeographic essay of the book directly states: the surviving
copy describes the part of the lands with a non-Russian service and yasak-paying population;
the existing book of Russian settlements probably perished in the Kazan fire
of 1815. A Kazan zhilets of the Russian service circle may therefore not have got into
the surviving volume. The tactic is refined: search not the general Kazan cadastres but
**lists of Russian Kazan zhiltsy, otdelnye (allotment) and vvoznye (installation) charters of the 1550s–1580s
and Istoma's original Vladimir grant**.

[Card of the full edition of 1565–1568](https://kitap.tatar.ru/ru/ssearch/detail/qefybetwhg/),
[open publication of the book of 1602–1603](https://xn--80ad7bbk5c.xn--p1ai/ru/book/export/html/354),
[Rostov donation deed with the signature of Latynya Volkov](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1500-1520/Jaroslav_mon/text2.htm).

#### Poluekt Dolmatov: Not a Kazan–Oryol Bridge but the Route Mozhaysk → Kazan

The publication of an early Kazan charter of 1553/54 does indeed name, at
the Spassky Gate, **«двор Полуехтов Федорова сына Немцова»** ("the homestead of Poluekht Fedorov syn Nemtsov"). But extending
the search to the Mozhaysk acts completely changed the meaning of this entry.

In the **Dvorovaya tetrad (Court Register) of the 1550s**, in the section "Mozhaysk" on manuscript fol. 138,
he is recorded with a full formula: **«Полуект Федоров сын Немцова Долматова»** (Poluekt Fedorov syn Nemtsova Dolmatova).
The name index identifies him as a dvorovy syn boyarsky of Mozhaysk.
Thus we have before us not a nameless Kazan Nemtsov, but a serviceman
from the Mozhaysk family of the Dolmatovs.

Even more reliable is a family act of **9 December 1547**. Stefan Fyodorov syn
Dolmatov reports that his father **Fyodor Vasilyevich Dolmatov** left the village of
Eskino «двема нам с братом с Полиектом вопче по половинам» ("to the two of us, me and my brother Poliekt, jointly in halves"). Their
stepmother **Matryona**, who held the village for life, is also named. On the next page
Stefan writes once more: **«брату моему Полиекту»** ("to my brother Poliekt"). This is a direct, not a
reconstructed, family: father Fyodor Vasilyevich; sons Stefan and Poluekt;
a joint inheritance in Mozhaysk district.

Names and meaning. **Poluekht / Poluekt** are documentary variants of the name Polyeuctus;
«двор Полуехтов» is Poluekt's homestead. **Dolmatov** is now confirmed both by a service
list and by a family charter. But the element **«Немцов»** (Nemtsov) cannot without reservation
be considered a separate stable surname: a long 16th-century formula could
combine a patronymic, a branch nickname and a family name. It has not yet been established
whether "Nemtsov" referred to Poluekt, to his father's nickname or to the line of
the Dolmatovs.

Therefore the earlier hypothesis of a possible brotherhood with the Oryol **Gavrilka
Fyodorov syn Nemtsov** has been downgraded. A noticeable fragment of the naming matches,
but Poluekt's known brother is Stefan Dolmatov, his father Fyodor Vasilyevich
Dolmatov, and Gavrila does not appear in these documents. Another route is reliably proven:
**Mozhaysk → Kazan by 1553/54** for Poluekt himself. This is a good
example of the mechanism of transferring servicemen to conquered Kazan, but not the path
of Anfilogovs and not a proven path of the Nemtsovs to Oryol.

The text of the Kazan charter has come down in a copy book of the second half of the 1580s:
**РГБ ОР, Troitskoe sobranie, kn. 526, fols. 47–48**; publication by S. M. Kashtanov,
document no. 9, p. 25. A scan of the 1892 Mozhaysk act and a short fragment of
the Dvorovaya tetrad are kept in the card's evidence. No profiles for the unrelated
Dolmatovs and Nemtsovs and no line on the map are created. Card:
`PUB-KASHTANOV-1553-POLUEKT-NEMTSOV-KAZAN-OREL-CROSSCHECK`.

[Text of document no. 9](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1540-1560/Russ_zemlevlad_kazan_kraj/text.htm),
[scan of the Dvorovaya tetrad](https://djvu.online/file/LkCyGoYkf8EXC),
[scan of the «Можайские акты» (Mozhaysk Acts) of 1892](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Dionisij_Mozhajskie_akty_1506_1775_1892.pdf).

### The Rzhev Draft: Six Unknown Folios Instead of a Hundred

The new tactic gave a precise archival fork. Earlier, for the file **РГАДА, f. 210,
op. 9, d. 232**, it had been planned to order a broad block of fols. 263–370. Now
the official РГАДА inventory has been checked from an image of printed p. 108. It
confirms: the file has 375 folios; the required fragment **«поход ко Пскову великого
князя Семиона Бекбулатовича Тверского»** ("the campaign to Pskov of Grand
Prince Simeon Bekbulatovich of Tver"), whose beginning and end are torn off,
begins on **fol. 289 v.**, and the next separately described razryad entry comes
only on **fol. 322**. The inventory does not reveal what occupies the six folios between them.
A snapshot of the page is kept in the card. A detailed description of the file as issued
further divides it into five microfilms: the fourth contains fols. 214–286,
the fifth fols. 287–375.

The page markup of the scholarly publication of the Rzhev draft runs only from
**fol. 290 to fol. 315 v.** Between it and the next archival entry there remain
**fols. 316–321**. A subsequent full collation of the 1914 edition, however, removed from
this gap its former first priority: at the end of the footnoted text on
fol. 315 v. the editors directly noted that **«дальнейший текст не сохранился»** ("the further text has not survived"), and
part of the ending was reconstructed from a parallel razryad entry and from the text on
fol. 290 v. Folios 316–321 still need to be looked at together with their versos in order to
record their physical condition, but now this is a control order, not
the main expected carrier of the list of the two hundred Oryol men. If the archive issues only
a whole film, microfilm no. 5 is needed.

[Description of the file and the microfilm breakdown](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/252/21016/p2909163.htm),
[the published draft with foliation](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Razrjad_pochod_1580/text.htm).

### How a Second Copy of the List Could Have Survived

In **Акты Московского государства** (Acts of the Muscovite State), vol. I, no. 30, a document of the same
Simeon Bekbulatovich, close in its mechanics, was found. In May–June 1585 a sovereign's name list of the men of Tver
and Torzhok was sent to him from Moscow. Separate collectors
gathered the men in Tver and Torzhok «по имянным списком» ("according to the name lists"), after which they were led to
Novgorod. The reply report (otpiska) explained why five named men had not been
sent, and the appendix preserved a large list with town, rank, pomestye and
money allotment, the dead, the fled and even new places of service.

This is not a pre-Oryol list of Anfilogovs and not a basis for deriving the family from Tver.
The practical meaning is different: one urgent muster generated several documents —
the Moscow name list, the collectors' town lists, a register of the men led off and
the reply report. Therefore the list of the 200 Oryol men of 1580 should be sought not only
in an appendix to the campaign draft but also among incoming reports, papers of
collectors and documents on the leading-off of men. The search formulas are now more precise:
**«по имянному списку собрали»** ("gathered according to the name list"), **«отпустил»** ("dispatched"), **«отвести»** ("to lead off"), **«списки имян»** ("lists of names").

[The report and the full appended list of 1585](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/21-40/30.htm).

Names and meaning. In the 1585 appendix the personal names are needed not to create
profiles for unrelated men of Tver and Torzhok, but to understand how the record-keeping
was organised. Especially important are the notes «сбежал, а ныне служит из Рузы» ("fled, and now serves from Ruza") and «служит из
Торопца» ("serves from Toropets"): such lists really could directly name the former or new
place of service of a specific man. If a similar copy survived for
the Oryol men, it could answer our main question. So far neither Mikula, nor Vasily,
nor Timofey, nor Persha, nor Pakhom, nor Nechay, nor Terenty has been found in it;
the profiles and the map do not change.

### Four Holders of the Oryol Lists

The critical edition of the general Razryad Book gave two new controls. First,
the line on the muster of the Oryol men has been collated by the editors with five parallel copies
designated **O, P, U, E and Kh**. The variants correct the reading of the name of Mikhail
Turenin and of the verb «не ушли/шли» ("did not go / went"), but none adds names of rank-and-file men.
Consequently, the known redactions of the general razryad entry do not contain a simple
hidden duplicate of the Oryol register.

Second, the same book under **4 August 1580** directly describes the handling of
lists at Volok Lamsky. Simeon Bekbulatovich handed out to the boyars and voivodes
**«списки имянные дворян и детей боярских»** ("name lists of the dvoryane and deti boyarskie"). Some voivodes took the lists,
others refused because of a precedence (mestnichestvo) dispute; the sovereign ordered that the list be
handed over anyway, and in one case threatened punishment for refusal. This is no longer
an analogy from another year but the documented procedure of the same campaign.

The Rzhev draft allows four personal channels to be named. The lists of the Oryol men
or of mixed assignments with Oryol men were held by, or were to be passed
through, **Semyon Danilovich Pronsky, Mikhail Samsonovich Turenin, Vasily
Dmitrievich Khilkov and Vladimir Vasilyevich Golovin**. Therefore the search
is rebuilt around people: their service and precedence cases, incoming reports,
appendices to petitions and late extracts on regimental position. For a dispute over
seniority the very fact of "took the list" was legally significant and could be repeated in
a case together with the document or an extract.

[Razryad Book: handing out of name lists at Volok Lamsky](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XV/1460-1480/RK_1475_1605/text22.php),
[the Rzhev draft naming the holders](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Razrjad_pochod_1580/text.php).

Names and meaning. Pronsky, Turenin, Khilkov and Golovin are not presumed ancestors
nor original fellow countrymen of Anfilogovs. Their names matter as addresses of the movement of paper:
each could have received one of the copies of the town list. No profiles are created for these
voivodes, and their movements are not put on the family map.

### Khilkov's Complete Headquarters Archive: No Reply, but the Date Has Become Exact

All **32 documents** of the complex **AGAD, Archiwum Zamoyskich,
sygn. 3032** have been checked: thirty charters and reports and two records of prisoners. It is this
"headquarters archive" that was captured in the course of the campaign and remained in the family archive
of the Zamoyskis. It contains no Anfilof, Anfilog, Ampilog, Onfilog or variant
of the surname under study. The order of 5 September about the two hundred men has survived, but
there is no reply report or appended list in the complex.

On the other hand, the exact course of the paper is now known. The order was delivered by Ivan Bezobrazov
on **9 September 1580**. Pronsky was instructed to send from Rzhev two hundred
of the best deti boyarskie with their heads (golovy), and Khilkov to report their arrival and
send to Moscow **«списки имян их»** ("lists of their names"). Therefore the Moscow search begins
not in the autumn generally but from **10 September 1580**, above all in the September and
October incoming reports of the Razryad Prikaz.

Document no. 29 shows one more important feature. Khilkov explained that he had not
sent the names of several who had failed to appear because **«в списке у меня имяна их
не написаны»** ("their names are not written in my list"). Consequently, the original regimental list, the register of actual
arrivals and the roll of men beyond the list might not coincide. One should search not for one
ideal register but for a package: the main list, the arrivals, the nety (absentees), the additions and
the reply report.

[Full list of the 32 documents](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Dok_livon_vojne_2/index.htm),
[the order about the two hundred](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Dok_livon_vojne_2/3/27.htm),
[the report on men outside the list](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Dok_livon_vojne_2/3/29.htm).

### Precedence Cases: the Razryad of 1580 Lived On in Later Disputes

A scan of N. P. Likhachev's edition **«Местнические дела
1563–1605 гг.»** (Precedence Cases of 1563–1605) has been checked in full. In the reckoning case of Roman Olferyev against Pyotr Golovin of 1583
a participant reconstructed service seniority through the Rzhev disposition
of 7088/1580 and the position of Vladimir Golovin. This text does not list rank-and-file Oryol men,
but it proves that the required razryad entry was already being extracted three years later as
judicial evidence.

In a case of 1605 the Pushkins several times referred to old instructions and charters with
the formula **«и ныне у нас»** ("and now with us"). So the campaign documents could outlive the campaign
in two ways: in an official extract of a precedence court and in a private family
archive. None of the five cases published by Likhachev contains the list sought,
but the tactic has been refined: check the late lawsuits not only of the four
voivodes but also of their relatives who produced old instructions.

[Open scan of Likhachev's edition](https://archive.org/details/mestnicheskiedela15631605gg58).
Photocopies of printed pages 7 and 89 and the full PDF are kept in the research
archive of the site. The map does not change: these documents give no named bearer of the family series or his
route.

### A Strict Search in «Дом предков» and a New Moscow Channel

The short queries **Anpi, Ampi, Anfi, Onpi and Onfi** were rechecked not only
across the whole database but also with a separate filter for years beginning with **15**. For
Anp, Amp, Onp and Onf the result is zero. The only Anf match is
**Zolotukhin Anfinogen**, Obonezhskaya pyatina, 1583, РГАДА, f. 1209,
op. 1, d. 725. This is a different personal name, not Anfilog/Anfilof and not a variant
of the surname. No profile, Record or map point is created. The mass trawl of the current
version of the database is thereby exhausted: it does not contain the transitional layer of 1580–1594.

[The 1583 index line](https://dompredkov.ru/pomeshhiki-obonezhskoj-pyatiny-novgoroda-1583g-ch-1-nagornaya-polovina/).

On the other hand, the list of storage units of the edition **«Документы Ливонской войны»** (Documents of the Livonian War) opened
another channel. Besides Khilkov's captured archive in AGAD, the compilers worked
with the Moscow complexes **РГАДА, f. 64, op. 1, d. 7; f. 141, op. 1,
d. 2 for 1577–1579 and files 1–5 for 1578; f. 159, op. 1, files
1–24, 243–246, 248–249**. These call numbers are not Khilkov's sought-after report:
the section with his 32 papers comes from AGAD AZ 3032. But the list itself
proves that similar prikaz paperwork of the campaign was dispersed among
several fonds. But this information has been compared with the already completed full
check of the scholarly and old manuscript inventories of fond 141. On the official scans
the old inventory physically passes from the separate files of **1559 straight to
1589**; there is no missing page and no separate block for 1580. Inventory 1
of fond 159, in turn, begins only **from 1581**. Therefore neither
fond can be declared a new direct address of the reply report of September 1580.
They are kept only as possible locations of an enclosed document or
a late copy. The main search returns to the miscellaneous stolbtsy of the Razryad,
pomestye cases and late service extracts.

[List of the archival units used](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Dok_livon_vojne_2/spis_ed_chran.htm).
[Official guide to fond 141](https://guides.rusarchives.ru/terms/9/2550/prikaznye-dela-staryh-let).

Names and meaning. Anfinogen Zolotukhin is needed here precisely as an error control:
a similar beginning of a name does not make a man an Anfilogov. Fonds 141 and 159, in
turn, matter not for the geography of people but for the geography of paper. Until a
named list is found in them, the origin of Oryol Anfilogovs and the route of resettlement
remain open; the map does not change.

### The Database Checked Across the Whole Neighbourhood Cluster, Not Only by Surname

The public interface of «Дом предков» allows all **421,095**
rows to be sorted by year. Therefore the new pass was made not by the beginnings Anpi/Anfi but by
the people who sat next to Anfilogovs in the village of Bazdyrevo in 1594/95:
the Bazdyrevs, Trunovs, Krivolapovs and Nemtsovs. The rare
family formula of Mikula's three sons — Vasily, Timofey and Karp — was checked separately.

Before the Oryol book the database shows no joint appearance of the whole cluster.
The earliest later lines are distributed as follows: Fyodor Bazdyrev — Odoev,
1614; several Trunovs — Solova and Tula, 1617, Pyotr Trunov —
Dedilov, 1620; Parfyon Krivolapov — Yelets, composite range 1605–1618,
Ivashka Krivolapov — Tula, 1617; the early Nemtsovs — Suzdal,
1604. The Oryol Bazdyrevs, Trunovs and Krivolapovs confidently return in
the lists of the 1620s.

The full formulas of the neighbours gave an even stricter negative result.
Gavrilka Fyodorov syn Nemtsov, Onokha and Lyova Deevy deti Krivolapovy (Dey's children Krivolapov), Ovdey and
Pronya Ivanovy deti Krivolapovy (Ivan's children Krivolapov) did not recur at another early point.
Grishka Krivolapov appears in Oryol from 1620/21, but the index omits
the patronymic, so he cannot be identified with Grishka Ivanov syn of 1594/95.
Mikifor Bazdyrev recurs in Oryol only in 1648, and also without
a patronymic in the short line.

A new search by a brother's rare name gave a more precise late lead:
**«Баздырев Понкратий» — Oryol, 1697, РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6g, d. 25**.
Then an earlier line was discovered: **«Баздырев Панкрат» — Odoev, 1679,
РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6b, d. 18**. The eighteen-year gap allows that
this is one serviceman who moved from Odoev to Oryol, but the index gives no
patronymic, age or land. Both entries echo the Dedilov book
of 1588/89, where Ponkratko, brother of Ivanko Nechaev, and the Cossack
Olfim Bazdyrev are named separately. A century passed between the 16th and the end of the 17th century, so personal
identity is excluded and the origin is not proven.

An intermediate firm support has also appeared. In the Oryol list of 1627/28
**Olferko Maksimov syn Bazdyrev** is recorded with 100 chetverti in the same town
rubric that contains Vaska Dmitreev syn Onpilogov, Ivashka and Oleshka Vasilyevy
deti Onfilogovy (Vasily's children Onfilogov) and Marka Karpov syn Onfilokhov. Thus the neighbourhood of the two
surnames continued in Oryol at least from the survey book of 1594/95 to
1627/28. A five-step control sequence now emerges:
Dedilov 1588/89 → Oryol 1594/95 → Oryol 1627/28 → Odoev 1679 → Oryol 1697.
It proves the stability of the Upper Oka service network of the Bazdyrevs, but so far not
a pedigree and not a common route of Anfilogovs. The practical next step is
to compare the full family entries of Pankraty of 1679 and 1697.

The surname Mikulin is indeed present in the database in the 16th century, mainly
in the Novgorod pyatinas: Afonya Mikulin in 1556, several
Mikulins in 1582, Vasily Mikulin in 1588. But there is no simultaneous trio
Vasily—Timofey—Karp, no name Anfilog and no formula of transfer to Oryol.
The Novgorod Mikulins remain control namesakes, not a route.

The meaning of the result is not the choice of one town from a late output. After the Time of Troubles
servicemen families of the same name are visible simultaneously in Oryol, Odoev, Tula, Solova,
Dedilov, Yelets and Kursk; this may reflect both old branches and
dispersal from Oryol itself. Therefore the direction cannot be guessed from the first
available 17th-century date. The next search is conducted through the outgoing documents
of possible donor towns and through full father—children links.

[The open search database «Дом предков»](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/).
The map does not change: not a single line contains a direct transfer of a bearer of
the family series.

### Khilkov's Report Divided the List Sought into Three Documents

A reply report of Prince Vasily Dmitrievich Khilkov **after
17 June 1580** has been published. He reported that he had marked the dvoryane who appeared «по списку» ("according to the list"), and
then sent to the sovereign, with Fyodor Vorontsov, separate **«приезды и неты»** ("arrivals and absentees").
This is direct evidence that the original name list, the register of those who arrived and
the register of those who failed to appear existed separately and could have ended up in different
archival complexes.

The report concerns the men of Kolomna and Yaroslavl of the first Velikiye Luki dispatch, and
