does not name the Oryol men. So it does not prove the presence of Anfilogovs. But it
documents the procedure of the same chancery and corrects the archival tactic:
one must search not only for an appendix to the Rzhev draft but for separate
**arrivals**, **absentees**, the original list and additions of men «сверх списка» ("beyond the list").
For the September order about two hundred men the search begins after 9 September
1580.

[Khilkov's report after 17 June 1580, document no. 28](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Dok_livon_vojne_2/3/28.htm).

### A New Tactic: Searching for the Outgoing Selection Books of the Donor Town

K. V. Petrov described the paperwork of the resettlement of servicemen using the example of
the new towns of the Belgorod Line. The formula most important for us is in
the title of a list of 1647–1648: **«Орляне дети боярские выбранные в Карпов
на вечное житье»** ("Oryol deti boyarskie selected for Karpov for permanent residence"). The author stresses that the deti boyarskie who were transferred (svedentsy) kept
their rank, and that the men to be transferred were selected by the corporation of the former service
town itself. Disagreement also left a paper trail: a separate citation is given of
the petition of the Kursk man Voin Annenkov for return from Tsarev-Alekseev.

The exact archival address of the analogous complex: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 258,
fols. 2, 54, 63, 66a, 79** — desyatni of the svedentsy of Oryol, Kursk, Mtsensk and other
towns to Karpov of 7155–7156. Annenkov's petition: **f. 210, op. 12,
d. 354, fols. 58–61**. For streltsy and Cossacks one more form of
family selection has survived: **f. 210, op. 6d, kn. 20, fols. 1–1 v.**

Now a literal outgoing trace in the desyatnya of the former town has also been found. During
the review of the Kursk service town on 31 August 1642, against **A. K.
Zavolishin** was written: **«сшел на Орел»** ("left for Oryol"); his pomestye allotment was 250
cheti. Petrov gives the exact address of the entry: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 188,
fol. 615 v.** This is not a route of Anfilogovs, but direct proof that
the Kursk desyatnya preserved the name of a man who left and his town of destination. A snapshot
of p. 239 of the article is kept in the card.

These are 17th-century documents, and their procedure cannot be transferred directly to 1566.
But they change the unit of search. What is required is not only an incoming "list of Oryol" but
a whole package of outgoing papers of a possible donor town: a selection book, a list of
those sent and those who failed to appear, an instruction to the voivode, a reply report, a petition for
return and a late pomestye extract. Therefore Belyov, Mtsensk, Bolkhov,
Novosil, Karachev, Tula, Dedilov, Krapivna, Ryazhsk and Pronsk are now checked
not by a general surname search but with the formulas **«выбраны/высланы/сведены на Орёл»** ("selected/sent/transferred to Oryol") and
the family combinations **Vasily–Timofey–Karp, Mikula's children** and
**Belyay–Osip, Vasily's children**.

Names and meaning. Voin Annenkov and the compilers of the Karpov list are not
ancestors of Anfilogovs; A. K. Zavolishin also does not belong to the family series
under study. No separate profiles or points are created for them. Their documents
show how the name of a settler and even the final town could be preserved on
the side of the former town if the incoming book of the new town is lost. The map
of origin does not change for now: the route of a specific Kursk man is confirmed, but
not the route of the Anfilogov family.

Card: `PUB-PETROV-2021-SVEDENTSY-SELECTION-MECHANISM`.
[Article by K. V. Petrov](https://inslav.ru/sites/default/files/2021_shveykovskaya_petrov.pdf).

### An Exact Search Word Found for the 16th Century: "Perepiska" of Service

A facsimile of V. N. Storozhev's 1891 edition added a vocabulary of transfer contemporary with the
desyatni themselves. Analysing the Epifan desyatnya of 1591/92, the editor
drew attention to men who served **«из иных городов»** ("from other towns"), in this case
from Tula and Belyov. Behind such a note he distinguished two mechanisms: a man either
**had his service re-registered (perepisalsya) from another town**, or was **transferred by order
of the government**.

The most useful indication comes next: the circumstances of a transfer were revealed by
**petitions for re-registration (perepiska) from one town to another**, preserved in the stolbtsy of
the Razryad Prikaz. So the earlier search was too narrow. Alongside
the words «сведены» ("transferred"), «выбраны» ("selected") and «переведены» ("moved"), one must search for **«переписать служить
на Орёл»** ("to re-register to serve in Oryol"), **«служит из иных городов»** ("serves from other towns"), **«переписался с [города]»** ("re-registered from [town]") and the
petitions for re-registration themselves.

This is a 16th-century source, not a late 17th-century analogy, but it still
does not name Anfilogovs. Tula and Belyov here relate to the Epifan men and are not
declared places of origin of the Oryol family. Its value lies in the
proven mechanism and the name of the document. On the side of the old town a petition could
remain; in the new desyatnya, a brief note of former service; in the old
book, a disappearance or an outgoing mark.

On the same page Storozhev shows that the available strength of Epifan fell
from 300 men in 1585 to 272 in 1591. The full published list of
320 names does not contain the Cossack **Mikulka Moiseev**, known in Epifan in 1572;
nor is he in the comparative breakdown of 1606. This is compatible with
departure, death, a change of rank or absence of enrolment among the deti boyarskie, but does not
prove a transfer to Oryol and does not allow him to be named the father of the three Mikulichi
Anfilogovs.

A scan of printed p. 129 and the full volume are kept in the card
`PUB-STOROZHEV-1891-P129-SERVICE-TOWN-TRANSFER-FORMULA`.
[Open facsimile on the Internet Archive](https://archive.org/details/materalydliaist00storgoog).
No profile or map entry is created: a specific route of a bearer of the family series
has not yet been established.

### A Whole Group of Streltsy Was Transferred from Mtsensk to Oryol

Full recognition of the official inventory of the stolbtsy of the Moscow desk (Moskovsky stol) gave no longer
a methodological example but a direct collective formula. In the file
**РГАДА, f. 210, op. 9, d. 153** for 1639–1640, fols. **83–85** are described as
**«выдача пороха и свинца стрельцам, переведенным из Мценска в Орел»** ("issue of powder and lead to the streltsy transferred from Mtsensk to Oryol").
The Oryol rubric on the same page refers back: **«Орла: см. Мценск»** ("Oryol: see Mtsensk").

The inventory does not reveal the names and number of the streltsy, but the direction and the service
category are established literally. This is a primary archival check that
Oryol, restored after 1635, was manned not only by individual
newcomers: an organised group of streltsy was transferred from neighbouring Mtsensk,
and then supplied with ammunition from the Razryad.

For the origin of the 1594/95 core the date is too late. However, together with
the entry on Nester Birlov, who before September 1636 «изо Мценска на Орел
жить шол» ("went from Mtsensk to Oryol to live"), two independent kinds of Mtsensk trace emerge: the individual
departure of a landholder and a state transfer of streltsy. The next precise
order is **d. 153, fols. 83–85**: the names and a reference to the transfer order may be
in the text itself. A snapshot of p. 80 of the official inventory is kept in the card
`PUB-RGADA-210-9-153-MTSENSK-STRELTSY-TO-OREL`. The map of the family series does not
change, because Anfilogovs are not named in the heading.

### A Direct Transfer Formula Found on the Donor Town's Side

A Mtsensk otkaznaya (transfer) book gave the first literal example of exactly the type of
entry that now needs to be sought for the Oryol core. On **20 September
1636**, during the redistribution of an old pomestye in the village of Porakhina,
the people of the inquiry (obysk) reported under oath: **«Нестер Бирлов изо Мценска на Орел
жить шол»** ("Nester Birlov went from Mtsensk to Oryol to live"). Next the material consequence of the departure is explained: he
abandoned his exchanged pomestye in Porakhina, the land remained empty and was given to no one,
after which it was transferred to Mina Kurbatov syn Efimov.

The archival address is exact: **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 195, kn. 15938, fols. 441—443 v.**;
publication: S. I. Kotkov, N. S. Kotkova, *Pamyatniki yuzhnovelikorusskogo
narechiya: Otkaznye knigi* (Monuments of the South Great Russian Dialect: Transfer Books), Moscow, 1977, p. 198. A photocopy of the page is kept in
the site's archive.

The find changes the search fundamentally. A move might not have been preserved in a general
"list of settlers" of Oryol. It is discovered at a secondary event at the old
place — when the former plot was declared empty and handed to the next
service holder. Therefore in the books of Mtsensk, Novosil, Bolkhov, Belyov,
Karachev and Tula one must search not only for Anfilogovs but also for the formulas **«изо
[города] на Орёл жить шёл»** ("went from [town] to Oryol to live"), **«сошёл на Орёл»** ("left for Oryol"), **«покинул поместье»** ("abandoned the pomestye"),
**«поместье порожжо»** ("the pomestye is vacant"). Then check whether the abandoned
holding belonged to Mikula, Vasily, Persha, Timofey, Nechay, Pakhom or Terenty — the fathers
of the seven Oryol lines of 1594/95.

Names and meaning. **Nester Shumilin syn Birlov** is a documented settler
Mtsensk → Oryol, but not a bearer of the surname under study and not a presumed ancestor
of Anfilogovs. **Mina Kurbatov syn Efimov** received the land he left;
**Tomay Nefedyev** carried out the transfer; Voin Ochkasov, Nikita Bobrovsky, Vasily
Sindeev, Vasily and Emelyan Mertsalov were present as Mtsensk deti
boyarskie. Their names are kept to understand the document, but no profiles or map points
are created for them.

This entry does not confirm the popular story of the transfer of the Metsnyankins from Mtsensk at the founding of Oryol:
a different man, a different time. The nickname **Metsnyankin**
remains a good search key but is not yet a document about a route.
The map of the family does not change until there is an equally direct formula for an Anfilogov
or for the proven father of one of the lines of 1594/95.

Card: `PUB-KOTKOV-1977-P198-MTSENSK-OREL-MIGRATION-FORMULA`.
[Open full scan of the publication](https://libarch.nmu.org.ua/handle/GenofondUA/48670).

### The Full XLSX Ruled Out Reconstructing "sborn" from Neighbouring Rows

The whole open array of «Дом предков» has been checked, not only the family sample.
The call number **«сборн»** ("sborn", composite) is borne by 3,674 rows: 2,730 are marked with the range 1605–1618,
another 944 with "the 1620s". Within the first block there are exactly 90 Kursk rows,
including the single row **«Анпилогов Федор»** (Anpilogov Fedor). For all 3,674 rows
the additional public fields are empty.

The key methodological result: the whole book is sorted in a common alphabet by
name. Therefore the proximity of the Kursk Fyodor to the Oryol Fyodor of 1640 and to
any other people does not reflect proximity in the manuscript, a common folio or even
the same type of document. The compiler's publication directly says that the compilation
combines different files and different roles: captured servicemen, pomeshchiki
of captured peasants, recipients of money and pomestye additions, and other
mentions.

The rare names from the Kursk sample are still useful as an external "fingerprint": they
can be used to search for a matching published index or manuscript list. But
it is impossible to reconstruct the original file from the order of rows in the table itself. So
Fyodor so far proves neither residence in Kursk, nor service there, nor a route
Oryol → Kursk. No profile, separate Record or map point is created until the
call number and folio are found.

Working card: `LEAD-KURSK-1605-1618-FEDOR-ANPILOGOV`.
[Description of the compilation](https://dompredkov.ru/sluzhilye-lyudi-smuty-1605-1618gg/),
[the open spreadsheet](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1ZSoqya5thvsa2j_4Z-6RSqkCSLejHvy93V0MyxTK4Xk/).

### Fyodor Anfalov of 1624: an Exact Entry, but Not the Solution to the Early Fyodor

A change of source produced an independent Kursk namesake. In the name index to
the edition by S. I. Kotkov and N. S. Kotkova was found: **«Анфалов Федор, курский
жилец 14.22»** ("Anfalov Fedor, Kursk zhilets 14.22"). The full text of the book confirmed not only the surname but also the date and
occupation. On **30 January 1624** «курскои жилец Федор Анфалов» ("the Kursk zhilets Fedor Anfalov") declared at
the customs house **two cartloads of pike**. He was charged a grosh of svalnoe and kherno duty and
one altyn four dengi of toll on the assessed four roubles.

Exact address: **РГАДА, f. 210, Denezhny stol (modern op. 6-zh), kn. 79,
fol. 22**; «Памятники южновеликорусского наречия. Таможенные книги» (Monuments of the South Great Russian Dialect: Customs Books) (Moscow,
1982), p. 106; archival note on p. 132; name index on p. 295.
The page and a check fragment are kept in the Record
`PUB-KOTKOV-KOTKOVA-KURSK-1624-P106-L22-FEDOR-ANFALOV`.

This find does not allow Anfalov to be corrected to Anpilogov. The scholarly
publication twice confidently keeps the form without the syllable **-лог-** (-log-), whereas in
the same book on fol. 78 v. Ivan is printed precisely as **Онпилогов** (Onpilogov). The difference
may reflect two independent namings, or may be a rare
abbreviation; without an image of the manuscript and a kinship formula one cannot choose.
Therefore Fyodor gets a Record as a borderline reading, but not a profile or
a map point.

Nor is he merged with the indexed **Fyodor Anpilogov of 1605–1618**:
the date falls outside the compilation, the family form differs, and the archival address of
the early line is still hidden. However, the new entry gave a useful control:
the search for the original "sborn" must now check not only Anpilogov but also
a possible editorial expansion of Anfalov into a longer surname.

### A Change of Tactics Closed the Name Gap of the Mid-16th Century

Searching for the ready-made surname in service databases yielded no people of the 16th century. Therefore
the unit of search was changed: full publications of provincial
volost and election lists are checked separately for the personal name itself and for forms in `-ов` (-ov), even
if the document is not connected with Oryol. In the **Kurostrov stolbtsy** of the Northern Dvina
this tactic at once yielded a dense sequence.

The apportionment list (razrubny spisok) of **10 January 1549** directly records: **«Онфилоф Нестеров
и с племенником 10 алтын»** ("Onfilof Nesterov with his nephew, 10 altyn"). This is an adult independent payer or the head
of a kin payment group; the nephew's name is not given. In an election list
of the 1550s, in the ten of Vasily Rodionov, there is a damaged line
**«Анфилофей Несте…»** ("Anfilofey Neste…"). The rare name, the beginning of the second naming and the same volost
make identity with Onfilof Nesterov very probable, but the broken text
does not allow the reservation to be removed. In the neighbouring ten of Mitya Suk,
**«Петрок Анфилов»** (Petrok Anfilov) is independently recorded.

Names and meaning. **Onfilof / Anfilofey** here is the man's own personal name;
**Nesterov** is his second, patronymic or family, naming. **Petrok**
is the usual diminutive documentary form of the name Pyotr, not an indication of
a child. **Anfilov** is a form in `-ов`, formed from the name Anfil or a close
short form of the same name nest. Since the word «сын» ("son") is absent from the damaged
list, an immediate patronymic and an already stable family
naming cannot yet be distinguished. The main proven addition here is the living
personal name Onfilof / Anfilofey exactly in the middle of the 16th century. Petrok Anfilov
remains a strong comparative lead, but the link of his second naming specifically
with Anfilof requires a separate philological check.

The same archive has one more line, not yet included in the public core. In
1549 a separate payer **Anfal** is named; in 1578, **«Петр да
Терентей Анфаловы дети»** ("Pyotr and Terentey, Anfal's children"), and in 1580 Terenty is repeated as **«Терентей
Анфалов сын»** ("Terentey, Anfal's son"). The mechanism "father's name → sons' patronymic" is indisputable here, but
the identity of the names Anfal and Anfilof is not proven. Therefore Anfal, Pyotr and Terenty
remain comparative Record mentions without profiles or map
routes.

The find solves one problem and leaves another. The chronological void
between the acts of the 1510s and the family complexes of the 1590s is now filled:
the rare name continued to live in the middle of the century.
But Kurostrov was a chernososhnaya (state peasant) volost on the Northern Dvina, and the documents do not
contain service or resettlement to Oryol. Therefore this is evidence of a mechanism
and one more independent focus of the name, **not a northern route of the Oryol family**.

Photocopies of four pages of the publication are kept in the project's internal evidence
archive. The originals are in the Archive of the St Petersburg Institute of History of the Russian Academy of Sciences: the modern **f. 77
"Kurostrovskaya volostnaya zemskaya izba", 1539–1704**; the 1970 publication
gives the former f. 177. The next precise order is the originals of stolbtsy nos. 3, 5,
8 and 9, with a check of the damaged endings.

Card: `PUB-KOPANEV-1549-1580-KUROSTROV-ANFILOF-NAME`; profile:
`P9742`. Petrok Anfilov is left only as a Record mention. Sources:
A. I. Kopanev, «Куростровские столбцы XVI в.» (The Kurostrov Stolbtsy of the 16th Century),
*Severny arkheografichesky sbornik*, issue I, Vologda, 1970, pp. 398–431;
[open scan of the article](https://www.booksite.ru/fulltext/mater_1/2_10.pdf),
[full transcription](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1520-1540/Kurostrov_stolbcy/text1.htm),
[official list of fonds of the Archive of SPbII РАН](https://spbiiran.ru/spisok-fondov-rs/).

The map received only a point for the documented events of Onfilof Nesterov on
Kurostrov. Petrok Anfilov and the forms Anfal / Anfalov did not receive separate
points. No Kurostrov → Oryol line has been drawn.

### Continued Viewing Gave Onfil of 1592 and Separated Two Similar Stems

A folio-by-folio viewing of the same publication after 1580 gave a direct find almost
at the very boundary of the Oryol survey book. In document no. 21, among the peasants
of the Kurostrov volost, is printed: **«… Осип Трофимов сын, Онфил Иванов,
Василей Вахромеев, Петр Анфалов …»** ("… Osip Trofimov syn, Onfil Ivanov, Vasiley Vakhromeev, Petr Anfalov …"). This is not OCR and not a surname index:
the line has been checked against a photocopy of printed p. 418.

**Names and meaning.** Onfil is the man's personal name. Ivanov in a formula of the late 16th
century means above all "Ivan's son"; one document is not enough to
consider it already a hereditary surname. The form Onfil was not invented during
normalisation: in the Razryad Book of 1556/57 it is independently known as one
of the manuscript readings of the name of a Moscow dyak, alongside Onfim and Olfim. At the same time
the text does not name the canonical name of the Kurostrov Onfil in particular, so
the card does not automatically turn him into either Anfilof or Anfilog.

An internal discrepancy of date was discovered in the publication. The editorial heading
says **7 October 1592**, while the opening formula of the text says **7 September
7101**. The year coincides, the month does not; until the original is viewed both dates are kept
side by side.

At the same time it proved possible not to widen the family series with a false resemblance. The line
**Anfal → Pyotr and Terenty, Anfal's children** can now be traced considerably
longer:

- Anfal — an independent payer of 1549;
- Pyotr and Terenty, Anfal's children — 1578;
- Terenty, Anfal's son — 1580;
- Pyotr Anfalov — 1591 and 1592;
- Petrusha Anfalov — 1600.

This is an excellent 51-year sequence of the mechanism "father's name → children's
patronymic", but the source does not say directly that the Anfal of 1549 was precisely the father of
the brothers of 1578 and that all the later Pyotrs are one man. These identities are probable,
not proven. And still less is this proof of a variant of Anfilof. In
A. F. Bek's etymological dictionary
**Anfalov** is listed separately from **Anfilov**: the first is compared with Aifal /
Anfal, the second with Amphilochius or Anfim. Therefore the Anfalovs are kept in
the literal transcription and comparative analysis, but did not receive public
profiles of the family under study.

For **Onfil Ivanov** a separate Record
`PUB-KOPANEV-1592-KUROSTROV-ONFIL-IVANOV` and profile `P9743` have been created; he is included in
the register of the personal name before 1600. On the map only a local
observation on Kurostrov appears, without a route to Oryol. This is a new point of the name's existence
in 1592, but not a settler found: the document describes a chernososhny
peasant and a money collection for a land court, not service or a transfer to a southern
town.

Sources: A. I. Kopanev, «Куростровские столбцы XVI в.» (The Kurostrov Stolbtsy of the 16th Century), printed pp. 404,
408–409, 417–418, 420; [open scan](https://www.booksite.ru/fulltext/mater_1/2_10.pdf).
Onomastic control: A. F. Bek, *Svyatye ugodniki Bozhii v russkikh
familiyakh: etimologichesky slovar* (God's Holy Saints in Russian Surnames: an Etymological Dictionary). Samara, 2014, p. 23;
[open PDF](https://etrufa.ru/images/book.pdf).

### The Database Revealed Not an Origin but the Limit of Its Own Coverage

The full export of «Дом предков» has now been checked not only by the five beginnings
Anpi / Ampi / Anfi / Onpi / Onfi but also as a whole chronological corpus. A
strict filter was applied: only rows were kept in which **every explicitly stated year
is no later than 1593**. The result was 5,857 rows from twelve combinations
of "archival file — year — territory".

The result changes the meaning of the earlier negative search: **all 5,857 rows
relate exclusively to Novgorod** — the Vodskaya, Derevskaya, Obonezhskaya,
Bezhetskaya and Shelonskaya pyatinas. The largest groups are d. 716 of the Derevskaya
pyatina of 1582 (918 rows), d. 301 of the Vodskaya pyatina of 1582 (856) and
d. 725 of the Obonezhskaya pyatina of 1583 (789). There are no southern service towns in this
layer.

Therefore the absence of the family combinations Mikula → Vasily, Timofey, Karp and
Vasily → Belyay, Osip does indeed exclude them from the **represented
Novgorod complexes**, but says nothing negative about Belyov,
Bolkhov, Bryansk, Karachev, Meshchovsk, Mtsensk, Novosil, Odoev, Ryazhsk,
Ryazan or Tula. The database simply does not show these towns before 1594. Possible places of departure
cannot be ranked by zero results where the source
is absent from the array.

For reproducibility the slice has been fixed with a separate analyser
`scripts/genealogy/analyze-dompredkov-pre1594.py`; the quantitative correction
has been entered in the index and the card
`PUB-DOMPREDKOV-421677-PRE1594-FAMILY-SIGNATURE-AUDIT`. The map does not change:
a geographical gap in the database is not a family route.

[The open database «Дом предков»](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/).

### A Targeted Find: the Lost Oryol Inquiry List

V. N. Kozlyakov described the nationwide "inquiry" (sysk) at the beginning of the reign of Mikhail
Fyodorovich. After the Time of Troubles the Razryad Prikaz searched the towns for old
desyatni and lists, summoned servicemen and, with the help of local assessors,
restored the pomestye and money allotments that people had enjoyed
«при царе Василье» ("under Tsar Vasily"), as well as later increments. The first materials arrived
from 1613, and the main work unfolded from August 1614.

In the list of towns reconstructed by the researcher **Oryol** is directly named.
Next to it are listed almost all our possible donor towns: Belyov, Bolkhov,
Bryansk, Karachev, Meshchovsk, Mtsensk, Novosil, Odoev, Ryazhsk, Ryazan and Tula.
This is not a list of joint resettlement and not an indication of the origin of
Anfilogovs. The significance of the find lies elsewhere: for Oryol there existed a special
reference list capable of linking post-Troubles allotments with earlier
town documents.

The Oryol list itself has not yet been found, but the open full scan of Sukhotin
allowed it to be reconstructed much more precisely. In the preface on p. XXI Oryol
stands in the list of towns from which in 1613–1615 precisely
**inquiry lists (sysknye spiski)** were sent. The desyatni of Bryansk, Kazan, Karachev, Meshchovsk and
the Smolensk money distribution are listed separately. Consequently, the earlier
working name "Oryol inquiry desyatnya" is inaccurate.

A direct extract from the list has also survived. In Sukhotin, pp. 170–171, in the entry for
Grigory Ivanov syn Sotnikov, it is said that in the Razryad they consulted
«по орловскому списку, каков прислал с Орла Петр Данилов во 122-м году
за своею рукою» ("the Oryol list which Petr Danilov sent from Oryol in the year 122 under his own hand"). The year 122 is the period from 1 September 1613 to 31 August
1614. Grigory Sotnikov exactly matches an Oryol pomeshchik
of 1594/95: the name, father, surname and town corporation coincide.
This is the first proven personal continuity through the Time of Troubles in the very list
sought.

#### Names and Meaning

**Pyotr Danilov has received a full name.** On p. 148 of vol. I of the «Дворцовые
разряды» (Court Razryads) for 1613/14 is printed: «На Орле воевода князь Семен княж
Данилов сын Шеховской; и князю Семену велено быть к Москве, а на Орле
велено быть Петру Прокофьеву сыну Данилову» ("In Oryol the voivode Prince Semen, son of Prince Danila, Shekhovskoy; and Prince Semen was ordered to be in Moscow, and in Oryol Petr Prokofyev syn Danilov was ordered to be"). A. P. Barsukov on p. 162
repeats Pyotr Prokofyevich Danilov in the series of Oryol voivodes of 1614,
and on p. 364 directly refers to this page of the «Дворцовые разряды».

Thus the list was sent by **the Oryol voivode Pyotr Prokofyev syn
Danilov**, in modern reading Pyotr Prokofyevich Danilov. This is not Pyotr
Danilovich Islenyev, who appears in Sukhotin in a different service
context. Danilov is not declared an ancestor of Anfilogovs and does not receive
a separate family profile; his significance is his role as the established
official sender of the document.

**Meaning for Anfilogovs.** The list of the voivode's chancery really
remembered people of the Oryol complement of 1594/95 and their allotments under Tsar Vasily.
So Vasily, Timofey, Karp and the other
Anfilogovs could have been in it, even if they did not belong to the narrow group of chetvertchiki.
But no surviving extract yet names the surname. One should search for
the seven paternal lines — Mikula, Vasily, Persha, Timofey, Nechay, Pakhom and
Terenty — together with the family variants.

Kozlyakov's reference to **РГАДА, f. 210, Moskovsky stol, stolbtsy, d. 11,
fol. 137; d. 12, fols. 357, 384, 400** relates to a composite list of inquiry
materials of various towns. It does not assign folios to towns and therefore cannot
be called the exact address of the Oryol list. For Oryol there is so far only one exact
piece of evidence — Sukhotin's extract with the words about Pyotr Danilov's list.
The search formula remains targeted: **Oryol; the year 122; Pyotr Prokofyev syn
Danilov; a list under his hand; the voivode's reports and appendices; certificates of
allotments under Tsar Vasily**, but files 11 and 12 are checked as a general corpus, not
as a pre-established Oryol call number.

A check of the old palace numbering gave a confirmed address result.
Sukhotin reported that the fragment he published was in the Moscow Section of
the General Archive of the Ministry of the Imperial Court as **stolbets no. 37573**.
Direct viewing of [the official sheet of the РГАДА inventory](http://rgada.info/opisi/396-opis_1-24/0024.jpg)
located it as **РГАДА, f. 396, op. 1, ch. 24, d. 37573, carton 64,
522 fols.** On sheet 33 of the inventory the heading reads: «Роспись о выдаче жалованья
с 121 г. по 126 г. разным людям за Смоленскую службу и за раны» ("Register of the payment of salary from the year 121 to the year 126 to various people for the Smolensk service and for wounds"). That is
1612/13–1617/18.

The earlier reconstruction "ch. 1" was mistaken and has now been corrected. Part 1
contains stolbtsy of category I with a different numbering; no. 37573 is in part 24,
among the stolbtsy of category II. Therefore Kozlyakov's reference "f. 396, op. 24,
d. 38503" does not conflict with the structure of the fonds but points in abbreviated form to the same
section. In the official digital catalogue it is given as **op. 1, ch. 24**.

It is important to distinguish two documents. File 37573 is a large composite remnant
of a kormlenaya (pay) book, within which the extract about Grigory Sotnikov has already been found.
Pyotr Danilov's Oryol list itself may not have survived as a separate unit.
Sukhotin kept the original folio marks: Voin Vorobyov is printed from
**fol. 33**, Grigory Sotnikov from **fol. 145**, Mikhail Zhilin from **fol. 193**.
So the Oryol extracts do not lie in a single block but are scattered through the book.
The first cheap and precise order can now be made for **fol. 145 with its verso and
neighbouring folios**: it is there that the extract with the reference to Pyotr
Danilov's list is located. Folios 33 and 193 are needed as control points of the structure. After that
all 522 folios still need to be checked, including those misordered and not
published by Sukhotin, for the variants
Anfilogov/Anpilogov and for the names of the seven paternal lines.

The official РГАДА collection of unique storage units was checked with a separate
exact query **37573**. The service answers "No records found": the file is not
included in the open folio-by-folio viewer. This closes the false path of searching for
a "hidden" link to the frames, but does not mean the file itself is lost. To read
the unpublished remnant one needs to order a digital copy or work in the reading
room; the order should state the full call number, carton 64 and the extent of 522 folios.

The razryad entry on the appointments stands in the year section after the date **25 April
1614**. It does not give the exact day of Danilov's arrival in Oryol, but it narrows
the chancery context: the list for the year 122 was sent by him in the final
part of that service year or soon after his appointment.

The cards `PUB-KOZLYAKOV-2018-OREL-SYSKNY-LIST-TRACE` and
`PUB-SUKHOTIN-1912-OREL-QUARTERERS-AUDIT` have been updated. Photocopies are kept of
Sukhotin, pp. XXI, 47, 126, 170–171; Barsukov, pp. 162 and 364; and the spread of
the «Дворцовые разряды», pp. 147–148. The map does not change: the document does not report
the route of a bearer of the family series under study.

[Open scan of Kozlyakov's book](https://djvu.online/file/TjLCjL0xo3U9E),
[full scan of Sukhotin at the ГПИБ](https://elib.shpl.ru/ru/nodes/4821-vyp-9-chetvertchiki-smutnogo-vremeni-1604-1617-1912),
[«Дворцовые разряды» for 1613/14](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVII/1600-1620/Dvorc_razryady_1/4.htm),
[publication of the surviving northern «Книга сыскная» (Inquiry Book)](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVII/1600-1620/Sysk_spisok_detej_bojar_1613/pred.phtml).

### The New Tactic Gave Family Groups: Oryol → Kursk, 1697

The old open database «Служивые люди XVII века» (Servicemen of the 17th Century) was checked not as a simple
surname index but through its fields for notes, kinship, folios and former place of
service. This revealed the contents of two Kursk files that are not visible in
the modern short index: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12, d. 1491 and 1559**.

The densest group is on fols. 1057–1076 of file 1559. Ivan Fomin syn,
Terenty Gurov syn and Danila Stepanov syn Anpilogov spoke directly of leaving
**Korchakovsky stan of Oryol district**. Terenty dated the move
to the year 7200, that is 1691/92; Ivan and Danila counted about ten
years before the skazka (sworn statement). Dmitry Denisov syn named the same stan, but the year
7206 given by the database contradicts the general date of the file and is left as requiring a scan.

Other skazki preserve the names of Oryol fathers: Foma served as a reiter,
Nikifor as a pikeman, Efim/Elfim, Sergey, Denis and Alfim in the Oryol
service circle. On fols. 943–944 of file 1491 the reiters Samoyla and Ignat Gurovy
deti Anpilogovy (Gury's children Anpilogov) are independently marked «сшел с Орла» ("left Oryol"). On fol. 1348 Ivan Antonov
syn reported leaving Oryol district, and on fol. 1382 Afanasy Grigoryev
syn named another route: **Kromy district → a Kursk pomestye**.

#### Names and Meaning

Here for the first time not lone surnames but distinguishable family signatures are visible:
Gury's children; the sons of the Oryol servicemen Foma, Nikifor, Efim/Elfim,
Sergey, Denis, Ivan, Alfim and Anton; a separate Kromy branch of Afanasy
Grigoryevich. Especially valuable are the rare combinations **Mikhail Alfimovich** and
**Terenty Guryevich**: a reverse search by them in the Oryol reiter and
review lists will be more precise than by the surname alone.

The find so far explains the dispersal of the Oryol core outwards, not its
appearance before 1594/95. But it changes the further search: instead of one
unknown "Anpilogov ancestor" one must look for specific paternal groups and
compare them with the Oryol books of 1640–1687, and then trace the chains
back to the lines of Mikula, Vasily, Persha, Timofey, Nechay, Pakhom and Terenty.

A common Record has been created,
`PUB-SLUZHILYE-1697-OREL-KURSK-ANPILOGOV-MIGRATIONS`, with thirteen named
profiles and six routes of medium confidence. They are honestly marked as
index-based: scans of the folios have not yet been found. The full extracted composition of the families
and the literal notes are kept in the index
`SLUZHILYE-1697-ANPILOGOV-MIGRATIONS`.

[Publication about file 1491](https://dompredkov.ru/skazki-kurchan-1697-goda-f-210-op-12-d-1491/),
[page of the open database and the discussion](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5/64941/p1878181.htm).

### Oryol Before the Resettlement: the Review of Neplyuev's Regiment, 1689

The next search was carried out not by a single surname but by a rare combination of
**name + town + nearest date**. In the open index to the discharge
review list of Leonty Neplyuev's regiment for 1689, there still stand in Oryol
Vasily, Dementy, Denis, two Ivans, Kuzma, Merkul, Nikifor, Sergey,
Terenty and Foka Anpilogov, Akim Anfilogov and Filimon Onfilogov. A separate
line is rendered as **«Аепилогов Самойла»** (Aepilogov Samoyla) — a probable misreading or
OCR error, which cannot be silently corrected without the manuscript.

#### Names and Meaning

The main find is **Terenty**. The Kursk skazka of 1697 calls him
Terenty Gurov syn Anpilogov and reports in the first person that he left
Korchakovsky stan of Oryol district in the year 7200, that is in 1691/92.
The review of 1689 names the Oryol Terenty Anpilogov. The rare name,
surname, town and an almost adjacent date coincide. Until the patronymic is read on the folio the identity
remains highly probable, but the sequence is already very dense:
**Oryol, 1689 → departure from Korchakovsky stan, 1691/92 → Kursk district, 1697**.

Samoyla Guryevich is directly marked in the Kursk skazka with the words «сшел с Орла» ("left Oryol").
The probable line of 1689 narrows his move too, to the interval **1689–1697**.
Here confidence is lower because of the form «Аепилогов» and the absence of a patronymic.

The matches of Denis, Sergey and Nikifor are also important: the Kursk skazki of 1697
name the deceased Oryol reiter Denis, the deceased Oryol reiter Sergey
and the Oryol pikeman Nikifor. But one name is not enough to declare
the men of 1689 to be these fathers. For now they are targeted candidates for reading the folios,
not ready-made kinship links.

The inventory explains the military context: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 5, d. 68** —
the discharge review list of Neplyuev's regiment, which had returned from the second Crimean
campaign; the required part occupies fols. 185–534. A Record has been created,
`PUB-DOMPREDKOV-1689-OREL-ANPILOGOVY-NEPLYUEV`. No new route has been
added to the map: Terenty's direction is already documented by his own skazka,
and the entry of 1689 adds the initial event and narrows the date.

[Open publication of the 1689 list](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5703/135781/p5389207.htm),
[description of the file](https://dompredkov.ru/smotrennyj-spisok-polka-leontiya-neplyueva-1689g/).

### Why Akim "Anfinogenov(?)" of 1697 Has Not Yet Become a Separate Person

A live recheck of the full «Дом предков» database refined the weight of the doubtful
reading. For the exact form **Anfinogenov** only two are found among the 421,095 entries:
Rodion in Kozlov in 1703 and Akim in Oryol in 1697. Meanwhile, on the page
of the Oryol file itself the compiler confidently lists **18 lines of
Anpilogovs**, and puts Akim separately as **«Анфиногенов(?)»** ("Anfinogenov(?)"). Consequently,
this is not thoughtless search normalisation: the person who read the manuscript distinguished the form
Anpilogov in the same file and deliberately marked Akim's surname with a question
mark.

This strengthens, but does not prove, the version of a separate Akim Anfinogenov. The
open publication gives no folio, patronymic or family surroundings. The author reports
that 2,721 entries went into the index, about 15–20 per cent of the upper parts of the folios
are poorly preserved, and about a hundred entries were not entered because of the quality of the photographs;
only the surname and first name are given publicly. Therefore the right decision remains
asymmetrical: a separate Record of the doubtful reading already exists, but a profile
and a map point will appear only after a scan. If the original confirms
Anfinogenov, the man does not belong to the project's family core; if it shows
Anpilogov/Anfilogov, the line must be linked with Akim Ivanovich and his Oryol–Livny
chain.

[The open database](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/),
[description of the Oryol file of 1697](https://dompredkov.ru/orlovskie-dvoryane-deti-boyarskie-rejtary-draguny-i-dr/).

### The File on the Restoration of Oryol Must Not Be Cut Off by Estate

The status of **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12, ch. 1, d. 65** — documents on
the building of Oryol in 1635–1636 after the Lithuanian devastation — has been clarified. The article by
I. E. Kushelev deals above all with the town streltsy, Cossacks,
gunners (pushkari) and zatinshchiki, and deliberately does not include the district deti
boyarskie in its reconstruction. But this limitation of the author's sample cannot be transferred to a family
search through the whole file. An Anpilogov could have ended up in the pribor (recruited) category, changed his type of
service or got into a mixed list.

Therefore the file is not negatively checked and has been moved into mandatory
name-by-name viewing. Priority blocks: **fols. 156–157, 189, 268, 305–308 and
321–325 v.**; for context **fols. 131, 322, 378–379 and 431** are also needed.
One should search for the forms **Anpilogov, Anfilogov, Onpilogov, Onfilogov,
Ampilogov, Anpilov, Ampilov, Anpliogov**, without filtering the lines in advance by rank.

A control set of personal names has also appeared for collation. The open index to
the Oryol list of deti boyarskie of 1640 — **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 14,
d. 114** — contains eight family lines: **Aleksey, Afanasy, two
Vasilys, Grigory, Ivan, Kuzma and Fyodor Anpilogov**. Therefore in d. 65 one must
search not only for graphic variants of the surname but also for these names without the surname:
in an inquiry paper a man could be recorded by first name and patronymic, by nickname or in
another service category. A match of one name will prove nothing yet,
but a name together with a patronymic, a former town and a later line of 1640
can give a full identification.

The chronological boundary is maintained: this complex describes the search for people
who dispersed after the destruction of Oryol in 1611–1615 and their return in
1635–1636. It does not prove the family's departure before the founding of Oryol in 1566.
But an individual entry is able to name a bearer of the surname, his service status at the time
and his town of temporary residence. Therefore a route on the map
will appear only after such a line is read, not from a general list of towns.

[Publication by I. E. Kushelev with archival references](https://e-notabene.ru/view_article.php?aurora=0&id_article=69870&logged=0&nb=1).

One more false shortcut has been checked. The open ВГД index does indeed
have a detailed description of **"d. 65"**, but this is **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 65** —
the Starodub desyatnya of 1631 on 332 folios. The required Oryol complex has
a different full call number: **f. 210, op. 12, ch. 1, d. 65**. A coincidence of file number does not
mean a coincidence of manuscript; the description found and the microfilm of the Starodub
desyatnya cannot be passed off as access to the Oryol file. No open images of the required
folios have yet been found.

The coverage of the large open database itself was also checked. An exact query
**«210-12-65»** across all **421,095** rows returned zero. This does not mean that
there are no Anpilogovs in the file: the file itself is not in the database. Therefore repeating surname
queries in this table is a dead end here and has been stopped; the next move is
only the original, an archival copy or a separate index specifically to
**f. 210, op. 12, ch. 1, d. 65**.

On the other hand, the general query **«Анпи»** (Anpi) gave another working tactic — successive
slices of one Oryol service circle: **1620/21** (Vaska, Mark), **1626**
(two Oleshkas), **1628** (Afonka), **1629** (Vaska, Ivashka, Mark, Oleshka),
**1640** (eight lines), **1642** (eight lines) and **1648** (five lines).
Now d. 65 must be read between these slices as a missing link, checking
not only the surname but the already known full formulas: **Vasily Dmitrievich,
Aleksey Vasilyevich, Mark Karpovich, Afanasy Osipovich**. A match of the full
name, rank, relative and former town will be able to link a man even where
the surname is omitted or distorted.

In parallel, a full open photocopy of the neighbouring review book
**РГАДА, f. 1157, op. 1, d. 9 for 1686/87** has been found. In a public
Yandex.Disk collection the town part **fols. 871–940 v.** and
Oryol district **fols. 941–1118 v.** are marked separately. Reduced copies of the whole town part
have been viewed; the entry for Gur has not yet been located, because the name index
does not give the folio. This is no longer a search for an unknown file: the original has been found and
is accessible; it remains to match the specific line with the folio. Until then
no random spread is substituted into the Record and no patronymic is invented.

[Open collection of scans](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ)
(path inside the catalogue: «Орловский уезд / Другие документы / РГАДА ф. 1157 /
РГАДА 1157-1-9»).

### Two Different Recruitments to Kursk in 1596 Must Not Be Merged

It has been possible to establish the origin of a widespread retelling about the Bolkhov and
Kromy absentees (netchiki). The text was posted on the official information server
of the Kursk Oblast administration; a dated copy of it from
20 March 2009 is now available. According to a tsar's charter, in August or early September 1596
the voivode Ivan Polev and the head Nelyub Ogarev reported that dvoryane and deti
boyarskie from **Bolkhov and Kromy**, assigned to serve in the new Kursk, had not arrived
or had left service. The reply ordered them to be found, returned and garrison
service to be ensured.

This is not the same source as Larionov's report of **transferees (perevedentsy) from Mtsensk and
Oryol**. Larionov refers to the stroennye (construction) books; the Kursk historical note refers
to a separate tsar's charter. Consequently, in 1596 at least two channels of
manning operated: an organised transfer from Oryol and
Mtsensk, and the assignment of pomeshchiki from Bolkhov and Kromy. Volunteers from other
towns formed yet another category. None of the available texts so far
names Anfilogovs/Anpilogovs among them.

The archival call number of the charter is not given in the note. A search for the exact phrases, the names of Polev and
Ogarev and the combination Bolkhov—Kromy in open publications and recognised inventories of
РГАДА did not yield the text of the charter. Therefore no route of a specific family branch is
created, and the find remains a mechanism of settlement and an address for archival search.

The digital availability of a candidate source for the Kursk line of
Fyodor Anpilogov of 1605–1618 — **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 24, d. 5** — was checked separately. In
the ГИС УИАД interface, inventories 1, 4, 9, 12, 18, 19, 22
and 25 are presented for fond 210; there is no inventory 24. This closes the possibility of reading the 138 folios
of the file online immediately, but does not rule out ordering the original or a copy from РГАДА.

[Surviving copy of the Kursk historical note](https://www.krug-shar.ru/forum/viewtopic.php?f=87&t=5137&view=print),
[description of the presumed file 210-24-5](https://inv.velikie.org/archive/rgada/210/24/).

### A Namesake Historian Left an Exact Archival Address

The link of the 1981 book with the historian **Grigory Nikolaevich Anpilogov**
turned out to be not merely a bibliographic coincidence. Anpilogov (1902–1987),
a native of the village of Vyazovoe in Shchigry district of Kursk province, was one of its
compilers and professionally published documents of the late 16th — early 17th
century. In his 1979 article «О городе Курске X–XVI вв.» (On the Town of Kursk in the 10th–16th Centuries) he directly stated that
the stroelnaya (construction) book of Kursk of the voivode Ivan Polev and the dozornaya book of 1617
burned in the Moscow fire of 1626, but that **he had found separate extracts from these books
in the archive**.

Most important, a reference is given: **ЦГАДА, f. 210, Razryadny prikaz, Prikazny stol,
stb. 34, fols. 44–45**. This is the first exact archival address of a surviving document
connected with the fate of the Kursk stroennaya book.

The full official inventory of part 1 of inventory 13 has been checked page by page. The re-numbering
turned out to be direct: scan 54 bears the heading **«Ст. 34»** ("Stb. 34"), that is, the old
stolbets number has been kept as the number of the modern storage unit. The full order
address: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 13, ch. 1, d. 34, fols. 43–46**. Two neighbouring folios
have been added so as not to lose the beginning and end of Anpilogov's document.

The file contains 675 folios and is dated 1628–1634. Folios 44–45 belong to
the first section, "Documents on servicemen". The start of the file after the Moscow
fire of 1626 makes plausible a certificate about the lost books or an extract
drawn up during a subsequent service inquiry. But whether the folios contain
only the news of the fire or also the literal text of the old books can be decided
only from the four scans.

G. N. Anpilogov is not declared a relative of Oryol Anfilogovs on the basis of
the surname alone. His role here is different: he is a researcher who saw archival
extracts and later joined the authors of the Kursk chronicle. Therefore it is his
working references, article, possible preparatory materials and correspondence with
Kursk archivists that become a new path to the call number of the 1596 charter.

[G. N. Anpilogov's article with archival references](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVII/1620-1640/Kursk_gramoty/text.php),
[official inventory of part 1 of inventory 13 of fond 210](http://rgada.info/poisk/index2.php?str=210-opis_13-1),
[official biographical note of the Institute of Russian History of the Russian Academy of Sciences](https://iriran.ru/sites/default/files/2025-02/%D0%98%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%82%D1%83%D1%82_%D1%80%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%81%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B9_%D0%B8%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D1%80%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%A0%D0%90%D0%9D_1936%E2%80%932021%D0%B3%D0%B3_2021.pdf).

### G. N. Anpilogov's Personal Reader's File in the Kursk Archive

In the official inventory of the combined reading-room fonds of the State
Archive of Kursk Oblast a separate file has been found: **ГАКО, f. R-2969, op. 7,
d. 1478, "Anpilogov Grigory Nikolaevich"**, 20 folios. Two dates — 24 June
1960 and 23 July 1968 — show at least two of his visits to
the archive.

The preface of the inventory explains the contents of such files. Besides the researcher's application and
questionnaire, they include **requisitions for the issue of specific archival
materials** and **sheets recording the issue of copies and extracts from documents**. Therefore file
1478 is able to give not an abstract biography of the historian but a list of the Kursk
sources he actually worked with and, possibly, traces of extracts issued
to him. The twenty folios themselves are not openly available, so it cannot yet
be asserted that copies of ancient texts are enclosed in the file.

This is the second targeted order after РГАДА: to obtain the file in full and copy out all
the archival call numbers from the requisitions of 1960 and 1968. Especially check any references
to the founding of Kursk, Ivan Polev, Oryol, Mtsensk, Bolkhov, Kromy, the stroennye
books and servicemen of the late 16th century.

[Official inventory of ГАКО, f. R-2969, op. 7](https://archive.rkursk.ru/sites/default/files/Opisi/2_-_sovet/R-2969%20-%207.pdf).

### A New Tactic: Not Towns in General, but Exact Files Across All Service Categories

The public version of Aleksandr Shatokhin's database «Служилые люди XVII века» (Servicemen of
the Seventeenth Century) was downloaded and checked in full for the forms Anpil-, Anfil-, Onpil-
and Onfil-. It contains no names, folios or photocopies, but it gives dozens of exact archival
addresses. The earliest new non-Oryol address is **Belgorod, 1633, siege roster: РГАДА, f. 210,
op. 12, d. 50, one occurrence of Anpilogov**. Then come the Kursk desyatnya (muster roll) of
1636, the Belgorod files of 1639–1648, Khotmyzhsk, Oboyan and a broad network in the second half
of the century.

This changes the method. Bearers of the surname appear not only in the desyatni of deti
boyarskie but also in skazki (sworn statements), review, estimate, allocation and inspection
books and petitions. So the file of 1635–1636 with streltsy, Cossacks and gunners remains a
priority: rank or section of a document must not be a preliminary filter. All variants of the
surname are searched for among all participants.

At the same time, the earliest named non-Oryol trace so far after the cadastre of 1594/95 has
been found. In her article «Село Сажное — самое крупное поселение Белгородского уезда XVII века»
(The Village of Sazhnoe, the Largest Settlement of Belgorod District in the Seventeenth Century),
E. V. Krivtsova lists among 42 holders of pomestya (service estates) in 1616 **Nikita Efimov syn
Anpilogov** (Nikita, son of Efim, Anpilogov). A profile and a point for Sazhnoe have been created,
but without a route: the article does not give Nikita's earlier town. The primary archival call
number and folio still have to be established; separately, it is being checked whether the name
Nikita might have arisen from a reading of Mikifor, since in 1646 Mikifor Efimov syn Onpilogov is
documented in the Belgorod circle.

An important correction to the Livny hypothesis: Krivtsova's article lists eight Livny guides of
1599, but Anpilogov is not among them. She compares part of the neighbouring surnames of the
Sazhnoe circle with Livny, not Nikita himself. Consequently, the entry of 1616 proves a presence in
Belgorod, but neither a route Livny → Belgorod nor a link with the Oryol core.

Finally, within the mixed file **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6v, d. 10 for 1653–1662** the open name index
does indeed name **Zinov Onpilogov, Belgorod**. A separate index profile has been created for him.
This is direct confirmation of the remark about service categories: lines with the surname may lie
precisely in such heterogeneous collections.

[Public database of seventeenth-century files](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/DmUeZAUG4DM0yw),
[quotation from E. V. Krivtsova's article](https://www.klassika.ru/2020/03/22/1002),
[open index of the file of 1653–1662](https://dompredkov.ru/umershie-ubitye-i-vzyatye-v-plen-dvoryane-i-deti-boyarskie-1653-1662gg-familii-k-p/).
## 28 August 2026: Two New Lines — Kamenny Zaton and Derivative Oryol Extracts

A consolidated name search across open archival indexes turned up a new item:
**РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6d, d. 186, 1704 — «Анпилогов» (Anpilogov), ll. 117, 187**.
The official electronic inventory clarifies the content of the file: it is a 1,021-folio collection
of documents from 1703–1704 — lists, charters, reports (otpiski), petitions, decrees, news,
interrogations and other papers on the administration and defence of Kamenny Zaton. Thus the
surname gains a new address in the records of a distant southern fortress. But this is not yet a
route: the index gives neither a first name nor a town of origin, and the file heading does not
guarantee that the person was personally present in Kamenny Zaton. The two folios may refer to one
person or to two different people. Priority order: ll. 117–117 v. and 187–187 v.; look especially
for the formulas «из города» ("from the town"), «прислан» ("sent"), «прибыл» ("arrived"),
«служил» ("served").

A check of the full open database «Дом предков» (Dom predkov) did not reveal these folios. At the
same time, the trap of file 210-14-502 for 1620–1621 was checked again: the machine database still
shows «Анпилогов Марк» (Anpilogov Mark) and «Онпилогов Илюшка» (Onpilogov Ilyushka), but the full
index and the digital collection we examined earlier exclude these two lines. The genuine target
entries of the file are Vasily Dmitrievich Anpilogov (l. 67), Aleksey Vasilyevich Anfilokhov
(l. 94 v.) and Ivan Vasilyevich Anfilokhov (l. 109). The new item of 1704 must not be joined to the
false machine lines of 1620/21.

In the open inventory **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 78, pt. 2** two addresses were found for switching the
search tactic from the surname itself to late copies of land evidence:

- **d. 2227, 3 folios** — an extract (vypis) drawn up no earlier than 1675–1676 from the
  cadastral survey book (pistsovaya kniga) of Oryol district of 1625–1626 by Ivan Kutuzov and Ivan
  Korovin concerning lands in Korchakov stan. The Anpilogov surname is not in the heading, but the
  very survival of a separate extract proves that derivative copies of the book we need exist.
- **d. 2223, 80 folios, 19 August 1671 — 18 January 1672** — a court case of Artemy Kamynin
  against Semyon and Yakov Trunov over pomestya in Oryol district. It is especially interesting
  because in the ГАОО file of 1794–1809 Kamynin is already in dispute with the Trunovs and the
  Anpilogovs. This is not yet proven continuity of the dispute, but a strong candidate for an
  earlier chain of land acts.

The cards `PUB-RGADA-210-6D-186-1704-KAMENNY-ZATON-ANPILOGOV-INDEX`,
`PUB-RGADA-1209-78-2-D2227-OREL-1625-DERIVATIVE-EXTRACT` and
`PUB-RGADA-1209-78-2-D2223-KAMYNIN-TRUNOV-LAND-CASE` have been created. For Kamenny Zaton the
profile and the map are postponed until the originals are read; for the two files of fond 1209
these are search cards without profiles of other people and without family points.

### The Open РГАДА Inventories Checked at Three Levels

The public search of the «Великие описи» (Great Inventories) could be checked not only through the
pages of individual inventories but across the whole РГАДА database and across three separate
layers: file headings, enclosed documents and entries. The roots **Ampil-, Anpil-, Onpil-, Anfil-,
Onfil-** were covered, the full forms of the surname, the personal names **Анфилоф, Анфилог,
Онфилоф, Онфилог, Амфилох** (Anfilof, Anfilog, Onfilof, Onfilog, Amfilokh), seven patronymic
formulas of the Oryol core, Onokha Deev syn Krivolapov and the surnames of the Bazdyrevo
neighbourhood cluster.

No independent catalogue item of the target series before 1600 was found. Onokha Deevich
Krivolapov also did not reappear outside the entry of 1594/95. This closes a new typescript corpus
of headings, but not the content of the files themselves: the compilers of an inventory usually
bring out only a few main names, and an early surname may lie inside an unread extract.

### Pavel Onakhin Did Not Turn Out to Be a Continuator of Anpilog Onanyin

One inventory item at first looked like a possible continuation of the main local hypothesis.
**РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 78, pt. 2, d. 2217** holds a three-folio, dilapidated «Запись о поместьях
Никиты Куракова, Павла Ивановича Онахина и Михаила Борисовича Селехова в [Орловском] уезде»
(Record of the pomestya of Nikita Kurakov, Pavel Ivanovich Onakhin and Mikhail Borisovich Selekhov
in [Oryol] district), dated no earlier than 1643–1644. The form `Онахин` could have seemed a
mistaken rendering of `Онаньин` — the surname of the very Oryol family in which Anpilog Ivanovich
lived.

A check against the primary scan removed this possibility. In the open digital copy of the
estimate book (smetnaya kniga) of Oryol of 1642, **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 260, l. 386**, one
reads **«Павел Иванов сын Онахин»** (Pavel Ivanov syn Onakhin); the name index renders the initial
vowel as `Анахин`. On **l. 403 v. of the same book** there is a separate entry **«Ивашко Офремов
сын Онаньин»** (Ivashko Ofremov syn Onanyin). So the forms Onakhin/Anakhin and Onanyin coexist in
one Oryol documentary complex and are not automatic variants of one surname.

The identity of Pavel in the two files is further secured by a second participant. In the heading
of d. 2217 Mikhail Borisovich Selekhov is named together with him; in the estimate of 1642 the same
Mikhail Borisov syn Selekhov is on l. 376. The full name, the town, the narrow time interval and
the connected person all coincide. Therefore file 2217 is excluded from the line of Anpilog
Ivanovich Onanyin and from the priorities for the origin of Anpilogovs. It remains a useful
negative control, showing the cost of a single consonant in a search through archival indexes.
The full spread of l. 386 and two comparative fragments have been saved. The Record
`PUB-RGADA-1642-1644-PAVEL-ONAKHIN-ONANIN-CONTROL` has been created. No profiles of other people
and no new point on the family map are created.

[Open inventory of file 2217](https://inv.velikie.org/archive/rgada/1209/78-2/#case1121286),
[publication of the index to the estimate of 1642](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5703/135781/p5369181.htm#pp5369181),
[open folder of photocopies](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ).

This is exactly how a new address appeared. In **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 78, pt. 2, d. 2253** the
following lie together on four folios:

- an extract from the survey books concerning pomestya in Kamensky and Taichukovsky stany of Oryol
  district;
- a memorandum (pamyat) from the Razryad (Military Service Office) with information on the
  pomestye oklady (land entitlements) of Oryol men;
- an address note on the reverse to Luka Antonovich Tarsukov.

This is not a found list of resettlers: the query «сведен Орел» ("transferred Oryol") was triggered
by the word **«сведеньями»** ("information") and not by the formula «сведён на Орёл» ("transferred
to Oryol"). But the composition of the packet itself is very important. Survey land and Razryad
service are already joined here in one small unit from the scattered archival remnants — precisely
the kind of derivative document that could have preserved a reference to a lost early book.

After the initial find the full catalogue heading was opened, and the priority had to be lowered.
The collection is dated **no earlier than 1687–1688**; the survey extract concerns the named holders
of Kamensky and Taichukovsky stany — Pyotr Bersenev, Gavrila Boev, the Denisovs, the Zelyonys and
the Kofanovs. Korchakov stan and Anpilogovs are absent from the heading. So the four folios
remain a useful sample of late derivative documentation, but they cease to be the first order for
the family's origin. Ranked above them are the Korchakov extracts of 1625–1626 and personal files
with a possible formula of earlier service.

The card
`PUB-RGADA-1209-78-2-D2253-OREL-RAZRYAD-POMESTYE-MEMORY` has been created.
[Open inventory of file 2253](https://inv.velikie.org/archive/rgada/1209/78-2/#case1122086).
Profiles and the map do not change: no bearer of the researched series and no individual route
have been named yet.

### Oskol Anpilogovs: a Household Found Beyond the Earlier Monastery Statement

A repeated check of A. P. Nikulov's full edition revealed an important detail of the already known
entry **РГАДА, f. 1209, kn. 344, l. 91 v.** In the supplementary search of 16 December 1646 there
is the entry: **«Двор — крестьянин Андрюшка Петров сын Анпилогов, у него сын Ивашко»** ("Household
— the peasant Andryushka Petrov syn Anpilogov, he has a son Ivashko"). These are Andrey Petrovich
Anpilogov and his son Ivan Andreevich, who lived under the St Nicholas Monastery at Kholkovo
gorodishche in Orlinsky stan of Oskol district.

The meaning of the entry is more precise than the simple word "census". The scribe Afanasy
Bobarykin and the clerk (podyachy) Ivan Gavrilov were looking for peasants and bobyli (landless
cottars) **«сверх их прежних сказок»** ("beyond their earlier statements"). The summary reports that
beyond the earlier statement 24 peasant and two bobyl households were found under the monastery.
But Andrey cannot be counted as a new settler: for three genuinely new households the book
separately writes **«поселился после прежнего письма»** ("settled after the earlier survey"), and for
Anpilogovs there is no such formula. So what is proven is an under-recorded monastery household,
but not the date of its origin.

On pp. 159–161 Nikulov separately discusses the geography of the settlement of the Oskol region:
among the documented places of departure of the first settlers he names Tula, Dedilov, Dankov and
Oryol. This makes the hypothesis of an Oryol origin of the Oskol branch historically possible, but
Andrey's line does not give an earlier town. The route **Oryol → Oskol** is not drawn, and kinship
with the landholders of 1594/95 is not assigned.

The new exact target is not a general search through Oskol but the **earlier statement of the St
Nicholas Monastery** against which the search of 1646 was checked, and the census of Oskol district
of 1643. In them one must look for Andrey, Ivan and Andrey's possible father, Pyotr Anpilogov, as
well as formulas of bringing in, transfer and earlier place of residence. The card
`PUB-NIKULOV-OSKOL-1646-P45-L91OB-ANPILOGOVY` and both profiles have been updated; the confirmed
household has been added to the people's geography without an unproven migration line.

[Open full publication by Nikulov](https://www.osk-cbs.ru/books/Nikulov_Oskolskie_drevnosti_3.pdf).

### Ponkratko Gains a Patronymic: There Were Two Sons of Nechay in Dedilov

A repeated check not only of the folio-by-folio transcription but also of A. N. Lepyokhin's name
index to the same Dedilov book of 1588/89 refined the family label of the main candidate. The
literal household entry still reads: **«десяцкой Иванко Нечаев да на ево земле ... новоприборной
казак брат ево Понкратко»** ("the desyatsky [foreman of ten] Ivanko Nechaev, and on his land ... the
newly recruited Cossack his brother Ponkratko"). However, the index distributes them among service
headings as **«Нечаев Иванко»** (Nechaev Ivanko) among the desyatskie and **«Нечаев Понкратко»**
(Nechaev Ponkratko) among the newly recruited Cossacks.

This is not a new verbatim surname line. The compiler quite properly restored Ponkrat's patronymic
from the direct word **«брат»** ("brother"): the modern family reading is **Ivan Nechaevich and
Pankrat Nechaevich**, sons of Nechay. Such a restoration is more reliable than a simple coincidence
of Ivan + Nechay, because it gives a rare pair of brothers and at the same time different service
positions within one Dergilevskaya sotnya (hundred).

The boundary remains strict. Neither Ivan nor Pankrat is called Anpilogov in the Dedilov book; in
the Oryol entry of 1595 no brother Pankraty is named alongside Ivan. So this is a **strengthened
candidate**, not a proven route. The test interval has not changed: after the Dedilov description of
1588/89 and before Ivan's new Oryol exemption grant of 20 January 1595. What is needed is the
original l. 36 v. and an outgoing Dedilov extract about the storehouse (klet), household, enrolment
(verstanye) or transfer of both brothers. The map does not change.

[Name index to the Dedilov book](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1262/80101/p2486473.htm),
[folio-by-folio transcription of the book](https://document.wikireading.ru/hPk6fadeA0).

#### The Original Dedilov Manuscript Is Already Digitised

The path to verification has now become exact. In the ГИС УИАД catalogue a separate file was
found: **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 124**: the pripravochnaya (reference) book of Dedilov of
1588–1589, a seventeenth-century copy, **174 folios**. The official identifier of the digital card is
`10000393439014`. So there is no longer any need to look for an unknown original or to order the
whole book blindly. Three addressed frames are needed:

- **l. 15 v.** — Ivan Nechaev's storehouse inside the town;
- **l. 25** — the bobyl Osipko Vasilyev;
- **l. 36 v.** — the desyatsky Ivanko Nechaev and his brother, the newly recruited Cossack
  Ponkratko.

[Catalogue line for d. 124](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=1209&inv=1&case=124#rgada_opisi),
[official card of the digital file](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000001151014/10000005098014/10000393439014/).

A second path has also been found, but it requires correcting the archival address. The inventory
of former **d. 487** says that the Tula book occupied ll. 1–860 and the Dedilov one ll. 861–1024;
then the file was transferred to the РНБ (National Library of Russia) under the call number
**f. 550, ОСРК, Q.IV.238**. A check of the official electronic catalogue of the РНБ gave a
contradiction: under the exact call number **Q.IV.238** it describes a different document — «О
зачатии и рождении Петра I» (On the Conception and Birth of Peter I), eighteenth century; a search
for the words "Dedilov" and "Tula district" did not show the book we need. So this call number cannot
be repeated as a reliable current address: it may be out of date, transmitted with an error, or
refer to a move not yet reflected. A separate inquiry is needed from the Department
of Manuscripts of the РНБ, giving the former РГАДА call number and the first and last folios.

This contradiction had already been noticed independently before us. In inquiry No. 51100 of
27 January 2024 a researcher quoted the same inventory of d. 487 in full and asked the РНБ how to
find the manuscript. The virtual reference service did not give a corrected call number and
forwarded the question directly to the Department of Manuscripts. So the problem is not our typo;
at the same time such an answer does not prove that the book is lost — it only leaves its current
address unestablished.

[Inventory of former d. 487](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=1209&inv=1&case=487#rgada_opisi),
[official РНБ manuscript catalogue](https://nlr.ru/manuscripts/RA1527/elektronnyiy-katalog),
[РНБ answer to inquiry No. 51100](https://www.vss.nlr.ru/query_info_print.php?query_ID=51100).

One more boundary matters. The Yandex link already used leads to a 123-megabyte PDF of the
**printed edition** «Писцовые книги Московского государства» (Survey Books of the Muscovite State),
not to a photocopy of the manuscript. The digital card of d. 124 confirms that a digitised file
exists, but the official viewer did not open the three folios themselves in the current session. So
the reading of Ivanko, Pankratko and Osipko is not yet raised to "checked against the scan", and no
profile or route Dedilov → Oryol is created.

### Ivan a Desyatsky, Pankrat a New Recruit: the Family as a Recruitment Channel

The full context of the Dergilevskaya sotnya changes the meaning of the brothers' line. Ivanko
Nechaev was not just a Cossack with a household. He is called a **desyatsky** (foreman of ten), had a
storehouse in the fortress, a household in the sloboda (settlement) and land. His brother Ponkratko
stood on another rung: a **newly recruited Cossack**, settled on Ivan's land. So the source shows,
within one proven family, a concrete way of replenishing town service: a recently enlisted Cossack
was initially settled with an already rooted serving relative.

This is not a one-off domestic accident. The summary on ll. 38 v.–39 counts in the Dergilevskaya
sotnya 3 households of pyatidesyatniki (foremen of fifty), 9 of desyatniki and 132 of rank-and-file
Cossacks. Separately recorded are 2 newly recruited Cossacks in old households and 15 newly recruited
households **on the lands of former servicemen**. Ivan belonged to the narrow layer of nine junior
commanders, and Pankrat to the mass-registered layer of the new recruitment. So the transition
**Cossack desyatsky → syn boyarsky** is historically plausible for Ivan: if the two Ivans are
identical, the Oryol grant of 1595 would be not a first entry into service but a new enrolment
(verstanye) or the transfer of an experienced serviceman.

There is also an important negative correction. On l. 52 and ll. 60 v.–61 the book names additional
lands given to Cossacks of the Lugovaya and Dergilevskaya sotni **«за озовскую и за черкасскую
службу»** ("for the Azov and the Cherkassian service"). But Ivan and Pankrat were not found among the
surviving named recipients of these additions. The corresponding campaigns or rewards must not be
ascribed to them. The account of these sotni being stationed in Dedilov in 1554 and of their
connection with the campaign of 1558 is a reconstruction by the author of the publication; the
primary text reliably proves only that some Cossacks were given lands for such service.

The boundary of the conclusion is the same as before: the word **«новоприборный»** ("newly
recruited") proves Pankrat's recent enlistment in Cossack service, but does not say which town he
came from. The Dedilov Ivan is not called Anpilogov, and the Oryol Ivan of 1595 is not called
Pankrat's brother. So the map remains without a route, and the next decisive source is an extract of
1589–1595 on the transfer, enrolment or departure of the brothers.

[Folio-by-folio transcription of the Dedilov survey book](https://www.universalinternetlibrary.ru/book/96720/chitat_knigu.shtml).

### The Database of 421,095 Servicemen: a Zero Result Turned Out to Be a Coverage Test

The open database «Служилые люди XVI–XVIII века» (Servicemen of the Sixteenth to Eighteenth
Centuries) was checked not only for surname variants but for the rare Dedilov family label. The
query **«Нечаев»** (Nechaev) gave 498 rows. The formula **«Нечаев Понкрат»** (Nechaev Ponkrat) gave
none, and the modern form **«Нечаев Панкрат»** (Nechaev Pankrat) gave four entries of 1640–1671 from
Arzamas and Nizhny Novgorod. They are too late to be Ivanko's brother of 1588/89 himself, and without
intermediate generations they do not form a continuation of his family.

The main result is an established boundary of this database. With a filter for the 1590s it finds
not a single Nechaev and returns no **Anpilogovs, Trunovs or Bazdyrevs**, although all three
surnames are independently known in the Oryol survey book of 1594/95. For the 1580s, among the
Nechaevs the index shows only Grigory Nechaev from Bezhetskaya pyatina in 1588; the Dedilov Ivan and
Pankrat are not in it.

Consequently, the zero for the brothers is not negative proof and does not weaken the Dedilov
candidate. The database is useful for later control slices, but the transition we need,
**1589–1595**, lies outside its observable coverage. The search returns to extracts of the Razryad
and Pomestny prikaz (Estates Office) on enrolment, transfer, removal from Dedilov service and the
Oryol grant.

[Public database of servicemen](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/).

## 1595: the Western Korchakov Cluster of Anfilogovs

R. I. Ovsyannikov's map, compiled directly from the survey book of Dementy Yakovlev and Leonty
Sofonov, made it possible to see not a list of separate holders but their positions relative to one
another. **Eight of the nine** known Anfilogovs/Anpilogovs of 1594/95 form one western cluster: Belyay
and Osip Vasilyevich, as well as Vasily, Timofey and Karp Mikulich, sit in Bazdyrevo; Ivan Pershich
in Belaya; Sofon Timofeevich in Sofonova; Ivan Nechaevich received an unnamed zaimishche on the
Radomlya. On the scale of the reconstruction the whole group occupies a strip of about ten
kilometres roughly 40–45 km west of the Oryol fortress.

The only exception is Ermak Pakhomovich Anpilogov in the village of Maltsovskaya in Nougorsk stan.
This makes the search model two-part: first of all one must look for the origin of the western
Korchakov group as a whole, and check Ermak as a possible separate line.

The alphabetical index to the map gave one more important but limited confirmation: Gosudarnino
selishche, where **Anpilog Ivanovich Onanyin** had earlier held a zherebye (share), did indeed belong
to Korchakov stan. However, the place itself could not yet be confidently placed inside the
ten-kilometre cluster. So the shared stan supports the hypothesis that the family naming arose
locally from the personal name Anpilog, but does not prove that Onanyin was an ancestor of this
family.

The practical conclusion is to look not only for "Anfilogovs" but for a group move of the western
service milieu before 1594: Mikula, Vasily, Persha, Timofey and Nechay together with the Bazdyrevs,
Trunovs, Nemtsovs and Krivolapovs. The western position raises the priority of the Karachev and
neighbouring Upper Oka lists, but the route **Karachev → Oryol** is not put on the map until a
document names the earlier town directly.

Research card:
`PUB-OVSYANNIKOV-OREL-1595-ANFILOGOV-MICROGEOGRAPHY`.
[Map at the original resolution](https://valerista.narod.ru/photo_struk/Orlov_uezd_1595.jpg),
[publication page](https://valerista.narod.ru/index/0-17),
[description of the localisation method](https://www.dangralas.ru/zametki/mesta/36-orlovskij-uezd-story).
### Savyolov, 1906: Three Early Centres of the Bazdyrevs on One Page

In the first issue of L. M. Savyolov's «Родословные записи» (Genealogical Notes) the entry
**«Баздыревы»** (The Bazdyrevs) on p. 102 was found and checked against the scan. It is short, but
important precisely as an early genealogical summary of the nearest neighbouring surname of the
Anfilogovs:

- **Alfim Bazdyrev** served in Dedilov in Cossack service and held a pomestye there in 1588;

- **Nikifor Bazdyrev** held a pomestye in Oryol district in 1594;

- in 1595 in Oryol district the deceased **Maksim Bazdyrev** is recorded, with his widow Arina and
  his young sons Alfery aged seven, Agap aged five and Rodion aged two;

- **Pyotr Nikiforovich Bazdyrev** served among the deti boyarskie of Ryazhsk in 1591.

Each line is checked against the fuller primary text already found. Alfim is the Dedilov **Olfim
Bazdyrev** of the Lutyuva sotnya, who received land «за послугу» ("for service"). The Oryol Nikifor
is named in full as **Nikifor Oksyonov syn Bazdyrev** and held his father's pomestye. Maksim's family
in the survey book keeps the diminutive forms of the children — Olferko, Ogapko and Radko. Finally,
the Ryazhsk desyatnya reveals Pyotr as **«Петруша Никифоров сын Баздырев, Свиридов тож»** ("Petrusha
Nikiforov syn Bazdyrev, also called Sviridov"), who had earlier served directly from Ryazan, from
Pekhletsky stan.

The point of the find is not a new ready-made route. Savyolov does not build a genealogy between
these people and does not prove that the Dedilov or the Ryazan branch moved to Oryol. But the
independent summary confirms that by 1588–1595 the rare neighbouring surname really existed at once
in three service milieus: the Dedilov Cossack, the Ryazan–Ryazhsk pomestye and the Oryol. This
strengthens the group tactic of searching for the origin, but a route of Anfilogovs is still not
created on the map.

Source card: `PUB-SAVELOV-1906-V1-P102-BAZDYREVY`. A photocopy of p. 102 has been saved in the
research archive.
[Scan of the issue at the ГПИБ](https://elib.shpl.ru/ru/nodes/42877-vyp-1-a-b-1906).

### 1573: the Full List of the Sovereign's Court Does Not Contain the Family We Need

The new tactic was used to check not another search for one surname, but the full alphabetical
index to the **1,849 names** of the list of Ivan the Terrible's court of 20 March 1573. The document
survives in the РНБ, Hermitage collection, No. 542, 40 folios, and was published by D. N. Alshits in
2003.

The forms Anfilogov, Anpilogov, Onfilogov, Ampilogov and Anfilof are absent. Also absent is the more
reliable family signature **Mikula → Vasily, Timofey and Karp**. The machine match «анфилов» turned
out to be part of the entry **«Панфилов Иванко»** (Panfilov Ivanko) on l. 30. That is a different
surname series.

For the names of the older generation only isolated namesakes were found: **Mikula Fyodorov syn
Lekhchanov** on l. 7 v., **Terentey Ivanov** on l. 14 and **Neustroy Nechaev syn Kultashev** on
l. 3. The last is especially important as a control: a patronymic from the name Nechay existed
independently of Anpilogovs, and the same list contains a large independent circle of Kultashevs
and Kultashovs. So the combination "Nechaev syn" alone is not enough for a link with the Oryol Ivan
Nechaevich Anpilogov.

The negative result is limited by the composition of the source. It is a full list of a special
court corporation, but not a general desyatnya of town deti boyarskie; the absence of the family does
not exclude its service in Oryol or a neighbouring Upper Oka town. The corpus of 1573 is now closed
to repeat searches. Priority remains with town lists, extracts from lost desyatni and late lawsuits
with a formula of earlier service.

Verification card:
`PUB-ALSHITS-1573-OPRICHNY-DVOR-ANFILOGOV-AUDIT`.
[Official card of the edition in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000200_000018_RU_NLR_bibl_492373/),
[full name index](https://kdkv.narod.ru/Opr1573/Opr-1573-Spis.html).

### 1563: the Pre-Oryol Service Belt in the Book of the Polotsk Campaign

One more full corpus older than Oryol was checked: the published text of the Book of the Polotsk
Campaign of 1563, all 29 scanned spreads. It contains no direct forms Anfilogov, Anpilogov,
Onfilogov, Ampilogov and no personal name Anfilof/Anfilog. But this is a limited negative result: the
book usually does not name rank-and-file deti boyarskie individually, but counts them as town
collectives.

The main find is the pre-Oryol structure of service itself. In the initial distribution the
following are named together: **«Карачевы, Болховичи з больших статей, Мецняне и казаки»** ("the
Karachev men, the Bolkhov men of the higher grades, the Mtsensk men and the Cossacks"). At the muster
