in Kaluga an even broader group stands: the Seversk towns, Pochep, Starodub, **Bryansk, Karachev,
Bolkhov, Mtsensk, Meshchovsk and Tula**. So three years before Oryol was founded these neighbouring
towns were no longer isolated points: their men were brought together into common military
assignments.

The numerical roster shows the scale of individual corporations and assignments:

- 170 Bolkhov men under Prince Mikhail Repnin;
- 49 Karachev men under Mikhail Voronoy-Volynsky;
- 85 Bryansk men under Ivan Sheremetev;
- 45 court (dvorovye) and 128 town (gorodovye) Belyov men, 173 men in all;
- separately 50 Belyov men under Aleksey Basmanov;
- 20 court and 248 town Tula men;
- 9 court and 289 town Meshchovsk men.

These numbers cannot be added up across different assignments as the full count of unique people:
one town could appear in several parts of the roster. And above all, not a single line speaks of a
future transfer specifically to Oryol.

The meaning for the search is now more concrete. Karachev, Bolkhov and Mtsensk receive first
priority as a directly grouped neighbouring set. Bryansk and Meshchovsk remain in the same circle and
are additionally supported by the family bridges found for other Oryol landholders of 1584–1595. One
should look for the named campaign lists, desyatni, allotment and pomestye files of these towns,
especially the formulas «служил прежде» ("served earlier"), «сведён» ("transferred"), «переведён»
("moved") and «дано в Орле» ("granted in Oryol"). But a general military muster is not yet a route of
Anfilogovs, so the map does not change.

Research card:
`PUB-PETROV-1563-POLOTSK-UPPER-OKA-PRE-OREL-POOL`.
[Official section of free РНБ publications](https://nlr.ru/publ/book_free.html),
[description of the publication and full text](https://opentextnn.ru/old/history/arkheography/metod/polockiypokhod/index.html%40id%3D5228).

### 1589–1595: Why It Is Pointless to Look for the Next Dedilov Census

A check of the public inventory of the fond of the Pomestny prikaz refined the very structure of
the gap. For Dedilov there survives the pripravochnaya book of 1588–1589 —
**РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 124**, as well as a second, incomplete copy of the same
text within d. 487. There is no independent next Dedilov survey, allotment or otkaznaya (land
transfer) book for the needed **1589–1595** in the published list. Comparable named books appear
only in the seventeenth century.

This is an important negative result specifically for tactics. Ivanko Nechaev cannot be found by
simply leafing through "the next census": no such transitional volume is visible at present. If the
desyatsky really became the Oryol Ivan Nechaevich Anpilogov, the trace must have entered a derivative
document — a memorandum on enrolment from Cossacks into deti boyarskie, an otkaznaya vypis (land
transfer extract) for the Oryol zaimishche, a record of removal from Dedilov service, a surety bond or
a late petition with a formula naming the earlier town.

An additional full-text search of the published РГАДА inventories did not produce an early file
annotated with the name of Ivan Nechaev or the forms Anpilogov/Anfilogov. This does not mean the man
is absent from the files: archival headings rarely list all participants. But general queries by
surname are now exhausted; the next search must proceed by type of act and by the formula of
transition.

The candidate card has been updated:
`PUB-PKMG-1587-TULA-DEDILOV-TRUNOV-BAZDYREV`.
[Public list of Dedilov books](https://dompredkov.ru/dedilovskie-pistsovye-i-perepisnye-knigi/),
[full-text search of the РГАДА inventories](https://rgada.sovadev.pro/).

### The False Northern Rezanovo Route Cut Off by the Primary Text

A local-history version proposed an attractive chain: the former owners of the hamlet (seltso)
Rezanovo near Moscow → the Dedilov Rezanovs/Rezantsovs → the Oryol Rezantsovs. A check not of a
retelling but of the printed primary text destroyed the first link.

The survey book of 1573/74 literally records **«Андреевское поместье Щелкалова, а ныне на оброке за
дьяком Рохманином Русиновым: сельцо Резаново»** ("Andrey Shchelkalov's pomestye, and now on quitrent
(obrok) under the dyak Rokhmanin Rusinov: the hamlet Rezanovo"). In the book of 1584–1586 the same
place had already become **«пустошью, что было сельцо, Резаново»** ("a waste that was the hamlet
Rezanovo") and was on quitrent under the stable dyak Shemet Ivanov. Not a single entry names a former
owner called Rezanov or Rezantsov. The coincidence of the place name with the surname is real, but the
source contains no conclusion about a family.

So the line Moscow district → Dedilov → Oryol is not put on the map. The Dedilov hypothesis is kept on
another, independent support — the group coincidence of Nechaev, Rezantsov, Suvorov, Polyansky and the
Bredikhins — but the Moscow Rezanovo is excluded from the chain of evidence.

[Survey Books of the Muscovite State, pt. I, sect. 1: pp. 11 and 261–262](https://archive.org/details/pistsovyeknigimoskovskogogosudarstvapis29).

### Yelets instead of Moscow: Two Dedilov Families Dispersed Among the New Southern Towns

After the negative result the unit of search again became not the surname Anpilogov but the whole
neighbourhood packet. The full alphabet of the Yelets desyatni, lists and payment books of
1604/05–1621/22 contains no Anfilogovs and no documentary variants of the surname. But it revealed two
large clusters from the Dedilov control core.

The Yelets **Rezantsovs** are represented by no fewer than thirteen full name entries. A large line
of Timofey Danilovich and his sons Vasily, Ivan, Ignat, Konstantin, Nikita and Faddey is visible;
separately named are Vasily Nikitich, Vasily Pavlovich, Ivan Savelyevich, the young Ivan Fyodorovich,
Rodion Fyodorovich and **Fedka Ivanov syn Rezantsov**. The last is especially important: he stands on
the same paternal rung as the Oryol Maksim and Lomaka Ivanovich Rezantsov. Now his own entry has also
been found: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4, d. 86, l. 60 v.; printed p. 47**. Fedka is placed in the section
of noviki of 1602/03 who were enrolled with a money salary in 1604; he was assigned a pomestye oklad
of **100 chetverti**, and the money was not paid out pending the sovereign's decree. This is the exact
date of his inclusion in the Yelets service corporation. He may still be a third son of one Ivan or a
relative from another branch, but the entry gives neither an earlier town, nor a common father, nor
direct kinship with Maksim and Lomaka. The people must not be merged.

The allotment book of Yelets of 1593/94 moved this branch back another decade. **Timofey Rezantsov**
already held a homestead site (usadishche) at the mouth of the Vorgol together with Stepan Luzhnov;
each was assigned 150 chetverti. On l. 12 v. Timofey is repeated in the common boundary of the Yelets
deti boyarskie next to **Aleksey Polyansky**. Both family stems belong to the Dedilov–Oryol control
packet. The book gives Timofey no patronymic, so he cannot yet be identified with Timofey Danilovich of
1604.

The main explanation was found in an earlier report (otpiska) of the Yelets voivodes of 1592 (РГАДА,
f. 141, op. 1, 1592, d. 1, ll. 63–64). In Yelets 200 deti boyarskie were to be settled and recruited
**from various towns** on a family principle: «из-за отцов неслужилые: отцов — дети, от братии —
братья, от дядь — племянники» ("non-serving men from behind their fathers: sons from fathers, brothers
from brothers, nephews from uncles"). The voivodes had already enrolled 164 men and were going to
recruit 36 more. This is not a reconstruction but a direct description of the staffing mechanism of a
new town: the junior branch received a separate land and money enrolment, while the father, brother or
uncle could remain in the earlier town.

The Yelets **Bredikhins** form an even more useful family control. In the desyatnya of 1604/05 Venyuk
Ivanov syn Bredikhin receives three roubles, and four Yelets men stand surety for him. In the list of
1615–1618 Pyotr Ivanov syn Bredikhin has an oklad of 350 chetverti; later he is named as killed in the
sack of Yelets. The full complex also shows children and widows of several generations. The two
Ivanoviches could formally have been sons of the Dedilov Ivashka Bredikhin of 1588/89, but the source
does not yet give his patronymic or their transition.

The meaning of the find is wider than two surnames. The same families that link Dedilov with Oryol
produced independent branches in Yelets. This supports the model of **split recruitment**: relatives
and fellow servicemen were distributed among different new towns, rather than one family moving to one
point. This is exactly how the pre-Oryol father of Anfilogovs could have disappeared: he remained in
the old town without a stable surname, while his sons received land and a new naming in Oryol.

The full page with Fedka gave an even denser imprint. On **ll. 59 v.–60 v.**, in one list of Yelets
noviki of 1602/03, stand Grisha Rusinov syn Khitrovo, Fedka Olisov syn Vorypaev, Savka Polunin syn
Sukhinin, Fedka Ivanov syn Rezantsov and Sergey Nikitin syn Yakunin. All five family namings are already
documented in Oryol district in 1594/95. These are not the same people: first names and patronymics
differ. But the compactness of the group changes the scale of the conclusion — we are looking not at a
couple of chance namesakes but at a probable distribution of branches of one frontier service milieu.

A control across 71 town headings of the desyatnya of noviki of 1596 refined the rarity of the set.
**Yakunin** occurred among the noviki only in Oryol; Vorypaev in Mtsensk and Ryazan, Bredikhin in Tula,
Solova and Ryazan, Rezantsov in Mozhaysk; Sukhinin and Khitrovo are absent from the desyatnya.
Consequently, Oryol and Yelets may have received not a ready-made family from a single town but junior
and collateral branches of a common staffing reserve. An alternative mechanism is equally possible: some
junior relatives could have been enrolled into Yelets already from the Oryol milieu. Only a document
about an earlier place or a divided family will be able to distinguish these variants.
[Full alphabet of the Yelets complex](https://62info.ru/history/node/12611),
[Fedka Rezantsov's entry on l. 60 v.](https://nashi-predki.ru/mw/index.php?mobileaction=toggle_view_mobile&title=1604_%D0%B3._%D0%94%D0%B5%D1%81%D1%8F%D1%82%D0%BD%D1%8F_%D0%B2%D0%B5%D1%80%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B8%D1%8F_%D0%B8_%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D0%B5%D0%B6%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B9_%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%B7%D0%B4%D0%B0%D1%87%D0%B8._%D1%81%D1%82%D1%80.41-60),
[allotment book of Yelets of 1593/94](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Otvod_zalov_elec_1593/text.htm),
[report on the enrolment of Yelets deti boyarskie, 1592](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/25.php),
[list of Yelets deti boyarskie of 1615–1618](https://nashi-predki.ru/wiki/1615-1618_%D0%B3%D0%B3._%D0%A1%D0%BF%D0%B8%D1%81%D0%BE%D0%BA_%D0%B5%D0%BB%D0%B5%D1%86%D0%BA%D0%B8%D1%85_%D0%B4%D0%B5%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%B9_%D0%B1%D0%BE%D1%8F%D1%80%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8%D1%85).

### Two New Exact Archival Addresses from the Current РГАДА Catalogue

The machine-readable ГИС УИАД catalogue made it possible to obtain stable cards, not just old printed
descriptions.

The first address is **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, kn. 256, ll. 1–503**: extracts from the survey,
inspection (dozornye) and boundary books of the coachmen's (yamskie) slobody of 1581–1737. One
collection lists Bolkhov, Mtsensk, Novosil, Kursk and Stary Oskol. This is not a desyatnya of deti
boyarskie but an eighteenth-century copy of earlier books; the overall date of 1581 cannot yet be
transferred to each town section. But the source may preserve pre-Oryol households, nicknames and the
road milieu of neighbouring towns, so the first requirement is the table of contents and an exact
dating of three sections — Bolkhov, Mtsensk and Novosil.

The second address refines the already known transfer of streltsy: the official card of **РГАДА,
f. 210, op. 9, d. 153** has an exact digital path. The catalogue confirms the file and its dating to
1639–1640, but does not open ll. 83–85 and does not prove that images exist. During the check the
portal was unavailable, so the names of the streltsy transferred from Mtsensk to Oryol remain unknown.

[Book of the coachmen's slobody, f. 1209, op. 1, kn. 256](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000001151014/10000005098014/10000393399014/),
[transfer of the Mtsensk streltsy, f. 210, op. 9, d. 153](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000000358014/10000474641014/).

### A Full-Text Audit of All Inventories: What Has Really Been Ruled Out

The search was extended from individual official PDFs to an independent OCR layer of the published
РГАДА inventories. Checked were not only the forms Anfilogov, Anpilogov, Onfilogov, Onpilogov and
Ampilogov, but also the exact pairs of first names and patronymics of the early Oryol core, the
neighbouring surnames, the old words **орляне/орлян** (orlyane/orlyan, "Oryol men") and the formulas
**сведён, переведён, верстан, поместье** ("transferred, moved, enrolled, pomestye").

There is no direct record of the transfer of a specific Anfilogov to Oryol before 1600. For the words
"orlyan/orlyane" the system gave 26 matches, but the combination «орлян верстан» ("Oryol men
enrolled") led only to files of 1672 and later. Four matches for the form "Anfilogov" turned out to be
late or unrelated, one "Anpilogov" belongs to 1767; the forms Onfilogov, Onpilogov and Ampilogov did
not occur in the headings.

The control proved more useful than a simple zero. Elena Chernova's consolidated index confirmed the
Novy Oskol and Stary Oskol Anpilovs of the late seventeenth century, but did not make them earlier.
The only exact "Pavel Terentyev" has a third naming, **Kalinin**, and so is not the Oryol Pavel
Terentyev syn Anfilogov.

The boundary of the result is strict: the full-text service searches the OCR of inventories and
ready-made name indexes, not every line of the manuscripts. A file heading almost never lists
rank-and-file deti boyarskie. So a repeat search for the same words in the catalogues is closed, but
not the search inside late extracts, otkaznye and distribution (razdatochnye) books. The next target
is a personal formula of earlier service for Ivan Nechaevich or Pavel Terentyevich and a document on
the initial Oryol grant. No route has been added to the map: the man's earlier town has not yet been
named.

The check has been entered in the card
`PUB-RGADA-F210-EARLY-OREL-CATALOG-AUDIT`.
[Full-text search of the inventories](https://rgada.sovadev.pro/),
[official РГАДА inventories](http://rgada.info/).

### 1585: One More Kashira Serviceman Was "at Oryol"

The original list of members of the Sovereign's Court who remained in Moscow during Tsar Fyodor
Ivanovich's journey to the Trinity–St Sergius Monastery gave a second direct personal link between
Kashira and early Oryol. In the section **«Кошира»** (Koshira, i.e. Kashira) on l. 101 it is recorded:

> «Петр Федоров сын Ондреева. (Нет). На Орле».
>
> *Translation:* "Pyotr Fyodorov syn Ondreeva. (Absent.) At Oryol."

Modern archival address: **ОР РГБ, f. 29, No. 103, l. 101**; scholarly publication by Yu. V.
Ankhimyuk, 1990, p. 65. The full formula reads as Pyotr Fyodorovich Andreev. The heading preserves his
membership of the Kashira vybor (selected servicemen), and the note explains his absence from Moscow by
current service in Oryol.

An independent Razryad entry revealed the post and the sequence of service. In the roster of the
ukrainnye (frontier) towns of **28 February 1585** first named are the Oryol voivode Vladimir
Koltsov-Mosalsky and the golova (commander) Ivan Myasnoy; after they were released, under the
namestnik and voivode Foma Afanasyevich Buturlin stood the golova **Ivan Andreev syn Fefilatyev**, and
**«после Ивана был голова Петр Федоров»** ("after Ivan the golova was Pyotr Fyodorov"). Yu. V.
Ankhimyuk directly compared this Pyotr with the Kashira man Andreev. So the note "At Oryol" denotes not
an indefinite trip but command service as golova of the Oryol garrison.

Source of the second line: «Разрядная книга 1475–1598 гг.» (Razryad Book of 1475–1598), Moscow, 1966,
pp. 350–351; the main Uvarov copy — ГИМ, Uvarov collection, No. 950, ll. 481 v.–482. The new link to a
post sets a concrete archival cluster: the papers of Foma Buturlin, Ivan Fefilatyev and Pyotr Andreev,
especially the lists of subordinates, arrivals and absences (nety) attached at the change of golova.

The control of the Kashira desyatnya of 1599 proved crucial. The same **Pyotr Fyodorov syn Ondreev** is
again recorded among the Kashira vybornye with an oklad of 700 chetverti. There is no such holder in the
full Oryol survey book of 1594/95. Consequently, "At Oryol" means a temporary posting, and not
necessarily a transfer of the family or the receipt of an Oryol pomestye.

Together with the Kashira commanders of Oryol of 1575/76–1577 and Filipp Fyodorovich Teplov, directly
**granted a pomestye in Oryol** (ispomeshchyon v Orle), three different forms of one corridor emerged:
command, temporary service and permanent settlement on a pomestye. This strengthens Kashira as a real
donor town for early Oryol cadres, but at the same time forbids turning every note "at Oryol" into a
resettlement route. Pyotr does not belong to the researched surname, so no profile and no map point are
created. Card:
`PUB-ANKHIMYUK-1585-KASHIRA-PETR-ANDREEV-ON-OREL`.
[Article and full text of the publication](https://ebookiriran.ru/index.php?id=696&section=9&view=article),
[official PDF of the ИРИ РАН](https://iriran.ru/sites/default/files/2022-07/IpoI_1990.pdf),
[official card of the open copy of the Razryad Book in the РГБ](https://search.rsl.ru/ru/record/01002708318),
[Kashira desyatnya of 1599](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Desjatni_XVI_v/text6.htm).

### 1550s–1585: Pyotr's Predecessor Reveals One More Route — Yuryev-Polsky → Oryol

A search for the biography of Ivan Andreevich Fefilatyev, whom Pyotr Andreev replaced in Oryol, gave a
second exact old-town channel. In the Dvorovaya tetrad (Court Register) of the 1550s, in the section
**«Юрьев»** (Yuryev), on manuscript l. 114 it is recorded:

> «Матвей да Иван Андреевы дети Фефилатьева. В жильцех. Умре».
>
> *Translation:* "Matvey and Ivan, the sons of Andrey Fefilatyev. Among the zhiltsy. Died."
The name index of the scholarly edition identifies Ivan as a **court (dvorovy) syn boyarsky of
Yuryev**. The brief notes are distributed between the brothers: «В жильцех» ("among the zhiltsy")
refers to Ivan, «Умре» ("died") to Matvey. This is confirmed not by a guess from punctuation but by two
later documents. In the surety records (poruchnye zapisi) of 1563 and 1564/65 the full formula **«Иван
Ондреев сын Фефилатьев»** (Ivan Ondreev syn Fefilatyev) appears independently. In the second record he
is one of 81 guarantors for the boyar Lev Andreevich Saltykov and his sons; original: **РГАДА, f. 135,
section III, rubric II, No. 33, ll. 1–3**.

The Oryol Razryad entry of 1585 preserves all three elements of the same rare naming — Ivan, Ondreev
syn, Fefilatyev — and adds the post of golova. The result is a strong prosopographical chain:

**Yuryev-Polsky, 1550s → surety records of 1563 and 1564/65 → golova in Oryol, 1585**.

This is more important than a simple coincidence of first name and surname: across four time layers
the full three-part formula coincides. But there is no direct phrase "transferred from Yuryev to Oryol",
so the result is recorded as a strong identification of one serviceman, not as a literal resettlement
record.

The conclusion changes the tactic. Early Oryol received its commanders through at least several old
service corporations: direct settlement on a pomestye has already been proven for the Kashira man
Filipp Teplov, temporary command for the Kashira man Pyotr Andreev, and now the Yuryev biography of his
predecessor Ivan Fefilatyev has been revealed. So the origin of the first Anfilogovs must not be sought
in one town only; priority goes to lists of subordinates at changes of golova and to a parallel check of
the Kashira and Yuryev desyatni.

The boundary remains strict: Fefilatyev does not belong to the researched surname series, and no
document links him with Anfilogovs. So no separate profile and no route on the family map are
created. A context card has been created: `PUB-DVOROVAYA-1550S-YURYEV-IVAN-FEFILATIEV-TO-OREL-1585`.
[Name index of the Dvorovaya tetrad](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1540-1560/Tys_kniga_1550/imena_5.php),
[surety records of 1527–1571](https://vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1520-1540/Porucn_zapisi_1527_1571/text.phtml),
[Razryad Book: the Oryol golovy of 1585](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XV/1460-1480/RK_1475_1605/text27.htm).

### 1563: Next to the Future Oryol Golova — Rychko Onfalov/Anfalov

The same surety record of 20 April 1563 that confirms the continuity of Ivan Andreevich Fefilatyev's
naming gave an unexpected pre-Oryol name trace. Among the 102 guarantors for Prince Aleksandr Ivanovich
Vorotynsky, on the recto of the original there is the entry:

> «Рычко Онфалов сын Волотцкого».
>
> *Translation:* "Rychko Onfalov, son of Volotskoy."

In the money clause his personal surety is set at a hundred roubles, and again the form **«на Рычке
на Онфалове сыне Волотцкого»** ("upon Rychko Onfalov, son of Volotskoy") is given. However, on the
verso the same man is already called **«Рычко Анфалов сын Волотцкого»** (Rychko Anfalov, son of
Volotskoy), and on l. 7 v. stands the formula of signature:

> «Рычко Анфалов князя Александра ручял и руку приложил».
>
> *Translation:* "Rychko Anfalov stood surety for Prince Aleksandr and set his hand."

This is a fundamentally important observation: the variants **Онфалов/Анфалов** (Onfalov/Anfalov)
belong to one man and alternate within one original act. Consequently, the initial O/A could waver as
early as the sixteenth century; this is not a result of modern OCR and not an accidental merger of
different entries. The formula is built as an old naming by the father: Rychko, son of Onfal/Anfal;
«Волотцкого» denotes his belonging to the Volotskys. In modern normalisation: Rychko Anfalovich
Volotsky.

The personal name **Rychko** is a non-calendar diminutive of Ryk, connected with the meaning of a loud
cry or roar. The meaning of the father's name has not yet been established. Anfal must not automatically
be taken as a shortening of Anfilog or Amfilokhy: the document proves only the existence and internal
wavering of the forms Anfal/Onfal.

The link with the Oryol search is concrete but indirect. The same list includes **Ivan Ondreev syn
Fefilatyev**, the future golova of Oryol in 1585. Rychko and Ivan stand far apart among the 102
guarantors, so the document does not prove personal closeness, a common town or a joint resettlement.
It shows something else: the rare patronymic naming Onfalov/Anfalov already existed in the central
service milieu from which an Oryol commander was later appointed.

The practical conclusion is that before a ready-made surname appears one should look not only for
Anfilogovs, but also for the formulas **«сын Анфала», «сын Онфала», «Анфалов», «Онфалов»** ("son of
Anfal", "son of Onfal", "Anfalov", "Onfalov") in town desyatni, the Dvorovaya tetrad, surety and
genealogical materials. As long as there is neither a formula of transfer to Oryol nor kinship with the
Oryol Anfilogovs of the 1590s, no profile and no route on the family map are created.

Original: **РГАДА, f. 135, section III, rubric II, No. 27, ll. 1–7**; a copy of the 1770s — ibid.,
ll. 8–19 v. The card `PUB-PORUCHNAYA-1563-RYCHKO-ONFALOV-ANFALOV-VOLOTSKY` has been created.
[Scholarly publication of the original](https://vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1520-1540/Porucn_zapisi_1527_1571/text.phtml).

### 1504/05–1563: Rychko Gains a Real Northern Family Milieu, but Not a Proven Father

A check of the Volotskys produced an earlier pair of original acts. In two deeds of purchase (kupchie)
of 1504/05 Onfal and Istoma buy from their own brothers shares of the ancestral village of Demyanovo.
The formula of act No. 430 is literal:

> «Се язъ, Онфалъ да Истома Семеновы дети Волоцкого, купили есма у своее
> братьи у Василья да у Семена у Семеновых же детей ихъ вотчину треть села
> Демьянова…»
>
> *Translation:* "Behold, I, Onfal and Istoma, the sons of Semyon Volotskoy, have bought from our
> brothers Vasily and Semyon, likewise sons of Semyon, their votchina, a third of the village of
> Demyanovo…"

In the second act Onfal and Istoma remain the buyers, and the sellers are two more brothers, Aleksey
and Evus. So the source names at least **six sons of Semyon Volotsky**. Onfal here is a personal name,
"Semyonovy deti" ("sons of Semyon") a patronymic, and the Volotskys an already existing family. This is
a ready documentary mechanism for the later formula «Рычко Онфалов сын Волотцкого», but not yet proof
that Rychko was the son of this particular Onfal Semyonovich.

The check of the genealogical legend is especially important. In a roll of the late seventeenth century
the Volotskys declared as their founder an "honourable man" (chestny muzh), Aleksandr Anfal, who had
come out of Poland under Vasily III. I. V. Zaitsev compared the roll with the acts and showed a
**«подмену реалий»** ("substitution of realities"): the deeds contemporary with the events know neither
Aleksandr nor a departure from Poland, but show Onfal Semyonovich as one of several brothers of a native
Vologda service family. According to Zaitsev's study, the Volotskys held votchiny (hereditary estates)
from the fifteenth century in the Kostroma Korezhskaya volost and the Vologda volost of Lezhsky Volok.
The deeds themselves are placed by the editors in Galich district and name the village of Demyanovo.

Thus Rychko acquires not an invented Polish biography but a verifiable north-eastern cluster: **Galich
district — Korezhskaya volost — Lezhsky Volok — the Volotsky family**. The coincidence of the rare name
of the father and of the family makes Onfal Semyonovich a possible father or elder relative of Rychko,
but a gap of almost sixty years requires an intermediate charter or genealogical act. So far this line
is brought to Oryol only through Rychko's joint presence with the future Oryol golova Ivan Fefilatyev in
the surety record of 1563; a resettlement of the Volotskys or their kinship with Anfilogovs has not been established.

The card `PUB-ASEI-1504-1505-ONFAL-SEMENOV-VOLOTSKY` has been created, with photocopies of printed
pages 473–474 saved in it. Sources: **АСЭИ, vol. II, Moscow, 1958, Nos. 430–431, pp. 473–474**;
originals — **РГАДА, f. 281, Nos. 3340 and 3341**;
[critical analysis by I. V. Zaitsev](https://vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1500-1520/Sinodik_Kazan/text.phtml),
[archaeographic check by L. V. Moshkova](https://aarheo.ru/files/Vestnik_Alyans-Arkheo._Vyp._31.pdf).

### 1686: the Genealogical Roll Did Not Join Rychko to Anfal but Created a Different Chain

The full publication of the original genealogical roll (rodoslovnaya rospis) of the Volotskys,
submitted on 7 May 1686, has been found and visually checked. It is held in **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 18,
ed. khr. 51, ll. 1–2** and was printed by L. E. Shabaev in the almanac «Российская генеалогия»
(Russian Genealogy), issue 7, pp. 305–306.

The roll does indeed begin the family with a legendary émigré from Poland:

> «честен муж имянем Александр Волоцкий — прозвище Анфал».
>
> *Translation:* "an honourable man named Aleksandr Volotsky — by nickname Anfal."

But the subsequent generational scheme turned out not to be the one that could have been expected from
the surety record of 1563:

> «А у Никиты Александр. А у Александра Тихан — прозвище Рычко».
>
> *Translation:* "And Nikita had Aleksandr. And Aleksandr had Tikhan — by nickname Rychko."

That is, the late roll builds the chain **Aleksandr Anfal → Nikita → Aleksandr → Tikhan nicknamed
Rychko**. In it Rychko is the great-grandson of the declared founder and the son of Aleksandr. The
original surety record of 1563, by contrast, four times calls him **Rychko Onfalov/Anfalov, son of
Volotskoy**. These two formulas cannot be joined without an additional document.

The contradiction is not isolated. The roll declares Levash and Nikita to be sons of Aleksandr Anfal
and moves their death at Kazan to 1552. The Sinodik (commemoration book) preserves one **Levash
Nikitin syn Volotsky**, killed in the campaign of 1506. I. V. Zaitsev has already shown that the late
compiler split one man into two brothers, changed the kinship and moved the event by almost half a
century.

Consequently, the name Tikhon attached to Rychko may also be the late compiler's attempt to restore the
church name of a known ancestor, or it may refer to another Rychko Volotsky. The rare nickname and the
single family make the identity probable but not proven. The main practical consequence: **the Polish
chain is not a bridge to Oryol and does not prove who Rychko's father was**.

The roll itself admits that the earlier charters of grant and instructions allegedly «в Московское
разоренье утерялось» ("were lost in the Moscow devastation"). It also asserts that «окроме того роду
нашего по иным городам ни в каких чинех нет» ("apart from this, there are none of our family in other
towns in any ranks"). So searching for a move of the Volotskys to Oryol by this roll is unpromising;
what has to be checked are independent acts concerning the five declared sons of Tikhan Rychko: Eftifey
Posnik, Nikita, Aleksandr, Estegney Sutorma and Andrey.

The control card
`PUB-RUSGEN-1686-VOLOTSKY-ANFAL-RYCHKO-PEDIGREE` has been created; photocopies of printed pp. 305–306
have been saved. [Open publication of the roll](https://files.vgd.ru/170688/fwPjhLYvuWZRcTg7YsRmrS80vcfOh8ao/almanah-7.pdf).

### 1594/95: Ermak Pakhomovich Turns Out to Have a Possible Brother and an Earlier Land Layer

A repeated reading of Nougorsk stan gave a new link at the junction of two printed pages. Immediately
before the entry for Ermak Pakhomovich Anpilogov the scribe describes the holding of Danila Fyodorovich
Deriglazov:

> «что было въ помѣстьѣ за Малцомъ за Пахомовымъ въ дер. въ Малцовской».
>
> *Translation:* "which was in the pomestye of Malets Pakhomov in the village of Maltsovskaya."

On the next page stands:

> «За Ермакомъ за Пахомовымъ сыномъ Анпилогова въ той же дер. въ
> Малцовской».
>
> *Translation:* "[Held] by Ermak Pakhomov syn Anpilogov in the same village of Maltsovskaya."

The line about Malets was checked visually against an independent digital image of the РГБ, not just
against OCR. A full photocopy of p. 1015 has been saved in the project archive. **Malets** is a
documented secular personal name of the sixteenth century; in other acts one meets, for example, a
separate man Malets Timonin. So "Malets Pakhomov" is read as a name and a possible patronymic, not as
an explanation "a little boy".

Three features coincide: the father's name Pakhom, one village and the immediate sequence of land
entries. This makes Malets a probable second son of Pakhom and a possible brother of Ermak. Moreover,
his zherebye had passed to Deriglazov by the time of the census, while Ermak kept the neighbouring
family plot. So a separate Nougorsk line could have existed in Oryol district before the census and
need not be reduced to a lone appearance of Ermak.

But the evidentiary boundary remains strict. Malets is not called Anpilogov or Ermak's brother; the date
and reason for the transfer of the land are absent. The similarity of the name Malets and the place
name Maltsovskaya is tempting, but it does not prove that this particular man founded the village. No
separate profile and no point on the map are created.

The new priority document is an otkaznaya or allotment record by which Malets Pakhomov's zherebye passed
to Danila Deriglazov. Such a record may name the death or departure of the former holder, his heirs, the
date and earlier service. The find has been entered in the card `PUB-PKOU-1594-P1016-ERMAK` and in
Pakhom's profile.
[Page 1015 in the РГБ viewer](https://viewer.rsl.ru/ru/rsl01003862088?page=1016&rotate=0&theme=white),
[card of the РГО volume](https://elib.rgo.ru/handle/123456789/228023).

### The Deriglazovs Gave the First Multi-Generation Trace of the Oryol–Kursk Service Network

Danila Deriglazov's proximity to Malets Pakhomov's former land turned out not to be an isolated
episode. On the same printed p. 1015 the survey book shows one more, large family:

> «За вдовою за Парасковьею за Охремовою женою Дериглазова да за еѣ
> дѣтми за Гришкою, да за Ларкою, да за Ивашком за меншим…»
>
> *Translation:* "[Held] by the widow Paraskovya, wife of Okhrem Deriglazov, and by her children
> Grishka, and Larka, and Ivashko the younger…"

The next entry separately records **Ivashko the elder, Efremov syn Deriglazov**. Thus in Oryol in
1594/95 Efrem and Paraskovya Deriglazov are documented with at least four sons: Grigory, Larka, Ivan
the younger and Ivan the elder. Larka is the chancery form of the name Larion.

In the review list (razborny spisok) of Kursk deti boyarskie of 1622 **Grigory Larin syn Deriglazov**
was found. The assessors (okladchiki) reported for him a pomestye oklad of 200 chetverti and a money
oklad of six roubles; he actually held 75 chetverti of pomestye and one peasant household, and could
serve on a gelding with a pishchal (matchlock gun) and a sabre. The rare surname, the patronymic Larin
and a correct succession of generations give a strong model:

**Larka (Larion) Efremovich Deriglazov, Oryol 1594/95 → Grigory Larionovich Deriglazov, Kursk 1622.**

This is not yet proven kinship: the Kursk line does not call Grigory an Oryol man and makes no reference
to a father or an Oryol pomestye. But the model received a second independent control in the list of
1630. There the repetition is not isolated:

- l. 98 — Fyodor Grigoryevich Onpilogov; l. 99 — Grigory Larionovich
  Deriglazov, neighbouring folios of one entry at 200 chetverti each;
- l. 121 — Semyon Osipovich Onpilogov; l. 122 — Semyon Yakovlevich Onpilogov and, on the same folio,
  Fyodor Gavrilovich Deriglazov, one entry at 100 chetverti;
- l. 127 — Afanasy Fyodorovich Deriglazov and Avdey Olferyevich Bazdyrev in neighbouring positions;
  the Bazdyrevs belonged to the Oryol land cluster of the early Anfilogovs.

This is the first repeated recurrence not of one surname but of a whole Oryol neighbourhood complex
within the early Kursk service corporation. It supports a model in which, after the founding of Kursk,
already formed service networks, including Anpilogovs, moved south-east and reproduced themselves.
But there are as yet no lines «сошёл с Орла» ("came down from Oryol") for these people; a shared oklad
entry is not the same as a resettlement party. So no Oryol–Kursk route is drawn on the map.

The priority now is not general surname lists but the original Kursk pomestye grants of two specific
people: Grigory Larin syn Deriglazov and Fyodor Grigoryev syn Onpilogov. Needed are РГАДА, f. 210,
op. 4, d. 300, l. 6 v. and f. 210, op. 12, d. 7, ll. 98–99, 121–122, 127. A formula naming an earlier
town, a father's grant or a surety could turn the network trace into a proven transition.

The find has been entered in the Record
`PUB-OREL-1594-KURSK-1622-1630-ANPILOGOV-DERIGLAZOV-NETWORK` and in the profiles of Fyodor
Grigoryevich, Semyon Osipovich and Semyon Yakovlevich Onpilogov.
[Kursk desyatnya of 1622](https://old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/ds2603111.html),
[Kursk list of 1630](https://www.old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/ku260428.html).

### The Leading Corridor as of 28 August: Pekhletsky Stan — Ryazhsk — Oryol

After the check of neighbouring surnames this path can be ranked as **the strongest geographical
candidate**, but it cannot yet be called the place of origin of Anfilogovs. Its strength comes not
from a single similarity of sound but from four different types of observation:

- Pyotr Nikiforovich Bazdyrev, also called Sviridov, in 1591 was directly re-registered for service
  from Ryazan, Pekhletsky stan, into Ryazhsk;

- the payment book of 1594–1597 independently continues him as Petrushka Nikiforov syn Sviridov with
  land in Myakishevo;

- the Ryazhsk–Pekhlets men Radko Dmitrievich Pakhomov, Senka Ivanovich Novikov and Onashka Vasilyevich
  Sviridov have parallels in Oryol bearing the same family namings — Danila Pakhomov, Pavel Novikov
  and Kurdyum Sviridov; one more such group is formed by the Ovdokimovs;

- the Oryol Nikifor Oksyonovich Bazdyrev held his father's pomestye precisely in the village of
  Bazdyrevo, where five Anfilogovs lived.

The boundary of the conclusion is fundamental. Pyotr is not called the son of the Oryol Nikifor
specifically, the possible pairs are not called brothers, and Anfilogovs have not been found in the
Ryazan books checked. Even proven kinship of the Bazdyrevs would still allow not only a path to Oryol
but also dispersal from an earlier Oryol nest, the division of one family between towns, or the
retention of old land after a change of service.

A repeated check of the live database «Дом предков» (Dom predkov) refined one more methodological
boundary. The search gives **17 rows for «Дериглаз»** (Deriglaz), starting from about 1631, and **68
rows for «Баздыр»** (Bazdyr), starting from 1614. But the combination of the surname **Pakhomov** with
the year `159` gives zero rows; the same combination for **Sviridov** also gives zero. Meanwhile the
published and checked texts directly name Radko Pakhomov and Onashka Sviridov in 1591 and repeat their
land entries in 1594–1597. Consequently, the database shows the later continuation of the network well,
but does not contain the full Ryazan–Ryazhsk layer of the sixteenth century and cannot serve as a
negative check of this direction.

A new catalogue address shows where else documents of the original recruitment may be hiding. In
**РГАДА, f. 281, op. 17, d. 12222** lies an extract of 20 March 1700 in a land case whose heading
directly recalls villages taken in 1591/92 **«к Ряскому городу новоприборным казаком»** ("for the
town of Ryazhsk, for newly recruited Cossacks"). The same extract includes a summary of charters of
1592 and 1597. This is neither a route of Anfilogovs nor an Oryol act: not a single bearer of the
researched surname is named in the description. But the find proves an important preservation model:
the circumstances of a sixteenth-century recruitment and land grant could survive inside a lawsuit and
an official extract drawn up more than a hundred years later. So for Oryol one has to look through not
only desyatni but also late disputes over old pomestya, even if the surname and the word "Oryol" are
absent from the brief heading.
[Card of the file in the ГИС УИАД](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000228014/10000000652014/10000045661014/).

The next decisive search is now addressed: extracts from the lost books of Mikhail Ozerov of 1573/74
and the early entries of Nikifor, Oksyon and Pyotr Bazdyrev-Sviridov together with the Novikovs,
Pakhomovs and Sviridovs. What is needed is a direct formula of an earlier town, brotherhood, shared
land or the initial Oryol grant. The corridor is not yet transferred to the map.

### The Ozerov Extract Found in Full — and It Changes Where to Look for Oryol Charters

The complete first and third volumes of «Акты служилых землевладельцев» (Acts of Service Landholders)
made it possible to check all three officially cited attestations of the lost books of Mikhail Ozerov
and the podyachy Krestyanin Petrov of 1573/74. This is no longer a retelling of a catalogue.
Photocopies of printed page 285 of the first volume and pages 211–212 and 314–315 of the third have
been saved in the project's closed research archive; they are not shown on the public site because of
the rights to scholarly editions.

**Act I No. 293** shows a supplementary pomestye grant (doispomeshchenie) to a man after Crimean
captivity. On 17 November 1586 Ivan Vasilyev syn Chaplygin received in Korost, also called Ivkino,
100 chetverti of ploughland and fallow (perelog) on top of his earlier 36 chetverti, against a pomestye
oklad of 350 chetverti. But the land was not a nameless waste: this zherebye had earlier been the
votchina of the elder (monk) Misail Petrov, and the charter says directly that the votchina was ordered
to be taken from Misail. The shares of the newly baptised Ivashka and Karpik Zykov in the same village
are separately excluded from the grant. The size of the plot is confirmed by a reference to the books
of Ozerov and Krestyanin Petrov.

This is a fourth important mechanism: the state could turn a confiscated votchina into a service
pomestye and top up a returned captive (polonyanik) to his oklad. The formula «крымский полоняник»
("Crimean captive") tells of Ivan's fate, but not of his earlier town or birthplace. So the act explains
how the corporation was staffed, but gives no route to Oryol. The original is known through a late
transmission: РГАДА, f. 286, op. 2, kn. 28, ll. 394–394 v.; a copy of 1776 from a copy of 1686–1688.

**Act No. 259** is a genuine extract from the inspection (dozornye) books for the pomestye of Andrey
Mikulin in the villages of Volkovo and Moshkino in Kamensky stan. In Volkovo Andrey himself had a
landholder's household, a peasant household, 25 chetverti and 40 kopny (stacks) of hay. There too
Kuzma Slotov held 50 chetverti and 100 kopny in total; by the grant of 1574/75 his pomestye was
transferred to Andrey. In Moshkino Tarasko Monulkov held a household, 15 chetverti and 15 kopny; by the
grant of 1575/76 this plot also passed to Andrey. At the same time he received the 35 chetverti of
Danilka Kozheretsky. The summary entry directly totals **150 chetverti** in two villages for Andrey
Mikulin.

This is an important example not of migration but of the **assembly of a new service pomestye**:
the plots of at least three former holders were consolidated in two years under one recipient. The
archival original of the extract is РГАДА, f. 1209, stolbtsy (scroll files) for Ryazhsk,
No. 1193/42611; the editors date the extract itself to 1578–1593. The similarity of "Mikulin" to the
Oryol "Mikulin deti Anfilogovy" (sons of Mikula Anfilogov) is not taken as kinship: for the Oryol
brothers Mikula is the personal name of the common father, while here Mikulin is Andrey's independent
second naming.

**Act No. 389** gave an even more useful methodological result. In August 1587 an extract from Ozerov's
books «слово в слово» ("word for word") was sent to Ryazhsk with an order to allot to Bogdan Obryutin
syn Skobeev 50 chetverti in the village of Nazemnaya. But the editors' note reconstructs the
continuation of the story from a sovereign's case of 1638. Bogdan's son Ivashko submitted to the case
**his father's allotment charter (otdelnaya gramota) of 1587** signed by the dyak Elizar Vyluzgin. He
explained that the charter had been with his father in Pronsk during the «межусобной брани» ("internecine
strife"), because of the devastation had remained without a seal, was then kept by Bogdan and later
obtained by the son from the scribe Grigory Kireevsky.

So a sixteenth-century title document could survive the fire of a prikaz archive in **private family
keeping** and appear in a land case 51 years later. This changes the next priority for Oryol. Looking
only for the lost first allotment book is not enough. One must look through the property lawsuits of
the children and grandchildren of the first Oryol landholders from the 1620s–1640s: original allotment,
vvoznye (installation) and charters of grant, extracts from old books, and explanations of where the
document had been kept during the Time of Troubles may have been attached to the petitions.

The first addressed circle is the files of Anfilogovs and their land neighbours in the villages of
Bazdyrevo, Belaya and Maltsovskaya: the Bazdyrevs, Deriglazovs and Trunovs. The formulas sought are
«положил в дело грамоту» ("submitted a charter to the case"), «отдельная грамота» ("allotment
charter"), «по старым орловским книгам» ("according to the old Oryol books"), «отцовское поместье»
("father's pomestye"), «дано против старого поместья» ("granted in place of the old pomestye") and an
indication of the earlier town of service. All three known Ozerov acts are now closed by their full
text; the next step is to look not for repeated retellings of these numbers but for other extracts and
property files in which such charters were presented. The map does not change: the new acts prove the
mechanism of redistribution and the way documents of unrelated families were preserved, but not a route
of Anfilogovs.

### The Kursk Provincial Archive Checked: the Proofs of the Ancient Branch Went to the Senate

The official seven-folio inventory **ГАКО, f. 1436, op. 1** made it possible to check the local fond of
the Kursk Noble Deputies' Assembly in full. The result turned out to be more important in terms of
source criticism than a single surname match. The fond preserves only **19 scattered files of
1760–1864**; there are no proceedings for the Bazdyrevs and no entries of 1828 or 1835 among them. The
preface records extreme fragmentation: on 23 January 1951 only 17 files were listed, after transfers
between fonds 15 remained, and the current 19 units arose after one composite file was reprocessed and
divided in 2001.

This absence cannot be turned into a conclusion that the Bazdyrevs did not submit proofs of ancient
lineage. The same official inventory describes the procedure: the provincial genealogical book was read
out at the noble assembly, and after approval **two copies** were made — one was handed to the
provincial administration, the other was sent **to the Senate for keeping in the Heraldry Office**. So
the late Senate complex in the РГИА is not an accidental duplicate: after the loss of almost the entire
local fond it becomes the main surviving channel to the proofs of the sixth-part (ancient nobility)
branch of the Bazdyrevs.

The priority has been refined. There is no longer any need to order ГАКО, f. 1436: the open inventory
exhausts its contents. First needed is **РГИА, f. 1343, op. 17, d. 307**: the internal inventory, the
petition, the generational roll and the attachments. The test keys are **Yakov Semyonovich Bazdyrev**,
the provincial decision of 24 June 1835, the entries in part VI of 1828 and 1835, and any old charters
mentioning Bazdyrevo, Minov les (Minov Wood), Korchakov stan or an earlier town of service. File 308 of
1859 remains second: by chronology it more probably belongs to the late part-II family of Dementy
Ivanovich.

[Official page of the ГАКО inventories](https://archive.rkursk.ru/gako/dorev_opisi/),
[ГАКО inventory, f. 1436](https://archive.rkursk.ru/sites/default/files/Opisi/1_-_dorev/f1436.pdf),
[the Bazdyrevs' file No. 307 in the РГИА](https://fgurgia.ru/object/6904559).

### From the Oryol Olfer to the Oboyan Mikifor: an Address Key to the Bazdyrevs' File Found

A check of Kursk–Oboyan publications gave not a ready-made genealogy, but for the first time assembled
a chain of names by which file No. 307 can be read. In Oryol in 1625 **Olfer Bazdyrev** is named, and in
the list of 1627/28 his formula is revealed as **Olferko Maksimov syn Bazdyrev**. In Kursk in 1630
**Avdeyko Olferyev syn Bazdyrev** is recorded. Finally, the officially published Oboyan desyatnya of
1651 on l. 25 v. names **Mikifor Olferyev syn Bazdyrev** and his two sons — **Ivashka, aged 15**, and
**Ermoshka, aged 13**. Mikifor appeared on a gelding with a pishchal; his guarantors were Klementey
Derensky, Ivan Maltsov and Mikhail Babenok.

The geography here is fundamental. Oboyan was built in 1649, and the first description of the district
says directly that long-enrolled Kursk and Oryol men were transferred to the new town. So the appearance
of a son of Olfer Bazdyrev in Oboyan is not a chance distant duplicate but a historically possible
continuation of an Oryol–Kursk service family. Later records show the stability of the branch: in 1684
**Terentey Mikiforov syn Bazdyrev** owned a household in the village of Dmitrovskoe in Rudavsky stan; in
1686 **Samson Ivanov syn Bazdyrev** is named together with his father Ivan; in the alphabet of the
Oboyan skazki of 1697 the surname is present again.

The degree of proof must be held strictly. Not a single open act calls Avdey and Mikifor brothers or
says that their father was the Oryol Olfer Maksimovich specifically. Nor is the descent of the late
Yakov Semyonovich from this branch proven. But now the file РГИА, f. 1343, op. 17, d. 307 has address
keys: **Olfer — Mikifor — Ivan / Ermolay / Terenty**, the towns **Oryol — Kursk — Oboyan** and **the
village of Dmitrovskoe**. If Yakov Semyonovich's generational roll leads to this cluster, the Senate file
may preserve copies of lost Oryol documents of the seventeenth century.

This is an outgoing movement of a neighbouring surname, not a found path of Anfilogovs into Oryol. So
no public map entry and no profiles of the Bazdyrevs are created.

[Oboyan desyatnya of 1651 — official publication of the Archival Administration of Kursk Oblast](https://archive.rkursk.ru/sites/default/files/documents/event_hum_ed_15.pdf),
[first description of Oboyan district, 1649](https://old-kursk.ru/book/babin/190127.html),
[Tankov, «Историческая летопись курского дворянства» (Historical Chronicle of the Kursk Nobility)](https://www.old-kursk.ru/book/tankov/index.html),
[Oboyan skazki of 1697](https://www.otkudarodom.ua/ru/1697-god-skazki-detey-boyarskih-strelcov-kazakov-i-pushkarey-goroda-oboyani).

### The Mitusovs: a Direct Belyov and a Disputed Reading of the Oryol Zaimishche

The Epifan desyatnya of 1591 gave a new independent parallel to the mechanism of staffing early Oryol.
In the heading of men who had left the Epifan or
Venyov service, there stand in succession **Danilo Parfenyev syn Mitusov** and **Dey Parfenyev syn
Mitusov**. The summary note refers to entries 351–360 and says directly: **«служат из Белева»**
("they serve from Belyov"). Both are then repeated among the noviki of the enrolment of 1583/84 who had
not been entered in the Moscow Razryad list. Thus the source establishes their Belyov service by 1591.
The identical patronymic and surname form make them probable brothers, but the direct word "brothers"
is not in the text.

The edition of the copy of the Oryol book of 1594/95 preserves a third name on p. 879. The land of Ovonya
Ignatyev syn Retkansky is described as **«что было займище Гриши Козлова да Васки Митусова»** ("which was
the zaimishche of Grisha Kozlov and Vaska Mitusov") in the village of Gustovari in Nepolotsky stan.
Vaska is no longer the current holder in the entry, but his name shows his participation in the
clearing or claiming of this plot before the survey description.

Now the obligatory control has been found. On the official image of the historical inventory **РГАДА,
f. 1209, op. 85, d. 13, l. 241**, entry No. 704, the same land memorandum is copied as **«в займище
Гриши Козлова да Васьки Титулова»** ("in the zaimishche of Grisha Kozlov and Vaska Titulov"). The
modern forum transcription made no mistake: in the manuscript of the register *Титулова* (Titulova) is
indeed clearly legible. But this is not the book of 1625 itself, but a late abbreviated register to it.
Its compiler also made other distortions — for example, he called the scribe Ivan Kutuzov Ivan Koptev.
The 1877 edition also reproduces a copy, not the field original of 1594/95. So there is no single winner
yet: Vaska's surname must be recorded as **Mitusov / Titulov, disputed reading**.

The link **Belyov — Epifan/Venyov — early Oryol** is thereby downgraded from a documented surname
parallel to a working hypothesis. Only the Belyov service of Danila and Dey Mitusov and the memory of
Vaska as a former participant in the Oryol zaimishche are firm; the coincidence of their surname is no
longer proven. This is an important methodological result for the search for Anfilogovs themselves:
a late register can confidently change a surname, so an inter-town route must not be built on one index
or one copy.

The first next address is the full text of **kn. 330** around Gustovari, or a third independent extract
from the Oryol book capable of deciding Mitusov/Titulov. Only after that does it make sense to return to
the search for Parfeny, Danila, Dey and Vasily Mitusov in Belyov lawsuits. The Mitusovs do not belong to
the researched surname series, so no separate public profiles and no route Belyov — Oryol are created.

[Official image of the historical inventory, file 0244](http://rgada.info/dela/1209/1/1209-opis_13i/0244.jpg).
[Public register with the archival call number and a warning about its errors](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1851/161210/p5099960.htm).

#### The Full Belyov Book Is Lost, but Its Extracts Survived the Time of Troubles

A check of the official catalogue of survey books and of two pre-revolutionary publications clarified
what exactly has survived of the Belyov descriptions of the 1580s. The catalogue knows two works: the
description of the court (dvortsovye) lands by Nikita Grigoryevich Rzhevsky in 1583/84–1584/85 and the
book of Savin Grigoryevich Pleshcheev, Mikhail Avksentyevich Zybin and the podyachy Mikhail Nikitin of
1584/85. The full books are not listed as surviving manuscripts; they appear through later mentions.

However, this is not a dead end. In the first issue of S. A. Shumakov's «Сотницы, грамоты и записи»
(Sotnitsy, Charters and Records) two genuine derivative copies were found and checked. **No. XVII** on
printed p. 91 begins with the formula «Лета 7093-го. Выпись из белевских книг письма и меры Савина
Григорьевича Плещеева да Михаила Оксентьевича Зыбина да подьячего Михаила Никитина» ("In the year 7093.
An extract from the Belyov books of the survey and measurement of Savin Grigoryevich Pleshcheev and
Mikhail Oksentyevich Zybin and the podyachy Mikhail Nikitin") and describes the votchiny of the Belyov
Monastery of the Saviour. **No. XVIII** on p. 95 is a quitrent (obrochnaya) extract of Nikita Rzhevsky and
his colleagues to the same monastery for a stretch of the Oka and fisheries. Both records are narrow and
are not lists of Belyov deti boyarskie. The Mitusovs and the forms Anfilogov/Anpilogov were not found in
them, but this zero cannot be extended to the whole district.

The decisive formula was found in «Белёвская вивлиофика» (Belyov Bibliotheca), vol. 2, on printed
pp. 143–144. During the proceedings of 1627–1630 church people presented to the scribes **seven old
extracts**, among them «выпись Савина Плещеева с товарищи 193 года» ("the extract of Savin Pleshcheev
and colleagues of the year [7]193") and «две выписи Микиты Ржевского с товарищи 192 года, 193 г.» ("two
extracts of Mikita Rzhevsky and colleagues of the years [7]192 and [7]193"). So four decades after the
books were compiled, their individual entries still lived on in private and church keeping and were used
as proofs of land. Photocopies of the four key pages have been saved in the project archive.

The official catalogue reference to **АСЗ, vol. III, No. 521** has also been opened. This is a file of
the Pomestny prikaz of February — 5 March 1597 concerning the Belyov pomestya of the okolnichy Semyon
Fyodorovich Saburov, Oksinya, wife of Posnik Chelyustkin, and Anastasia, widow of the okolnichy Ivan
Ivanovich Saburov; the text is on pp. 441–443. According to the available search fragments, on p. 442
one more extract from the Belyov books is used — of the survey of Bogdan Polev — for the pomestye of the
Lithuanian Yury Ivanov syn Podolsky in the village of Verkhnie Luchki. On the next page Belyov chetverti
are added to pomestya in Zubtsov, Rzhev and Venyov. A full page-by-page copy of the act still has to be
obtained: the search fragments do not allow every name and holding to be checked.

The tactic now changes definitively. The Belyov layer sought should be looked for in sotnitsy, monastery
and church extracts, files of the Pomestny prikaz and lawsuits of the children and grandchildren of
holders of the 1590s–1640s. It is precisely there that the entries of Parfeny, Danila, Dey or Vasily
Mitusov and the original charters of the neighbours of the first Oryol Anfilogovs could have been
submitted to a case. A public card for the Mitusovs has been created as a research Record; profiles of
people and a route on the map are not created until there is direct proof of kinship or transition.

[Epifan desyatnya of 1591](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Desjatni_XVI_v/text5.htm),
[Oryol survey book, p. 879](https://archive.org/details/moscha-regia/page/n879/mode/1up),
[catalogue of РГАДА survey books](https://rgada.info/materiali/baranov_catalog.pdf),
[«Сотницы, грамоты и записи» (Sotnitsy, Charters and Records), issue 1](https://www.koob.ru/shumakov_s_a/shumakov_sotnicy_gramoty_t1),
[«Белёвская вивлиофика» (Belyov Bibliotheca), vol. 2](https://www.koob.ru/elagin_n_v/elagin_belevskaya_vivliofika_t2),
[АСЗ, vol. III in Google Books](https://books.google.com/books?id=4WDuAAAAMAAJ).
### A Full-Text Check of the Inventories: The 1577 List Has Not Surfaced

The verdicts (prigovory) of 1577 document not one but two closely related name complexes.
The February order required that deti boyarskie be selected in winter for three field
shifts, that their names be written down by entries (stati) and that the lists be kept in the
Razryad. The March verdict prescribed a personal inspection of the Oryol Cossacks and the
deti boyarskie with small pomestyes, the compiling of separate lists with their land and money
entitlements (oklady) and the delivery of these lists to the Moscow clerks (dyaki). A full
reading of the Watch Book (Storozhevaya kniga) — РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6, d. 1 — showed that
none of these executive lists was bound in after the corresponding verdict.

On 28 August 2026 an additional full-text check was carried out through
[РГАДА Search](https://rgada.sovadev.pro/), which indexes the published inventories (opisi)
and the OCR-recognised PDFs of РГАДА. In fonds 210, 1209 and 396, for the early period, the
forms Анпилог, Анфилог, Онфилог, Онпилог and Ампилог were checked, as were the queries
«Орловский список» ("the Oryol list"), «орловскому списку» ("to the Oryol list"), «Пётр
Данилов» ("Pyotr Danilov"), the number 37573 and the rare family cluster Mikula — Vasily —
Timofey — Karp. No new early match and no call number for the Oryol list was found. The form
«Анпилов» (Anpilov) leads only to Chernov's later index, which has already been taken into account.

This is a meaningful negative result for the catalogues only. The service does not
read undigitised manuscripts, so the absence of a match cannot be taken as
proof that the list perished. Separately checked was
[the official viewer for file 210-9-232](http://rgada.info/kueh/index2.php?str=210_9_232):
the page for the section exists, but it contains no digital frames. The unread junction at
fols. 315 v.–322 can be checked only with an archival copy.

At this stage the order of targeted requests was as follows; the later collation with the
1914 edition demoted the first item to a control reading and moved to first
place the independent name registers, the documents of earlier land grants (dachi) and the complete
Oryol books of 1624–1626:

1. РГАДА, f. 210, op. 9, d. 232, fols. 315 v.–322 — by a control reading,
   record the physical junction between the broken-off roster (rospis) and the next document;
   a complete name list is no longer expected on these folios.
2. РГАДА, f. 396, op. 1, pt. 24, d. 37573 — obtain the confirmed file
   in full: the large complex may
   contain extracts from the Oryol search list (sysknoy spisok) of 1613/14 and earlier
   proofs of service.
3. РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, bks. 330–331 — read the full entries that use the
   old copy of the survey and measurement of 1594/95, especially the earlier holdings of Mikula,
   Vasily, Timofey, Nechay, Pakhom, Persha and Terenty.
4. In the papers of the Razryad and the Pomestny Prikaz, search for the two independent
   complexes of 1577: the lists of the three field entries and the lists of the personal inspection with
   the increase of Cossack pomestyes from 20 to 50 chetverts.

Neither a profile nor a map route is created from this result: the name of
an Anfilogov in the early list has not yet been read, and the family's earlier town has not been
established.

### Command Geography Has Yielded a Ranked Circle of Old Towns

Changing the unit of search worked once again. Instead of the surname, the whole roster
of the Oryol siege heads (osadnye golovy) was checked using A. Yu. Savosichev's article «Орловские воеводы» ("The Oryol Voivodes").
A siege head was the commander of the garrison and the commandant of the fortress, so his
papers could include the admission of men, inspections, failures to appear and local lists.

In the boyar list of 1577 the author found nine Oryol siege heads. All
of them were selected deti boyarskie (vybornye) of old towns: **Dorogobuzh — one,
Kashira — three, Meshchovsk — two, Ryazan — two, Tula — one**. In the lists of
1588/89, 1598/99 and 1602/03, Kashira, Tula and Ryazan recur; later
Kaluga, Rzhev and Yuryev-Polskoy are added. This is not a route of Anfilogovs, but
a stable administrative network rather than the chance appointment of one man.

The full boyar list and the annual Oryol rosters made it possible to identify these
nine by name. They are **Elka (Elizary) Leontyev syn Rzhevskoy** of Dorogobuzh;
the Kashira men **Ivan Timofeev syn Fustov, Ivan Sudokov syn Myasnogo and Grigory
Fyodorov Andreeva-Pavlov**; the Ryazan men **Mikhail Nazaryev Glebov and Andrey Yakovlev
syn Izmaylov**; the Tula man **Denis Fyodorov syn Ivashkin**; and the Meshchovsk brothers
**Pyotr Menshoy and Bolanda, the sons of Grigory Sovin**. Their Oryol service
stretches from 1571 to 1585: this is an overlap of careers with the list of selected men, not
the garrison of a single day.

A check of the marginal notes gave a substantial correction to the meaning of the find. Only Grigory
Andreev-Pavlov has **«на Орле»** ("at Oryol") in the list itself. Izmaylov is noted at that time
in Pronsk, Ivashkin at Tula, Rzhevskoy in Crimea, Myasnoy with the Cossacks, and
the Sovins with a foreigner (nemchin) and in the streltsy patrol. Consequently, the town heading
shows the old service corporation, the marginal note the current
posting, and an Oryol line from another year a separate stage of the career. This is
a circulation of cadres along the frontier, not a joint resettlement of nine men.

The article at the same time guards against too direct a conclusion. Six Oryol
heads are not found in the main lists of the Sovereign's Court; Afanasy Zhilin was
himself an Oryol landholder, and for the Kandaurovs, Lamonovs and Trubitsyns the author
sees local landed or family continuations. Consequently, two personnel channels
coexisted in Oryol: a selected syn boyarsky sent from an
old town, and a man of the already established Oryol corporation. This agrees well
with the order of 1577, which required that fit men be recruited from among
the rank-and-file Cossacks and the deti boyarskie with small pomestyes directly at Oryol.

The practical priority is now uneven. In first place stand
the **Kashira** surety records (poruchnye zapisi), acknowledgements of receipt (priyomnye otpiski) and the papers on failures to appear of 1576–1578;
then those of Tula, Ryazan and Meshchovsk. Solova/Krapivna keeps a
separate high priority because of the family structure of the noviki of 1596: this is
a different line of proof, and it must not be mixed with the origin of the commanders.
Card:
`PUB-SAVOSICHEV-2015-OREL-COMMAND-CADRE-CORRIDORS`.

[Open-access PDF of the article, pp. 41–51](https://okmuseum.ru/wp-content/uploads/2021/08/12-kraevedcheskie-zapiski.pdf).
[Publication of the 1577 boyar list](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/21-40/26.htm).

### A Pre-Oryol Network Has Turned Up in the Original Surety Records

The search by the heads' full names led out of the service registers into another type of source.
The original surety records show the same men **before Oryol was founded**. In
1562 Ivan Timofeev syn Fustov appears in one large act together with the future
Oryol head Mikhail Nazaryev Glebov and Ivan Grigoryev Sovin.
The latter belongs to the same family naming as the Oryol heads
Pyotr Menshoy and Bolanda Grigoryev Sovin; direct kinship is still being checked.

The act of 20 April 1563 is especially important. In one list of 102 guarantors
there are named together **Dmitry Fyodorov syn Turgenev, Mikhail Kulnev, Rychko
Onfalov/Anfalov syn Volotskogo, Andrey Yakovlev syn Izmaylov and Ivan Andreev syn
Fefilatyev**. Turgenev is the strongest candidate for the Cossack head
«на Орле» ("at Oryol") of 1564/65; Izmaylov and Fefilatyev later undoubtedly held command
in Oryol in 1576 and 1585. Therefore the rare patronymic naming
«Онфалов сын» ("son of Onfal") now stands inside a whole pre-Oryol command body,
and not next to one chance Oryol man.

The next act, drawn up around 28 March 1565, a year before the fortress was founded,
preserves the old attributions: **Mikhail Kulnev — with a pomestye in
Meshchovsk, Posol Ivashkin — a man of Tula, Ivan Fustov — a man of Kashira, Andrey Izmaylov
with his brother — men of Ryazan**. This is an almost exact pre-Oryol anticipation of the geography
of the siege heads. Kashira, Ryazan, Tula and Meshchovsk were connected by a common service
milieu even before Oryol appeared.

But a surety record is not a list of settlers. It shows men known
to the court and able to stand guarantee for a prince for large sums of money. From this milieu
the Razryad chose the commanders for new fortresses; the rank and file could be recruited
separately. So the new two-stage model is as follows:

1. the central multi-town network supplied Oryol with commanders;
2. at a specific posting a separate list of the men accepted,
   enrolled (poverstannye) or transferred was compiled, and it has to be sought in the executive
   papers of that commander.

Card created:
`PUB-PORUCHNYE-1562-1571-PRE-OREL-CADRE-NETWORK`. The map does not change:
Rychko Volotskoy is not called a man of Oryol, Anfal has not yet been proved to be a form of Anfilog, and
the service milieu of outsiders is not a route of the surname.

[Full scholarly publication of the surety records](https://vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1520-1540/Porucn_zapisi_1527_1571/text.phtml).

### Not the City of Moscow but the Southern Service Belt: Where Oryol Was Most Likely Recruited From

For 1566–1577 it is more accurate to speak no longer of the Grand Principality of Moscow but of
the Muscovite state and its Razryad Prikaz. **Moscow was the centre of
decision and record-keeping**, but this does not mean that the first men of Oryol came from
Moscow or the central districts. Oryol was built as a frontier fortress, and
the most natural reserve was the men of the already active southern service
belt.

On the available data, the most likely mechanism is not one mass transfer but a mixed
one. The surviving book of Epifan, founded at the same time, shows it
literally: written side by side are Karachevets, Rezantsovy, Dedilovsky, Krapivensky,
Kolomnetin and Medynets, as well as «новоприходцы» ("newcomers") and places for Cossacks not yet
recruited. Sixteenth-century materials separately know the voluntary **«переписка» (re-registration) of service
from one town to another** and transfer by government order.
After the move a man was enrolled (verstan) afresh and given a pomestye in the new district.

The current ranking of directions is as follows:

1. **Pekhlets stan of Ryazan district → Ryazhsk → Oryol** — the strongest
   network corridor. Pyotr Nikiforovich Bazdyrev-Sviridov documentably
   re-registered from Pekhlets stan to service in Ryazhsk; the Bazdyrevs, Sviridovs,
   Pakhomovs, Novikovs and Ovdokimovs give several parallels with the same patronymics
   to the Oryol circle. But there are as yet no Anfilogovs in the Ryazan books checked.
2. **Dedilov — Tula → Oryol** — the strongest individual trace. In
   Dedilov in 1588/89 there served the brothers Ivan Nechaev, a Cossack desyatsky (head of ten), and
   Pankrat Nechaev, a newly recruited Cossack. In Oryol in 1595 Ivan
   Nechaevich Anpilogov appears. The rare name and patronymic, the generation and the type of
   service coincide, but the Dedilov Ivan is not called Anpilogov, so the identity is not yet
   proved.
3. **Karachev — Bolkhov — Mtsensk — Novosil — Belyov — Odoev** — the main
   Upper Oka belt. These corporations were already being combined in joint field postings
   before Oryol was founded. The western cluster of the eight early Anfilogovs lay on the
   Karachev side, and later documents directly show a possible
   mechanism: «изо Мценска на Орёл жить шёл» ("went from Mtsensk to live at Oryol") and the organised transfer of the
   Mtsensk streltsy. These direct examples belong to the seventeenth century, so they
   prove the practice, but not the family's route in the sixteenth century.

**Moscow, Vladimir and more distant service centres are not excluded**, but for now
they are weaker. The full list of 1,849 men of the Sovereign's Court of 1573 yielded no
Anfilogovs. The neighbouring surname of the Trunovs shows that one man could be
registered as a Kazan zhilets and hold land in Vladimir district, that is, the
service geography really could be distant and complex. But for the
family under research itself there is no such line yet.

Interim conclusion: the most likely formula is **"recruitment by the Razryad from the
frontier towns of the Upper Oka and the Ryazan–Tula belt"**, not
"resettlement from Moscow". It explains both the guarding of the frontier and the mixed make-up of
the new town. This is a working search model, not a finished family route; the map
and the profiles do not change until the earlier town is named directly.

#### An Important Correction to the Verdict of 15 March 1577

A repeated literal reading of the whole act refined the limits of the conclusion. The number **420** is
the shortfall against the planned strength of 500 Oryol mounted Cossacks, not an order to
transfer 420 men from Moscow or another town. The verdict ordered unfit and
horseless watchmen to be replaced by fit mounted men **«из рядовых казаков»** ("from the rank-and-file Cossacks").
Since after 78 watchmen had been assigned only two rank-and-file
Cossacks remained in Oryol, the shortfall in watchmen and in field (polskie) missions was to be covered
by deti boyarskie with small pomestyes — just as in Mtsensk and Karachev.

The names of the selected Cossacks and deti boyarskie were indeed to be written down
«подлинно порознь» ("exactly, separately") for each town, provided with land and money entitlements
and brought to the Razryad office (Razryadnaya izba). But this was **a list of local service selection**,
not a register of the towns of origin of new settlers. If the Oryol
roster being sought is found, it will prove a man's service from Oryol in 1577; his earlier
town will be revealed only by a special note on transfer, an old pomestye or
previous service.

The act at the same time lists Shatsk, Ryazhsk, Dankov, Epifan, Dedilov,
Krapivna, Novosil and Oryol, but requires their lists to be compiled **separately**.
The proximity of towns in a common roster must not be turned into a route between them.
Thus the hypothesis of recruitment from the southern service belt remains strong on
the general mechanism and on individual proven transfers, but the verdict
of 1577 itself does not prove it.

[Full text of the boyar verdict](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/21-40/23.php).
## 28 August 2026: The РГИА Name Index Has Led to the Kursk and Chernigov Branches

The official name search of the РГИА for the form «Анфилогов» (Anfilogov) yielded twenty cards with
exact references to the folios of fond 1343, inventory 51. For the main file on nobility,
f. 1343, op. 16, d. 2345, there are still no digital folios in the catalogue: the card
returns `images = 0`, `files = 0`, `downloadPdf = false`, and the field for the availability of
scans is empty. On the other hand, two index cards lead to d. 607, a public copy
of which is available on velikie.org.

On fol. 472, in entry No. 7400 of 13 April 1827, there is printed:
«Анфилоговыхъ» ("of Anfilogovs"), «Курской» ("of Kursk [province]"), «О Карпинѣ» ("concerning Karpin"). The official index abbreviates
the person as «Анфилогов К.» ("Anfilogov K."). A cautious profile of Karpin
Anfilogov has been created for the project; the rare nominative form is not replaced with Karp without a documentary
file.

On fol. 519, in entry No. 8137 of 30 May 1828, there is printed:
«Анфилоговыхъ» ("of Anfilogovs"), «Черниговской» ("of Chernigov [province]"), «О Ильѣ» ("concerning Ilya"), «По Высоч. повел. 1827» ("By Imperial command of 1827").
The official index shows «Анфилогов П.» ("Anfilogov P."), but the primary scan clearly
names Ilya. A profile of Ilya Anfilogov has been created; the index's "P." is kept only
as an erroneous or doubtful external rendering.

The Chernigov entry matters more than the initials themselves: it documents yet another
provincial noble circle of the surname and supports the working model of several
branches. It is not an answer to the question of where the first Oryol Anfilogovs came from, but
a strong warning against automatically reducing all bearers of the surname
to one Oryol root. The next address is the Chernigov proceedings under the
Imperial command of 1827 and the documentary file of Karpin of Kursk.

## 28 August 2026: The First Proven Personal Route into Early Oryol — Kashira → Oryol

A change of tactics gave the first direct result. Instead of yet another sweep through one
surname, the administrative vocabulary of owners' departure was checked in the full
text of the cadastral survey books: «испомещён» ("granted a pomestye"), «переведён» ("transferred"), a new place of service. In
the Kashira book of 1578–1579 a literal line was found:

> «Пуст. Нечаевская, что было въ помѣстьѣ за Филипомъ за Федоровымъ сыномъ
> Теплово, а Филипъ испомѣщенъ въ Орлѣ...»
>
> *Translation:* "The waste plot (pustosh) Nechaevskaya, which was in the pomestye of Filip Fyodorov syn
> Teplovo, and Filip has been granted a pomestye at Oryol..."

Source: «Писцовые книги Московского государства» (Cadastral Survey Books of the Muscovite State), pt. I, sec. 2, St Petersburg,
1877, p. 1528. This is not a historian's later guess and not a coincidence of surnames, but
the scribe's own explanation of why the former Kashira pomestye is no longer registered
to its holder.

In the Oryol book of 1594/95, on p. 885, the same man is found with the full
three-part formula of his name:

> «За Филипомъ за Федоровымъ сыномъ Теплово въ той же дер. Черемошнѣ...»
>
> *Translation:* "Held by Filip Fyodorov syn Teplovo, in the same village of Cheremoshnya..."

Besides a share of the village of Cheremoshnya in Kamensky stan, half of
the new settlement (pochinok) of Nikitinsky Zavalishin is registered to him. The coincidence of the name Filipp, the patronymic Fyodorov
syn and the family naming Teplov makes it possible to join the two entries with confidence.

There is also a cautious micro-geographical clue for our family. Teplov sat
in Cheremoshnya of **Kamensky stan**, «под Черемошным лесом» ("below the Cheremoshny wood"). Sofon Timofeevich
Anfilogov held Sofonova of **Korchakovsky stan**, «под Черемошенским
леском» ("below the little Cheremoshensky wood"). The closeness of the forms is very striking, but the running headings of the book clearly
assign the entries to different stany. Until the boundary between the stany has been reconstructed and
both villages have been located, this cannot be declared one wood or direct
neighbourhood. It is a priority cartographic check, not a finished
proof of Sofon's Kashira origin.

R. I. Ovsyannikov's thematic map made the next step possible. On it
Cheremoshnya and Sofonova are placed side by side, and across the space between them runs
the label **«Черемошенские лески»** ("the Cheremoshensky woods"). Consequently, the similar formulas of the survey book
do refer to one small land area on the boundary of two
stany, and not simply to two woods that happen to share a name. The first documented
Kashira settler in Oryol turned out to be in the immediate micro-geographical
surroundings of one of the early Anfilogovs. Since the map is a secondary
reconstruction and there is no joint transfer list, this strengthens the Kashira
hypothesis, but does not turn it into a proven origin of the family.

### The Name and Its Meaning

**Filipp** is a personal baptismal name. **Fyodorov syn** (son of Fyodor) directly names the father —
Fyodor. **Teplovo**, in an old case form, corresponds to the naming Teplov.
It is precisely such a full formula that is especially valuable: it sharply reduces the risk of confusing
namesakes.

The key words are **«испомещен в Орле»** ("granted a pomestye at Oryol"). In sixteenth-century language this does not simply mean
"left for Oryol": the serviceman was endowed with a pomestye at his new place of service.
What we have is an administrative change in the centre of landholding. The paired chain now
looks like this:

1. Filipp Teplov held a pomestye in the waste plot (pustosh) Nechaevskaya of Kashira district.
2. Before the book of 1578–1579 was compiled, he was granted a pomestye at Oryol.
3. The Oryol book of 1594/95 confirms him as an actual
   landholder of Kamensky stan.

The exact year of the transfer is not yet named. The reliable upper limit is 1578/79,
that is, the move had already taken place in the first generation after Oryol was founded in
1566. The same Kashira page says that the household buildings on the waste plot
were burned by the Crimeans in the year 7080, but this phrase describes the state of
the land and does not by itself date Filipp's grant of a pomestye.

### What Changes in the Hypothesis on the Origin of the First Men of Oryol

For the first time Kashira moves from the category of merely probable donor towns into the category of
**a documented district of origin**, at least for one man of the early
Oryol service core. Previously only the general Venyov pattern of recruiting
«новых помещиков каширян и тулян» ("new landholders, men of Kashira and Tula") and strong but indirect surname
parallels were known. Now there is a name, an old pomestye, a direct formula of the grant of a pomestye at Oryol
and a subsequent Oryol holding.

This does not prove a Kashira origin for Anfilogovs and does not make Teplov their
relative. Therefore no separate profile or route on the
family map is created for him. What has been created is a card for the document and the mechanism of resettlement.

The new priority tactic: write out all the entries of the Kashira book in which the scribe
explains the departure of a former landholder by a new town of service, then search for these
same men in the Oryol book and check their neighbourhood, surety links and shared
landholding groups with Anfilogovs. In parallel, an early Oryol
service list or a separate allotment book (otdelnaya kniga) of 1566–1578 is needed: it is now known which
literal formula and which paired structure of entries to look for in them.

A first continuous pass through the inter-town formulas of the entire published Kashira
