book has already been completed. The scribe separately notes Stepan Likharev, granted a pomestye at
Venyov; Ilya Nashchokin, Ivan Veneslavlev and Yury Teleshov, who served from
Kolomna; and the son of Vasily Kryukov, who served from Tula and had a pomestye there.
Thus the book does systematically distinguish the former land from the
new town of service. Filipp Teplov has so far turned out to be the only man found
with a direct formula of a grant of a pomestye at Oryol specifically. This does not prove that he was
alone: badly distorted OCR, a different formula or the absence of old land
could have concealed other settlers.

### Semyon Fyodorovich in Kashira and a Second Link of the Teplovs in Oryol

A repeated check, covering not only the departure formulas but every occurrence of the surname, gave
an important continuation. On pp. 1482–1483 of the same Kashira book, under the heading
**«За новики»** ("Held by noviki"), is written:

> «За новики: за Сенкою Федоровымъ сыномъ Теплова, да за Левкою Теплымъ,
> да за Иванкомъ Теплымъ жеребей дер. Лашинской...; да къ тому жъ припущено
> въ пашню жеребей дер. Тельшина...; а окладъ имъ 50 четьи».
>
> *Translation:* "Held by noviki: by Senka Fyodorov syn Teplova, and by Levka Teply,
> and by Ivanko Teply, a share of the village of Lashinskaya...; and in addition there has been added
> to the ploughland a share of the village of Telshino...; and their entitlement is 50 cheti."

Here **Senka** is a diminutive form of Semyon. The main thing is the full formula
**Semyon Fyodorov syn Teplov**. In the same book Filipp, transferred to Oryol, is also
called **Fyodorov syn Teplov**. The patronymic, the family naming,
the district and the time coincide. Most probably these are brothers or very close
relatives of one generation. But the scribe did not write "brothers" and gave no separate
entry for their father Fyodor, so the kinship is kept as a strong hypothesis.

Semyon's status is important too. The word **novik** means the beginning of his independent
pomestye service, but not resettlement. While Filipp had already been granted a pomestye at Oryol,
Semyon was part of the new service intake of the old Kashira district. This is the first
concrete model of a possible division of one family between two town
corporations.

The Oryol book strengthens this model with one more observation. Immediately after the entry
for Filipp, on p. 886, in the same Cheremoshnya there is written:

> «За вдовою за Овдотьею за Ивановою женою Теплово, да за еѣ дѣтми за
> Степанкомъ, а онъ служитъ, да за Онисимкомъ, а онъ 10 лѣтъ, да за Гришкою,
> а онъ 5 лѣтъ, отца ихъ помѣстье въ той же дер. Черемошнѣ...»
>
> *Translation:* "Held by the widow Ovdotya, wife of Ivan Teplovo, and by her children,
> Stepanko, and he serves, and Onisimko, and he is 10 years old, and Grishka,
> and he is 5 years old, their father's pomestye in the same village of Cheremoshnya..."

This is a second household link of the surname: the deceased Ivan Teplov, his widow
Evdokiya and three sons; the eldest, Stepan, already serves, and the younger ones are given exact
ages. Ivan's kinship with Filipp and with Semyon of Kashira is not stated, and
Ivan himself has not been found in the published Kashira book. Nevertheless it is now
known that Filipp was not the only Teplov in Oryol's Cheremoshnya.

The working scheme has become more concrete:

1. Filipp Fyodorovich Teplov leaves his Kashira pomestye and before 1578/79
   is granted a pomestye at Oryol.
2. Semyon Fyodorovich Teplov at the same time begins independent service in
   Kashira — possibly as a brother in the link of the family that remained there.
3. In Oryol's Cheremoshnya by 1594/95 there exist Filipp's holding and
   a separate paternal pomestye of the family of Ivan Teplov.
4. Next to them, according to the reconstructed micro-geography, lies the Sofonova of Sofon
   Timofeevich Anfilogov.

The last point does not prove a joint recruitment of the Teplovs and Anfilogovs. But the example
changes the method of searching for our surname: one should look not for a single "resettled
Anfilogov" but for **a split paternal family group** — men with the same
patronymic and the variants Anfilogov/Anpilogov, some of whom could have remained in
Kashira, Tula or Ryazan, while others received lands in Oryol.

### Cheremoshnya Turned Out to Be a Multi-Channel, Not a Purely Kashira Node

A check of all the surnames on the same printed p. 885 added an important correction.
Next to Teplov sat **Ivan Avvakumov syn Kutepov** and the family of
**Nekras Bredikhin**. These surnames do not form a noticeable Kashira group in
the book checked, but they are widely represented in the Tula book of 1587–1589.

On pp. 1196–1197 are named Bogdan Ivanovich Kutepov, Mikhail and Matvey Fyodorovich
Kutepov and Fyodor Yuryevich Kutepov. Mikhail and Matvey additionally held a
pomestye **at Dedilov** of 82 chetverts, and Fyodor one **in Odoev** of 30
chetverts. This entry alone shows how one surname was simultaneously
distributed among several service towns.

The Bredikhins are represented in the Tula book even more widely: Sava Fomin, Danilo and Radko
Fyodorovich, Manuil Stepanovich, Timofey Onishin, Timofey Fomin and others. However,
the full name of the Oryol Nekras or Matyushka Bredikhin has not been found there. Likewise
no Tula Ivan Avvakumovich Kutepov has been found. So one cannot write
that these particular men came from Tula; only a strong surname milieu has been proved.

The result is a more realistic model of one Oryol village:

- Filipp Teplov has a direct route **Kashira → Oryol**;
- the Kutepovs and Bredikhins have dense parallels in **Tula—Dedilov—Odoev**;
- some of the land within Cheremoshnya passed by inheritance and through marriage;
- the Gulidovs and Goryushin have not yet given an external district trace in the same corpus.

This is the first concrete argument against the idea of a single obligatory town of origin
for the whole Oryol core. Even a compact landed node could have been
assembled from separate family links of different service corporations. For
Anfilogovs, therefore, several paternal lines must be checked at the same time —
the sons of Vasily, Mikula, Persha, Timofey, Nechay and Pakhom —
rather than waiting for one list that will explain all nine at once. A separate
comparative card has been created:
`PUB-PKMG-1587-TULA-1595-OREL-CHEREMOSHNYA-MULTI-CORRIDOR`.

## 28 August 2026: The Closest Surviving Form of the Result — the Ryazhsk Desyatnya of 1579

Two years after the Oryol verdict an original
desyatnya (a muster roll of servicemen) was compiled in Ryazhsk, giving the closest model of how the outcome
of an enrolment (verstanye) carried out on the spot could have been drawn up. Its exact archival address is **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4,
bk. 94, fols. 1–80**. The heading of the publication reads:

> «Десятня денежная детей боярских Ряского города верстанья боярина князя
> Ивана Юрьевича Булгакова, да дьяка Дея Губастого лета 7087».
>
> *Translation:* "Money desyatnya of the deti boyarskie of the town of Ryazhsk, of the enrolment by the boyar Prince
> Ivan Yuryevich Bulgakov and the dyak Dey Gubasty, in the year 7087."

The book was compiled not by a lone low-level office but by a commission of a boyar and
a dyak; Dey Gubasty authenticated it with his signature across the folios (skrepa). Inside, for each service
man, the land and money entitlements, weapons, horses,
accompanying men and guarantors were recorded. Separate sections covered those selected for
the sovereign's campaign, those remaining for frontier (ukrainnaya) and regimental service, foot service
in the town, men without places, those serving from Ryazan, Cossack atamans, service Cossacks and
Cossacks «на поле» ("in the field"). This sets the expected form of the Oryol
result much more precisely: one should look not only for a short list of names, but also for a desyatnya,
an entitlement section, surety groups, a dyak's skrepa and later extracts from them.

The full published text has been checked for the forms Анпилог-, Ампилог-,
Анфилог-, Онпилог-, Онфилог-, Амфилог- and the beginnings Анпи-/Ампи-/Анфи-/
Онпи-/Онфи-. There are no matches. This is a negative control for Ryazhsk, not
evidence that the family was absent from Oryol.

A. A. Zimin called Dey Gubasty an official of the Gorodovoy Prikaz (Fortifications Chancellery) in 1576–1577
and wrote that in 1578/79 he enrolled the deti boyarskie of Ryazhsk «и других
городов» ("and other towns"). A modern reference work of 2015 places Gubasty in 1577/78
in the table of the Pushkarsky Prikaz (Artillery Chancellery), which the same reference work also calls
the Pushechny, the Gorodovoe delo and the Zasechny. This discrepancy reflects the unstable
terminology of the early prikazy and gives no right to connect the dyak with Oryol.

M. G. Krotov's reconstruction precisely reveals the "other towns". In January 1579
one commission of Ivan Yuryevich Bulgakov and Dey Gubasty compiled three
independent desyatni: **Vladimir, Ryazhsk and Yuryev**. The Vladimir one is
dated 23 January; its original perished in the fire of 1812, but there survive a
copy of the preface, **РГАДА, f. 388, bk. 856, fol. 273**, and extracts in fonds 388,
288 and in fond 210, Moscow desk (Moskovsky stol), scroll (stolbets) 48, fol. 489. The Yuryev book also
perished; the inventory of 1735 called it an enrolment book with pomestye entitlements. Oryol is not in
this reconstructed route of the commission. Consequently, Gubasty becomes
not more but **less likely** as the Oryol executor: the known
commission of 1579 is geographically confined to Vladimir, Ryazhsk and
Yuryev-Polskoy.

Ivan Yuryevich Bulgakov and Dey Gubasty are now target names for
the search for similar executive papers, but **not established executors
of the Oryol inspection**. No profiles or map changes are created. Comparative card:
`PUB-RYAZHSK-DESYATNYA-1579-PROCEDURAL-ANALOGY`.

[The Ryazhsk desyatnya of 1579](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Desjatni_XVI_v/text7.htm),
[archival record](https://www.62info.ru/history/node/12182),
[2015 reference work on the prikazy](https://iriran.ru/sites/default/files/2024-05/PrikazyMoskovskogoGosudarstvaXVI-XVIIveka_2015.pdf),
[M. G. Krotov's reconstruction of the desyatni](https://muscovitearmor.wordpress.com/2018/07/08/%D1%8F%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B2-%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%BE%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2-%D0%B4%D0%B5%D1%81%D1%8F%D1%82%D0%BD%D0%B8-xvi-%D0%BD%D0%B0%D1%87%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B0-xvii-%D0%B2%D0%B2-%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BA-%D0%B8/).

## 28 August 2026: The Oryol List of 1577 May Have Remained an Intermediate Document

M. G. Krotov's chronological inventory makes it possible to separate the ordered «списки
имянные» ("name lists") from ordinary desyatni. For 1577 the researcher reconstructs
the desyatni of Vladimir, Kolomna, Moscow and Yuryev-Polskoy; for 1577/78, those of
Meshchovsk and Serpeysk. In the town index Oryol first appears only under
1596. So a separate Oryol desyatnya of 1577 is unknown not only
among the ten surviving books: it is also absent from the broader list of lost
desyatni reconstructed from old inventories and extracts.

This does not cancel the literal order of 15 March. It ordered that
the selected deti boyarskie and Cossacks be written down «на список подлинно порознь» ("on a list, exactly, separately"), that their
land and money entitlements be attached and that the materials be brought to the Razryad office. But such
an executive list may not have taken the form of an independent desyatnya.

V. A. Volkov provides an additional fork. In his book «Войны и войска Московского
государства» (Wars and Armies of the Muscovite State), on p. 289, he writes that the decision of 1577 was fully implemented
only in Dankov district, where the Cossacks received 50 chetverts each; in other
districts their grants remained at 20 chetverts. On this page no separate archival
call number is given for the statement, so it cannot be applied to Oryol as a proven
fact. But the hypothesis of incomplete implementation must now be checked
on an equal footing with the hypothesis of loss.

The Dankov half of the conclusion has received independent support. A. S. Rakitin cites
a late «сказка» (statement) by the inhabitants of Dankov, according to which 117 watch
Cossacks were settled there with a land entitlement of 50 chetverts each and a money entitlement of six rubles. His
archival reference: **РГАДА, f. 210, Scrolls of the Vladimir desk (Stolbtsy Vladimirskogo stola), d. 7, fol. 161**.
This confirms the actual existence of the full Dankov entitlement, but does not
automatically prove that the addition did not take place in Oryol.

The practical search splits in two:

- if the inspection took place, look for an intermediate name list, an entitlement extract,
  a duplicate copy (protiven), surety records and a voivode's report (otpiska) on delivery to Moscow;
- if implementation was delayed or failed, look for reports on the lack of
  land, horses or men, petitions about an incomplete grant and later entries on the
  retention of the old 20-chetvert Cossack pomestyes.

This fork agrees with the broader answer about the origin of the first
men of Oryol. Oryol may have been formed not by a single transfer from distant Moscow but through
a network of old service towns on the Oka and the Field frontier (Polskaya ukraina). Already proven is the personal route
Kashira → Oryol for Filipp Fyodorov syn Teplov; documented is the general mechanism of
granting pomestyes to men of Kashira and Tula in the new Venyov district; family parallels lead to
Tula, Mtsensk, Epifan, Ryazan and Ryazhsk. For Anfilogovs this remains a circle of
search, not an established route.

Separately checked was the source of the frequently repeated formula about the "Moscow revision".
In «Орловские епархиальные ведомости» (Oryol Diocesan Gazette) of 1872, No. 13, in an essay by G. M.
Pyasetsky, on **p. 935**, is printed: «До 1574 года Орловские сторожевые казаки
получали всего по 20 четвертей» ("Until 1574 the Oryol watch Cossacks
received only 20 chetverts each"), after which the prescribed inspection is called
«Московской ревизией из детей боярских и благонадежных подьячих» ("a Moscow revision of deti boyarskie and trustworthy clerks"). The date 1574
conflicts with the primary verdict of 15 March 1577 and is Pyasetsky's
error. The only footnote leads to Solovyov, vol. 7, p. 29: there is no new archival
file, nor any names of revisers or of men of Oryol, in the article. The checked text of Solovyov also
places this retelling under 1574 and gives neither names nor an archival call number:
Pyasetsky did not rely on a separate source naming individuals. "Moscow" here means a commission
appointed by the central authority, not the recruitment of men from Moscow. So this
retelling is not proof of a Moscow → Oryol route and brings the
name list no closer.

Research card created:
`PUB-VOLKOV-OREL-STOROZHEVOE-VERSTANIE-1577-IMPLEMENTATION`. No profiles or map
points are created.

[M. G. Krotov's chronological inventory](https://muscovitearmor.wordpress.com/2018/07/08/%D1%8F%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B2-%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%BE%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2-%D0%B4%D0%B5%D1%81%D1%8F%D1%82%D0%BD%D0%B8-xvi-%D0%BD%D0%B0%D1%87%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B0-xvii-%D0%B2%D0%B2-%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%BA-%D0%B8/),
[V. A. Volkov's book](https://www.klex.ru/1vqh),
[A. S. Rakitin's study with the archival call number of the Dankov statement](https://www.reenactor.ru/ARH/PDF/Rakitin_00.pdf),
[text of the verdict of 15 March 1577](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/21-40/23.htm).

## 28 August 2026: A Primary Chain of Incomplete Implementation Has Been Found

A. L. Stanislavsky makes it possible to move from Volkov's general conclusion to specific
archival folios. Right after describing the different outcomes of the 1577 verdict,
he gives three call numbers in the sequence Epifan — Dankov — Shatsk:

- **Epifan:** РГАДА, f. 1209, bk. 140, fol. 277 v.;
- **Dankov:** РГАДА, f. 210, Vladimir desk, scroll 7, fol. 160;
- **Shatsk:** ibid., scroll 102, fols. 174–176.

The official inventories confirmed that this is not a chance juxtaposition of references.
Book 140 is the original of the cadastral and boundary book of Epifan district of 1627/28;
on fols. 277 v.–287 v. it contains a separate section, **«поместья сторожевых
казаков в уезде»** ("pomestyes of the watch Cossacks in the district"). A later copy of the same book, f. 1209, bk. 14248, preserves
the corresponding section on fols. 364 v.–384.

The old designation "Vladimir desk, scroll 7" translates into the modern full
call number **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 10, d. 7**. The official inventory calls fols. 159–164
**a census of servicemen settled in Dankov under Tsar Ivan Vasilyevich**.
Folio 160 lies exactly within this block; the neighbouring fol. 161 was used by
A. S. Rakitin for his report on the 117 Dankov watch Cossacks with an entitlement of
50 chetverts each.

Scroll 102 is the modern **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 10, d. 102**. This is a complex of
documents of the Chelobitenny Prikaz (Petitions Chancellery) of 1641 on atamans, esauls and Cossacks of
various towns; the brief inventory separately notes a grant to the Shatsk Cossacks of
escheated lands. Fols. 174–176 still need to be read in the original.

So the thesis that the verdict was implemented differently now has a primary basis:
Dankov received the full 50-chetvert entitlement; the men of Epifan, with a nominal entitlement,
showed actual old grants of 21 chetverts each; the Shatsk Cossacks claimed
that they had received no additions at all. But **Oryol is not among these three examples**.
The Epifan or Shatsk result must not be transferred to Oryol.

For the Oryol search this sets very precise formulas and types of documents:

- a section on the pomestyes of watch Cossacks in a cadastral survey book;
- a census of men «устроенных при царе Иване Васильевиче» ("settled under Tsar Ivan Vasilyevich");
- a later petition about land not added;
- the formulas «изстари» ("of old"), «в дачах по 20/21 четверти» ("in grants of 20/21 chetverts each"), «как город стал» ("since the town was founded").

In the official inventory of the Vladimir desk, across all word forms of Oryol, only
a late separate heading was found — the annual budget estimate (smeta) of Oryol for 1663. There is no early Oryol
analogue of the Dankov block in the headings of the inventory. This is a negative result
for the inventory, not proof that the document is absent from within an undescribed
complex.

Control card created:
`PUB-STANISLAVSKY-1577-COSSACK-LAND-GRANTS-PRIMARY-CHAIN`. It contains no surname
mentions; no profiles or map points are created.

[Text of A. L. Stanislavsky's study](https://yakovkrotov.com/history/17/1610/stanislav_01.htm),
[official catalogue of РГАДА cadastral survey books](http://rgada.info/sprav/sprav/zenchenko_ivanova_2013.pdf),
[official inventory 10 of fond 210](http://rgada.info/opisi/210-opis_10.pdf).

## 28 August 2026: The Current 421,095-Row Database Gave No Pre-Oryol Surname Trace

The new public front end of the database of servicemen was checked not by manually
paging through the table but through its own search interface. At the time of the
check the server reports **421,095 records**. For the beginnings **Анпил-, Ампил-,
Анфил-, Онпил- and Онфил-**, 206 rows were found; all of them were cross-checked against year,
town and archival call number.

The database returns not a single target surname row from the sixteenth century. The earliest
remains the Kursk entry **«Анпилогов Федор»** ("Anpilogov Fedor") in the composite complex of servicemen
of the Time of Troubles, 1605–1618. Next come the already known Oryol entries of
1620/21–1629 and the Kursk ones of 1628–1631. For the short beginning **Ампил-** there are
no results. For **Анфил-** before 1650 there are the Oryol Fyodor
Anfilogov of 1642 and, not a surname but a personal name, Anfilofey, for Peshkov in Solova
in 1648. For **Онпил-** the known Oryol and Kursk entries of 1626–1648
are confirmed; **Онфил-** appears only for the Oryol Filimon of 1689.

Separately checked was the new two-generation key from the transfer inventory (otkaznaya opis) of 1649 —
**Neustroy Rusinov syn Ampilov** (Neustroy, son of Rusin, Ampilov). The query "Neustroy + Oryol" gave thirteen
rows of 1628–1664, but the men named belong to the surnames
Ashikhmin/Oshikhmin/Oshchikhmin, Merkulov, Mikhaylov and Shchukin. The query "Rusin + Oryol"
gave Rusin Zbitnev, Rusin Kornilov, the Rusinovs and Rusinsky; **there is no Rusin
Ampilov**. So the general index does not yet connect Neustroy either with
the census of 1664 or with the known service lists.

This is not proof that the men are absent from the manuscripts: the site publishes the name, year,
town and call number, but not the full text of each entry. But the negative control
is methodologically important — there is no longer any need to repeat the same broad queries. The next
result should be sought in the original of entry No. 400 of book 10054, in a line-by-line
reading of the census of 1664 and in later extracts from early Oryol lists.

Using the same database, not the surname beginnings but seven rare formulas of the
older generation of the 1594/95 book were checked separately: **Ivan Pershin, Ermak Pakhomov, Sofon
Timofeev, Pavel Terentyev, Vasily Mikulin, Timofey Mikulin and Karp Mikulin**.
The exact combinations gave no rows. A search by the forms Pershin, Pakhomov,
Timofeev, Terentyev and Mikulin alone returns dozens and hundreds of namesakes,
but the open field usually contains only the surname and first name, without the required
three-part formula. So such an index can neither confirm nor
close negatively the pre-Oryol father of a branch: a folio-by-folio transcription is required
of a document of the earlier town.

At the same time the priority of the file **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12, pt. 1,
d. 65** has been adjusted. According to I. E. Kushelev's publication, its name blocks of 1635–1636
relate to the search for former town streltsy, Cossacks and gunners (pushkari)
who had scattered after the Lithuanian devastation; deti boyarskie are explicitly left outside
the scope of the study. The file is useful as a control for a possible change of service
category, but it does not replace the lost Oryol desyatnya and is no longer
the main address for the origin of Anfilogovs.

[Public search in the database of servicemen](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/).

## 28 August 2026: The Land Signal Singles Out the Paternal Line of Mikula

The cadastral survey book of 1594/95 does not give the entitlements of 1577, but it makes it possible to rank
the search. For Vasily, Timofey and Karp, «Микулиных детей Анфилогова» ("the sons of Mikula Anfilogov"), there are recorded three
separate landholders' homesteads and one shared allotment (zherebey): **6 chetverts of ploughed land and
51 chetverts of wild field**, 57 chetverts in all in one field. On a purely notional
equal division this is 19 chetverts per homestead — almost the old actual grant
of a watch Cossack of 20/21 chetverts known from other frontier towns.

The manuscript entry at **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 329, fols. 718–720** has been checked
again in full. It confirms the three homesteads and the shared land formula, but does not
divide the 57 chetverts into equal shares, does not call the brothers Cossacks and contains no
words about an earlier grant, an earlier town or the inspection of 1577. Moreover, a cadastral survey
book measures the holding actually in hand, whereas the order of 1577 speaks of the
service pomestye entitlement. Therefore 57 ÷ 3 = 19 is **a search signal, not
proof**.

By closeness of size the order of priority is now as follows:

- first the old pomestye of the father, **Mikula**, and the documents on the division of the shared allotment;
- then Osip Vasilyevich with 24 chetverts, but his land is explicitly called the former
  pomestye of Anokha Deevich Krivolapov, so this is not the original family grant;
- then Ermak Pakhomovich with 26 chetverts and Ivan Nechaevich with 15 chetverts;
- Belyay Vasilyevich (60), Ivan Pershin (33) and Sofon Timofeevich (40) remain in
  the search by fathers' names, but their sizes match the old norm less well.

Only an extract with the formulas «изстари» ("of old"), «как город стал» ("since the town was founded"), «в дачах
по 20/21 четверти» ("in grants of 20/21 chetverts each"), with Mikula's name or a direct reference to the enrolment of 1577, will be decisive.
Until then the family's origin and the route on the map do not change.

## 28 August 2026: A Second Name Channel Has Been Found — the Seasonal Reports of 1576

The search no longer depends on one final list of the 1577 inspection. In act
No. 18 of the same Razryad complex the procedure for handling the
watch roster has survived. On receiving it, the Temnikov voivode was to:

> «с тое росписи списал противень и держал бы у собя»;
>
> *Translation:* "make a duplicate copy of that roster and keep it with himself";

and after sending the men out — write to Moscow

> «которую пору и кого имяны на которую сторожу пошлешь […] и имяна их
> прислал».
>
> *Translation:* "at what time and whom by name you send to which watch post […] and send
> their names."

Further, the same act directly records a separate dispatch to Oryol: **«На Орел к
воеводе — от государя писано и роспись Орельским сторожам послана»** ("To Oryol, to
the voivode — written from the sovereign, and the roster for the Oryol watch posts sent"). In
the Oryol line of the abbreviated registration the point about the duplicate copy and sending names is not
repeated word for word, so the Temnikov text must not be passed off as a literally
surviving Oryol charter. But within one circular complex it
shows the normal flow of documents: the working copy stayed with the voivode, while
Moscow received the names of the men actually sent to the posts.

According to V. P. Zagorovsky's corrected dating, this act belongs to **15 March
1576**, that is, a year before the special inspection. The Oryol roster
contained nine watch posts, 26 Cossacks per entry and **78 men in three
shifts**. Consequently, there potentially existed not one but several
name documents:

- the local duplicate copy of the Oryol watch roster;
- the report on the first spring entry;
- the reports on the second and third shifts;
- then a separate final list after the inspection of 1577.

New search formulas: **«кого имяны на которую сторожу»** ("whom by name to which watch post"), **«имяна их
прислал»** ("sent their names"), **«с тое росписи списал противень»** ("made a duplicate copy of that roster"), **«Орельским сторожам»** ("for the Oryol watch posts").
The chronological window is widened from March 1577 alone to March 1576 — autumn
1577. It is precisely in a seasonal report that the full complement of the 78
watchmen may turn up, even if the final enrolment register perished.

At the same time the official РГАДА viewer has conclusively checked the physical
junction after the verdict of 1577. Frame 0298 contains fol. 243, frame 0299 the end on
fol. 243 v. and the blank fol. 244, and frame 0300 begins a new document on fol. 245.
There is indeed no immediate appendix with names after the verdict.

[Act No. 18 with the procedure for keeping the duplicate copy and sending names](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/1-20/18.htm),
[official viewer for the РГАДА file](http://rgada.info/kueh/index2.php?str=210_6_1).

### In Whose Name the Oryol Reports of 1576 May Have Been Catalogued

The full order of the service-register entries gave a more precise target key. In the field
roster of 1576, where Prince Ivan Okhlyabinin is posted **«на Сосне»** ("on the Sosna"), in Oryol
stand **Prince Vasily Menshoy Ivanovich Krivoborsky** and **the head Andrey
Yakovlevich Izmaylov**. Okhlyabinin's posting is the very chronological
anchor by which Zagorovsky corrected the date of act No. 18. So the spring and
summer reports on the three entries of watchmen should be sought first of all under the names of
Krivoborsky and Izmaylov.

The previous Oryol roster names Prince Mikhail Fyodorovich
Gvozdev-Rostovsky and Ivan Timofeevich Fustov. They remain useful
control keys for the winter correspondence and the handover of affairs, but the document does not
allow them to be considered the addressees of the charter of exactly 15 March. The abbreviated
registration text itself says only **«На Орел к воеводе»** ("To Oryol, to the voivode") and preserves
no name.

Zagorovsky's printed notes 78–84 have been checked separately. Behind the correction of the
date stand the Razryad books and the published АМГ (Akty Moskovskogo gosudarstva); there is no hidden archival call number
for a named Oryol roster in the notes. A full-text search of
the published РГАДА inventories also gave no exact matches for the four
commanders or for the formulas «Орельским сторожам», «имяна их прислал» and «списал
противень». A control search of the inventories of the Archive of the St Petersburg Institute of History (SPbII РАН) gave the same result;
several hits turned out to be a chance proximity of the words «гвоздей» ("nails") and
«Ростовскому» ("to Rostovsky") or late watchmen. This closes any repeated general OCR search,
but not the reading of undigitised scrolls.

The minimum set of search names is now: **Василей Меньшой Иванович
Кривоборской** (Vasiley Menshoy Ivanovich Krivoborskoy), **Ондрей Яковлев сын Измайлов** (Ondrey Yakovlev syn Izmaylov), and for the previous shift —
**Михайло Федорович Гвоздев Ростовской** (Mikhaylo Fedorovich Gvozdev Rostovskoy) and **Иван Тимофеев сын Фустов** (Ivan Timofeev syn Fustov).

[Razryad book of 1475–1605, roster of 1576](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XV/1460-1480/RK_1475_1605/text16.htm),
[full-text search of the РГАДА inventories](https://rgada.sovadev.pro/).

### The End of the 1576 Act Checked against the Manuscript

The official frames of the Watch Book have ruled out a simple appendix.
Frame 0244 contains the final fol. 202, frame 0245 the end on fol. 202 v.
and a blank following folio, and frame 0246 begins a new document on fol. 203. There is no name
roster, duplicate copy or incoming report immediately after the act.

Consequently, the name documents existed outside this book copy:
separately, as the working duplicate copy held by the Oryol voivode and/or as reports reaching
the Razryad on the first, second and third entries actually sent out. This
removes the task of leafing further through the neighbouring folios of the book and moves
the search to independent scrolls catalogued by sender, date or opening
phrase.

[Official viewer for the Watch Book](http://rgada.info/kueh/index2.php?str=210_6_1).

### Original Rosters Had to Be Sent as Early as February 1571

An earlier general chancery channel has been found. The boyar verdict on stanitsa
and watch service of **16 February 1571** twice requires the voivodes to report
immediately to Moscow. For the stanitsa details it says:

> «им о том к государю отписати тотчас, с первым гонцом, и роспись тому
> подлинную к государю прислати».
>
> *Translation:* "they are to report on this to the sovereign at once, by the first courier, and send the original
> roster of it to the sovereign."

The same obligation is repeated separately for the three watch entries:

> «о том им писати к государю тот же час, с первым гонцом, и роспись тому
> подлинная прислати».
>
> *Translation:* "they are to write on this to the sovereign at the same hour, by the first courier, and send the
> original roster of it."

This matters for Oryol, although the town is not named in these two general formulas. Five
days later, on **21 February 1571**, another verdict assigned from Oryol ten
deti boyarskie and twenty Cossacks to the Oskol at the mouth of the Ublya, and nine deti
boyarskie and twenty Cossacks to the Seym at the mouth of the Khon. Both groups were to be
relieved three times by men **«из тех же городов по той же росписи»** ("from the same towns according to the same roster"). So
there are now grounds to look not for an abstract early list but for the executive
reports and original rosters of these two Oryol details and their three shifts.

The manuscript boundary has been checked against the official copy. Frame 0040 contains fol. 31
v. — 32 with the end of the instruction (nakaz); frame 0041 shows the blank verso of the last
folio, and frame 0042 begins a new document on fol. 33. There is no appendix after the act.
Consequently, the prescribed original rosters existed separately from
the book copy — as incoming reports, appendices to them or working duplicate copies.

The chronological window of the name search has been widened to **February 1571**.
New exact keys: **«с первым гонцом»** ("by the first courier"), **«роспись тому подлинную»** ("the original roster of it"),
**«из Орла детей боярских»** ("deti boyarskie from Oryol"), **«Осколе усть Убли»** ("on the Oskol at the mouth of the Ublya"), **«Семи усть Хону»** ("on the Seym at the mouth of the Khon"),
the first, second and third entries. So far there are no names of Anfilogovs and no earlier town in
these documents; the profiles and the map route do not change.

[Boyar verdict of 16 February 1571](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/1-20/2.htm),
[official РГАДА viewer](http://rgada.info/kueh/index2.php?str=210_6_1).

### Ivan Semichev's Bryansk Line Has Acquired a Family and Landed Trace

All three commanders under whom the Oryol Cossacks served in succession in 1577
are directly called men of Bryansk: **Ignaty Andreevich Tyutchev, Ivan Semichev and
Afanasy Panyutin**. Each entry on the Seversky Donets had fifteen
Cossacks from Oryol. This proves a Bryansk command channel, but not the earlier town
of the men of Oryol themselves.

A family and landed trace has now been found for Ivan Semichev too. The third volume
of «Акты служилых землевладельцев» (Acts of Service Landholders) publishes a survey extract of 19 February
1595 for **Dmitry Ivanov syn Semichov** (Dmitry, son of Ivan, Semichov) for his **old pomestye** in
Bryansk district:

> «дали выпись с книг писма своево и меры Дмитрею Иванову сыну Семичову на
> старое его помесье в его оклад в четыреста чети».
>
> *Translation:* "[we] gave an extract from the books of our survey and measurement to Dmitrey Ivanov syn Semichov for
> his old pomestye, within his entitlement of four hundred cheti."

The pomestye included the hamlet (seltso) of Boboedovo on the stream Pyata, the village of Pogost and a waste plot
that had formerly been the village of Ermakova, in the Voronets and Potsyn volosts. The same
volume gives one more contemporary: in a Ryazan deed of purchase of 1569/70, among the
witnesses (poslukhi), **Fyodor Ivanov syn Semichov** is named.

Dmitry shares the surname, patronymic, generation and Bryansk service milieu with
the head Ivan. So he is a strong candidate for a son or close
relative of Ivan Semichev. But there is no direct kinship formula; moreover Fyodor
is named only as a witness to a Ryazan transaction, without any indication of his town. These
men must not be turned into a ready-made pedigree.

The practical result is more substantial than the kinship hypothesis itself. The extract of 1595
has survived in two later copies: **РГАДА, f. 1209, Scrolls for Bryansk,
No. 315/31833, fols. 296–298** and **No. 10/31661, fols. 64–68**. This means the papers of the entry
of Ivan Semichev and possible name appendices should be sought first of all in the
Bryansk family and pomestye complex, including the files on the hamlet of Boboedovo, and not under
a single heading "Oryol".

This refines the model of early Oryol's origin: **Moscow was the administrative
centre, but one of the real personnel channels ran through Bryansk**. For the
Anfilogovs themselves a Bryansk route has not yet been proved — the acts contain neither their surname nor
the names of the fifteen Oryol Cossacks. The profiles and the map do not change.

Research card:
`PUB-ASZ3-1569-1595-SEMICHEV-BRYANSK-RYAZAN-CHAIN`.
[Scan of the third volume of АСЗ](https://djvu.online/file/XShJZdkt8XXun),
[catalogue record of the edition in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000199_000009_002859015/),
[field act of 1577](https://vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/21-40/27.htm).

### Panyutin and Tyutchev Can Be Traced in Stanitsa Service after 1577

The Bryansk command channel is now confirmed by more than one entry. In the name
roster of the deti boyarskie of Meshchovsk, Opakov and Bryansk of 1584 there is a separate
section, **«Брянчаня ж, служат станичную службу»** ("Also men of Bryansk, serving stanitsa service"). With an entitlement of 500 chetverts there
is named **Офонасей Иванов сын Панютин** (Ofonasey Ivanov syn Panyutin). The coincidence of the rare name, surname,
Bryansk affiliation and stanitsa function allows him to be considered with high confidence
the same Afanasy Panyutin who in 1577 commanded the third
shift of fifteen Oryol and fifteen Novosil Cossacks. The limitation
remains formal: the act of 1577 did not preserve his patronymic.

The make-up of his entry at the same time shows why Bryansk must not be turned into
the sole "homeland" of the detachment. Under Panyutin stood **39 deti boyarskie from
Bryansk, Starodub, Karachev and Bolkhov** and **30 Cossacks from Novosil and Oryol**.
This is a combined inter-town unit assembled by a central assignment.

His later fate is found in an archival report of 1588: from Putivl to
the Donets the stanitsa head Afanasy Panyutin was sent with his comrades, and **«того
Афанасия черкасы на поле, на Донцу, убили до смерти»** ("that Afanasy the Cherkasy [Cossacks] killed dead in the field, on the Donets"). In the following year, 1589,
among the heads from Putivl and Rylsk **Ignaty Tyutchev** is named again. This is
with high probability Ignaty Andreevich Tyutchev, the commander of the first Oryol
entry of 1577; the later entry simply omits the patronymic.
The open-access publication of the source book makes it possible to specify the folios: Panyutin —
**fol. 143 v.**, Tyutchev — **fol. 144**.

For the search this matters more than the biographical detail. The service complex of the two
heads lived for at least twelve years and passed through Bryansk, Putivl, Rylsk
and the Donets. Consequently, possible lists of their comrades should be sought in
**РГАДА, f. 210, scrolls of the Moscow desk, No. 1094, stolpik 3, fols. 1–20** and
in **РГАДА, f. 79, op. 1, bk. 22**, especially among the reports, payments and the handling
of the service consequences of Panyutin's death. This is a targeted path to the names of men, but
not yet proof of a Bryansk origin for the rank-and-file men of Oryol.

A similar entry has been closed separately: in a Mtsensk act of 1569 the reading is
**Офонька Пашутин** (Ofonka Pashutin), not Panyutin. He cannot be used as an early
mention of Afanasy.

The surname forms Anfilogov / Anpilogov do not yet appear in these texts. The profiles and the map
do not change. A full-text check of the 1584 roster also yielded no stems
Анфил-, Анпил-, Ампил-, Онфил-, Офил- or the closest Oryol family names
Trunov, Bazdyrev, Nemtsov, Krivolapov, Onanyin. But this does not close Bryansk:
the editor explicitly warns that the roster is incomplete and that the assessors (okladchiki), guarantors and
other lines of the source desyatnya were abridged.

Research card:
`PUB-BRYANSK-1577-1589-PANYUTIN-TYUTCHEV-SERVICE-CONTINUITY`.
[Roster of the men of Bryansk of 1584](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Rospis_det_boj_m_o_b/text.htm),
[extracts from book 22 of the Posolsky Prikaz](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/1-20/2.htm),
[article with documents of 1588–1589](https://clio-brgu.ru/wp-content/Archiv/HSP2021-N2.pdf).

### What Became of the Full Desyatnya of 1583/84

The inventory of the Razryad files carried out of the Moscow fire of 7 May 1626
makes it possible to check the archival fate of the source. In the town list published by N. P.
Likhachev there is a separate heading, **«Мещоскъ»** (Meshchovsk). Under it are
named the enrolment of **7080 (1571/72)** and a later desyatnya of **7130/7134
(1621/22–1625/26)**. The source desyatnya of **7092 (1583/84)**, on
which Stanislavsky's roster is based, is not in the section. There is also no separate section for
Bryansk anywhere in the published list; the list ends with No. 136,
Starodub.

This does not allow the exact date of the book's loss to be named. It could have burned in 1626,
disappeared earlier or never been entered in the inventory. But it can now be stated
that by 1626 the full desyatnya cannot be traced as an independent
surviving book. Stolpik No. 1094 of the Moscow desk is its abridged
derivative, in which the editor explicitly noted the omission of assessors,
guarantors and other lines. Consequently, the absence of the surname from the known
roster must not be turned into proof of its absence from the source desyatnya.

Research card:
`PUB-LIKHACHEV-1888-RAZRYAD-1626-MESCHOVSK-BRYANSK-DESYATNI-AUDIT`.
[Open-access scan of Likhachev's edition](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Special:Redirect/file/%D0%A0%D0%B0%D0%B7%D1%80%D1%8F%D0%B4%D0%BD%D1%8B%D0%B5_%D0%B4%D1%8F%D0%BA%D0%B8_XVI_%D0%B2%D0%B5%D0%BA%D0%B0_1888.pdf).

### Putivl Has Given the First Synchronous Candidate for Belyay Onfilogov

A continuous check of all four parts of the Putivl allotment book (otdelnaya kniga) of 1594
yielded no surname stems Анфилог-, Анпилог-, Ампилог-, Онфилог- or
Онпилог-. But among the men being transferred to mounted samopal service
an exact paternal signature of one Oryol branch was found:

> «Отделено стрельцу самопальнику **Беляйку Васильеву** в ево оклад 20
> чети…»
>
> *Translation:* "Allotted to the samopal strelets **Belyayko Vasilyev**, within his entitlement, 20
> cheti…"

The entry is on **fol. 256—256 v.** (pp. 278—279 in the publication). The man of Putivl
was given the full 20-chetvert entitlement: 11 1/8 chetverts of already ploughed land and 8 1/8
chetverts of wild field. On his homestead stood his own threshing barn (ovin), sheds and
kitchen gardens; part of the land he had previously held on quitrent (obrok). So this is not an abstract
name from a later index but a living serviceman with a local household.
The introductory formula of the book describes recruitment specifically from the population of Putivl and its district,
and the entry itself says that the quitrent ploughland had formerly been **«да за ним же за
Беляйком»** ("and also held by him, by Belyayko"). Consequently, Belyay was already a settled Putivl strelets before
the new land entitlement was completed. If this is the future Oryol Onfilogov,
then we are looking not at a chance passer-by but at a possible move by a man who
left a household behind.

The chronology is especially tight. The Putivl book belongs to **7102
(1593/1594)**. The Oryol book of literally the following year, **7103
(1594/1595)**, names in Bazdyrevo **Belyay Vasilyev syn Onfilogov** (Belyay, son of Vasily, Onfilogov).
The rare name and the father's name coincide; both men belong to the southern service
milieu. Unlike Belyay Vasilyev Korostelyov of Ryazhsk, who had already died in
7101, the Putivl namesake could chronologically have moved to Oryol.

The sequence turned out to be even tighter at the level of the books. In the final
part of the Putivl complex there is an entry dated **8 June 1594**;
the Oryol year 7103 began on 1 September. This date must not be ascribed to the entry for
Belyay — it has no day of its own — but the book was still being compiled
only a few months before the start of the Oryol survey year.

But the route cannot yet be considered proven. The Putivl entry gives no
third naming, reports no departure and shows an already established
household. The Oryol entry does not say that Belyay came from Putivl. The
combination of name and patronymic itself certainly recurred. So this is the first
**synchronous candidate for a specific person**, not an established
identity.

The complete book at the same time shows the mechanism we were looking for. By decree,
the mounted samopal men were recruited from deti boyarskie left behind the Putivl
stanitsy, landless noviki, streltsy, belodvortsy (tax-exempt householders), gunners,
zatinshchiki (rampart gunners), Cherkasy, atamans and field Cossacks, who were then endowed with land.
Thus a recruited strelets could become a mounted landholder within a single
campaign of the sovereign. The document does not prove that this was exactly how the fate of
Belyay Onfilogov unfolded, but for the first time it gives, side by side, a suitable man and a real
administrative mechanism.

All the paternal pairs of the early Oryol circle were checked separately: Osip
Vasilyev, Vasily, Timofey and Karp Mikulin, Ivan Pershin, Ivan Nechaev, Sofon
Timofeev, Ermak Pakhomov and Pavel Terentyev. Not one recurred in the full
Putivl text. The pair **Belyay + Vasily** remains the only one; its three
occurrences belong to one entry and the common boundary of the same man. The similar
line «Якушу Нечаеву сыну Власова» ("to Yakush Nechaev syn Vlasova") is a different man, not Ivan Nechaevich.
So for now Putivl gives a candidate for only one Oryol branch,
but does so more precisely than any other town checked.

Widening the search from ready-made pairs to **the possible fathers themselves** gave one more
important check. On p. 197 of the edition, fols. 128 v.–129 v. of the manuscript, is recorded
**«сын боярский самопальник Нечай Васильев сын Чюркин»** ("the syn boyarsky samopal man Nechay Vasilyev syn Chyurkin"). He was allotted the full
40-chetvert entitlement in Kolodezskaya volost: an allotment in the village of Lopuga, land by
the Nameless gorodishche (Bezymyannoe gorodishche) and beyond the stream Lopuga. The last plot had formerly been held on
quitrent by the Spassky archimandrite of Novgorod-Seversky; Nechay's immediate
co-holders were Iev Barsukov and Ivan Chemodurov with their comrades.

By generation and the rare name he is a formal candidate for the father of the Oryol
**Ivan Nechaevich Anpilogov**, but the source at the same time gives a good way
to refute this: the man of Putivl bears the third naming **Chyurkin**, and next to
the Oryol Ivan neither the Chyurkins nor the Barsukovs with Chemodurov recur. Nor is there
any formula about a son Ivan or a move to Oryol. So Nechay Chyurkin receives no
profile and no point on the map. His significance is methodological: a search by the father's
name does find the generation before the surname, but the connection can be accepted
only when the third naming, a relative, an earlier holding
or a service group coincide.

An additional geographical signal: among the 53 landless Cherkasy who
refused a land entitlement, the book names **Semyon Onanyin**. He is not
connected by any document with the Oryol Onanyins and receives no separate profile,
but he shows that this family naming existed in the south-western
frontier stream of the same time.

Candidate card:
`PUB-PUTIVL-1594-BELYAY-VASILIEV-OREL-CANDIDATE`.
[Belyay's full entry on Vostlit](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/1-20/7_4.htm),
[Nechay Chyurkin's entry on Vostlit](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/1-20/7_2.htm),
[archaeographic preface and the composition of the recruitment](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/1-20/pred2.htm),
[catalogue record of the scan of the edition in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000199_000009_005945568/).

### The Comrades of Belyay of Putivl Did Not Pass with Him into the Oryol Book

Belyay's entry turned out to be part of a broader service and landed cluster. On
the next plot five newly recruited streltsy and one Cossack received wild
field **«вопче стрельцы з Беляйком Васильевым с товарыщи»** ("jointly, the streltsy with Belyayko Vasilyev and comrades"): Yakush Ondreev syn
Nosov, Ponkrat Usach, Olfim Kireev's brother, Maksim Larin, Mikifor Merkulov and
the Cossack Ogafon Ostroukhov. In Belyay's own entry there are additionally named
Zhdan Radyukin, Bogdan Kholuev, Istomka Rogov and the Cossacks of the sotnya of Yury Bezzubtsev.
The forms Ondrey, Ponkrat, Mikifor and Ogafon are kept as in the publication. The expression
«Олфимку Кирееву брату» ("to Olfimko, Kireev's brother") does not allow Kireev to be taken without reservation as a stable
surname. In Belyay's own entry «з Жаткою Радюкиным» ("with Zhatka Radyukin") is printed, but several
lines earlier the same participant is called **«Жданку Радюкину»** ("to Zhdanko Radyukin"); the working
normalisation is Zhdan, and Zhatka is kept as a variant reading or a misprint in the edition.

All ten name keys have been checked against the full Oryol section of the
1594/95 edition. There is not a single exact pair and no recurrence of the whole group.
The common stems Larin and Rogov occur elsewhere in the large
volume, but not as Maksim Larin and Istomka Rogov next to Belyay. So
the Putivl find does not yet turn into a group route. If this is one
man, he could have moved only individually, leaving his former comrades and
his landed cluster behind in Putivl.

There is also a weak later signal of local continuity: the name index to
the Kursk customs books names in 1619 **a Putivl Cossack
Radyukin** without a first name. He cannot be identified with Zhatka Radyukin, but the
surname stem itself continues to live in the Putivl Cossack milieu.

A new exact path of verification has appeared. In 2026 the Institute of Russian History of the Russian Academy of Sciences (ИРИ РАН)
published the complete volume **«Писцовые книги Путивльского уезда 1628–1629
годов»** (Cadastral Survey Books of Putivl District, 1628–1629) from РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, bk. 10553. The name index occupies
pp. 477–527. Only the five-page table of contents is currently openly available,
not the whole 596-page text.

However, an older open derivative publication of the list of
landholders based specifically on the book of Pyotr Musorgsky and Gavrila Fedorov of 1628–1629
has been found. Only the section between the headings **«Путивльский уезд»** ("Putivl district") and
**«Рыльский уезд»** ("Rylsk district") was checked, so namesakes from Kursk, Rylsk and Belgorod did not enter
the sample. It contains no Belyay Vasilyev, Yakush Nosov, Ponkrat Usach, Maksim
Larin, Mikifor Merkulov, Ogafon Ostroukhov, Zhdan Radyukin, Bogdan
Kholuev, Istoma Rogov or Semyon Onanyin. The only partial echo is
the rare name **Alfim Nesterov** in Kolodeznaya volost. The second naming does not
match the 1594 formula «Олфимку Кирееву брату» ("to Olfimko, Kireev's brother"), so this is not an exact
match and no ground for merging the men.

The negative result must not be turned into proof that Belyay moved to
Oryol. Between the books lie 34–35 years and the Time of Troubles; a man could have died, lost his
land, changed status or been left out of the abridged list. But it has now been
established that the open later sample retains neither Belyay nor his
immediate group as exact name pairs. The full index of the new volume is still
needed for former owners, witnesses and other roles that are not in
the brief list.

[Official catalogue record of the ИРИ РАН edition](https://iriran.ru/publications/4059),
[table of contents of the volume](https://iriran.ru/sites/default/files/2026-08/%D0%90%D0%BA%D1%82%D1%8B_2026_6.pdf),
[open list of landholders of Putivl district, 1628–1629](https://pomnirod.ru/materialy-k-statyam/administrativno-territorialnoe-delenie-rossijskoj-imperii/gubernii-rossii/kurskoe-namestnichestva-1779-1797.-kurskaya-guberniya-1797-1924/%D0%B4%D0%B2%D0%BE%D1%80%D1%8F%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B2%D0%BE3/%D0%B4%D0%B2%D0%BE%D1%80%D1%8F%D0%BD%D0%B5-%D0%B8-%D0%B4%D0%B5%D1%82%D0%B8-%D0%B1%D0%BE%D1%8F%D1%80%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B5-%D0%BA%D1%83%D1%80%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B3%D0%BE-%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B0%D1%8F-%D0%B2-xvii-%D0%B2%D0%B5%D0%BA%D0%B5-%D0%BF%D0%BE-%D0%BF%D0%B8%D1%81%D1%86%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B%D0%BC-%D0%BA%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B3%D0%B0%D0%BC.html),
[name index to the Kursk customs books](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/395/70014/p1994779.htm).

### The Unindexed Oryol List of 1631

A full-text search of the official inventory of fond 210 gave a new exact archival
address: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 10, d. 45, pt. II, fols. 29–117**. The inventory calls
it a list of the dvoryane and deti boyarskie of Belyov, Bolkhov, Oryol, Chern, Karachev and
Mtsensk for 7139/1631. The document has survived without its beginning and is authenticated by the signature of
the dyak Fyodor Stepanov. The boundaries of the six town sections within the common
block of folios are not yet known.

This is not a list of Oryol's first complement and not a direct document on resettlement.
But it falls into an important gap between the landholders of the Oryol book of
1594–1596 and the already known noviki of 1628 and 1636. From the folios themselves
it will be possible to check whether the seven early paternal lines continued and whether
the list contains additional service notes. The presence of an earlier town,
a time of enrolment, kinship or an entitlement cannot be assumed from a heading alone.

A check of the open database revealed a substantial gap. For the call number `210-10-45` it
returns 87 people, and all of them belong to Borovsk — the first part of the file, on
fols. 1–28. When the town of Oryol is added, the output becomes zero. So
a surname search in the ready-made table could not have found people from part II: the Oryol
section has not yet been indexed and must be extracted directly from
the file.

Archival target card:
`PUB-RGADA-210-10-45-II-OREL-SERVICE-LIST-1631`.
[Official РГАДА inventory](http://rgada.info/opisi/210-opis_10.pdf),
[open name index](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/).

#### A Check of Remote Access: Files 232 and 45 Diverge

On 28 August 2026 the complete public export of the catalogue of РГАДА files
available through ГИС УИАД was checked, not just the web search by headings. In it
**РГАДА, f. 210, op. 9, d. 232** was found and its exact internal address obtained:
`10000000001014/10000000157014/10000000358014/10000474722014`. Consequently,
microfilm No. 5 with the target junction at fols. 315 v.–322 can already be ordered for
remote viewing through ГИС УИАД.

The exact combination **f. 210, op. 10, d. 45** was not found in the same export. This
does not refute the existence of the 1631 list: its heading and folios are reliably
established from the official inventory. The practical meaning is different: right now the two files
require different actions. File 232 is available through the remote reading room,
whereas copies of file 45 have to be ordered directly from РГАДА, or one must wait for
the next addition to ГИС УИАД.

[Digital card of file 232](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000000358014/10000474722014/),
[public catalogue of digitisations](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=210&inv=9&case=232#rgada_opisi).

#### The Kama Oryol Ruled Out as a False Trail with the Same Name

A continuous search of the entire РГАДА catalogue gave only two headings before 1600
in which Oryol stands out as an independent place name. One is the already known
cadastral survey book of Oryol district of 1594/95. The second at first looked like
a new intermediate entry: a charter of 17 December 1588 «на Орел» ("to Oryol") to Andrey
Mikhaylovich Akinfov, РГАДА, f. 281, op. 17, d. 11143.

A check of the geography showed that this is **not the Oryol on the Oka**. The charter is addressed to
Oryol-gorodok of the Stroganovs on the Kama. Akinfov was the sovereign's head there during
the establishment of the Siberian road, and the order concerned the Pyskor Monastery and
the release of its peasants from the usual labour duties. The document is completely excluded from
the search line of Oryol Anfilogovs. No new card, profile or map point
is created from it.

The negative result is nevertheless useful: in the current complete export there is no other
independent sixteenth-century heading about the recruitment or transfer of people specifically to
our Oryol. Consequently, a repeated search by the single word «Орел» ("Oryol") is exhausted;
what is needed are the internal folios of composite files, duplicate copies of name lists and extracts from
lost pomestye books.

[Catalogue card of the Kama charter](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000228014/10000000652014/10000045741014/),
[study of Akinfov and Oryol on the Kama](https://www.permgaspi.ru/deyatelnost/doklady-romanovskie-chteniya-2023/reformy-imperatora-petra-i-istoricheskoe-nasledie-i-sovremennost/lykova-l-a-moschanskij-a-b-duhovnyj-predtecha-gornozavodskoj-tsivilizatsii.html).

#### The General Search for Later Extracts by Heading Is Exhausted

The whole export of 623,876 headings was additionally checked without restriction
to the sixteenth century. For the exact surname and name forms, 36 headings were found across all
years. Before 1700 only three already processed traces are relevant in substance:
Nikita Anpilov syn Vsevolodsky of 1677, Kuzma Anfilov of 1663 and
the Kozelsk village of Anpilogovo, known from the cadastral survey books of 1628–1631.
There is no later Oryol extract whose heading names the target surname.

Separately checked were Oryol, Bazdyrevo, Belaya and the neighbouring lines of the Nechaevs,
Pakhomovs, Terentyevs, Krivolapovs, Trunovs, Nemtsovs, Tokhtamyshevs,
Kostins, Grevtsovs, Mikulins, Timofeevs and Lunins. The headings gave no exact continuations
of the early Oryol cluster. The individual Mikulin, Timofeev and
Lunin turned out to be a scribe or other later people; the headings with the Bazdyrevs
relate to other districts.

This is not the weakness of one search query. The Oryol sequence
of f. 281, op. 14 from 1643 shows that the catalogue is capable of naming in detail
the parties, owners and places, but neither the early Bazdyrevo chain nor a quotation from
the grant of Belyay, Mikula or Nechay appears in the headings. So there is no longer any need to repeat a general search
by surname or by neighbours. A later extract, if it has survived,
must be sought from the history of a specific pomestye and its subsequent holders, or
within the folios, not by the heading of a file.

The full-audit card has been supplemented with the results of the check:
`PUB-RGADA-ALL-CATALOG-PRE1600-ANFILOGOV-AUDIT`.

#### A Full Collation of Three Published "Original" Lists

It proved possible to obtain and check in full issue 13 of the almanac «Российская
генеалогия» (Russian Genealogy) (2023), with L. E. Shabaev's publication on pp. 284–375. It
prints three original lists, but Oryol is included only in the first:

- **1627/28**, РГАДА, f. 210, op. 14, d. 22: Oryol occupies fols. 118–140;
  here are the already known Vaska Dmitreev syn Onpilogov, Ivashko and
  Oleshka, the sons of Vasily Onfilogov, and Marka Karpov syn «Онфилохов» (Onfilokhov);
- **1630/31–1631/32**, f. 210, op. 10, d. 86: Tula, Solova, Odoev,
  Aleksin, Tarusa, Serpukhov and the Cossacks — **Oryol is absent**;
- **1633/34**, f. 210, op. 10, d. 97: the list of towns again does not include
  Oryol.

So the absence of Anfilogovs from the second and third lists cannot be taken as
the disappearance of the family: these documents did not cover the Oryol service
corporation at all. At the same time the collation strengthens the importance of file 45. The 1631 list
from **f. 210, op. 10, d. 45, pt. II** is not a duplicate of the published file
86 of the same time: they have a different set of towns, and only file 45 explicitly
promises an Oryol section.

The full PDF has been saved in the private research layer; pages 284–287 and
318–320 have been rendered separately. Verification copies of pages 318–320 have been attached to the cards of the four Oryol Anfilogovs,
and their "Names and Meaning" sections have been expanded.

[Open-access PDF of the issue](https://cloud.mail.ru/public/YbCU/htca3PqxG),
[page of the edition](https://oldbasman.ru/magazin-2/product/rossijskaya-genealogiya-nauchnyj-almanah-vypusk-trinadcatyj).

### The Name "Ivan Nechaevich" Turned Out Not to Be a Unique Signature

A continuous search of the full text of the pre-revolutionary edition of the cadastral survey books gave
an important negative control. In the Tula book of 1587–1589, in Zaupsky
stan, on printed p. 1193, is written:

> «В той же дер. Васильевской-Объединина, за Иваном за Нечаевым сыном
> Бредихина старое его поместье...»
>
> *Translation:* "In the same village of Vasilyevskaya-Obyedinina, held by Ivan Nechaev syn
> Bredikhina, his old pomestye..."

This is **Ivan Nechaevich Bredikhin**. He cannot be the Oryol Ivan Nechaevich
Anpilogov: the surname Bredikhin is named directly, and the Tula holding is described
as his old pomestye. But the find changes the assessment of the Dedilov candidate.
Until now the coincidence of the rare pair of name and patronymic — the Dedilov desyatsky
Ivanko Nechaev in 1588/89 and the Oryol Ivan Nechaevich Anpilogov in 1595
— was one of the main arguments. It has now been proved that the same pair
belonged at the same time to yet another man in the same broad
Tula–Dedilov milieu.

Consequently, the formula **"Ivan Nechaevich" is insufficient on its own**. To
link the Dedilov desyatsky with the Oryol Anpilogov an independent
second feature is needed: the brother Pankrat, a direct surname entry, a note on transfer,
an earlier town or a proven continuity of land. The candidate is not discarded,
because he retains a strong family detail — the brother Ponkratko and the double
record in the Dedilov book — but the level of confidence has been lowered.

At the same time Bredikhin strengthens another, network part of the hypothesis. The Bredikhins
were an old Tula landholding family, and a few years later the Bredikhin
family is present in Oryol's Cheremoshnya next to the landed cluster of
Anfilogovs. This is not the route of a specific person but evidence of a common
service corridor in which identical names, patronymics and neighbouring surnames
could recur.

A full check of the volume also found no stronger family signature
**Mikula → Vasily, Timofey, Karp** outside Oryol. The negative result does not prove
a local Oryol origin for this line: the published corpus is incomplete,
and many sixteenth-century service lists have not survived or have not been published. But it
shows that further search must be conducted not by a single name but by clusters
of relatives, offices and lands.

A photocopy of the official copy held by the Russian Geographical Society (РГО) has been saved in the cards for the Tula
neighbourhood corridor and the Dedilov candidate. No separate profile or map point
is created for Bredikhin: he does not belong to the surname series under research.
[Official catalogue record of the РГО edition](https://elib.rgo.ru/handle/123456789/228023).

### The 1668 Inventory Has Led to a Register of Lost Oryol Desyatni

The new tactic yielded not yet another similar-sounding name but a precise meta-source.
V. N. Kozlyakov established that **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 20, d. 221–224**
preserve an inventory of the Razryad archive compiled under the dumny dyak (council secretary)
D. M. Bashmakov in 1668. It was created at a time when the archive still
held many sixteenth-century service books that were later lost.

For Oryol a specific address has been established: **d. 222, fols. 45–45 v. and 75**.
On fol. 45 begins the «Список Тульской» ("Tula list"), in which, together with Tula, were counted
Solova, Serpukhov, Tarusa, Aleksin, Odoev, Mozhaysk, Vereya, Zvenigorod,
Dedilov, Bogoroditsky, Mtsensk, **Oryol**, Novosil, Chern, Kromy and Cossacks
of various kinds. Next comes the heading **«А тех городов по переписке
десятенные всякие дела»** ("And of those towns, per the inventory, all kinds of desyatnya files"), and separately «списки тульские и иных городов» ("lists of Tula and other towns").

This does not make Tula a proven place of origin for Anfilogovs: what is involved is
an archival grouping, not a party of settlers. The value of the find lies
elsewhere. On three folios a list of Oryol desyatni by year,
compiler and condition may have survived. Even if the early desyatnya itself perished, its exact
title will make it possible to search for **an old alphabetical index to the desyatnya**, the eighteenth-century reference books (knigi spravok)
and later extracts — by exactly the method by which M. G. Krotov has already
reconstructed the lost desyatni of other towns.

The next order is now extremely small: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 20, d. 222,
fols. 45–45 v. and 75**, necessarily with the neighbouring folios. Until they have been read it cannot be
asserted that the register names an Oryol desyatnya of 1577, 1584, 1590 or 1591.
The map and the profiles do not change.

On 29 August 2026 the complete public CSV export of
the catalogue of digitised files of ГИС УИАД was additionally checked. It contains not only no **f. 210, op. 20,
d. 222**, but no rows at all for fond 210, inventory 20. This is not evidence
that the file has been lost: it merely confirms that the unit needed is not currently included in
the public catalogue for remote viewing. The identically numbered **f. 210, op. 9,
d. 222** belongs to 1648/49 and is not the register needed; the available
**f. 210, op. 4, d. 270** is a different book, a Moscow enrolment book of 1628.
So the next practical step remains the same: a separate order for exactly
three folios, or a search for an already made private copy.

Source: V. N. Kozlyakov, *Служилые люди России XVI–XVII веков* (Servicemen of Russia in the Sixteenth and Seventeenth Centuries), 2018,
pp. 296–299. Card:
`PUB-KOZLYAKOV-2018-RGADA-1668-OREL-DECENNIA-INVENTORY`.
[Open text of the book](https://djvu.online/file/TjLCjL0xo3U9E),
[official guide to fond 210](https://guides.rusarchives.ru/terms/9/2464/razryadnyy),
[ГИС УИАД catalogue](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad).

### The Key Books of 1625 Have Appeared in the Remote-Viewing Catalogue

A fresh check of the full public export of ГИС УИАД removed the earlier technical
dead end for **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, kn. 330–331**. Both units are present not
only in the paper inventory but also in a separate catalogue of **digitised files**, whose links
lead to the official portal for selection and paid remote
viewing:

- **kn. 330**, 1625–1626, ll. 1–426: the Korchakovsky and Nepolotsky stany (district subdivisions);
  official digital identifier `10000393856014`;
- **kn. 331**, 1625–1626, ll. 1–637: the Kamensky, Toychukovsky and Nougorsky
  stany; identifier `10000393855014`.

This changes the practical tactics. Kn. 330 contains the Korchakovsky stan and
**Bazdyrevo** — the main landholding cluster of Anfilogovs in 1594/95 and 1625.
That is why it must be viewed first: the full entries for Ivan
Vasilyevich, Vasily Dmitriyevich, Aleksey and Grigory Vasilyevich, Martyn and
Kuzma Karpovich, and also all references to the reference book (pripravochnaya kniga) of 1594/95,
the old salary list, the original grant and the previous holder. Kn. 331
is needed in a second pass for Ermak Pakhomovich and Maltsovskaya on the Nugr.

On 29 August 2026 the official domain `online.archives.ru` did not open from
the research environment: the connection ended with `ERR_ADDRESS_UNREACHABLE`.
This is a technical unavailability of the portal, not an absence of images. The earlier
statement that the books can be obtained only by a paper order or in the reading
room is no longer current: the next step is a remote ГИС УИАД session once the
portal restores access.

[Catalogue entry for kn. 330](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=1209&inv=1&case=330#rgada_opisi),
[catalogue entry for kn. 331](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=1209&inv=1&case=331#rgada_opisi),
[official card for kn. 330](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000001151014/10000005098014/10000393856014/),
[official card for kn. 331](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000001151014/10000005098014/10000393855014/).

#### Ten Needed Spreads Had Already Been Ordered by a Researcher of Bazdyrevo

A search by the archival call number gave a far more targeted result. On 30 March 2016
A. S. Nilogov reported that he had received good colour copies from РГАДА of
**f. 1209, op. 1, d. 330**. He listed ten spreads:

- ll. **72 v.–73, 73 v.–74, 74 v.–75**;
- ll. **76 v.–77, 77 v.–78**;
- ll. **82 v.–83, 83 v.–84, 84 v.–85, 85 v.–86, 86 v.–87**.

This is not a random order for the Oryol district. In the same message Nilogov
listed copies from the census books (perepisnye knigi) of 1646 and 1678 and explicitly marked in them
**Bazdyrevo** and **Sychov Kolodez**. So it is most likely that the selection
from book 330 also covers the full entries of the Bazdyrevo complex. But for now this is
an inference from the content of the order, not a reading of the manuscript: without the images themselves one cannot
attribute each spread to Anfilogovs or extract kinship from it.

The next day the researcher asked on the forum who could help read
a fragment of the cadastral survey book (pistsovaya kniga) of 1626, but the current version of the message has no
attachment. He was directed to a separate palaeography thread; the thread was later deleted,
and the needed fragment was not found in its surviving Web Archive pages. A check
of Nilogov's open books of 2020 and 2024 also did not reveal a publication of these
folios.

A repeated check of the live page removed one false digital trail. The note
**«Изображение на стороннем сайте: 9213045m.jpg на savepic.ru»** ("Image on a third-party site: 9213045m.jpg on savepic.ru") belongs not
to Nilogov's message but to the following message by another author — an announcement of
the book «Новосильско-Одоевское княжество» (The Novosil-Odoyev Principality). It is not a folio of file 330, and there is no longer any need to look for it in
old caches. In January 2018 a researcher of the Nemtsovs once
again asked Nilogov to check the inhabitants of Bazdyrevo against the same copies, but
did not receive a public reading: he was referred to the short register. So
the photocopies really did remain private and were not later published in the thread.

The practical priority has now changed: instead of a complete viewing of 426 folios
one must first obtain **ll. 72 v.–78 and 82 v.–87** — from the owner of the copies or
by a repeated order through ГИС УИАД/РГАДА. Each spread should be read together with
the neighbouring page, writing out the formulas «по старым писцовым книгам» ("according to the old cadastral books"), the previous
holder, the father's or brother's pomestye, the grant, the transfer (otkaz) and the previous town.
Only such a literal formula will give grounds for a new kinship link or route
on the map.

[A. S. Nilogov's message with the exact folio numbers](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1758/62261/p2148092.htm#pp2148092).

#### The Open Cloud and the Full Register Have Been Checked to the End

A direct viewing of the public cloud of the project «Сказочки, сказки и сказищи
Орловщины» (Little Tales, Tales and Great Tales of the Oryol Land) clarified the limit of the available material. In the folder
`Орловский уезд/Другие документы/РГАДА ф. 1209` **d. 329** is published, but
there is no **d. 330, d. 331 or d. 10047**. This matches the forum list: d. 329
is marked as bought out, while next to books 330–331 there are no notes of purchase or donation.

On the other hand, the cloud holds the full text of **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 85, d. 13**, of
222622 bytes. It was checked by a full search for Анфилог-/Анпилог-/Онфилог- and
close graphic variants. Besides the Bazdyrevo entries 79–81 and 84, the register
gives the already known entries: 129 — Ilya, Vasily and Anton Ivanovich in Belaya;
512 and 523 — the empty pomestya of Sofon Timofeyevich and Ivan Nechayevich; 1347 —
the empty pomestye of Ermak Anpilogov. No new bearers of the family series were found in the whole
text. Entry 1361 is not a hit: the earlier reading
«Анфилоговское» was corrected against the manuscript to **«Филипповское»** (Filippovskoye).

Thus the short register is finally exhausted. It contains neither
the previous town, nor the original grant, nor inheritance formulas capable of
explaining the family's appearance in Oryol. Continuation is possible only through the full
books 330–331 or through Nilogov's ten already located colour spreads.

[List of the project's Oryol files](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1851/142603/p5034895.htm#pp5034895),
[the project's public cloud](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ).

### Six Early Belgorod–Kursk Files Are Already Available for Targeted Reading

The check of the full ГИС УИАД export gave a second practical result. All
six files to which the derived index points for the early non-Oryol forms
Anpilogov/Onpilogov have already been digitised:

- Belgorod, 1633–1634 — **f. 210, op. 12, d. 50**, one Anpilogov;
- Belgorod, 1638–1639 — **f. 210, op. 4, d. 1**, one Anpilogov;
- Kursk, 1642 — **f. 210, op. 4, d. 188**, three Onpilogovs;
- Belgorod, 1642 — **f. 210, op. 4, d. 251**, one Onpilogov;
- Belgorod, 1646 — **f. 210, op. 4, d. 213**, one Anpilogov;
- Belgorod, 1648 — **f. 210, op. 4, d. 250**, one Onpilogov.

The Kursk book of 620 folios is especially valuable. A note is already known in it about another
serviceman, **«сшел на Орел»** ("left for Oryol"), that is, the compiler recorded the former and
new town of service. Therefore the three Onpilogovs must be checked not only for
names and salaries, but also for such notes. In the Belgorod book of 1646 the line sought
has been located not in the whole composite file but in the razbornaya desyatnya (sorting register) on
**ll. 81–237**; in the book of 1642, on **ll. 1–82**.

Later it also proved possible to narrow down the internal structure of Kursk file 188 without paid
viewing of the images. In the compiler's open section-by-section list two lines
**«Онпилогов»** (Onpilogov) are in the main body, opened by the heading **«служат по
выбору»** ("serve by selection"), and the third is set apart among the **«новиков верстанья 7147 года»** ("new recruits enrolled in the year 7147"), that is,
1638/39. This is not just an overall count: the three positions really are divided
as `2 + 1` between two service categories.

The distribution exactly repeats the known snapshot of 1640: **Semyon Yakovlevich** and
**Sergey Grigoryevich** stand in the main body, while **Ivan Semyonovich** is named
a new recruit enrolled by the voivode Ivan Buturlin. That is why precisely these three become
the strongest candidates for the lines of 1642. But the names in the list of file 188 are not
disclosed, and a candidate identification does not replace reading the manuscript. Three
full entries are needed, with folios, salaries, actual pomestya and possible
notes on the previous town.

This is not yet an answer about the source of the Oryol core of the 1590s: the documents are younger
than it by almost half a century and may describe descendants or independent groups. But
they make it possible for the first time to check whether the Oryol paternal lines — the Karpovichi, Vasilyevichi, Pakhomovichi, Nechayevichi and
others — recur in Belgorod and Kursk, and whether a direct note on a move from Oryol or to Oryol has survived.
Direct remote-viewing addresses have been entered into each file card. The images on
the official portal are paid; no order was
placed without the user's confirmation.

[Belgorod 1633](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=210&inv=12&case=50#rgada_opisi),
[Belgorod 1639](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=210&inv=4&case=1#rgada_opisi),
[Kursk 1642](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=210&inv=4&case=188#rgada_opisi),
[Belgorod 1642](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=210&inv=4&case=251#rgada_opisi),
[Belgorod 1646](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=210&inv=4&case=213#rgada_opisi),
[Belgorod 1648](https://metrics.tilda.ws/gisuiad?fund=210&inv=4&case=250#rgada_opisi).

### Krotov's Compendium Dispelled a False Expectation but Gave a Chain of Archival Reference Files

M. G. Krotov's full index of towns was checked again: for **Oryol only
1596 is given**. Neither an early Oryol desyatnya of 1577 nor a separate book of
1590–1591 is named in the corpus known to him of surviving, lost and
reconstructed desyatni. So searching for such a book by
an assumed year as an already known storage unit is a false tactic.

On the other hand, Krotov described in detail the mechanism by which lost books were restored. In
1788–1791 the Razryad Archive compiled family alphabets by district with
the folio numbers of the original desyatni. For the Heraldry Office (Gerold­meysterskaya kontora)
draft reference statements (spravki) were made, into which names, patronymics, salaries, notes and the folios of
the originals were transferred. The chain of addresses looks like this:

1. **f. 210, op. 20, d. 222, ll. 45–45 v., 75** — the Oryol part of the register
   of 1668;
2. **f. 210, op. 20, d. 225–226** — the draft and fair copy of the inventory of the Razryad
   Archive of 1735;
3. Krotov's historical reference **f. 248, op. 106, kn. 1180** — the inventory of the old
   alphabets; the modern call number needs clarification;
4. **f. 388, op. 1, d. 102** — the book of reference statements of 1787; the former
   office No. **853**, under which researchers continue to cite
   it;
5. books of draft genealogical reference statements of **f. 388** — possible literal
   extracts on the surname from the already lost desyatnya.

The full digital export of ГИС УИАД contains **not a single file of f. 388**.
Consequently, the absence of Anfilogovs in a catalogue search says nothing
about the content of the reference statements: the fond itself is not covered by this check. It must be sought through
the paper inventory and ordered separately. This is now the main archival route to
pre-Oryol service, parallel to reading the cadastral books of 1625.

A substantial OCR error has been corrected here. In the earlier machine text Krotov's
reference read as «л. 2680» ("l. 2680"), but the photocopy of printed page 59 clearly gives
**«ЦГАДА, ф. 248, оп. 106, кн. 1180»** ("ЦГАДА, f. 248, op. 106, kn. 1180"). The same page confirms a second
address — the book with former No. **853**, in which the documentation on compiling the
alphabets was deposited. The official inventory shows the modern sequential number:
**РГАДА, f. 388, op. 1, d. 102**, «Книга справок Разрядного архива» (Book of Reference Statements of the Razryad Archive), 1787.
Images of the printed reference and of the official inventory page have been saved in the project;
the order can now be formulated without guessing at a non-existent folio 2680 and
without mixing the old and modern numbering.

However, the OCR correction revealed a second, now archival, conflict. In the modern
official РГАДА inventory the same **f. 248, op. 106, kn. 1180** is titled
«Росписной список г. Карачева, составленный при передаче его новому воеводе
подполковнику Осипу Стрекалову» ("Handover list of the town of Karachev, compiled on its transfer to the new voivode,
Lieutenant Colonel Osip Strekalov"), 1733. Such a title does not correspond to
a central inventory of alphabets to the desyatni. So book 1180 cannot yet
be ordered as demonstrably needed: the document may have been renumbered or
moved, or there was an archival error in the 1983 reference.

The correct wording of a reference inquiry now includes both sides
of the discrepancy: Krotov's reference on p. 55, note 13, and the present title of book
