1180 — with a request to report the modern call number of the **inventory of alphabets to the desyatni
of the Razryad Archive**. The independent address remains valid in a dual form:
the modern **f. 388, op. 1, d. 102**, the former office No. **853**.
Krotov directly calls this book the documentation on compiling the alphabets themselves.
[The modern official inventory of f. 248, op. 106](https://rgada.info/opisi/248-opis_106.pdf).

A modern publication independently confirmed the content of the book, although
A. Yu. Kabanov cites it by the former number **853**. In the official inventory
the sequential number for ordering is **d. 102**. On l. 201 Kabanov read the alphabet
of the lost Lukh desyatnya of 1580/81: 52 men and 31 surnames. Then
he compared the surnames, kinship ties and cadastral books and established that
part of the Lukh corporation had come from the Kashin, Pereslavl-Zalessky,
Rostov, Uglich and Suzdal districts. This is an almost exact model of
our task — the origin of a new service "town" (gorod, a district corps of servitors) is established not by
a single similar-sounding name but by the whole family cluster.

The published Lukh list has no forms Anfilogov / Anpilogov / Onfilogov,
so it gives no direct route to Oryol. But it does prove that
an order for **d. 102 (former No. 853)** is real and can yield the family network
of vanished desyatni.
A photocopy of the page with the list and the conclusion about the places of origin has been saved in the project.
[A. Yu. Kabanov, «Служилый “город” Луховского уезда в XVI–XVII веках» (The Service "Town" of the Lukh District in the 16th–17th Centuries)](http://www.roiarch.com/doc/RG17/%D0%90%D0%BB%D1%8C%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B0%D1%85-17.pdf).

Even more important, the official inventory made it possible to link the books of reference statements with the already
found Kursk nobility proceedings. The earliest Anfilogovs were entered in
Part VI by the protocol of **20 January 1798**. Two
specific units correspond to that year: **f. 388, op. 1, d. 120** (former No. **871**, 1798)
and **d. 121** (former No. **872**, 1798–1801). The draft reply may have
preserved not only the extracts for Ivan, Yefrem, Yakov and Mikhail Vasilyevich,
but also crossed-out lines for other people of the same surname, and from the 1780s on, a register of
all the desyatni and muster lists (smotrennye spiski) consulted. That is why d. 120–121,
rather than a general viewing of the whole fond, become the first order in the search for
the family's pre-Oryol service.

A folio-by-folio control check of the inventory on 30 August 2026 revealed an important
numbering trap. On archival l. 12 there is another series with the same sequential
Nos. 120–121: a list of files of the Razryad and the Senate for 1812 and a register of heraldic
files gathered after the French left Moscow. The books of reference statements sought stand
on l. 46 and are distinguished by the former office Nos. 871–872. So in
an order one cannot write only «д. 120–121» ("d. 120–121"): the old numbers,
the years and the title «Книга справок Разрядного архива» are all needed together.
Pages 12 and 45–46 of the official inventory are saved in
`public/archive/evidence/rgada/388-1-inventory/`.

This lead connects with a specific person. The «Новый энциклопедический
словарь» (New Encyclopaedic Dictionary) of 1911 derives the noble branch from **Vasily Dmitriyevich
Anfilogov**, «записанного по городу Орлу московского стола в десятках» ("entered under the town of Oryol, Moscow desk, in the desyatni"). The
database already has not a supposed namesake but a primary record **P0034**: on l. 67
of the Oryol muster review (razbor) of 1620/21 one reads «Анпилогов Василей Дмитреев» ("Anpilogov Vasiley Dmitreyev"), and
he is in the category of those newly entered into service; in 1625 the same Vasily
Dmitriyevich holds land in Bozdyrevo. The coincidence of the full rare name,
the town and the family series makes the identity very strong. But the dictionary names
the legal starting point of the proven noble line; it does not prove that Vasily
was the first bearer of the surname or that he came to Oryol from another town.

The result is a testable documentary chain: **the Oryol original of 1620/21
→ a possible Razryad reference statement for the Kursk decision of 1798 in d.
120–121 → the printed genealogical formula of 1911**. The first and last links
have been read. The middle link now has an exact archival address and is capable of naming
the original desyatnya, its folios and the books consulted when the reference statement was compiled.

A printed list of the Moscow Archive of the Ministry of Justice of 1888
confirmed that this was not only a series of chronological books. From the reference statements
issued by the Senate Razryad Archive in 1715–1844
**two consolidated alphabets were compiled: the first by noble families, the second by various
subjects**. In the same table, book No. 120 for 1798 contains 124 reference statements
on 137 surnames, and book No. 121 for 1798–1802, 150 reference statements on 164
surnames. These are old book numbers within the list; the official inventory has already
linked them with the modern d. 120–121 and the former office
Nos. 871–872. Printed pages 150–152 are saved in the project. The table itself
does not disclose the surnames, so Anfilogovs have not yet been found, but the separate
noble alphabet gives a second way to reach the needed reference statement if its place
in the chronological book turns out to be unclear.

[Description of the documents and papers of the МАМЮ, bk. 5 (1888)](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:%D0%9E%D0%BF%D0%B8%D1%81%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%BA%D1%83%D0%BC%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_%D0%B8_%D0%B1%D1%83%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%B3_%D1%85%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BD_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%BC_%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%85%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B5_%D0%BC%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B8%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%80%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B2%D0%B0_%D1%8E%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%86%D0%B8%D0%B8_05_1888.pdf).

### The Kursk Branch Is No Longer a Set of Nameless Lines

A. Kurkulev's open folio-by-folio transcriptions made it possible to restore not only
the number of surnames in the derived index but specific people and their service
sequence. In the list of 1630 we find Fyodor Grigoryevich, Semyon
Osipovich, Semyon Yakovlevich, Vasily Semyonovich and the sixteen-year-old Savoska
Onpilogov. In the desyatnya of 1636 Semyon Yakovlevich and Sergey
Grigoryevich recur; in the estimate book (smetnaya kniga) of 1640, Semyon Yakovlevich, Sergey Grigoryevich
and a new Ivan Semyonovich.

The biography of **Semyon Yakovlevich Onpilogov** is especially reliable. A rare full
name, one town, the same early salary of 100 chetverti and an unbroken series of entries for
1630, presumably 1632, 1636 and 1640 end with a line of the regimental
list of 1655, already checked against a digital copy of the manuscript. Three former
profiles have been merged into one: these are not three random namesakes but the twenty-five-year
service biography of one man of Kursk.

For the link with Oryol the key figure remains **Semyon Osipovich**. Osip Vasilyevich
Anfilogov is documented in the Oryol district in 1594/95, and by date he
could have been his father. But not a single Kursk list so far says «из Орла» ("from Oryol") or
«сын орлянина Осипа Васильевича» ("son of the Oryol man Osip Vasilyevich"). So the kinship remains a strong address
hypothesis, not a genealogical line. The second network indicator is the Deriglazovs:
they recur next to Onpilogovs in one salary milieu in Kursk after
a shared landholding neighbourhood in the Oryol district. This strengthens the model of
a group transfer of servitors but does not replace a transfer entry.

[The Kursk list of 1630](https://www.old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/ku260428.html),
[the desyatnya of 1636](https://old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/ku260605.html),
[the estimate book of 1640](https://www.old-kursk.ru/book/kurkulev_a/sm260226.html).

### A Real Resettlement Mechanism Has Been Found: Kursk/Oryol — Karpov — Oboyan

A publication by I. P. Babin and M. M. Ozerov based on РГАДА documents gave exactly the
type of material that was being sought: the state selection of service families for a new
town. On 26 February 1649 it was ordered to build an ostrog (stockaded fort) on the Oboyan
gorodishche (old fortified site) and to settle deti boyarskie **of Kursk and Oryol** around it. The future
inhabitants were chosen «на вечное житьё» ("for permanent residence") from the Kursk and Oryol resettled men (svedentsy);
most of them had already been sent a year or two earlier to serve and receive
land at the Karpov sentry post (Karpovo storozhevye). On 29 July the combined detachment set out from Kursk for
the Oboyan gorodishche.

This proves a common organised corridor **Oryol/Kursk → Karpov → Oboyan**.
Candidates were chosen among deti boyarskie with families and means, able to
plough, build and serve; preference was given to men with sons and a large
male kin group. First went the heads of families with their elder sons, then
the rest of the household, grain and livestock were moved.

**Sergey Grigoryevich Onpilogov** fits this mechanism almost exactly. He
is named in Kursk in 1636 and 1640, and in February 1651 already in the newly
built Oboyan, with his sons Sidor, aged twenty, and Yeremey, aged ten.
Both sons were born before Oboyan was founded, that is, a ready-made family resettled,
not a single new recruit. The rare full name and a consistent chronology allow
the Kursk and Oboyan lines to be treated as one biography with high confidence.

But the personal stage via Karpov has not yet been proven: the published fragments of the files of
1649–1650 do not name Sergey. The decisive address is **РГАДА,
f. 210, op. 12, ch. 1, d. 290 and 293**, as well as the review at Oboyan of the Kursk and Oryol
resettled men on 19 September 1650 mentioned by researchers.
Files 290 and 293 have been located in the ГИС УИАД digital catalogue. Until a line naming him is found,
two proven events remain on the map — Kursk and Oboyan — with no added-in
stop at Karpov.

[Publication on the founding of Oboyan](https://old-kursk.ru/book/obojan/ra032.html),
[the first description of the Oboyan district](https://old-kursk.ru/book/babin/190127.html),
[file 290 in ГИС УИАД](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000003223014/10000476015014),
[file 293 in ГИС УИАД](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000003223014/10000476018014).

### A Mass Match Between Kursk and Oboyan: Sergey Is Not a Lone Namesake

The full text of the Oboyan desyatnya of 1651 from **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 4,
d. 228** was compared with all the three-part names in the published
Kursk lists of 1636 and 1640. The result: **38 verbatim identical
combinations of name, patronymic and family name**. Twenty of them are in
the desyatnya of 1636, twenty-nine in the estimate book of 1640; some of the people
occur in both Kursk lists. In addition, a number of easily
explained graphic variants were found.

Among the exact matches: Arkhip Yefimov syn Umrikhin, Filip Nasonov syn
Melekhov, Yermol Ondreyev syn Bykanov, Yerofey Ivanov syn Goryainov, Karp
Semenov syn Golovin, Logvin Fomin syn Koptev, Semen Yefimov syn Mikitin,
Timofey Onofreyev syn Krivolapov, Fedor Mitrofanov syn Zakharyin. Sergey
belongs to a close orthographic pair: **«Сергей Григорьев сын Онпилогов»** ("Sergey Grigoryev syn Onpilogov")
in Kursk and **«Сергей Григорьев сын Анпилогов»** ("Sergey Grigoryev syn Anpilogov") in Oboyan.

This changes the strength of the conclusion. Sergey's move from the Kursk service corporation
to the Oboyan one now rests not only on his rare name but also on a mass
match of his milieu. The personal stage **via Karpov** still cannot be put on
the map: there is no Karpov line naming Sergey. But the sequence
**Kursk 1636/1640 → Oboyan 1651** has become highly reliable.

At the same time the main archival addresses have been refined. The material closest to the sought-for
transfer list is in **f. 210, op. 12, ch. 1, d. 324,
ll. 49–84** (a list of men of Oboyan) and **d. 328**: ll. 77–92 and 135–170 — lists
of the Oboyan deti boyarskie from the muster of Ivan Koltovsky and Osip Arsenyev, l. 264 —
one more list. File 328 also contains numerous documents on
men of Oboyan, Oryol and Kursk assigned to service in Oboyan. It is the one that must be
checked first; files 290 and 293 remain the second echelon.

The desyatnya itself names four men who received Oboyan pay in
1649/50 but stayed in their former towns: Luka Kutafin lived in Kursk;
Mikita Borisov syn Melekhov, Karp Semenov syn Golovin and Sidor Fedorov syn
Mezintsov, in Oryol. Sidor's father and brothers were already in Oboyan. This is
rare direct evidence that a transfer could split one family and that
an entry in an early Oboyan list does not yet guarantee an actual move.

[Full publication of the Oboyan desyatnya of 1651](https://archive.rkursk.ru/sites/default/files/documents/event_hum_ed_15.pdf),
[description of the contents of file 328](https://forum.vgd.ru/3244/122141/),
[file 324 in ГИС УИАД](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000003223014/10000476052014),
[file 328 in ГИС УИАД](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000000157014/10000003223014/10000476056014).

### The Desyatnya of 1596: Families Divided Between Oryol and the Older Towns

The new tactic gave a result on the material closest to the early Oryol core.
All the town sections of a single **Enrolment Desyatnya of New Recruits of 1596**
(Verstalnaya desyatnya novikov) were compared not only by surname but also by the stricter link
**father's name + family name**. A manual pass through 143 Oryol lines and
twelve possible towns of origin was then extended to the whole document:
in 71 town sections 2253 new recruits were automatically recognised, among them 134
men of Oryol with a full three-part name formula. Exactly three pairs were found:

- **Solova — Oryol:** Afonasey and Prokuda **Vasilyevy synovya Penkovy** (sons of Vasily, Penkov);
- **Bolkhov — Oryol:** Zamyatnya and Gavrilo **Stepanovy synovya Pavlovy** (sons of Stepan, Pavlov);
- **Oryol — Ryazan:** Ivan and Vasiley **Ivanovy synovya Kamenevy** (sons of Ivan, Kamenev).

All six readings were checked not only against the publication but also against the open
РГАДА photocopies: f. 181, op. 2, d. 120, ll. 49 v., 57 v., 58 v., 60,
61 v. and 88. On l. 58 v., together with Prokuda Penkov, stands the man who interests
us, **Pavel Terentyev syn Anfilogov** (Pavel, son of Terenty, Anfilogov).

These pairs cannot automatically be declared brothers: there is no such word in the desyatnya,
and Pavlov and Ivan are too widespread. But a simultaneous match of both
the father and the family name, with different personal names, is considerably stronger than
an ordinary surname parallel. The Penkov pair is especially distinctive. The material
shows a real way in which a new service "town" was formed: the sons of
a possibly single paternal family could be listed at the same time under Oryol and under
an older service corporation.

For Anfilogovs no direct match **Terentyev syn Anfilogov** was found in another
town section. However, the mechanism found changes the search:
one should look not only for the ready-made surname but also for other children of Terenty,
Timofey, Ivan and the other fathers of the early Oryol Anfilogovs, including
cases where the family form had not yet become fixed. No route on the map is built
from these three control pairs for now.

Possible scribal and editorial fluctuations were additionally checked: for
each man of Oryol one letter substitution, insertion or loss was allowed either in
the father's name or in the family part. This did not yield a single fourth pair.
The negative result is important: within one synchronous desyatnya there is no
hidden dense family cluster that would point to a single
donor town. Three separate bridges are visible — to Solova, Bolkhov and Ryazan. This
supports the model of a mixed recruitment of Oryol and requires searching for the former
place of each paternal household separately.

One more control specifically tested a situation dangerous for this research:
the same personal name and father's name but a different third name. In
the data set twelve rare inter-town pairs of this type were found. For example,
the Oryol Dmitrey Danilov syn Pankov and the Tula Dmitrey Danilov syn Bredikhin
do not become one person merely because the first two parts coincide.
This also calibrates our best individual hypothesis: the Ryazhsk Pavel Terentyev
syn Iyevlev of 1591 cannot be declared the Oryol Pavel Terentyev
syn Anfilogov of 1596 on the basis of name and father alone. His candidacy remains
strong because of two sureties, his personal disappearance from subsequent
Ryazhsk records and a fitting chronology, but proof still requires
an entry with a double name, a transfer, shared land or an inheritance.

[Publication of the desyatnya of 1596](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1580-1600/Desjatnja_novikov/text.php).
The card with all the saved spreads:
`PUB-RGADA-181-2-120-1596-OREL-CROSSCITY-FAMILY-PAIRS`.

### Two Populated Holdings in 1762: Kursk and Livny

In the full edition of S. V. Chernikov's monograph not only the alphabet
of surnames was found but also the whole quantitative row of table 31 on p. 231:

> «Анпилоговы | – | – | 2 | 19 | Курск: II – 1 (1); Ливны: II – 1 (18)».
>
> *Translation:* "Anpilogovs | – | – | 2 | 19 | Kursk: II – 1 (1); Livny: II – 1 (18)."

The table heading allows the figures to be unpacked. In the source layer of 1700
no populated estates or peasant households are recorded for Anpilogovs. This does not
mean that there were no people of the surname: the known service entries of 1700 do not
disappear because of it, and unpopulated land and information outside the scope of the Alphabets to
the Skazki of the General Court could have been left out of the row. By 1762 the compendium
records **two estates and 19 male serfs**: one holding with one soul
in the Kursk district and one with eighteen in the Livny district.

The completeness tables make it possible to measure the limitation. For the 1700 layer Oryol and
Livny are represented at 100 %, Kursk at 70 %, the whole region at 90 %. For
the Third Revision Oryol is represented at 100 %, Kursk at 98 %, Livny only at
54 %. So the 18 Livny souls are a reliable number found, but not
a guaranteed final total for the whole district.

The geography confirms two already visible branches, but not the origin of the Oryol
core. The row does not name the owners and does not allow a route to be drawn
Oryol → Kursk or Oryol → Livny. On the other hand it points to the next name-bearing layer:
Chernikov built the 1762 part from the procurators' lists and the skazki (declarations) of the Third
Revision. They should contain the owner's surname, name and patronymic, the settlement,
the composition of the serfs, and sometimes the previous owner under the Second Revision.
The list of sources and the archival headings already narrow the order: **Kursk district —
РГАДА, f. 350, op. 2, d. 1676–1682; Livny district — d. 1736–1739**.

A separate Record has been created,
`PUB-CHERNIKOV-2003-P231-ANPILOGOV-ESTATES-1700-1762`; the table
heading, the full spread and a large fragment of the row are attached to it. No new profiles
are created, because there are no personal names in the table; no route on the map is
added.

## Savyolov's Archive: ОПИ ГИМ, Fond 216

The archival address of the working materials of the author of the first special article
on Anfilogovs has been established. The official list of fonds of the Department of Written Sources
of the State Historical Museum names **fond 216 — Leonid Mikhaylovich
Savyolov**. The description explicitly lists the genealogical materials he collected,
his scholarly works, the papers of the Historical-Genealogical Society and his scholarly correspondence.
Consequently, the draft of the article «Анфилоговы» (Anfilogovs) of 1906, alphabetical extracts or
references to the desyatni used should be sought here first of all.

The public catalogue of ОПИ ГИМ was checked on 29 August 2026. It shows only
30 archival fonds, 4260 storage units and 25,718 documents; the queries
«Савелов» (Savelov) and «Анфилогов» (Anfilogov) gave zero results. This is not a negative check
of the fond itself: fond 216 is present in the full official list but is not part
of the published portion of the catalogue. The internal inventory of the fond or a reference from
the ОПИ staff is needed.

The priority order is the preparatory materials for the first issue
of «Родословные записи» (Genealogical Notes), the alphabetical folder for the letter A and a possible separate folder
«Анфилоговы (Анпилоговы, Онпилоговы)» ("Anfilogovs (Anpilogovs, Onpilogovs)"). In it, look for old archival call numbers,
generations that did not make it into print, the former town of service and the source of the line about
Ivan Anpilogov in Stary Oskol in 1695. Officially, requests for files
are submitted in person; the reading room is open on Wednesdays and Thursdays and is closed in July–August.
Reference address: opi@shm.ru.

## Savyolov Named His Working Archives: The Main Trail Leads to the МАМЮ

The preface to the first issue of «Родословные записи» changes the search tactic.
Savyolov stated directly that, besides printed sources, he had used his own
extracts from the **Moscow Archive of the Ministry of Justice** and the Moscow
Noble Deputies' Assembly, and had also looked through the «Портфели Миллера» (Miller's Portfolios) in
the Moscow Archive of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and Sakharov's papers in
the Rumyantsev Museum. This is primary testimony from the author himself, not a later
assumption about his method.

A comparison with a pre-revolutionary edition made it possible to separate the known part from
the unknown. **All eight of Savyolov's Oryol names were already printed in 1877**
in «Писцовые книги Московского государства» (Cadastral Books of the Muscovite State), pp. 975–1016. The same place
contains a ninth holder — Osip Vasilyevich Anfilogov, whom Savyolov did not include in
his short article. The heading of the publication gives an old archival address:
**Moscow Archive of the Ministry of Justice, No. 399 of the cadastral books**. The main
modern unit is РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 329.

Thus for the Oryol line there is no need to assume a lost personal
extract: Savyolov had an accessible printed prototype, published 29 years before
his dictionary. The working papers of fond 216 remain valuable, but now primarily
for **Stary Oskol 1695**, possible notes on the Kursk
line of 1628 that has been found, and information he did not include in the article.

An alternative path has also been checked: the «Список рукописей
до 1825 года, принадлежащих Леониду Михайловичу Савелову» (List of Manuscripts before 1825 Belonging to Leonid Mikhaylovich Savelov), published in 1898 (pp. 118–120). It contains
no Oryol or Anfilogov materials: after the charters of grant of the Savyolovs
it lists mainly papers of the Radziwiłłs, Ukrainian hetmans,
the Kochubeys and Little Russia. So the short article of 1906 most likely
grew not out of this personal collection of acts but out of a research card index and
archival extracts.

The general catalogue of ГИМ confirms that this type of paper really is
in fond 216: for example, item 843, l. 13 is described as Savyolov's
summary of extracts of 1628/29 and 1686. The next precise request to ОПИ ГИМ:
**extracts from the МАМЮ for the first issue, «Родословные записи», letter A,
Anfilogovs / Anpilogovs / Onpilogovs**. What is needed is a storage unit where, next to the
names, a Stary Oskol archival call number, the former town of service or a
generation before the Oryol core of the 1590s has been preserved.

### The Stary Oskol Line: A Candidate of 1685 Has Been Found

In the full publication of the itemised muster list (rospisnoy smotrenny spisok) of Stary Oskol of 1685
the first of the pikemen (kopeyshchiki) of interest to us is **Ivan Mikhaylov syn
Anpilov** (Ivan, son of Mikhail, Anpilov). Exact address: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 7a, d. 55, l. 189 v.** In
the same list, further down, stand Iyev Mikhaylov and Stepan Gerasimov deti Anpilovy (sons of Anpilov).

Savyolov's note and the line of 1685 coincide in four features: Ivan,
Anpilov/Anpilogov, Stary Oskol and pike service. Only the year differs —
**1685 versus 1695**, exactly ten years. This makes Ivan Mikhaylovich the strongest
candidate found and at the same time creates a testable fork:
either Savyolov or the typesetter erred in one digit, or there existed a separate
list of 1695 in which the same or another Ivan continued to serve.

Until the 1695 source is checked, the date on Savyolov's card has not been corrected, and
the Ivan without a patronymic has not been merged with Ivan Mikhaylovich. On the map there remains
only the confirmed point of Stary Oskol, with no new route.

## Full-Text Catalogue of Inventories: Bazdyrevo and Teterevinka, 1797

In the live full-text search of the АИС «Великія описи» (Great Inventories) the variants
Anfilogov, Anpilogov, Onpilogov and Amfilogov were checked. At the time of the check the catalogue
showed 4,413,436 headings and 899,007 entries. No early, pre-1700,
file with a reliable surname heading was found. The control query «Онахин» (Onakhin)
returned the already known file РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 78, ch. 2, d. 2217; so
the search really does distinguish Onakhin from Onanyin. The negative result
applies only to the indexed headings, not to names inside the folios.

On the other hand, a precise late landholding node was found in ГАОО (the State Archive of the Oryol Region). Two almost simultaneous
files — **f. 822, op. 1, d. 254 and 255** — were opened on the petition of the odnodvortsy (single-homestead freeholders)
Anpilogov for the registration of their purchase of land in the village of Bazdyrova /
Bazdyreva (12 and 14 January — 13 April 1797). The related proceedings
**f. 19, op. 1, d. 692** already name the place of residence: the Anpilogov odnodvortsy of the village of
Teterevinka disputed land with the Dolgiye, the Golovins and Shirokova
in 1797–1798. The same party is named in the correspondence **f. 9, op. 1,
d. 339**. One more file, **f. 19, op. 1, d. 525**, concerns the dispute
of the Zhukovs with Anpilogov M. in the Kromy district in 1793–1800.

These headings do not prove the origin of the sixteenth-century Oryol core. Their
value is practical: when a purchase was registered and disputed land examined, deeds of purchase,
cadastral extracts, boundary documents and references to previous
holders could be attached. So the first order to ГАОО is now precise: begin with d. 254–255 and
692, write down the names of all Anpilogovs, the seller and the dates of the attached documents,
then check d. 339 and 525. A Record has been created,
`PUB-VELIKIE-OPISI-1793-1800-ANPILOGOV-BAZDYREVO-LAND-CASES`; without new
profiles and without a route on the map until the files themselves are read.
### The Control Family of the Pereverzevs: Early Oryol Confirmed, the 1566 Enrolment Not

The new tactic was tested on another prominent family of the cadastral book —
the **Pereverzevs**. Fyodor Sergeyev's modern genealogy asserts that they
were enrolled when Oryol was built in 1566, after which they remained in
town service. For this phrase itself the author gives no archival call number,
folio, quotation or scholarly reference. No independent confirmation of the formula has yet been
found, so this is not a proven fact but a testable hypothesis.

On the other hand, primary and scholarly published sources give three stricter layers.

1. In the «Синодик по убиенных во брани» (Synodikon of Those Slain in Battle), among the dvoryane and deti boyarskie killed
   in the regiment of Prince Vasily Khilkov near Toropets, **Andrey Pereverzev** is recorded.
   The razryad book (service register) and a charter to Khilkov date this campaign to 1580. The original
   formula on l. 26: «Федору Давыдову, Грише Наумову, Андрею Переверзеву...» ("To Fedor Davydov, Grisha Naumov, Andrey Pereverzev...").
   Modern call number of the manuscript: **РНБ ОР, F. IV. 254, ll. 1–68**. This proves
   the existence of the family name by 1580, but the Synodikon does not name
   the service district of Andrey or of his father.
2. The Oryol cadastral book of 1594/95 already shows several branches:
   Melekh Vasilyevich held in Kobuzevo **«отца его поместье»** ("his father's pomestye"); separately
   the empty Grigoryevskoye pomestye of Pereverzev is named; the previous holders of
   the halves of a zaimishche (taken-up tract) on the Sukhoy Bensk were Trenka and Tretyak Pereverzev; Andrey
   Onisimovich held Medkovo gorodishche and land together with his brothers Mitya and
   Artemy; Afanasy Ivanovich had land in Grigoryeva Polyana and the Taychukovsky
   stan. This is a mature family cluster, not a lone newcomer.
3. The full digital text of the Thousand Book (Tysyachnaya kniga) and the Court Register (Dvorovaya tetrad) of the 1550s
   was checked with OCR spaces removed and for the variants Переверз-/Переверж-/Верзев-.
   The surname is not there. The zero is limited: the central register did not list all
   the town deti boyarskie, but it does not reveal the family's former town.

Names and meaning. **Pereverzev** in the 1580 entry already looks like a stable
family name. However, Andrey without a patronymic cannot automatically
be glued to Andrey Onisimovich of 1594/95. Melekh's formula «отца его поместье»
proves continuity across generations, but the father could have received the Oryol
grant at any moment between 1566 and 1594. So the early rooting of the family
is confirmed, but participation in the original enrolment of the builders of Oryol is not.

The comparison nevertheless changes the search address. The priority now is not yet another general
surname search, but **the name lists of Prince Vasily Khilkov's regiment of 1580**,
early Oryol separate books (otdelnye knigi) and salary books (okladnye knigi), and later petitions of the Pereverzevs
with extracts about the first grant. If Oryol neighbours or an older generation of Anfilogovs
turn up next to Andrey Pereverzev in the regimental list, this will give an already
testable model of how early Oryol was manned.

The comparative block has been entered into the card
`PUB-PKOU-1594-OREL-INHERITANCE-AUDIT`. Profiles of the Pereverzevs and map points are not
created: this is an unrelated surname, and a documented route of Anfilogovs
has still not been established.

[A modern hypothesis about 1566](https://proza.ru/2025/03/31/1068),
[publication of the Synodikon](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1540-1560/Sinodik_ubiennych_vo_brani/text.htm),
[the tsar's charter to Khilkov of 13 August 1580](https://vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Dok_livon_vojne_2/3/9.htm),
[the Court Register in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000199_000009_006039889/).

### Correcting a False Merge: the Reiter Anfilogov of 1698 Remains Nameless

A full check of the primary forum publication and of the author's open macro workbook
corrected an earlier error in the queue of files. For **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12,
d. 1698** only one surname item has been published: in the Oryol part of the file
there is **Анфилогов, рейтар** ("Anfilogov, reiter"). The first name, patronymic and folio are not
named in the open message. In the workbook «БД_Служилые_XVII в (v36).xlsm» this is row 480 of the sheet
«Челобитные_XVII_в»: `Анфилогов | Орел | 7207 | 210 | 12 | 1698 | 1`.
There are no additional hidden columns, notes or links to an image with it;
the introductory sheet states directly that personal extracts and copies are kept separate from
the public surname database.

The earlier reading **«Офонасей Осипов сын Анфилогов, л. 392 об.»** ("Ofonasey Osipov syn Anfilogov, l. 392 v.") does not belong
to this file. It is taken from the Oryol estimate book **of 1641/42 — РГАДА,
f. 210, op. 4, d. 260**. The error arose because a search snippet
combined the text of the message about the 1698 file with another message from the same long
forum thread. The 1698 post itself contains no name.

This removes an artificial extension of one biography by 56 years and forbids
creating an "Afanasy Osipovich of 1698" without a document. The Lead
`LEAD-210-12-1698-OREL-1698` is kept as the address of the file of one nameless
reiter: the source is potentially very valuable, because its Razryad reference statements
may report origin, the father's service and the composition of the family, but first one must
obtain the exact folio from the holder of the copies and then check the original.

[The primary post about the 1698 file](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5703/135781/p5341781.htm),
[the open workbook v36](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/DmUeZAUG4DM0yw).

### A New Fork: the Older Generation May Not Have Resettled but Been Enrolled on the Spot

A full check of M. M. Bentsianov's monograph «Князья, бояре и дети
боярские» (Princes, Boyars and Deti Boyarskie) yielded not one more hypothetical donor town but a more important
correction to the search model. On pp. 229–231 the author explicitly includes the **Oryol
district** among the new, thinly settled southern districts to which deti boyarskie
of the central regions went reluctantly. In his conclusion, the problem could not be solved mainly
by forced resettlers. The rapid growth of the pomestye-holding layer
was ensured by the mass enrolment of people «худородного происхождения» ("of humble origin") — above
all Cossacks and other lower service groups.

Bentsianov does not rely on family tradition. He compares the enrolment of 300
Cossacks of Epifan in 1585, a later report by men of Bryansk about samopalniki (musketeers)
settled under Ivan IV in Belyov and Bolkhov, and the instruction at the founding of Yelets
to recruit local deti boyarskie without reducing the old district corporations.
Especially expressive is the petition of the Ryazhsk dvoryane and deti boyarskie of 1648/49:
they objected to being put on a par with the servitors of the new towns, where,
in their words, their own people and peasants, the children of streltsy, Cossacks,
gunners and of «всяких неслужилых отцов» ("all kinds of non-serving fathers") had been enrolled. This is a biased late complaint
of an "old town", not a list of sixteenth-century men of Oryol; it cannot literally be ascribed
to every early Oryol family. But it documents the very mechanism
that had previously been underestimated in our search.

A full search of the 369 pages of the monograph yielded no forms Анпил-, Ампил-,
Анфил-, Онпил-, Онфил- or Амфил- and found no pre-Oryol family group by
rare fathers' names. This zero does not close the direction, because in the new
model the first generation may not yet have had a surname. **Mikula, the two Vasilys,
Persha, Timofey, Nechay, Pakhom and Terenty** must now also be checked in early
Oryol lists of Cossacks, streltsy, samopalniki and gunners' children
without a third name. The transitional formula sought is not only «переведён из
такого-то города» ("transferred from such-and-such a town"), but also **«повёрстан из казаков/стрельцов в дети боярские»** ("enrolled from the Cossacks/streltsy into the deti boyarskie").

This does not cancel the Kashira, Bryansk, Ryazan–Ryazhsk and Upper Oka
corridors: for other Oryol families real transfers have already been proven. But it
explains why seven branches of Anfilogovs could have arisen in Oryol without a single
common resettlement list. The big gap before 1594 may conceal not
the move of a ready-made family but the social transformation of several lines within
the frontier service milieu.

The new model and the exact pages have been entered into the Record
`PUB-PKOU-1594-OREL-INHERITANCE-AUDIT`. Profiles and the map do not change: no
specific Anfilogov has yet been named as promoted from the pribornye lyudi (recruited servicemen).

[M. M. Bentsianov's monograph](https://militera.org/books/pdf/research/bentsianov_mm01.pdf),
[register of the case on the petition of the Ryazhsk servitors of 1648](https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A1%D0%BF%D0%B8%D1%81%D0%BE%D0%BA_%D0%BC%D0%B5%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BD%D0%B8%D1%87%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8%D1%85_%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BB).

### Three Legal Paths into the Deti Boyarskie — and a Negative Control of Neighbouring Desyatni

K. V. Petrov's article made it possible to turn the new model from a general
assumption into a concrete scheme of archival search. The author distinguishes three
legal paths to the status: a personal grant from the sovereign,
ordinary enrolment, above all of the sons of deti boyarskie, and a separate decision of
the Razryad Prikaz to transfer serving Cossacks, streltsy or pomestye-holding
atamans. In the last case the transfer might not depend on the wishes of the
serviceman himself, but it was still accompanied by enrolment — the assignment of
a land and money salary (oklad).

For the end of the sixteenth century two direct control examples are given. In **Epifan in
1585** 300 serving Cossacks were transferred into the deti boyarskie. In
**Ryazhsk in 1597** the strelets's son Rodka Mikhaylov
Pavlov and the coachman's (yamshchik's) son Mikitka Grigoryev Balashev were admitted to enrolment; Petrov refers to
V. N. Storozhev's publication, pp. 423–424. So the fathers of Oryol Anfilogovs lacking a surname
does not prevent searching for them in the pribornye milieu: in a transitional
entry the third name may not yet have been in use.

At the same time the full open texts of the desyatni of Epifan of 1585
and 1591 and of Ryazhsk of 1579, 1591, 1594 and 1597 were checked again. They contain no forms Анфил-, Анпил-,
Онфил-, Онпил-, Ампил- or Амфил-. Nor was the strongest Oryol
family signature found — **Vasily, Timofey and Karp, Mikulin's children**. Individual
two-part matches were cut off by the third name: for example, the Ryazhsk Pavel
Terentyev syn **Iyevlev** is not the same as the Oryol Pavel Terentyev syn
**Anfilogov**.

The outcome changes not the map but the address of the next order. What is needed is an early name-by-name
Oryol list of Cossacks, streltsy and pishchalniki (arquebusiers) before the book of 1594/95, and in
later reference statements one should look not only for «переведён» ("transferred") and «испомещён» ("granted a pomestye"), but also for
the formulas **«верстан из казаков»** ("enrolled from the Cossacks"), **«стрелецкий сын»** ("strelets's son"), **«казачий сын»** ("Cossack's son") and
**«служил вместо отца»** ("served in place of his father"). So far no specific Anfilogov has been named in this way,
so no profiles or route are created.

[K. V. Petrov's article](https://inslav.ru/sites/default/files/2021_shveykovskaya_petrov.pdf),
[the Epifan desyatnya of 1585](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Desjatni_XVI_v/text4.php),
[the Ryazhsk desyatnya of 1597, part 1](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Desjatni_XVI_v/text10_1.php),
[the Ryazhsk desyatnya of 1597, part 2](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Desjatni_XVI_v/text10_2.php).

### The Roll of 1604: Scale Is There, the Names of Oryol Men Are Not

A new direct source turned out to be available in the official РГАДА viewer:
**f. 210, op. 9, d. 1074 — «Роспись русского войска, посланного против
самозванца в 1604 году»** ("Roll of the Russian army sent against the Pretender in 1604"). The file has been digitised in full in 179 frames. This is exactly
the document on which the literature bases the large Oryol contingent
at the beginning of the Time of Troubles.

However, a short check of the scans dispelled a false expectation. The roll does not give
a name-by-name Oryol desyatnya. On manuscript **l. 79** come the names of the categories and
numerical totals; the rank-and-file deti boyarskie and pribornye lyudi are reduced to numbers. The
file may name voivodes, heads (golovy) and individual appointed persons, but
the Oryol hundreds are not disclosed surname by surname. So searching these 179
frames for Mikula, Nechay or Anfilogovs as rank-and-file servitors
is unproductive.

A full-size photocopy of l. 79 has been saved in the project:
`/archive/evidence/publications/rgada-210-9-1074/0083-folio-79.jpg`. There is also
an important caveat: the secondary literature names the second Oryol
total differently — deti boyarskie with arquebuses (pishchali) or, more broadly, servicemen by recruitment (po priboru).
Until the Oryol lines of the manuscript have been reconciled with the 1979 publication, these
terms cannot be considered identical.

The result is useful all the same. The roll confirms the scale of the Oryol military
corporation in 1604, but not the origin of the people. So this corridor
is closed as a source of surnames, and priority remains with early name-by-name
lists of Cossacks, streltsy and pishchalniki and with files on enrolment into the deti boyarskie.

[The official РГАДА viewer](http://rgada.info/kueh/index2.php?str=210_9_1074&name=Роспись%20русского%20войска),
[the card of the 1979 publication in the RSL](https://search.rsl.ru/ru/record/01007592477).

### Inverted Patronymics and Searching by Family Cluster

In the open database of servicemen not only the Anfilogov variants were checked, but also
all the full pairs of the early branches in both word orders: Mikulin
Vasily / Vasily Mikulin, Nechayev Ivan / Ivan Nechayev, Pakhomov Ermak / Ermak
Pakhomov, Pershin Ivan / Ivan Pershin, Timofeyev Sofon / Sofon Timofeyev,
Terentyev Pavel / Pavel Terentyev. For the Mikulin branch
Vasily, Timofey and Karp were checked separately.

The database yielded no independent pre-Oryol family signature. The only exact
sixteenth-century line is **Mikulin Vasily**, the Obonezhskaya pyatina of Novgorod, 1588,
РГАДА, f. 137, op. 1, d. 11. But there is neither Timofey nor Karp Mikulin next to him;
a lone Novgorod surname does not reproduce the Oryol trio of brothers and does not
create a Novgorod — Oryol route.

The check revealed a limitation of the database itself: it mainly normalises names in
the order "surname, first name". A zero for the query «Василий Микулин» (Vasily Mikulin) does not mean that the
original nowhere has the formula «Василий Микулин сын» ("Vasily Mikulin syn") — this must be checked against
the linked publication or scan. So the next unit of search is not a single
namesake, and not even a single pair, but a **cluster**: several brothers, their fellow servicemen,
the previous holder of the Oryol land and the same service group. Such a cluster
can point to a donor town even where the surname had not yet become fixed.

[The open database of servicemen](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/).

### The Family Control Worked: Lomaka Rezantsov Gained a Son, Bogdan

The search was continued not by the single surname Anpilogov but by the whole neighbouring family
that accompanied Ivan Nechayevich in the privileged distribution of 1595. This technique
gave a direct link across a generation. The Oryol oath book (krestoprivodnaya kniga) of 1645
records **«Резанцов — Богдан Ламакин»** ("Rezantsov — Bogdan Lamakin" [son of Lomaka]), l. 1157 v. So Lomaka Ivanovich
Rezantsov of the cadastral book of 1594/95 had a son, Bogdan. Already in the razbornaya
kniga (sorting book) of 1628/29, among the Oryol new recruits stands **Bogdashka Rezantsov**; the same
name recurs in the Oryol indexes of 1648 and 1664. Until the primary lines
of 1628, 1648 and 1664 have been reconciled by age, pomestye and kinship, they cannot
be mechanically declared four mentions of one person. But the formula of 1645
itself documents the chain:

`Ломака Иванов сын Резанцов (Орёл, 1595) → Богдан Ломакин Резанцов (Орёл, 1645)`

(Lomaka Ivanov syn Rezantsov (Oryol, 1595) → Bogdan Lomakin Rezantsov (Oryol, 1645).)

The supposed older link — Ivanko Rezantsev of Dedilov of 1588/89 —
remains unproven. Thus what has been checked is the Oryol continuity of the
neighbouring family, not a Dedilov → Oryol route. For Anpilogovs this is important as
a control: Lomaka was in one compact distribution with Ivan Nechayevich, and
his family really did take root in Oryol for at least two generations.

A full copy of **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6d, d. 1** was found in an open archive and
downloaded. The Oryol section begins on l. 72. It has three Bogdashkas — Rezantsov,
Smetsky and Shcherbatoy. The entry on the first spread viewed turned out to be
Bogdashka Shcherbatoy, so it was not passed off as Rezantsov on the strength of one similar
flourish; Rezantsov's exact folio is still being located folio by folio.

In parallel the detailed Oryol desyatnya of 1628, **РГАДА,
f. 210, op. 4, d. 270, ll. 141–151**, was checked. All 21 digital frames are available, but in
the name sample of more than a hundred servitors there are neither Rezantsovs nor Anpilogovs.
This is a different set, not a detailed version of the same entry for Bogdashka; the direction is
closed as a false shortcut.

No new profiles of the Rezantsovs and no route on the map have been created: this is a control
family, and the document names no direct move of the researched family series between towns.
The new information has been added to the Record of Ivan Nechayevich Anpilogov.

[New recruits of Oryol in 1628](https://dompredkov.ru/noviki-orla-1628-goda/),
[name index of the oath book of 1645](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5703/126711/p4748429.htm),
[servitors of Oryol according to the book of 1664](https://dompredkov.ru/sluzhilye-lyudi-orla-i-uezda-po-pistsovoj-knige-1664-g/),
[open archive of scans](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ).

### The Onanyins as a Control of Database Coverage, Not a Ready-Made Route

After the direct search for Anpilogovs had been exhausted, another unit was checked —
the surname **Onanyin**, because it was borne by the local previous holder
Anpilog Ivanovich. In the open database of servicemen the exact form «Онаньин» (Onanyin) gave
**55 rows**. The earliest record represented is **Bogdan Onanyin** in
the Derevskaya pyatina of Novgorod in 1593–1595; two rows refer to two
mentions or categories of one name in the inspection book (dozornaya kniga) **РГАДА, f. 1209,
op. 1, d. 974**. The linked publication warns that current, former and
vacant landholders were entered as separate rows, so the repeats do not mean
two Bogdans.

At first glance Novgorod might become a new donor town. The control
shows the opposite. The same database does not find the already independently read
**Anpilog Ivanovich Onanyin** in the Oryol district of 1594/95 or
**Anton Yevseyevich Onanyin** in Epifan in 1591. The queries «Вавил Онаньин»,
«Онаньин Вавил», «Анпилог Онаньин», «Онаньин Анпилог» and «Онаньин Антон»
gave zero. Consequently, the 55 rows are a sample from the publications entered, not
a geographical census of the surname. The absence of a town or a person in this
index cannot be turned into a historical negative conclusion.

Bogdan Onanyin remains a useful parallel but not a relative of the Oryol
nest: he so far has no patronymic, shared pomestye, fellow servicemen or formula
of transfer. A Novgorod → Oryol route is not added to the map. The practical outcome of
the change of tactic is different: from here on one must compare **primary books of one type**
and look for the middle generation `Анпилог → Вавил → Кондратий` (Anpilog → Vavil → Kondraty), rather than counting
matches in an aggregator. The priority formula remains «Вавил Анпилогов сын
Онаньин» ("Vavil Anpilogov syn Onanyin") in early Oryol, Upper Oka and Razryad extracts.

[Database of servicemen](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/),
[list of landholders of the Derevskaya pyatina](https://dompredkov.ru/novgorodskie-pomeshhiki-derevskoj-pyatiny-1593-1595-gg/).

### Not Only a Donor Town: People of the Former Oprichnina Court

The tactic was changed once more: instead of yet another provincial desyatnya,
the central list of the people and prikazy of the former Oprichnina Court (Oprichny dvor) of
20 March 1573 was checked in full — **1886 men**, roughly twenty-two years before
the Oryol survey. Anfilogovs, Anpilogovs and Onfilogovs themselves are not in the list.
But surnames from the immediate circle of Ivan Nechayevich
Anpilogov turned up.

The narrowest find is **«Василий Шалимов»** (Vasily Shalimov), l. 6, in the group with a money
salary of 12 roubles. In the Oryol district of 1594/95 lived **Vasily Leontyev syn
Shalimov** (Vasily, son of Leonty, Shalimov); there too Parfen Fyodorovich Shalimov received a new privileged grant in
one section with Ivan Anpilogov. The gap of twenty-two years allows
that the two Vasilys are one man who moved from central court service to
landholding service in Oryol. But the early entry gives no patronymic, so for now this is
not a biography but an address hypothesis. Now one must look specifically for the patronymic and
the former pomestye of Vasily Shalimov, not for another hundred matches of one surname.

This hypothesis has acquired a previous address — and it has now been checked not only against
a modern dictionary. N. M. Tupikov on printed p. 830 gives the exact line:
**«Левонтий Иванов с. Ш-а, Владимирский боярский сын. 1541. с. в. А. Ф. I,
90»** ("Levonty Ivanov s[on of] Sh[alimov], a syn boyarsky of Vladimir. 1541. ... A. F. I, 90"). Tupikov's own list of abbreviations expands «А. Ф.» as «Акты,
изданные Федотовым-Чеховским» ("Acts published by Fedotov-Chekhovsky"). In the first volume of that edition act No. 67 was found, about
the disputed Krugly Meadow on the border of the Vladimir and Suzdal districts.

The act is dated **1 July 1556**, but it refers directly to earlier cadastral
books of Dmitry Ivanovich Volynsky — it is to this documentary layer that Tupikov
assigns the date 1541. On printed p. 139 are named «Левонтьевы, да на
Григорьевы крестьяне Шалимовы» ("Levontyev's, and Grigoryev's peasants, the Shalimovs"), and on p. 141 the group is repeated more clearly:
**the peasants of Levonty, Grigory and Aleksey Shalimov**. So Levonty is now
not a random example from a late dictionary: the publication of the act shows in
the Vladimir district a stable landholding nest of at least three
Shalimovs.

The name Levonty matches the patronymic of the Oryol Vasily, and the intervals between
1541, 1573 and 1594/95 suit a chain father in Vladimir → son at
the sovereign's court → the same son on an Oryol pomestye. The source gap for
the Vladimir family has been closed, but the genealogical one not yet: act No. 67 does not call
Vasily the son of Levonty, the court line of 1573 gives no patronymic, and
no direct document on a transfer to Oryol has been found. The new precise tasks are to find
Vasily **Leontyevich** in the court documents, his former pomestye and the modern
archival call number of Volynsky's early cadastral book. The map has not changed: the Shalimovs are
a control family of another surname, and the movement of the researched series itself has not been
established by this.

The second find at first looked like a strelets route into a new town:
on l. 18 v.–19 **Oleksey Ryazantsov** is named, one of five «самопальных
стрельцов» ("musket streltsy"). A special study by S. P. Orlenko changed the interpretation.
The musket streltsy of the 1573 list were attached to the court armoury
workshop — the Bronny Prikaz; they tested and escorted the tsar's weapons and
were not an ordinary party of town streltsy. So Oleksey does not prove
a route of the Ryazantsovs or Anpilogovs to Oryol. He shows something else: a family stem familiar from
Oryol already existed in the central service milieu in 1573.

Three Nemtsovs in the stable staff of the same court give a weaker control:
in the Bazdyrevo neighbourhood cluster of Anfilogovs there later stood Gavrila Fyodorovich
Nemtsov, but neither the name nor the patronymic matches. The outcome of the new direction is not
a route but a new model of recruitment: Oryol may have been filled not by one ready-made party
from one town, but by individual appointments of people and the sons of people from
various town, court and prikaz services. No such unverified
transfer has been put on the map.

[The list of 20 March 1573](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Opricn_dvor_1572/text.php),
[D. N. Alshits's introduction](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Opricn_dvor_1572/pred.php),
[S. P. Orlenko's study of the musket streltsy](https://kreml.ru/storage/mediafiles/files/researches/47881/sbornik28blockprintst2.pdf),
[the dictionary record of Levonty Shalimov in Tupikov](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000199_000009_003717568/),
[the first volume of Fedotov-Chekhovsky's acts](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Акты,_относящиеся_до_гражданской_расправы_древней_России_Том_1_1860.pdf),
[RSL catalogue entry](https://search.rsl.ru/ru/record/01003831309),
[control against S. B. Veselovsky's «Ономастикон» (Onomasticon)](https://azbyka.ru/otechnik/Spravochniki/onomastikon-drevnerusskie-imena-prozvishha-i-familii/).

### The Vladimir Desyatnya of 1590: Two Neighbouring Families Had Already Left

To check whether the Shalimovs were still listed under Vladimir just before
their appearance in Oryol, not a sample of a campaign regiment but the whole published
Vladimir part of the desyatnya of 1590 was viewed. It includes both dvoryane with money
pay and a separate section of those who were **not given** money because they had not
been on the Novgorod service. So the absence of a surname here is more meaningful than
an ordinary zero search of a single campaign list.

The result turned out to be twofold: in the whole text there are neither **Shalimovs**, nor
**Trunovs**, nor forms of the researched series Anfilogov/Anpilogov/Onfilogov. But for
Trunov a successor control has survived: in the list serve **Latynya Neronov syn
Volkov** and **Stepan Neronov syn Volkov**. They very probably belong
to the immediate family of Karp Neronovich Volkov, who received the former land of Istoma
Trunov according to the books of 1579/80. This does not prove brotherhood, but it shows
that not the whole chain of information has vanished: the new landholding family continued
Vladimir service.

Names and meaning. Latynya and Stepan are personal names; «Неронов сын» ("Neronov syn") names their father,
Neron; Volkov is the family name. The same desyatnya has **Mikita Ivanov syn
Neronov**, but this is a different construction without the surname Volkov: he cannot be automatically
attached to the family. The absence of Shalimov or Trunov means only
that the surname did not occur even among the non-serving men of Vladimir in this list;
it is compatible with an earlier transfer, death, minority or another reason for
leaving, but does not choose one of them.

The main result is no longer a single coincidence. **Two rare families of the immediate
circle of Oryol Anfilogovs**, the Shalimovs and the Trunovs, independently have
a pre-Oryol Vladimir prehistory, and both are absent from the Vladimir desyatnya
of 1590. This strengthens the model in which the new Oryol gathered individual families
from different service-and-land networks, rather than receiving one ready-made corporation from
one town. But Anfilogovs themselves are not yet in the Vladimir documents,
the kinship of the early and the Oryol Shalimovs and Trunovs has not been formulated, so
no route has been added to the map.

The tactic is now narrow: search not for yet another general desyatnya, but for the Vladimir
**separate grants (otdelnye), installation (vvoznye), transfer (otkaznye) and sorting (razbornye) documents of 1573–1590** — above
all the reason for the transfer of Istoma Trunov's land and a line in which Vasily Shalimov
is called Leontyevich or receives land outside Vladimir. The result has been put into
the card `PUB-GAUTIER-1911-VLADIMIR-1590-SHALIMOV-TRUNOV-OREL-AUDIT`; no profiles
of unrelated surnames were created.

But a new source forces a change in this tactic as well. A considerable part
of the original sixteenth-century desyatni perished, especially in 1812. Their content
is partly preserved in the **name extracts for
genealogical proofs** made in the seventeenth and eighteenth centuries, in РГАДА, f. 388, and in the old **family alphabets (azbuki)** of
f. 210. Such extracts sometimes repeat the title of the lost book, the position
of the man in the entry, the notes and the original folio. This is exactly how Karp's line
was restored. For Vladimir control call numbers are already known: **f. 388, No. 851,
ll. 234–234 v.** (Karp, 1581/82), **No. 856, l. 273** (the preface of the lost
desyatnya of 1579) and **f. 210, Moscow desk (Moskovsky stol), stb. 48, l. 489** (an extract from
the same complex).

This is the next practical move: to look in the old alphabet not for a town and not for
a ready-made "list of resettlers", but for the surnames **Anfilogov / Anpilogov / Onfilogov**,
then **Shalimov** and **Trunov**. An index line found should lead to
a lost desyatnya, a new recruit's entry, the original allotment or a later extract.
The example of Karp shows what document could have stood immediately before
a man's appearance in the Oryol cadastral book: **enrolment of a new recruit → allotment of
a pomestye → an extract or a charter correcting it**.

[The full published text of the Vladimir desyatnya](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1580-1600/Desjatni_Wladimir_Mesera/text.php),
[the official card of the edition in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/005664_000048_RuPRLIB12050997/),
[publication of the Oryol cadastral book](https://archive.org/details/moscha-regia),
[a study of Karp Volkov's service entry](https://cyberleninka.ru/article/n/konnost-lyudnost-i-oruzhnost-russkoy-konnitsy-v-epohu-livonskoy-voyny-1558-1583-gg.pdf),
[an explanation of the method of reconstructing lost desyatni](https://muscovitearmor.wordpress.com/2018/07/08/).

### A Full Comparison Revealed Two Onanyins at Court

To distinguish a real recruitment channel from a single coincidence, **742 full formulas**
of the type "name + patronymic + family name" were automatically extracted from the Oryol
book and compared with the whole court list of 1573. After
the common Ivanovs, Sidorovs, Fyodorovs and similar forms were cut off, the only
narrow match of name and family name left was **Vasily Shalimov**.
This weakens the model of a mass transfer to Oryol of one ready-made court group,
but keeps the possibility of an individual appointment of Vasily or his family.

On the other hand, the search by family forms for the first time found in the same list two
**Onanyins** at once. On l. 38 v., among the pomyasy (meat-handlers) of the Bread Palace (Khlebenny dvorets), is recorded **Vaska
Onanyin**, who received two roubles less a quarter. On l. 40 there is a separate line:
**«Коровник Митя Онаньин. 3 рубли с полтиною. За соль 5 алтын»** ("Cowherd Mitya Onanyin. 3 roubles and a half. For salt 5 altyn"). This now
concerns not merely neighbours of Anpilogovs but the second name of the very earliest
Oryol bearer of the given name — Anpilog Ivanovich Onanyin.

An independent publication of wedding records gave Mitya an earlier point. At the end of
January 1547 he was assigned to the Kremlin guard **at the Rizpolozhensky
Doors** (the Doors of the Deposition of the Robe). The archival address of the line: РГАДА, f. 135, otd. IV, rubr. II, No. 2,
ll. 9–13. Twenty-six years later the same full short name is again found
at the sovereign's court. One milieu and a rare combination make the identity probable,
but there is no patronymic in either entry.

Grammar is especially important here. The short «Васка Онаньин» or «Митя Онаньин»
could still mean simply **Vaska or Mitya, son of Onanya**, and not a hereditary
surname. In the Oryol entry the form is already built into a full three-part chain:
Kondraty Vavilov syn Onanyin and Anpilog Ivanov syn Onanyin. So the court
people cannot be declared relatives of the Oryol nest. The new precise query
is different: to look at court and in its land acts not for any Onanyin, but for
**Ivan Onanyin, his son Anpilog and his grandson Vavil**, or for a document in which
a court Onanyin receives a pomestye in the Oryol district.

[Roll of the Kremlin guards of 1547](https://library.by/portalus/modules/russianlaw/readme.php?archive=&id=1499740583&start_from=&subaction=showfull&ucat=),
[the court list of 1573](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Opricn_dvor_1572/text.php).

### Working from Both Ends: the Razryad Archive Draft and the Heraldry Fair Copy

The method had to be refined. A. B. Kamensky's study of the books of reference statements
of the Razryad Archive shows that what remained in the archive were mainly
**drafts**, while the final reference statement went to the institution that had ordered it.
Moreover, a draft could preserve additional extracts about people of the same surname
which were then crossed out of the fair copy. So searching for one "ideal" document
in one fond is wrong: the chain of proof must be assembled from both sides of
the correspondence.

This corrects an earlier conclusion that was too strong. The Kursk noble decision
on Anfilogovs is dated **20 January 1798**, and the РГАДА books of reference statements,
f. 388, op. 1, d. 120–121, also belong to 1798. The coincidence makes them
good candidates but does not yet prove that a family reference statement is precisely
there. Several Anfilogovs are brought together by a later Senate decree of
**18 October 1849, No. 7806**. An archival check of the old service may have been
carried out, or repeated, precisely during these proceedings.

So the first priority now is **РГИА, f. 1343, op. 16, d. 2345**
(1826–1887): look for the block of decree No. 7806, the genealogical table, the fair-copy Razryad
reference statements and the outgoing numbers of the inquiries. These numbers will allow their
drafts in f. 388 to be found by address. Files 120–121 remain in the order as candidates of 1798,
and d. 102, former No. 853, as the alphabetical path to the lost desyatni.
An additional path is the Heraldry Office, РГАДА, f. 286; the open
full-text search there does not cover the whole fond, so the absence of the surname
in the available inventory 2 does not close the direction.

[A. B. Kamensky's article on the books of reference statements](https://ebookiriran.ru/index.php?id=223&section=9&view=article),
[the official guide to the Heraldry Office](https://guides.rusarchives.ru/terms/10/2694/geroldmeysterskaya-kontora).

### Vasily Anpilogov: a Kursk Case of 1767

A global search not of surname databases but of the text of all available РГАДА
inventories gave a new address find: **РГАДА, f. 520, op. 5, d. 256, 1767**.
The fond belongs to the Kursk Voivode's Chancellery. The inventory heading:
«Доношение гренадера Андрея Лащилова о неприводе им по инструкции разбойника
однодворца Василия Анпилогова» ("Report of the grenadier Andrey Lashchilov on his failure, per instruction, to bring in the robber,
the odnodvorets Vasily Anpilogov").

Names and meaning. **Vasily** is a calendar personal name; the inventory gives no patronymic or village.
**Anpilogov** is an exact documented form of the researched
family series, but it does not connect the man with a specific Oryol branch.
**Odnodvorets** here means membership of a particular southern
service-and-landholding group of the eighteenth century, not simply the owner of one
homestead. The formula **«неприводе по инструкции»** ("failure to bring in per instruction") speaks of an unfulfilled
search, escort or enforcement assignment of the grenadier. The word
**«разбойник»** ("robber") remains for now only an accusatory label of the archival
heading: without the folios of the file one cannot establish guilt, circumstances or outcome.

A separate profile has been created for Vasily as a bearer of the target surname; Andrey Lashchilov is
kept only in the mentions. The map has not been extended: the Kursk Voivode's
Chancellery is the jurisdiction of the case, not a proven place of residence of Vasily.
The next step is to obtain the folios of the file and find the patronymic, village, age,
relatives and the route of the intended bringing-in.

[Full-text search of the РГАДА inventories](https://rgada.sovadev.pro/),
[inventory of fond 520, the file on page 13](http://rgada.info/opisi/520-opis_5.pdf#page=13).

### A Third Dedilov Imprint: Suvorov Next to Nechayev Twice

The search was rebuilt once more: instead of a single surname, all the rare
people and land names around the candidate were compared. This revealed a third independent
signal from the same service milieu.

In the reference book (pripravochnaya kniga) of Dedilov of 1588/89 **Semyon Suvorov and Ivan Nechayev
stand next to each other twice**. Inside the fortress, on l. 15 v.–16, first
«кл. Семенки Суворова» ("the storehouse of Semenka Suvorov") is listed, then, after the storehouse (klet) of Okul Parfyonov, «кл. Ивана
Нечаева» ("the storehouse of Ivan Nechayev"). In the second count, by the households of the Dergilevskaya sotnya (hundred), on l. 35 v. stands
the sentry Cossack **Sevka Suvorov**, and on the next l. 36 v. the desyatsky (headman of ten)
**Ivanko Nechayev** with his brother Ponkratko. Semyonka and Sevka are most likely two
diminutive forms of the name of one Semyon; the family name,
the sotnya and the two kinds of holding — a town storehouse and a homestead — coincide. Until the scan is seen, this
nonetheless remains a well-grounded rather than a final identification.

The Oryol book of 1594/95 independently preserves on p. 944
**«Суворовское займище Кисленское»** ("the Suvorov zaimishche Kislenskoye") in the Korchakovsky stan. It had been
added (pripushcheno) to the share (zherebey) of Sava Semyonovich Sizov in the village of Zvyagintsova on the Orlik.
The register of about 1625 repeats the same entry No. 266 as «Савино поместье
Семенова сына Сизова в пустоши Звегинцове, **Суворова займище** и
Ислансково» ("the pomestye of Sava Semenov syn Sizov in the waste plot of Zvegintsovo, **the Suvorov zaimishche** and
Islanskovo"). So the name existed stably for at least thirty years and
is not an error of the typesetting.

The Dedilov hypothesis now rests on three parallel traces:

1. Ivan Nechayev, a desyatsky with his brother Ponkratko → Ivan Nechayevich Anpilogov in
   Oryol.
2. Ivanko Rezantsev → the probable brothers Maksim and Lomaka Ivanovich Rezantsov in
   Oryol; Lomaka is in one privileged distribution with Ivan Anpilogov.
3. Semyon Suvorov, twice a neighbour of Ivan Nechayev → the old Suvorov zaimishche in
   the Oryol Korchakovsky stan.

This is now a **group migration imprint**, not a single random namesake. But
the limit of proof remains: the Oryol entry does not name the man
after whom the Suvorov zaimishche arose, and does not say that he was the Dedilov
Semyon. The Dedilov Ivan has still not been called Anpilogov. So no route has been drawn on
the map.

The next address target is now different: to look not only for Ivan's transfer, but for
a previous-holder or separate-grant entry of **the Suvorov zaimishche before 1594** and
joint extracts of Nechayev, Rezantsev and Suvorov. If the previous holder
turns out to be Semyon Suvorov of the Dergilevskaya sotnya, this will become an independent bridge
Dedilov → Oryol and sharply raise Ivanko Nechayev's candidacy.

[A folio-by-folio rendering of the Dedilov book](https://document.wikireading.ru/hPk6fadeA0),

### An Oryol Book Torn into Two Files: Old Folios 98–113

The new tactic worked not through yet another surname index, but through
the physical reconstruction of a document. The researcher Trubi97 compared two
fragments of **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 21**: file **2099** preserves the old numbering
ll. 98–111, file **1952**, ll. 112–113. The signature run (skrepa) from l. 111 passes directly onto
l. 112, and its handwriting is the same in both fragments. Consequently, the present two
storage units are consecutive pieces of one book.

The publisher cautiously identifies it as a muster book (smotrennaya kniga) or estimate book of the servitors
of Oryol from about the middle of the seventeenth century. The localisation is supported by a whole family
imprint: named together are **Anfilogov/Anpilogov, Bazdyrev, Krivolapov,
Ovsyanikov, Ryazantsev and Trunov** — the familiar circle of the Oryol books. But the list
is given alphabetically, without personal names, folios or distribution between the two files.

**Names and meaning.** The form «Анфилогов/Анпилогов» means two possible readings of
one cursive surname, not two published people. There is as yet no personal name or
patronymic, so one cannot create a profile or attach the line to one
of the nine Oryol bearers of the surname of 1641/42. But an exact and small block for a full order
is now known: sixteen old folios, and the check should
begin at the junction of ll. 111–112 and then find the target entry.

The main opportunity is to date the book by the voivode or the signature run and to obtain from
the line a rank, patronymic, salary or family formula. This could link
the Oryol generations of the 1620s–1640s, though in itself it does not yet take them back into
the sixteenth century. A Record has been created,
`PUB-VGD-RGADA-210-21-2099-1952-OREL-BOOK-RECONSTRUCTION`; the map does not change,
since no specific person or route has been established.

[Public reconstruction of the two fragments](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5903/161275/p5105030.htm#pp5105030).
[the register of the Oryol book of about 1625](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1851/161210/p5099980.htm),
[scan of the edition of the 1594/95 cadastral book](https://archive.org/details/moscha-regia/page/n944/mode/1up).

### One Folio Instead of a General Search: the Oryol New Recruits of 1639

The tactic was changed again: what is sought now is not a surname in the whole database but each
small Oryol section of the consolidated enrolment desyatni. The full scholarly description
of the books of the Razryad Prikaz, printed by the МАМЮ in 1890, revealed an exact snapshot
that is absent from the open name index: **30 December 1639, РГАДА,
f. 210, op. 4, d. 299, l. 154**.

This is a desyatnya of new recruits of all towns who were enrolled in Moscow in the Bolshoy Prikhod (Great Revenue Office) by
the okolnichy Prince **Vasily Petrovich Akhamashukov-Cherkassky** and the dyaki (secretaries)
**Aleksandr Durov** and **Ivan Dmitriyev**. The names refer to the organisers of
the enrolment, not to supposed relatives of Anfilogovs. Oryol occupies
a single l. 154; Kursk already begins on l. 154 v. So the safe order is
ll. 153 v.–154 v.: it will preserve the heading of the Oryol section and its boundaries.

A control against the «Дом предков» (House of Ancestors) database gave two different results. For file
**210-4-270** and the town of Oryol the database shows **108 rows** of the enrolment desyatnya
of 1628; among them there are no indexed forms Anfilogov, Anpilogov,
Onfilogov, Onpilogov or Ampilogov. This does not contradict Ofonka Osipov syn
Onfilogov of the Oryol book of 1628/29: a different document is at issue.
For file **210-4-299** and Oryol the database shows **0 rows**, although the printed inventory
explicitly names the Oryol folio. Consequently, what we have is a gap in the index, not
an empty desyatnya.

**Names and meaning.** A novik (new recruit) is a man receiving an official land
and money salary for the first time. So even the absence of the familiar surname on l. 154 will be
a result: it will show that the Oryol line known by 1641/42 was not
replenished through this particular enrolment. If the surname or the personal name
Anfilof/Anfilog is found, the patronymic, salary and sureties will give a new point
for moving backwards — to the father's service and the former town. Until the manuscript is read,
no profile or route on the map is created: an archival address is not in itself
an event in a person's life.

A card `RGADA-210-OP4-OPIS-D299` has been created; photocopies of the official
inventory and of the detailed description of 1890 have been saved.

[The open database of servicemen](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/),
[book 7 of the «Описание документов и бумаг МАМЮ» (Description of the Documents and Papers of the МАМЮ)](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:%D0%9E%D0%BF%D0%B8%D1%81%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B5_%D0%B4%D0%BE%D0%BA%D1%83%D0%BC%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_%D0%B8_%D0%B1%D1%83%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%B3,_%D1%85%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%8F%D1%89%D0%B8%D1%85%D1%81%D1%8F_%D0%B2_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%BC_%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%85%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B5_%D0%9C%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B8%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%80%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B2%D0%B0_%D1%8E%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%86%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%9A%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B3%D0%B0_07_1890.pdf).

### Searching by the Mechanism of Service: the Bridge 1668 → 1675 → 1685

A check of a large folio-by-folio transcription, rather than only a surname index,
gave three results at once in the **sorting book (razbornaya kniga) of the Stary Oskol deti boyarskie
of 1675: РГАДА, f. 1163, op. 1, d. 1**.

On l. 266, first under the heading «Городовыя ж службы неверстаные дети боярские» ("And of town service, unenrolled deti boyarskie"),
stands **Ivan Mikhailov syn Anpilov**. Before this he was known only as
a pikeman of 1685. On l. 315 v. is recorded **Stepan Gerasimov syn Anpilov**.
The full formula for the first time connects the diminutive "Stenka", son of the retired
Gerasim Anpilov, of 1668 with the pikeman Stepan Gerasimovich of 1685.
The result is a documented career chain: a son fit for service → an unenrolled
syn boyarsky of town service → a pikeman.

On l. 299 of the same heading **Anfil Porfenov syn Vendin** was found. This is not
an Anpilov but a new bearer of the personal name Anfil with a different family name.
The line is especially useful for understanding the mechanism: "Anfilov syn" of the next
generation would originally have been a patronymic. A profile and a Record were created under
the given-name exception, but Vendin has not been included in the family branch and has not been put on the family
map.

These lines do not directly answer the question of the origin of the Oryol core, but they
confirm the productiveness of the new tactic: one must look not only for the surname but also for
transitions between service categories, diminutive names alongside fathers and full
formulas in the next review. For the sixteenth century such an intermediate review could
turn out to be the very document that links a nameless son or Cossack with
a surname-bearing pomestye holder of 1594.

[Full folio-by-folio transcription of the sorting book](https://pomnirod.ru/materialy-k-statyam/administrativno-territorialnoe-delenie-rossijskoj-imperii/gubernii-rossii/kurskoe-namestnichestva-1779-1797.-kurskaya-guberniya-1797-1924/naselennye-mesta-gubernii-po-uezdam-i-volostyam19/starooskolskij-uezd-belgorodskoj-gubernii/istoricheskie-dokumenty-starooskolskogo-uezda-kurskoj-gubernii/skazki-poddanyya-v-starom-oskole-stolniku-a.-s.titovu.html).
### The Penkovs: not one line but at least three paternal axes

The review list (razborny spisok) of the Oryol servicemen of 1620/21 (РГАДА, f. 210, op. 14, d. 502) adds an important control to the hypothesis about the Vasilyevich Penkovs of 1596–1617. A single file locates Ivan Olekseev syn Penkov (fol. 17), Oleksey Penkov with no established patronymic (fol. 83), Oleksey Ignatyev syn Penkov (fol. 90) and Larka Ontonov syn Penkov (fol. 99). A chain Ignaty → Alexey → Ivan is possible, but the second Alexey does not allow it to be considered proven. A separate line Anton → Larion (Larka) is visible.

This changes the search technique: a surname match has to be broken down into paternal axes, and then one has to look for the otkaznye and grant formulas «после отца» ("after the father"), «с отцова поместья» ("from the father's pomestye"), «брат» ("brother"). The same method applies to Vasily Dmitrievich Anpilogov and to Alexey/Ivan Vasilyevich Anfilokhov from the same file. The matching patronymic makes the last two candidates for a single line of Vasily, but does not prove that their father was Vasily Dmitrievich in particular.

### Not "three newcomers" but three different service trajectories

Comparing the review list of 1620/21 with the complete published list
of 1627/28 changes the way the question is posed. By 1627/28 **Vasily Dmitrievich Onpilogov**
is among the town servicemen (gorodovye) with a pomestye oklad of **200 chetverti**;
**Ivan Vasilyevich Onfilogov** is among the town servicemen with an oklad of **150 chetverti**;
**Alexey Vasilyevich Onfilogov** is placed in a separate heading «Новики 130-го
году» ("Noviki of the year 130") and has an oklad of **200 chetverti**. The last formula precisely dates his
enrolment to 7130, that is, 1621/22.

For Alexey this yields a particularly informative sequence:
**1620/21 — «Поместья ему нету» ("he has no pomestye") → 1621/22 — enrolled as a novik → 1627/28 — oklad of
200 chetverti**. This is not yet a migration route but a documented transition
from a man without a formalised pomestye to a provided-for novik. Vasily and Ivan,
by contrast, already belong to the general town category by 1627/28. Consequently,
the word "novik" cannot automatically be translated as "recently resettled".

A control against the musters of 1626 strengthens the conclusion. Vasily appeared in person at Mtsensk
on 17 April and 7 June; Ivan and Alexey are named together among those who appeared on 2 July and
served until the dismissal on 4 October. In 1641/42 all three are still recorded in
Oryol service, and Alexey survives in the documents until the oath of 1645. What we have
are stable Oryol biographies, not one-off index matches.

A new kinship hypothesis is admissible but not yet proven: Alexey and Ivan are
Vasilyevichi, and Vasily Dmitrievich fits as their father by name and generation.
Their simultaneous service registration does not contradict this — a father could have entered
service late and his adult sons could have been enrolled separately. But there are no direct words «дети
Василия Дмитриевича» ("children of Vasily Dmitrievich") or «братья» ("brothers"). Therefore the map receives no invented
route and no kinship arrow; the next targeted search moves to the
petitions about Alexey's enrolment of 1621/22 and the pomestye grants, where the
formula «с отцова поместья» ("from the father's pomestye") might appear or a former town might be named.

[Publication of the Oryol list of 1627/28](https://forum.vgd.ru/5703/135781/70.htm?a=stdforum_view&o=),
[full index of the 1620/21 file](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/5703/135781/p5737379.htm).

### Anpilog Onanyin gets an exact address: present-day Shchelkunovo

Switching tactics from name searching to historical microgeography corrected an
old placeholder on the map. The full-size thematic map by R. I. Ovsyannikov
shows **«Государнино селище (Щелкуново)»** ("Gosudarnino selishche (Shchelkunovo)") on the Orlik. An independent article
by E. Ashikhmina of 2009 gives the same direct identification: the Gosudarynino selishche of
the cadastral book is the present-day village of **Shchelkunovo in Uritsky district**. The list of
inhabitants matches the primary text: the Shevelevs, Onanyins, Sotnikovs, Zherdevs
and Bogdanovs.

This is where the zherebye of **Anpilog Ivanovich Onanyin** lay, which by
1594/95 had passed to his grandson Kondraty Vavilovich. The point has been moved from the
notional centre of Oryol to Shchelkunovo: 52.9923699, 35.790105. This is an important
refinement, but it does not allow Anpilog to be made an ancestor of the surname group.

The new geography both strengthens and limits the local hypothesis.
Anpilog is documented in the same Korchakov stan and in the basin of the same river,
