the Orel/Orlik, where the early Anfilogovs lived. However, Shchelkunovo lies about
**27 km east** of the working point for Bazdyrevo. Consequently, what is proven is the
local use of the name from which the surname could have arisen independently, but
not the immediate neighbourhood of the Onanyins with the compact cluster of
Bazdyrevo—Belaya—Sofonova—Radomlya. No route or kinship line is drawn on the
map.

[E. Ashikhmina's article with the identification of the settlement](https://www.svrt.ru/lib/2009-02.htm),
[thematic map of Oryol district in 1595](https://valerista.narod.ru/photo_struk/Orlov_uezd_1595.jpg).

### The book of old grants: an exact address instead of a general surname search

The new route leads not to yet another desyatnya but backwards from the land survey of 1680–1682.
The official РГАДА catalogue locates a huge Oryol book:
**f. 1209, op. 1, kn. 336, fols. 1–546v.** In it Pyotr Vasilyevich Shamordin and
Stepan Repyev measured the pomestye "pridachi" from the Wild Field, and the trial and boundary-traced
lands of the Taichukov, Korchakov and Nepolotsk stans. The related set
**kn. 10048** preserves a copy of part of the book on fols. 1–216v, the commission's protocol
on fols. 217–282 and the instruction on fols. 283–320.

The value of the document lies in its method. The published instruction orders the land to be checked
«по писцовым книгам, по дачам, по крепостям, по старым межам» ("by the cadastral books, by the grants, by the deeds, by the old boundaries"). "Kreposti" in
this context are written titles of possession, not fortifications; a "pridacha" is
new land added to an already existing pomestye. Therefore an entry of 1681
could name the first holder of an old grant, inheritance after a father, or
a lost 16th-century book.

At the same time another dead end has been closed. The handwritten alphabetical index of inventory 97i
to books 181–395 has been checked against the official photocopies. In the continuous stretch of the letter
"A", after the group of the Anichkovs begins the group of the Antipovs: there is no separate group of
Ampilogovs, Anpilogovs or Anfilogovs between them. This does not mean that
the right man is absent from the book: he might be hidden in a collective «с товарищи» ("with companions"),
appear under another form or still be named patronymically — for example,
«Фёдор Анфилогов сын» ("Fyodor, son of Anfilog"). But there is no need to repeat the general alphabetical search.

The next priority is a territorial reading of Korchakov stan by the known
lands of the early Oryol Anfilogovs and their neighbours. In each entry one must look for the
formulas «после отца» ("after the father"), «по старой даче» ("by the old grant"), «по крепости» ("by deed") and references to an earlier
cadastral or separate book. The map does not change for now: what has been found is a search mechanism,
not a proven resettlement route.

[Official catalogue of fond 1209](https://rgada.info/sprav/sprav/zenchenko_ivanova_2013.pdf),
[publication of the 1681 instruction](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVII/1680-1700/Pisc_nakaz_1681/text.php).

### Not a list of settlers but a complaint after recruitment: Pervushka Penkov, 1592

A search on the control surname Penkov led to a rare document that
preserves exactly the formula missing for the early Oryol
Anfilogovs. In his own petition **Pervushka Dementyev syn Penkov** called
himself a «новоприборным елецким казаком» ("newly recruited Yelets Cossack") and wrote directly that in 1592 he
enlisted to serve «на твой государев новой город на Елец» ("in your sovereign's new town of Yelets"). Before the recruitment he lived
in Solova district under the syn boyarsky Danila Ivanovich Karpov — on quitrent,
«не на пашне и не на тягле» ("not on ploughland and not on tax-bearing status").

This is not a late genealogical legend but two mutually verifying acts of one
file: the petition on fols. 43–44 and the tsar's decree charter to the Solova voivode on
fols. 41–42. The Yelets Cossack sotnik Pavel Guryev formally released Pervushka from his
former master under the sovereign's charter. Karpov, however, kept his wife and
his brother Isay, and appropriated his cattle, clothing and grain. Three other brothers of Pervushka remained
living with him in Solova district. Thus the document records not only the
move of one man but also the division of a family between the old place and the new
fortress.

The spelling is especially important. The petition has **Penkov**, at the beginning of the decree
charter the same man is called **Pankov**, and at its end the copyist wrote
**Peshkov**. One act yields three search forms for one person. Consequently,
an early Anfilogov cannot be sought only under the modern spelling: one has to
check vowel substitutions, Ф/П and distortions of neighbouring consonants, and then
compare name, patronymic, master and route.

Pervushka is not yet connected by kinship with the Penkov Vasilyevichi — Afanasy of Solova,
Prokuda of Oryol and Sergey of Krapivna. He was a recruited
Cossack, whereas Prokuda received a novik's pomestye oklad in 1596. But
the mechanism and the direction have now been proven on a specific person of the same
control surname milieu: **Solova district → the new Yelets**, four years
before Prokuda Penkov and Pavel Anfilogov appeared together in the Oryol
desyatnya.

The necessary counter-example was found at once. A decree charter of 1593 to Oryol
reports that **Grishka Ivanov syn Andreev** enlisted in the Yelets streltsy
«из-за орлянина» ("from under the Oryol man") Alexey Pantyukhin, while his father remained living on
the former Oryol vyt. So Yelets recruited people from several neighbouring
districts, including Oryol itself. Pervushka's route confirms the existence of a
Solova channel but does not turn Solova into the only donor town.

The tactics change once again. The next body of material is not only desyatni but the petitions
of the 1590s about bringing away the family and property after recruitment, and also the otkaznye
charters addressed to the former towns. It is in them that one should look for Mikula,
Vasily, Persha/Pyotr, Timofey, Nechay, Pakhom and Terenty — the fathers of the early
Oryol Anfilogovs. A separate record has been created without a profile or a map entry:
the Penkovs remain a control surname, and the public map shows only
proven routes of the researched surname group.

[Decree charter on Pervushka's petition](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/1-20/17.htm),
[Pervushka's petition](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/1-20/18.htm),
[charter about Grishka Andreev from Oryol](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/30.htm).

### The Vorotynsky break: one should look for more than one donor town

The new turn has yielded not a ready-made route but a more plausible mechanism
for the emergence of the mixed Oryol core. On 15 September 1562 Mikhail and Alexander
Vorotynsky fell into disgrace. Their shares in Novosil, Peremyshl and Vorotynsk were
taken for the sovereign and their property confiscated. The publication by V. Yu. Belikov and
V. I. Kolycheva shows that after this the old military-territorial
corporation lost its autonomous leader: the pomeshchiki now held their land in
the name of the tsar. In 1566 Mikhail was given back the southern part of his holdings on a limited basis, and by
February 1569 he was again cut off from his accustomed corporation.

The founding of Oryol in 1566 falls exactly within this break. Therefore
the first population of the new fortress may have been the result not of a single resettlement
from Kashira, Meshchovsk or Solova, but of a state redistribution of people from
several already existing service milieus of the Upper Oka. For now this is a working
model, but it explains the heterogeneous geography of the early Oryol golovy (commanders) better
than a search for a single ancestral town.

The main archival find is the exact title of a lost name source.
The 1614 inventory of the archive of the Posolsky Prikaz (Ambassadorial Chancellery) still listed **«Сыскной список и
роспросные речи боярина князя Михаила Ивановича Воротынского людей 71-го
году»** ("Investigation list and interrogation statements of the people of the boyar Prince Mikhail Ivanovich Vorotynsky, of the year 71"). That is 1562/63. A modern article by N. V. Belov gives the exact reference:
*Описи Царского архива XVI века и архива Посольского приказа 1614 года* [Inventories of the 16th-Century Tsar's Archive and of the Archive of the Posolsky Prikaz of 1614],
Moscow, 1960, **p. 50**. The content of the file is unknown, but the title itself proves that
the prince's people were investigated and questioned by name. Now one can look not for
abstract "settlers" but for extracts, copies and references to precisely this
"investigation list".

It is particularly telling that as early as 20 April 1563, among the sureties for Alexander
Vorotynsky, stand together **Rychko Onfalov/Anfalov syn Volotskogo**, Dmitry
Turgenev, Andrey Izmaylov and Ivan Fefilatyev. The last three were later directly or
probably connected with the early Oryol command. Rychko is not called a man of
Vorotynsky and is not declared an ancestor of Anfilogovs; nor is the surety
a list of transferred people. But the rare naming has now been placed at the precise
moment of the state restructuring of the south-western service milieu.

The second node — the Sovins — has given the first personal staffing bridge. **Petrok
(Menshoy) Grigoryev syn Sovin** is recorded in the Thousand Book of 1550 as a syn
boyarsky of the third category for Vorotynsk; the Dvorovaya tetrad (Court Register) keeps him within the
same Upper Oka milieu, including that of Meshchovsk. In 1577–1578 **Pyotr
Menshoy Grigoryevich Sovin** is already in command at Oryol. The rare combination of
name and the distinguishing "Menshoy" ("the Younger"), the patronymic and the family name all match, so this is
a highly reliable trajectory of one man: **Vorotynsk/Meshchovsk service
milieu → early Oryol command**.

Ivan, Pyotr Bolshoy, Pyotr Menshoy, Nikita and Semyon Sovin are recorded as one group
as the children of Grigory, and Ivan Grigoryevich Sovin stands among the sureties of 1562 next to
future Oryol golovy. Their brotherhood is highly probable but not yet
supported by a direct genealogical or documentary formula. Nor has the
identity of Bolanda with one of the early brothers been established. The main point is already proven without these
conjectures: at least one specific man of the old Vorotynsky corporation later
ended up at the head of Oryol.

The next step yielded not a commander but a probable chain of an ordinary pomeshchik
family. In the Odoyev sotnaya (land-tax extract) of 25 May 1565, next to the road from Odoyev to
Novosil, are named **«Кости Иванова сына Савенкова да сына ево Нечаика»** ("Kostya Ivanov syn Savenkov and his son Nechaik").
The formula undoubtedly records two generations: Kostya Ivanovich Savenkov and his
son Nechay. On the photocopy of printed p. 109 the line reads clearly; the publication
gives the archival basis: РГАДА, f. 1209, stolbtsy for Belyov, No. 39848, pt. 1,
fols. 88–104, a copy of 1660, the target place on fol. 102v.

In the Oryol book of 1594/95, on p. 871, stands **Osip Nechaev syn
Savenkova** in the village of Savenkovo in Nepolotsk stan. Here the surname form and the rare
father's name match at once. Within twenty-nine to thirty years Nechay could
have become the father of an adult Osip, so a probable chain emerges:
**Kostya → Nechay → Osip Nechaevich**. This is the first candidate found for the move of
a rank-and-file pomeshchik family from the Odoyev–Novosil milieu to early Oryol.

But there is no intermediate act yet. The Oryol Osip is not called Kostya's grandson, and
Nechay has not yet been found with the full formula «Костин сын Савенков» ("Kostin syn Savenkov"). There were many
Savenkovs in Oryol, so the surname on its own is insufficient; what gives the hypothesis weight is
precisely the patronymic Nechaev. No route or profiles of the Savenkovs are created. A separate
record `PUB-ODOEV-1565-OREL-1594-SAVENKOV-PATRONYMIC-BRIDGE` keeps both
photocopies, the literal lines, the analysis of the names and the exact limit of the conclusion.

A late control gave a useful but not decisive result. In the name index
to РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 488, on the Novosil fol. 254, stand together Andrey and
Pronka Potapovy deti Savenkovy, Ivan Lomakin and Kuzma Fyodorov Savenkovy.
Nechay Kostin or Osip Nechaevich are not among them. So in the 17th century several
lines of the Savenkovs continued to live in the old Novosil district; a possible departure
of Osip's branch to Oryol did not require the resettlement of the whole family. But for now this is only
a model of the division of a surname circle. The next step is to read fol. 254 itself and look
in its land formulas for old grants, rather than take the index as proof.

A separate exhaustive control closed one more repeatable path. From the Dvorovaya
tetrad not only surnames but the full formulas of both relevant headings have been extracted —
Vorotynsk and Meshchovsk: name, father's name, family name, sons and brothers.
In a line-by-line comparison with Oryol in 1594/95, no one turned out to be a second direct person after
Pyotr Menshoy Sovin. A false candidate is especially useful:
**«Иван Иванов сын Шамского»** ("Ivan Ivanov syn Shamskogo") is literally present in both the early and the late
text, but the late line stands under the heading of **Tula**, not Oryol
district. So this is not a move Meshchovsk → Oryol. Such a control of the district heading
is mandatory for the whole multi-volume edition.

So the broad repetition of the Dvorovaya tetrad has been exhausted. The next corpus must
report not merely a name but the origin of a right or of service: otkaznye entries,
petitions about enrolment and late extracts with the formulas **«с отцова поместья»** ("from the father's pomestye"),
**«по старой даче»** ("by the old grant"), **«переведён из города»** ("transferred from the town"). It is there that the
lost intermediate step between a Cossack or a son without a surname and an
Oryol pomeshchik of the 1590s may appear.

Therefore the next search splits in two. Along the archival line — to search for
derivatives of the investigation file of 7071 and the name entries of the description of the
Odoyev–Novosil lands of 1565/66. Along the network line — to compare
pomeshchiki and former princely servants with the Oryol desyatnya of 1594, starting with the
Sovins and rare neighbouring namings. The map does not change: the institutional
possibility of a transfer is not yet a documented route of a bearer of the
researched surname group.

[N. V. Belov's article with the exact reference to the archival inventory](https://cyberleninka.ru/article/n/groza-kanuna-oprichniny-opala-knyazey-vorotynskih-v-1562-godu-i-serpuhovskoe-izmennoe-delo/pdf),
[publication of documents on the Vorotynsky lands](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1560-1580/Zemlevlad_Vorotyn/text.htm),
[index to the Thousand Book and the Dvorovaya tetrad](https://yakovkrotov.com/acts/16/2/tysyachnaya_10.htm),
[page 871 of the Oryol book](https://archive.org/details/moscha-regia/page/n871/mode/1up).

### 1645/46: only one explicitly named Onpilogov remained in all of Bazdyrevo

The six spreads found, **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 14, fols. 240v–246**,
have now been checked as one continuous block, not merely as the source of Ivan's
line. Among the headings of the numbered households the explicit surname form of the
researched group is read once: **No. 441, «Двор того ж помещика Ивана
Васильева сына Онпилогова»** ("Household of the same pomeshchik Ivan Vasilyev syn Onpilogov"). No other confident Anpilogov, Anfilogov or
Onpilogov has been found in the headings of this village block.

This changes the meaning of the comparison with 1625. Back then a short register listed six
holders in Bazdyrevo: Ivan Vasilyevich, Vasily Dmitrievich, the brothers
Alexey and Grigory Vasilyevich, and the brothers Martyn and Kuzma Karpovich. Twenty
years later only Ivan is clearly recognisable in the census sequence.
The others cannot be declared dead or resettled: a household could have passed to a
son or to an unrelated holder, a man could have lived in another village or been
counted in another section. But the model of an unchanging "family nest of six
households" has now been refuted by the original itself.

The new search address is not a general alphabet of the surname but the fate of five specific
zherebya between 1625 and 1645/46. Otkaznye and separate entries should
show who received the land of Vasily Dmitrievich, of Alexey and Grigory
Vasilyevich, and of Martyn and Kuzma Karpovich. Such a transfer may name a
son, a brother, a deceased father or an old extract — and can therefore give a
generational link that the census lacks. The map receives only the already
proven observation of Ivan in Bazdyrevo; the disappearance of the surname from other
headings is not turned into an invented route.

[Public folder with photocopies of the file](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ).

### Not another surname but the internal network of names in the 1594/95 book

After checking the open Oryol books No. 1056 and 1058, the dead-end branch has been closed
deliberately. **Kn. 1056, fols. 1–18** is a late copy of the already read census
of 1645/46; its other folios belong to the land survey of 1676. **Kn. 1058**
contains a late cadastral and survey complex of the 1680s. Neither of them
is the lost otkaznaya or separate book of the interval 1595–1625,
so the exhaustive leafing through hundreds of frames has not been continued.

Instead, the navigational layer of the 1594/95 book itself has been opened. Before its
main text there survives a **handwritten list of holders on fols. 1a–63a**.
The official catalogue names this section directly, and the first frames of the open
photocopy show names with references to entries. This is not proof of
resettlement nor a separate register of servicemen, but a new way of looking for
a man who did not yet bear the surname Anfilogov.

The check is now built on family signatures: **Mikula → Vasily, Timofey,
Karp; Nechay → Ivan; Persha → Ivan; Pakhom → Ermak; Terenty → Pavel**. If one
of the fathers or sons has a second reference in the list, the other
entry of the main text should be read: there may be an earlier holding, a different patronymic
or a kinship context. A match of one name form is not enough; only a repetition
with the same rare family or neighbourhood surroundings will have value.

Thus the search changes from an all-Russian trawl of the surname to an
**internal reconstruction of eight biographies within one land
corpus**. The profiles and the map do not change for now: an index reference on its own
is not a new event.

[Official catalogue of РГАДА cadastral books](https://rgada.info/sprav/sprav/zenchenko_ivanova_2013.pdf),
[open photocopy of kn. 329](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ).

### Archival foliation corrected: Bazdyrevo is on fols. 549–551v

Matching a printed page with the manuscript by a single similar word produced
an error: fols. 713–720 had earlier been attached to the records. The official structure
of the file shows that these folios already belong to Nougorsk stan, whereas
printed p. 975 has the running head of Korchakov stan. The repeat search was carried out
on three independent criteria at once: the stan, the sequence of neighbours
and the handwritten entry numbers.

The exact correspondence has now been established:

- No. 747, Belyay Vasilyev syn Onfilogova — **fols. 549–549v**;
- No. 748, Osip Vasilyev syn Anfilogova — **fols. 549v–550**;
- No. 751, Vasily, Timofey and Karp Mikuliny deti Anfilogova —
  **fols. 551–551v**.

Between them the manuscript keeps the same order of neighbours as the print: Gavrila
Nemtsov, Lyova Krivolapov, Mikifor Bazdyrev, then Ovdey, Pronya and Grisha
Krivolapov. This is stronger than a single surname match and definitively
localises the whole land cluster. The records now keep four genuine
spreads, fols. 548v–552; the false references have been removed from the records.

The manuscript did not name a new town of origin. Therefore the list of holders
in kn. 329 is no longer the main direction: it leads well to an
entry, but does not itself add biography. The search moves to **kn. 330 for
1625/26**, and not only to the actual holders of Bazdyrevo on fols.
72v–78 and 82v–87. The second, more promising layer is the section on vacant
pomestya of Korchakov stan, compiled by comparison with the old book.
It already shows the break-up of the neighbourhood complex: separately recorded are the widow's
pomestye of Orina, wife of Maxim Bazdyrev, with her sons Frol, Agap and Rodion
in Tokarna, and the former holdings of Krivtsov and Bredikhin. It is precisely the neighbouring empty
zherebya that may explain which families died out, left or handed over their land, and give the
formula omitted by the short register of holders.

### The Toropets control: Mikifor Oksenov a generation before Oryol

After the search on the variants Anfilog-/Anfilokh- alone had been exhausted, a check was made of
another signature — the rare full formula of the nearest neighbour. In the Toropets
book of 1540/41, on manuscript fol. 9v, among the posad (townsfolk) people is recorded
**«двор Микифора Оксенова сына»** ("the household of Mikifor Oksenov syn"). In another town list of the same book
Mikifor is repeated, and his brother **Ostratko** is named next to him.

Fifty-four years later the Oryol book names in Bazdyrevo
**Mikifor Oksenov syn Bazdyrev**, who held «отца его поместье» ("his father's pomestye").
The match of the personal name and the rare patronymic is not chronologically impossible:
a young adult of 1540 could have been an elderly holder in 1594/95. This is
a new north-western control candidate, found not by surname but by a
two-generation name signature.

But the identity is not accepted for now. In Toropets there is no naming Bazdyrev;
the man is recorded as a posad man, not as a syn boyarsky; the brother Ostratko has not been found in the Oryol
circle. Finally, the probable son of the Oryol Mikifor — Pyotr
Nikiforovich Bazdyrev, also known as Sviridov — is documented in Pekhlets stan of
Ryazan district and moved directly from there to Ryazhsk service. Therefore
the Ryazan–Ryazhsk corridor remains stronger, and Toropets becomes a useful
alternative capable of testing how unique the name formula itself
is.

The next distinguishing test is very specific: to look in the Toropets books
of the 1550s–1570s for **Mikifor Oksenov together with his brother Ostratko** and a second
naming Bazdyrev/Sviridov; in the Oryol otkaznye documents, for the former
service of Oksyon or Mikifor. Only a match of brother, status or land
will make it possible to turn a resemblance of names into a route. No separate profile or map point has been
created: only a man of another surname form is documented, and the move to
Oryol is not proven.

[Publication of the Toropets book on Vostochnaya literatura](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1540-1560/Toropec_kniga_1540/text1.htm),
[full PDF copy](https://toropetscbs.ru/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/toropeczkaya-pisczovaya-kniga-1540-goda.pdf).

### A change of scale: the northern "imprint" of the neighbouring surname Deriglazov

A search for the single surname Anpilogov runs up against the absence of an early Oryol
desyatnya. Therefore the unit of comparison has been made not an individual but a package of
rare surnames surrounding Anpilogovs in the Oryol land of 1594/95.
The first to work was the surname Deriglazov.

In A. V. Antonov's catalogue, act No. 1678 has been found: a separate extract of 1585 by the
guba elders Krestyanin Strakhov and Ivan Korsakov to Ivan Aristov syn
Lupandin for the former pomestye of **Ignaty Deriglazov with his nephew Pyotr** —
the homestead site Popovo Zasorodye and the waste plot Lazugino in the Pokrovsky Ivansky
Nikolsky na Voloke Derzhkove pogost of the Bezhetsk pyatina. Publication:
«Русский дипломатарий» [Russian Diplomatarium], issue 8, 2002, No. 1678, p. 214.

An essential correction to the type of source: there is no full text of the extract yet.
The catalogue marks the entry as a **mention** and gives its address: **РГАДА, f. 1209,
op. 1, kn. 972, fol. 66**. An independent catalogue of cadastral books identifies kn. 972
as the inspection book (dozornaya kniga) of the Bezhetsk pyatina of 1593/94, from the inspection by Voin Ivanovich
Zavalishin and the podyachy Zhdan Zinovyev, fols. 1–294. So the scribes of 1593/94
saw or retold the holder's extract of 1585; the act itself may not
have survived separately.

The chronology is tight: the Bezhetsk family is documented only nine years before
the Oryol cadastral book. In Oryol other formulas are already recorded: Danila
Fyodorovich Deriglazov next to the former land of Malets Pakhomov and Ermak
Pakhomovich Anpilogov, and also the widow of Efrem Deriglazov with four
sons. Neither Ignaty nor Pyotr has a patronymic in the catalogue entry;
kinship with Danila and Efrem is not stated. Therefore this is not a proven move,
but the first specific **north-western donor area** for one of the rare
surnames of the Oryol neighbourhood complex.

No Bezhetsk pyatina — Oryol line is put on the map and no profiles of the Deriglazovs
are created: this is a control surname outside the researched group.
The practical next test is a photocopy of kn. 972, fol. 66 with the neighbouring folios.
What is needed is the literal formula of the extract, the patronymics of Ignaty and Pyotr, the reason for the loss of the
pomestye and the neighbouring surnames. If one more rare surname of the
Oryol cluster recurs nearby, the version of a common northern recruitment will become substantially stronger;
if not, the Bezhetsk branch will remain an independent same-surname control.

[Antonov's catalogue in Google Books](https://books.google.com/books?id=ySknAQAAIAAJ),
[open name index to the catalogue](https://62info.ru/history/node/11442),
[catalogue of РГАДА cadastral books](http://rgada.info/materiali/baranov_catalog.pdf).

### Full-text search of the inventories changes the archival priority: f. 1209 before f. 141

A new independent path of verification has opened — a full-text search of **5,334
РГАДА inventories**, including the OCR of **1,035 PDFs**. In the early materials of fonds 210,
141 and 1209, Oryol, watch service, the year 1577 and the variants Anfilog-,
Anpilog-, Onfilog- and Ampilog- were checked. The search did not show an independently titled Oryol file
about a muster or a pridacha of 1577. This result applies
only to the indexed headings and the recognised text: the document may still
be inside a late book, a general stolbets or an undescribed
complex.

The main correction concerns not the result but the route. The boyar verdict
of 15 March 1577 directly created **two chancery traces**:

- name lists of the selected men and their oklady were delivered to the dyaki of the Razryadnaya
  izba (Military Records Office) — this is the line of fond 210;
- a memorandum about the pomestya was sent to the Pomestny Prikaz, from where scribes and podyachie
  were to go out to the towns for a personal muster of the men with their horses — this is the line of
  fond 1209, its late extracts, otkaznye books and land lawsuits.

Therefore the earlier formulation "fond 141 first" was too broad. Fond 141
is a composite collection of surviving files of various prikazy; it remains a control,
but not the main address. Priority now goes to derivative documents of the Pomestny
Prikaz with the formulas **«прибавлено к старому поместью»** ("added to the old pomestye"), **«с 20 до 50
четвертей»** ("from 20 to 50 chetverti"), **«по списку из Разряду»** ("by the list from the Razryad") and references to the books of 7085/1577.
It is precisely such a late reference that may name a man even if the original
memorandum perished in the fire of 1626.

For the map the result is negative for now: a new documentary channel has been found,
but not the town of origin of a specific Anfilogov. A route without a personal link
is not added.

[Publication of the verdict of 15 March 1577](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Akty_Popov_I/21-40/23.php),
[full-text search of РГАДА inventories](https://rgada.sovadev.pro/).

### Not a surname search but Pavel Ievlev's biographical gap

The tactics have been changed again. Instead of further trawling through similar surnames, a check was made of
what happened after the two suretyships of **Pavel Terentyev syn Ievlev** in
Ryazhsk in 1591. Both men for whom he stood surety did not disappear along with
him:

- Shestachko Ivanov syn Stroilov is repeated in the Ryazhsk desyatnya of 1594 under
  No. 494 and in the desyatnya of 1597 under No. 669;
- Posnik, also known as Poznyak, Savin syn Goltsov is repeated in 1594 under No. 578; in
  1597 under No. 570 the okladchiki (assessors) said that they did not know him, that he had not
  served with them and had not come to the Moscow muster.

Pavel himself is not repeated in the desyatni of 1594 and 1597. Consequently, this is not
the loss of the whole surety group and not a general move of people from one block. His
disappearance is an individual biographical signal.

In addition, the published land and tax-payment corpus of
1594–1597 has been checked exhaustively. Pavel's exact formula was not found there either, although other
Ievlevs are read with confidence. In Pekhlets stan, on printed pp. 142–143, under
**Paney Terekhov syn Ievleva** is recorded a zherebye in the village of Rozovaya: 20
chetverti of ploughland in inhabited and empty land, with payment for four chetverti of inhabited
ploughland. In Perevitsk stan of Zaraysk district, on p. 163, **Mikhail Ivanov syn
Ievlev** held a zherebye of the village of Inoshevka. In the Ryazan desyatnya of 1604
there also appears **Lyuban Petrov syn Ievlev**, a novik with an oklad of 200 chetverti.

These are not relatives of Pavel by any proven document and not bearers of the
researched surname group. Their significance is as a control: the surname Ievlev did not
vanish from the region and was not invisible to the search. The formulas «Семён Иевлев сын
Ляпунов» ("Semyon Ievlev syn Lyapunov") and «Меншик Иевлев сын Ляпунов» ("Menshik Ievlev syn Lyapunov") are separated from them: there Ievlev is a
patronymic, and the family's third naming is Lyapunov.

The new assessment cautiously strengthens the Ryazhsk candidate. The sequence
now looks like this: two suretyships in Ryazhsk in 1591 → individual
disappearance from the service and land books of 1594–1597 → the appearance in Oryol
in 1596 of a Pavel with the same personal name and a father Terenty, but with the naming
Anfilogov. Such a chain is compatible with a move to a new town, but death,
loss of land or cessation of service are equally
possible. Therefore the profile is not
split and no Ryazhsk — Oryol route is put on the map. The next goal is
not a new list of surnames but **an act of departure, a transfer of a pomestye or a late extract
from the lost Ryazhsk book**, where the reason for Pavel's disappearance would be named.

[Payment books of the Ryazan region in the Internet Archive](https://archive.org/details/pistsovyiaknigi00storgoog),
[Ryazan desyatnya of 1604](https://62info.ru/history/node/12181),
[Ryazhsk desyatni of 1594 and 1597](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1560-1580/Desjatni_XVI_v/text10_2.php).

### A reverse search: not the surname but the children of a possible Anfilog

Another broad trawl of the ready-made surname has been replaced by a testable generational
hypothesis. If the Oryol naming became fixed only in the generation of the
1590s, then their fathers Mikula, Persha, Nechay, Pakhom or Terenty could have been
recorded earlier with direct patronymics: «Микула Анфилогов сын» ("Mikula, son of Anfilog"), «Пахом
Анфилофов сын» ("Pakhom, son of Anfilof"), etc. In that case a search for the single word «Анфилогов» without
a child's name would indeed miss the relevant generation.

A full-text check of six volumes of «Писцовые книги Московского государства» [Cadastral Books of the Muscovite State]
gave a hard limit: combinations of the seven paternal stems with Anfil-/Anpil-/Onfil-
and Ampil- are found only in the already known Oryol entries of 1594/95.
There is not a single pre-Oryol Anfilog with a child of the right name in this land
corpus. This is not a refutation of a common ancestor — a considerable part of the southern
records has been lost — but the published Moscow corpus does not
confirm this model.

Two more independent directions have been checked. In the complete open text of
the «Дворовая тетрадь 50-х годов XVI века» [Court Register of the 1550s] there are no forms Anfilog-/Anpilog-/Onfilog-
or Anfilof-. Consequently, further searching among the upper layer of the Sovereign's
Court has low priority: the early Oryol men more plausibly belonged to
provincial Cossacks and deti boyarskie with small pomestya. The Polish-Lithuanian
variants `Onfilogow`, `Onfilohow`, `Anfilogow`, `Anfilohow` and spellings with
`ł` likewise did not lead to a documented zemyanin or boyar of the 16th century.
This null result is limited to the available full-text search and does not
replace a reading of the Lithuanian Metrica.

After the check the tactics change rather than repeat. The main layer now is
grassroots provincial record-keeping: the Oryol watch lists of 1571–1580,
lists of selected Cossacks and deti boyarskie with small pomestya, otkaznye charters about
moving to a new town and late extracts with formulas about former service. Nothing has been added to the
map: what has been found is a limitation of the method, not a person's route.

[Full text of the Dvorovaya tetrad](https://drevlit.ru/docs/russia/XVI/1540-1560/Dvor_tetrad/text1.php).

### Oskol moved back: Danilko Onpilogov, regimental Cossack of 1615

A search not by the modern surname but by the formula of a service heading has yielded a new
early bearer. In the Oskol inspection book (dozornaya kniga) of 1615, on fol. 78v, is recorded
**Danilko Onpilogov**. His position is determined not by a guess based on neighbours:
the heading «Полковые казаки» ("Regimental Cossacks") begins on fol. 77v, followed by the pyatidesyatniki,
«десятники и рядовые все» ("the desyatniki and all the rank and file"), and the independent list of belomestnye Cossacks
begins only on fol. 80. Consequently, Danilko was a regimental Cossack of Oskol.

This moves the documented presence of the surname group in Oskol back from 1646 to
1615. A later record shows a quite different status: monastery
peasants Andrey Petrovich Anpilogov and his son Ivan. Theoretically a service
family could have produced a dependent branch after the Time of Troubles or a change of owner, but the sources
do not call Danilko the father, grandfather or relative of Andrey. Nor is the Cossack's former
town named, so he cannot yet be linked with Oryol Anfilogovs of 1594–1596.

A separate profile of Danila and a Record with the full service framework have been created. The map
shows only Oskol in 1615; no route from Oryol is drawn. The next
point of verification is the manuscript fol. 78v of РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, kn. 342, and
the repeated Oskol books of 1623–1626.

[Open transcription of the book with folio references](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/2160/38740/p5351246.htm),
[catalogue entry for A. P. Nikulov's edition in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000199_000009_011833476/).

### A new turn: a possible brother and two genealogies of the Ievlevs

A check of page 296 and of the syntax of the whole book has changed the priority. Now one must
look not only for the fate of Pavel himself but for the link **Pavel — Panka, probable
sons of the double-named Orbey/Arbey — Terenty Ievlev**. Panka reliably remained
in Ryazhsk and may become an anchor for land inheritance, sureties and
later extracts. The manuscript original is still needed, but the printed formula already
no longer looks like a single editorial anomaly: its grammar is confirmed by
other double paternal names in the same desyatnya.

Both genealogical lists (rospisi) have now been found in full text: *Российская генеалогия* [Russian Genealogy], issue 8
(2020), Nos. 42–43, pp. 299–309; archival basis — РГАДА, f. 286, op. 1,
item 450, fols. 288–293v. The first, submitted by **Seliverst Ievlev on 19 March
1686**, derives his Tula branch from Vasily Vasilyevich Ievlev,
who came, according to family tradition, from Veliky Novgorod under Vasily
Dmitrievich. At the end of the genealogical listing the compiler declares: **«А
Иевлевых кроме сей нашей росписи к нашему поколению ни в котором городе иных
никого нет»** ("And of the Ievlevs, apart from this our list, there are no others of our lineage in any town"). Terenty, Pavel Terentyevich and Panka are absent from this list.

The second list, submitted by **Stepan and Andrey Ievlev on 18 June**, builds
another Tula–Aleksin line from Grigory Vasilyevich Ievlev. His
sons are named as Sava, Pavel, Vasily and Ulyan; moreover, **Pavel and Ulyan died
childless**. This is Pavel Grigoryevich, not Pavel Terentyevich of Ryazhsk. The compilers
also draw a hard boundary: to the Ievlevs who called themselves their kinsmen,
it is proposed that they present evidence themselves. So in 1686 bearers of the same surname
were no longer automatically regarded as one lineage. The well-known Tula noble
legend cannot be transferred to the Ryazhsk pair without a separate chain of proof.
The negative result changes the direction of the search: what is needed are the files of **unrecognised
kinsmen**, Ryazhsk pomestye inheritances and the descendants of Panka Arbeev, not a
repetition of the general history of the Tula Ievlevs.

The early trace has also been checked against the official digital copy of the National Library of Russia (РНБ), not only against
an alphabetical internet index. On printed p. 108, manuscript fol. 38 of the list of
the court of 1573, it literally reads: **«Помясы. По 5 рублей. Паня Иевлев. Юшко
Химин»** ("Pomyasy. 5 roubles each. Panya Ievlev. Yushko Khimin"). Thus Panya was not a nameless "oprichnik in general" but a pomyas
of the Khlebenny dvorets (Bread Palace) — a central household servant. Between this line and
the Ryazhsk Panka of 1591 there are eighteen years; the age sequence is
possible.

But the name is not unique: the same index knows Panya Kuzmin and Panya Pozdeev.
Moreover, the historical stem **Pan-** could yield everyday forms of
several calendar names — Pankraty, Panfil/Pamfil, Panteleymon and others.
Therefore Panya and Panka are close, but need not be one person or even
expand to the same full name. The line of 1573 has no patronymic Terentyev, no
form Orbeev/Arbeev, no town or land. The new narrow query is formulated as follows:
find for the palace servant Panya Ievlev a patronymic, a pomestye grant or a release from the court
between 1573 and 1591. Until there is such a bridge, no profile or route is created.

[Official viewer of the РНБ edition](https://vivaldi.nlr.ru/pm000000514/view),
[issues of the almanac «Российская генеалогия» (Russian Genealogy)](https://www.svrt.ru/lib/periodika.shtml),
[post with the archival folios of the lists](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/401/22205/p1133280.htm).

### Not all service in a new town was resettlement

A continuous reading of the neighbouring charters of the Yelets complex has given an important filter for the
Oryol search. On 5 September 1592 it was ordered that **100
Tula and 50 Ryazan deti boyarskie** be sent to the new Yelets; in fact 60 and 30 arrived. In
their petition they call themselves **«сменными»** ("rotating"), and the Ryazan men report: **«И нас
холопей твоих выбрали рязанцев однодворцев и безсемейных»** ("And us, your slaves, they chose from the Ryazan men who are single-household and without families").

Here "odnodvortsy" is not yet the name of the later social estate. In the literal
context these are men of one household, chosen together with those without families precisely because
it was easier to tear them away from their farms for a term. They asked to be released
after their rotating service. This is a different group from the 164 deti boyarskie who in the same
complex were recruited **«из розных городов» на житьё** ("from various towns", to live there), enrolled in three
pomestye categories, provided with land and money and exempted from watch duty so that they
could plough the land and set up households.

For the history of how Oryol Anfilogovs appeared, the distinction is fundamental. A line
«служил на Орле» ("served at Oryol") or a name in a regimental or watch list does not yet prove
a move. A genuine trace of departure must be accompanied by at least one of the
signs: **«на житье»** ("to live there"), an initial pomestye grant, a household set up,
the transfer of a wife and property, a surety bond or a direct naming of the former
town. Therefore the rotation lists remain a map of contacts, and the main search
moves to petitions after recruitment and to land documents — it is there that
a new Yelets serviceman usually states where he came from.

Neither of these two documents names Anfilogovs; the profiles and the map do not
change. Temporary service must not be turned into an invented route on the map.

[Order on the Tula and Ryazan rotation](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/21.htm),
[petition of the "single-household and those without families"](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/23.htm),
[report on the 164 permanent Yelets deti boyarskie](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVI/1580-1600/Anpilogov_new_dok/21-40/25.htm).

### A new public archive checked: book 336 has not yet been bought out

The volunteer project «Сказочки, сказки и сказищи Орловщины» ("Little Tales, Tales and Great Tales of the Oryol Region") did indeed
open a shared cloud and marked as bought out the Oryol cadastral book of 1594/95
— РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 329. However, the attractive formula
**«Орловский — 21 дело / 10 960 листов — выкуплено»** ("Oryol — 21 files / 10,960 folios — bought out") refers to fond 350,
that is, to the revision tales, not to the pomestye books of fond 1209.

The list for fond 1209 has been checked separately. File **336, the survey book of P. V.
Shamordin of 1680–1681, 527 folios**, is in it, but is not marked as bought out
and is absent from the public folder. Therefore the old grants of Korchakov stan cannot yet
be read from an open scan. On the other hand, the result closes a false expectation and
leaves two exact fallback addresses: a copy of the first 216 folios in **d. 10048** and
a copy of part of Shamordin's survey in **d. 1057, fols. 121–281**. Neither of these
files has been published in the cloud yet either.

[Public cloud of the project](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ),
[list of Oryol files of fond 1209](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1851/142603/p5034895.htm#pp5034895).

### A parallel search through neighbours: a live research channel found

Instead of yet another query by surname, the updated table of participants in the
project of open Oryol scans has been checked. The Anfilogov group itself, the Bazdyrevs,
Krivolapovs, Penkovs, Onanyins and Ievlevs are not declared in it. But a person has been found
who separately researches **the Kolugins (Kalugins) precisely from the Oryol cadastral book
of 1594**: the ВГД user `aleksgooo`, Telegram `@ignority`. His
open posts show the same productive technique that we need:
comparing an Oryol family with same-surname people of Bolkhov, Tula and Novosil and with the
noviki of 1596, rather than mechanically declaring all bearers of the surname kin.

The table also has a researcher of the Oryol Shalimovs — `irinakoh`, Telegram
`@Irina Kokhtenko`. The Shalimovs are especially important as a control: Parfen Fyodorovich
Shalimov stands directly in the exempted group of Ivan Nechaevich Anpilogov in
1594/95 and again neighbours his pomestye in the register of 1625/26. At the same time
the public table does not say that the researcher has 16th-century materials.

This is not a source of origin and not a basis for profiles. It is an address for
possible private extracts on the neighbouring layer. No messages have been sent to these people without
a separate instruction.

[Table of researchers](https://disk.yandex.ru/i/qXbYPci-MI1TmA),
[example of an inter-town analysis of families of 1594/1596](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/3131/110997/p4979283.htm).

### The Heraldry Office index checked and closed as a dead end

Instead of yet another general search by surname, the open alphabetical index of
the Heraldmeister's Office has been checked: РГАДА, fond 286, index 238, letters A–B for
1724–1796. The ВГД thread has published not only manual extracts but also a shared
Google Drive folder with two PDF copies of the index. This made it possible to check the
source itself rather than rely only on search results.

The forms Anfilogov, Anpilogov, Onfilogov and Ampilogov were not found in the available index.
Two similar but unrelated results came up: Ksenofont
Anfilatov and Corporal Anfilogy Alexeevich Semevsky. The first belongs to a different
surname, the second confirms only the use of a rare personal name. Neither a profile
nor a map point is created for them. This path has been closed deliberately: what matters more now are
the old surname alphabets of fonds 388 and 210 and extracts on the proven neighbours of
the Oryol book.

[ВГД thread on the alphabets of fond 286](https://forum.vgd.ru/252/60399/0.htm),
[open folder with the scans](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1cVp5vGt6TddL0AbIiiA1zcYZl8H9-oyK?usp=sharing).

### The Rylsk branch gets a lower time limit

For the independent line of Evpat Ivanovich Anpilogov the route
Marmyzhi in Rylsk district → Mamon between the earlier census and the revision tales of 1745 has already been proven.
Now the fully published Rylsk desyatnya of 1632 has been checked. None
of the forms of the researched surname group has been found in it.

This does not mean that the family was not in Rylsk district: a desyatnya describes
a particular body of servicemen, not the whole population and not all holders. But the search
has become substantially narrower. Evpat's household should be looked for in the census, otkaznye and
pomestye books of Marmyzhi and neighbouring holdings, specifically between 1632 and 1719.
Thus the Rylsk route confirms an important general model: different branches
of Anpilogovs could have arisen and moved through different service centres, not out of
Oryol alone.

[The Rylsk desyatnya of 1632 as published by A. A. Tankov](https://kladoiskatel.5bb.ru/viewtopic.php?id=220&p=2).

### The reverse search of 1635: the published layer is exhausted, the original not yet

The new tactic has been tested on the file about the restoration of Oryol after the Lithuanian
devastation. From the article by I. E. Kushelev the exact mechanism has been established: in 1635 the
voivode did not simply recruit a new garrison but searched for the old Oryol men **in the
towns to which they had dispersed**, after which he returned them to their former
household plots. This potentially yields a rare route of the type **Oryol → temporary
town after the devastation → Oryol** and allows a change of service category to be noticed.

The full text of the article has now been checked for all the stems Anpil-, Anfil-, Onpil-,
Onfil-, Ampil- and Anpliog-. The target group is absent from the published surname-by-surname tables of streltsy,
Cossack-dragoons and gunners. Among the several netchiki (absentees) of 1635 cited by the
author are Matvey Malakhov, Rodion Moleev, Khariton
Pavlov, Kuzma Borodin, Timofey Zanin and Moisey Soyminov — also without
Anfilogovs.

This is not a negative result for the file itself. The author does not print the lists of 1635
in full and explicitly excludes deti boyarskie from his study.
Therefore the published layer is exhausted, but the archival one is not. The address needed:
**РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12, pt. 1, d. 65, fols. 156–157, 189, 268, 305–308 and
321–325v.** Additional control — fols. 131, 322, 378–379 and 431.

At the same time a false digital double has been definitively cut off. The open ВГД card
with the number "d. 65" describes **f. 210, op. 4, d. 65** — the desyatnya of Bryansk,
Starodub, Pochep and Roslavl of 1631. This is not the Oryol file from inventory 12 and
not its photocopy. In the public database of 421,095 rows the required call number 210-12-65
is absent altogether, so a search for «Анпи» in this database does not check the archival
folios.

Practical conclusion: the reverse lists remain a working direction, but it is pointless to
trawl the article and the general index again. The next step is only a
targeted photocopy of the specified folios; the second pass through it should go not just
by surname but by the full formulas **Vasily Dmitrievich, Alexey Vasilyevich,
Mark Karpovich and Afanasy Osipovich**, and then by the names Grigory, Ivan, Kuzma
and Fyodor in all service categories. Until there is an individual line, the map does not
change.

[I. E. Kushelev's open article](https://e-notabene.ru/view_article.php?aurora=0&id_article=69870&logged=0&nb=1),
[description of another file with the same number on ВГД](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/252/21016/p3639826.htm#pp3639826).

### A new move: two unindexed files are read from the frames themselves

The repeated search by surname variants has been stopped where it ceased to yield
anything new: what was checked was not another search string but the contents of a recently opened folder for
fond 210. This is how a complete photocopy of **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12, d. 59**
for 1635–1636 was discovered. The official inventory shows two important
mechanisms inside the file at once: on fols. 93–102 — the enrolment and recruitment of new servicemen, on
fols. 169–185 — the payment of salaries to the gunners, zatinshchiki and smiths transferred
to permanent residence from Sevsk to Oryol.

The last block has been viewed in full, frame by frame. It proves a real channel
**Sevsk → Oryol** for a specific service group, but no confident form
Anpilogov / Anfilogov / Onpilogov / Onfilogov has yet been read in the difficult handwriting.
This is not a negative finding: the list has to be marked up line by line.
No family route is created on the map, because a general transfer of gunners does not
mean the transfer of an Anfilogov and already belongs to the restored Oryol.

A stronger find is **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12, d. 95, fols. 166–186**.
The official inventory singles out here a separate Oryol section of 1637–1638 in
the file about the Tatar incursion, the devastation and the dispatch of servicemen. Both
needed parts of the photocopy have been obtained, and the whole block from fol. 166 to fol. 186 has been isolated: these are twenty-one
folios of dense personal and service entries. For the exact call number `210-12-95`
the public database of 421,095 rows gives **zero**, so the queries «Анпи», «Ампи»,
«Анфи», «Онпи» and «Онфи» had previously not checked this source at all.

The initial review of all the frames is complete; a confident surname line has not yet been
read. A new pass is being made by full family signatures — name,
patronymic, surname form, oklad and neighbours — with control by Vasily
Dmitrievich, Ivan and Alexey Vasilyevich and Mark Karpovich. Chronologically
the file stands exactly between the list of 1627/28 with four proven bearers of
the group and the file of 1642 with Fyodor Anfilogov. Therefore it may not only give
a new name but also link two generations or preserve a formula of former service.

[Public cloud with the scans](https://disk.yandex.ru/d/beTOHQ7SI7x1RQ),
[description and folio boundaries of file 95](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1851/142603/p5034858.htm),
[official РГАДА inventory](http://rgada.info/poisk/index2.php?str=210-opis_12-1).

### A change of direction: the service lists of file 96 and the exact Oryol folios

The neighbouring **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 12, d. 96** turned out to be not a repetition of file 95 but
another type of source: service lists sent in by the voivodes after
the Tatar raid of 1637–1638. A note in the official inventory distributes
the parts of the stolbets among the towns and gives **fols. 179–204 for Oryol**. Thus
the primary target has been narrowed from the file's 384 folios to 26 folios.

The project «Дом предков» ("House of Ancestors") has published a derived list of 1,723 deciphered
appearances across the whole file. For Oryol it has 217 rows and 153 distinct literal
readings after removing exact repetitions. There are no forms Anf…, Amp…, Anp…, Onf… or Onp… in
the publication. However, the compiler himself warns that he included only
deciphered entries, and was able to establish the surname for about 57 per cent of the rows.
Therefore a zero result means only the absence of such a reading in an incomplete
index, not the absence of an Anfilogov in the original.

There is a useful quality control on the corpus. Among the Oryol men there again appear three
surnames from the short Oryol list of noviki of 1596: Kazarin Bulgakov,
the Penkovs Ivan, Fyodor, presumably Anton and undeciphered bearers, and also
Nevr Rtishchev. These are not relatives for whom profiles are needed, nor a proven
"convoy" of Anfilogovs. But the match shows that the file does indeed
preserve the long-standing Oryol service milieu and is suitable for checking
neighbours and generations.
The automatic reading of file 95 has been separately calibrated on five already known
lines of Anpilovs/Anfilogovs of 1642. Both ordinary OCR and an open model
for Russian historical handwritten text produced convincing-sounding late formulas and dates that are
absent from the scans. Such results have been completely
excluded from the name layer. For files 95 and 96 only line-by-line manual
reading remains, with verification of the whole formula and the neighbouring lines.

[Open index of file 96](https://dompredkov.ru/posluzhnye-spiski-ukrainnyh-gorodov-1637-1638gg/),
[the model tested and rejected for this handwriting](https://huggingface.co/Kansallisarkisto/cyrillic-large-handwritten),
[official РГАДА inventory](http://rgada.info/poisk/index2.php?str=210-opis_12-1).

#### A full audit of the open collection: file 96 is not there

The assumption that the photocopies might lie not in the Oryol folder but in a neighbouring
town section of the large open collection has been checked to the end. A recursive
search went through **3,033 directories and 332,499 objects** to a depth of 7. Neither a path nor a
file name with the exact call number `210-12-96` or the designation `д. 96` was found. This is no longer
a cursory look at a single folder but a negative result for the whole public
collection. There is no point in repeating this route until a new version of the collection
appears.

On the «Дом предков» page there remains one open fragment of the file measuring
690 × 828 pixels. Neither a folio number nor a town name is visible in the crop,
so it cannot be attributed to the Oryol fols. 179–204. The new working path is
to look for quotations and attachments relating to individual Oryol servicemen, to localise
fragments by the sequence of names, or to obtain copies of fols. 179–204 specifically.

A check of the site's media library also closed the hope of a hidden original: the two
WordPress attachments No. 6158 and 6160 have the same 155,035 bytes and the same
checksum. These are two addresses of one image, not a fragment and a full
folio.

### A new unit of search: not a surname but a family scene

When the exact search for Anpilogovs runs up against incomplete indexes, it is more useful to look
not for an individual match of letters but for the cluster **personal name — patronymic — third
naming — son — neighbouring household**. This change of direction at once yielded two
independent name nests of the 1620s.

The cadastral book of Totma district of 1622 records **Khomyakov Kalistatka
Onfilofyev syn** of the village of Bychenevo, also known as Kolotovskoe and Khomyakovo, in Uftyuga
volost: РГАДА, f. 1209, kn. 480/486, fol. 359. Here «Онфилофьев сын» ("son of Onfilofy") is
a literal patronymic, and Khomyakov is a separate family naming. The match
Khomyakov — Khomyakovo is interesting but does not prove that Kalistatka was the founder of
the village.

The Cherdyn find is even stronger. In the open manuscript copy of the cadastral book
of Mikhail Kaysarov of 1623/1624, on **a single fol. 95**, stand two men at once:
**Ovdokimko Anfiloev syn Onshakov** in the village of Kondratyeva and **Mitka
Onfilofyev syn Onshakov da syn evo Fedodko** ("and his son Fedodko") in the Chizhovsky pochinok (new settlement) on the Vishera.
The latter formula preserves three generations: Onfilof → Mitka → Fedodko. The shared
form Onshakov and the neighbourhood make it possible to test the hypothesis of kinship between Ovdokimko and
Mitka, but the book does not call them brothers, and their fathers' names are recorded differently.

These northern families do not automatically lead to Oryol. Their significance is methodological and
onomastic: they show a living mechanism in which the rare name of a senior
man becomes his son's patronymic, simultaneously with another family naming.
For 16th-century Oryol, priority now goes to documents where for a single person
one can catch precisely such a double formula or a son named alongside, and not
merely one more spelling of a ready-made surname.

[Publication of the Totma table](https://www.phantastike.com/toponymy/proishozhd_nazvaniy_dereven_rus_severa/pdf/),
[RSL catalogue entry for the manuscript](https://search.rsl.ru/ru/record/01004257022),
[open copy of the manuscript](https://viewer.rsl.ru/rsl01004257022),
[line-by-line transcription of the Cherdyn book](https://forum.vgd.ru/post/1865/186827/p5552262.htm).

### A change of question: not "where the noviki came from" but "whose relatives they were"

The full Oryol section of the list of 1627/1628 made it possible to see not four
simultaneous appearances of the surname but two service generations. **Vaska Dmitreev
syn Onpilogov** on fol. 123 and **Ivashko Vasilyev syn Onfilogov** on fol. 125 stand
among the old town deti boyarskie. **Oleshka Vasilyev syn Onfilogov** on
fol. 128 and **Marka Karpov syn Onfilokhov** on fol. 133 are in a separate heading
«Новики 130-го году» ("Noviki of the year 130"), that is, they were enrolled at the great review of 1621/1622.
Their oklady differed: Alexey had 200 chetverti, Mark 100.

The rules of this review change the meaning of the find. The voivodes were to take into account
the fathers' pomestya and service — «как было быть отцу его на службу» ("how his father was to be in service"); accepting the children
of non-serving fathers was forbidden. Consequently, Alexey and Mark continued already
existing service lines of Vasily and Karp. They are not evidence of
a new resettlement of Anfilogovs to Oryol in the 1620s.

The same principle turned up a generation earlier. The instruction for the desyatnya of 1596, in
which **Pavel Terentyev syn Anfilogov** is recorded, ordered enrolment «от отцов
детей и от братьев братью, и от дядей племянников» ("sons from fathers, brothers from brothers, and nephews from uncles"). So Pavel too entered
the Oryol corporation as the son, brother or nephew of a man already in service.
The source does not name this relative, but by 1594/1595 two groups of Anfilogov brothers
were already living in Bazdyrevo. This makes family continuity
much more likely than a late solitary arrival.

In this way the gap has been narrowed. The puzzle now lies not between the 1590s and the 1620s but
**before 1594**: where the elder Mikula, Vasily, Terenty and Karp served and by
what act their relatives received the first Oryol pomestya. The rules of
enrolment do not name the town of origin, so neither Solova, nor Ryazhsk, nor Bolkhov,
nor Ryazan has yet been plotted as a family route.

[V. N. Kozlyakov's study and the texts of the enrolment rules](https://www.vostlit.info/Texts/Dokumenty/Russ/XVII/1620-1640/Prisjaga_novikov_1628/pred.htm),
[open name index to the publication of the 1627/28 list](https://oldbasman.ru/f/almanax-13_boyare_spiski_imena.pdf).

### A turn to the biographies of rare names: who "Onfil Seliverstov" was

A search not by surname but by the career of a rare bearer of the name has revealed a previously
faceless line of the Razryad book of 1556/57. The dyak whom three manuscript
copies call **Onfim, Olfim and Onfil Seliverstov** turned out to be
**Anfim, son of the priest Silvestr** — the addressee of the paternal instruction in the
Domostroy.

The identity is established not by resemblance of sound but by primary records. A donation of 1546
directly names «благовещенского попа Селивестра и сына его Анфима» ("the Annunciation priest Selivestr and his son Anfim"); a
similar formula is repeated in 1552. After Silvestr's withdrawal to the
Kirillo-Belozersky Monastery, another manuscript was inscribed: «Сию книгу прислал
с Москвы Анфим к отцу своему Селивестру в Кирилов монастырь» ("This book was sent from Moscow by Anfim to his father Selivestr at the Kirillov monastery"). So
Selivestrov here is a transparent "son of Silvestr", and Anfim/Onfim/Onfil are
variants of the rendering of one personal name.

The biography is unexpectedly rich. Anfim was a major merchant with
foreign connections; by the early 1550s he had become a senior dyak of the treasury
department, in 1556/57 he served in Moscow, and in 1561–1566 he was a dyak in
Smolensk. In the summer of 1566 he was still alive and took part in the council on the Livonian
War; records of 1568/69 already allow him to be considered dead. Two pages
of publications with the primary formulas have been saved in the project and put into a separate
Record.

For the Oryol origin this is a useful negative result. The rare name
lived at the very top of the Moscow chancery and merchant milieu in the year Oryol was founded,
but Anfim's known career contains no Oryol land, no sons with the
form Anfilogov and no part in staffing the fortress. He cannot be declared
the founder of the family. On the other hand, the case proves the method: every pre-surname Onfil or
Anfim requires a separate biography, because behind a short line there may
hide a well-documented man of quite another social milieu.

[Primary donation records in the publication by N. N. Rozov](https://ruslang.ru/doc/lingistoch/1966/13-rozov.pdf),
[biographical study by I. V. Kurukin](https://djvu.online/file/6eRgvNre05ra8).

### A new search by document genre: the Anfilovskaya village and the sheepskin-maker Anfilofy Novik

After the surname queries had been exhausted, a new full-text layer of the
official inventories of РГАДА and of the Archive of the St Petersburg Institute of History of the Russian Academy of Sciences (SPbII РАН) was checked. The search was built not only on
Anfilogov but on the formulas «ввозная» ("letter of installation"), «отдельная» ("allotment"), «верстанье» ("enrolment"), «испомещён» ("granted a pomestye"),
«сошёл» ("left") and «отцовское поместье» ("father's pomestye"). No new document about a 16th-century Oryol
resettlement was found in the РГАДА inventories. The formula found about granting a pomestye on moving from Oryol
to Stary Oskol relates to Frol Vorobyov in 1693 and serves as a late
control of the mechanism, not as a route of the researched family.

The search in the SPbII РАН Archive, however, yielded two previously unrecorded entries of the fond of the
Antoniev-Siysky Monastery.

On **15 September 1573** Yakov Ivanov syn Shalyapin and Ivan Danilov syn
exchanged "scraps of land" **in the Anfilovskaya village**. Exact address:
SPbII РАН Archive, f. 5, op. 1, d. 554, 1 fol. The possessive form naturally
goes back to the name Anfil or the naming Anfilov, but does not reveal a specific
eponym. This is a new independent toponymic example of the 16th century, not yet connected
either with the Tver Onfilogovo or with Oryol.

On **11 February 1575** the sheepskin-maker of the Glinki posad **Anfilofy Novik Ivanov
syn** sold to Semyon Nikitin syn Sobakin half a household and household plot on
the Gostinny bereg in Glinki. Exact address: SPbII РАН Archive, f. 5, op. 1,
d. 567, 1 fol. The name Anfilofy is recorded directly; Ivanov syn is a patronymic, and
Novik is an additional naming whose type will become clear only from the
original deed of purchase. The word «овчинник» ("sheepskin-maker") for the first time gives one of the early bearers of the
name a precise posad craft occupation.

The search snippet wrongly attached the dates of neighbouring files to both lines. The dates
15 September 1573 and 11 February 1575 have been checked against whole pages of the
official inventory; images of the pages have been saved. Separate Records
`PUB-SPBII-1573-ANFILOVSKAYA-VILLAGE` and
`PUB-SPBII-1575-GLINKI-ANFILOFIY-NOVIK` have been created, and for the directly named man a
profile `P99688`. The entries have not been added to the map: the northern bearer of the name is not
connected with the surname group and does not report a movement to Oryol.

The find does not settle the origin of the Oryol core, but it strengthens an important
distinction: in the 1570s the personal name Anfilofy, the toponym Anfilovskaya and
dependent namings that were still taking shape all existed at the same time. Therefore the
search form «Анфилов» may denote a person, a patronymic or a place name,
and not necessarily one hereditary surname. For Oryol the decisive evidence
will remain only a full formula with a former town, a relative or the transfer
of an old pomestye.

[Official inventory of fond 5 of the SPbII РАН](https://spbiiran.ru/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/rs_005_01_fund_antoniev_siiskii_monastyr.pdf),
[new full-text search of the SPbII РАН](https://spbiiran.sovadev.pro/?r=%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%84%D0%B8%D0%BB%D0%BE%D1%84),
[full-text search of РГАДА inventories](https://rgada.sovadev.pro/).

### Testing the "service imprint": a rare name without a family does not work

The dynamic database of 421,095 service records has been checked once more, now not by
the ready-made surname but by the rare personal names of the older generation of the Oryol
core. For the 1580s and 1590s, **Mikula, Nechay, Pakhom,
Persha and Terenty** were searched separately.

The result revealed an important bias in the corpus. For Mikula, six lines of
the 1580s and four lines of the 1590s were found; for Nechay — 31 and eight; for Terenty — two
lines of the 1580s. But all the hits come from the Novgorod pyatinas: files
РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 301, 716, 725, 727 and 974, and also f. 137,
op. 1, d. 11. For Pakhom and Persha there are no lines. With the 1590s filter the
forms **Anf-, Anp-, Onf-, Onp-, Amp-, Opil- and Onil-** also gave zero.

This does not prove the absence of Oryol men. On the contrary, the control shows
that the table hardly covers the relevant southern layer: it contains neither the known
Oryol book of 1594/95 nor the name list of the two hundred Oryol men of 1580,
whereas the same Novgorod books are represented many times over. Therefore
searching for a single "rare" name here creates dozens of false candidates and does not
bring one closer to the family's homeland.

The tactics have been narrowed again. For Mikula, Pakhom, Nechay, Persha and Terenty only a
**family scene** is now accepted: a matching name and patronymic, a named
son or brother, shared land, a suretyship or a literal note of a former
town. A lone Mikula or Nechay outside such a scene is no longer considered
a candidate. The check has been entered in the Record
`PUB-RK-1580-1581-OREL-HUNDRED-RZHEV-NOVGOROD`; the map does not change.

[Dynamic database of servicemen](https://next-fast-table-gold.vercel.app/).

### Land instead of a list: the Ryazhsk recruitment of 1592

After the dead end with rare names, the search has moved from name indexes to late
land files capable of quoting lost 16th-century charters. An РГАДА catalogue
card already pointed to an extract of 20 March 1700 (f. 281, op. 17,
d. 12222) which sets out charters of 1592 and 1597. Now the
printed text of the report has also been found and checked.

The petitions of Metropolitan Avraamy of Ryazan of 8 (18) June 1699 and
11 (22) September 1700 reported that in 1592, by decree of Tsar Fyodor
Ivanovich, there were taken from Archbishop Mitrofan **«к Ряскому городу новоприборным
казакам»** ("for the town of Ryassk, for the newly recruited Cossacks") the village of Korenevoe, Podvelemye, the village of Sekirino, the hamlet of Gumennaya and
the village of Pupki. The tract comprised 3,850 chetverti of ploughland, fallow and wild field;
in exchange the church was given the former votchina of the boyar Fyodor Vasilyevich Sheremetev in
Makovsky stan of Kolomna district. The quotation was printed by I. V. Dobrolyubov in
1885 (vol. 2, p. 172); it is repeated in the reference book «Скопин от А до Я» [Skopin from A to Z],
vol. 1, pp. 92, 315, 654, and vol. 2, pp. 425, 518. Pages 92 and 425 have been checked
visually and saved as photocopies.

**Names and meaning.** Fyodor Ivanovich, Archbishop Mitrofan, Metropolitan
Avraamy and Fyodor Vasilyevich Sheremetev are named in the history of the land exchange,
but there is no name-by-name list of Cossacks in the quotation. On the other hand, the expression «новоприборные» ("newly recruited")
directly designates a recently recruited service group, which the state
settled all at once on a large compact tract. The event falls
exactly on the biographical gap of Pavel Terentyev syn Ievlev: two
suretyships in Ryazhsk in 1591, then absence from the desyatni of 1594 and
1597, whereas in 1596 Pavel Terentyev syn
Anfilogov appears in Oryol. This strengthens the new tactic but not the identity of the persons: Pavel in
is not named in the land quotation.

The next priority is not a general search result for the surname but the files on Korenevoe,
Podvelemye, Sekirino, Gumennaya and Pupki: otkaznye, installation, survey and
dispute extracts of the 17th–18th centuries. It is in them that the
original grants may be retold with the names of Cossacks, second namings and a former
town. The information has been entered in the Record
`PUB-RYAZHSK-1591-OREL-1594-BAZDYREV-NETWORK` and the profile `P0048`; the map has not
been changed, because the document does not show a direct movement of a bearer of the researched
group.

The РГАДА full-text index has been checked for all five names and for Ievlev with
a filter for fond 281 — zero matches. Without the filter dozens of unrelated
Korenevs, Sekerins and Pupki appear. This is a useful limit: the relevant inventory has not entered the
readable corpus, so a further general full-text search would again be a
dead end. The work switches to viewing neighbouring archival call numbers in
f. 281, op. 15 and 17.

#### Ryazhsk may have been an intermediate node: the Pronsk Cossacks of Sekirino

A side search through the five sloboda settlements gave a more substantial result than a new
surname search. In a charter of Tsar Vasily Shuisky sent to Mikhaylov and Pronsk
in August 1606, the parties to a land dispute are named **«пронские де казаки
Осипова приказу Секерина Ненашевой сотни Колышкина»** ("the Pronsk Cossacks, it is said, of Osip Sekerin's prikaz, of Nenash Kolyshkin's hundred"). Next is named
the pyatidesyatnik Kostya Letovskoy with his companions. The archival address of the quoted
act: **РГАДА, f. Pomestny Prikaz, Ryazan, stb. 40947, pt. 1, fols. 56–57**.

The hierarchy is fully legible: the strelets commander (golova) Osip Sekirin — the sotnik Nenash
Kolyshkin — the pyatidesyatnik Kostya Letovskoy. The reference book links this group with
the early settlement of Sekirino and reports that subsequently the Cossacks of the sloboda, like
other regimental Cossacks beyond the Verda, were subordinated to Ryazhsk. This is the author's
reconstruction, but the literal designation **«пронские казаки»** ("Pronsk Cossacks") belongs to
the act of 1606 itself.

A full check of the 1898 edition corrected the reference and revealed the mechanism of
recruitment. The late reference book points to p. 152, but that page contains the end of
Pekhlets stan; the Pronsk list is printed on **pp. 260–261**. In the Cossack
sloboda three subdivisions are listed:

- Ivan Kharitonov's hundred — 101 households and 103 men;
- Dmitry Molchanov's hundred — 73 households;
- Nenash Kolyshkin's hundred — the sotnik's household and 56 Cossack households.

The next page explains that this was an unfinished recruitment. By
the sovereign's decree **300 Cossacks** were to serve in Pronsk, each
being entitled to 30 chetverti, 9,000 chetverti of land in all. Only **235 men** with 7,050 chetverti
were actually present. For the **65 Cossacks still lacking**, 1,950 chetverti had been
set aside in advance; the Cossack commander Vasily Balakirev was ordered
to keep this land reserve until a new decree.

This is the first direct quantitative model of ongoing recruitment in the relevant
time corridor. In 1596–1598 the town neighbouring Ryazhsk was still actively
filling up its numbers and had vacant service places available together with land.
Therefore the disappearance of an individual Ryazhsk serviceman cannot be sought
only as an official "transfer": he may have ended up within a new recruitment,
a change of prikaz or a subsequently resubordinated sloboda. The targeted next
layer is the files about filling the 65 places and the transition from the prikaz of Vasily Balakirev to
the prikaz of Osip Sekirin.

The original manuscript has also ceased to be unknown. The catalogue of digitised files
shows **РГАДА, f. 1209, op. 1, d. 354, fols. 1–478** — the cadastral book of
Pereyaslavl-Ryazansky, Pronsk, and the Kamensky and Okologorodny stans of
1597–1598, printed inventory No. 2055. The official identifier of the digital
card is `10000393580014`; the relevant list is on fols. 15–16. Next to it
**d. 396, fols. 1–776**, with payment books of 1594–1597, has been digitised,
identifier `10000393829014`. Now a targeted order is formulated not as a
search for a Pronsk list in general but as a viewing of fols. 15–16 and the neighbouring corrections,
followed by a search for attached otkaznye and installation records about the 65 vacancies.

An access check on 31 August 2026 clarified the limit of this task. The old
official РГАДА viewer opened the card for d. 354 with an empty gallery, and
the exact card in the ГИС УИАД did not connect either through the project's browser or by a direct
request to `online.archives.ru`. So fols. 15–16 remain for now an exact
archival target but not a primary source we have read; the figures 300, 235 and 65
still rest on the verified printed edition.

On the origin of Oryol Anfilogovs this acts not as a ready-made route
but as a correction to the method. The formula of 1592 «к Ряскому городу новоприборным
казакам» ("for the town of Ryassk, for the newly recruited Cossacks") does not require all recipients to have been natives of Ryazhsk. A service
corporation could have been assembled from Pronsk, Pekhlets stan and other neighbouring
centres and then counted as a Ryazhsk one. Therefore Pavel Terentyevich must now
be sought not only in the Ryazhsk desyatni but also in the Pronsk hundreds, and in the
land files of Korenevo, Podvelemye, Sekirino, Gumennaya and Pupki.

The limit of the conclusion is unchanged: the act of 1606 does not name Pavel Ievlev,
Anfilogovs or the original recipients of 1592. No route
Pronsk — Ryazhsk — Oryol is put on the map.

[Vol. 1 of «Скопин от А до Я» (Skopin from A to Z)](https://www.delehovo.ru/i0/t1.pdf),
[vol. 2](https://www.delehovo.ru/i0/t2.pdf),
[«Писцовые книги Рязанского края» (Cadastral Books of the Ryazan Region), vol. I, issue 1](https://archive.org/details/pistsovyiaknigi00storgoog),
[digital card for d. 354](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000001151014/10000005098014/10000393580014/),
[digital card for d. 396](https://online.archives.ru/guide/10000000001014/10000001151014/10000005098014/10000393829014/),
[digital copy of Dobrolyubov in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000439_000034_RZN%7C%7C%7CBIBL%7C%7C%7C0000167996/).

### Oryol — Ust-Ublya: a direct service corridor of 1571 found

The search was moved from the ready-made surname to the organisation of distant field detachments.
This gave an earlier and more specific result. The boyar verdict of
21 February 1571 appointed the third field commander **from Dedilov to the Oskol,
to the mouth of the Ublya**. With him was to stand a mixed detachment from several
frontier (ukrainnye) towns. The Oryol quota is named literally: **10 deti boyarskie and
20 Cossacks from Oryol**.

The exact address of the original: **РГАДА, f. 210, op. 6, d. 1, fols. 39–46**. This is
the Watch Book (Storozhevaya kniga) of 1571–1592, open on the archive's website. The printed text is
«Акты Московского государства» [Acts of the Muscovite State], vol. I, No. 4, pp. 6–7. The official images of
fols. 44v–46 have been checked and saved in the project.

This is not a one-off paper norm. The field service list of 1577 shows
that under Mikhail Eskov of Kashira, on the Oskol at the mouth of the Ublya, there really
stood deti boyarskie from Tula, Plavsk, Solova and **Oryol**. The names of the rank and file are again
not revealed, but the Oryol — Ust-Ublya channel itself is confirmed in two different years.

For Danilko Onpilogov, a regimental Cossack of Oskol in 1615, this is an important new
context. Between early Oryol and the area of the future Oskol, forty-four
years before his entry, a regular service exchange already existed. Therefore
Danilko's Oryol origin is no longer an arbitrary connection of
two points; it is a testable hypothesis along a specific administrative channel.
But he cannot yet be called an Oryol man: the list of 1571 gives only town
quotas, not surnames, and field service is not equal to permanent resettlement.

There is an old arithmetical problem in the source's figures. The individual lines
and the total formula do not reconcile 46 deti boyarskie, 60 Cossacks and the grand total of 100;
the editor has already marked the last figure with `sic`. The text should not be corrected
by guesswork. What is needed is a separate palaeographic analysis of all the quotas on fols. 44v–46
and a comparison with the subsequent lists.

The next search has become targeted: not "Anpilogov in Oskol" in general but
**the executive name lists of the third commander**, duplicates of the Oryol quota of
10+20, the papers of Dedilov and Ust-Ublya of 1571, and then the entry for Mikhail Eskov
of 1577. If such a list has survived, it should be compared simultaneously
with the Oryol book of 1594/95 and the Oskol books of 1615–1626.

[Printed text of the acts of 1571](https://yakovkrotov.com/acts/16/dvor/1571_amg.htm),
[official РГАДА viewer of the Watch Book](http://rgada.info/kueh/index2.php?str=210_6_1),
[catalogue entry for vol. I in the НЭБ](https://rusneb.ru/catalog/000202_000043_80/).

### The first proven family route: Oskol — Tsarev-Alekseev, 1647/48

A search by first name, and not only by the beginning of the surname, led to the continuation of the Oskol
trail. In 1647/48 in Tsarev-Alekseev is recorded **the regimental Cossack Danilo
Levontyev syn Anpilov, brought over from Oskol**. Another folio reports that he was
selected with his family for permanent residence in Novy Oskol; with him are two different sons,
